# Decisions

## 2026-10-29 — Timer loop: the 1 s backoff applies only to sources that do not move `next()`

- **The F3 contract, not an every-fire cap.** `DeadlineSource.fire`'s contract is "must move `next()` past `now`, or the loop backs off 1 s and logs". `clock/loop.ts` originally also clamped every re-fire to `lastFire + 1 s`, which throttled a correct source that does move its deadline (eng-views' 50 ms ticker registered as `views`) to one fire a second. The per-fire clamp is removed; a stuck source still backs off through `backoffUntil`, and sustained spinning is bounded by the wake budget (`maxWakesPerHour`), which spaces out a source's fires once it exceeds it — the two contract-sanctioned rate limits.
- **The wake budget is per source and counts fires (review, 2026-10-29).** One shared 3,600-an-hour budget throttled eng-views' 20 Hz tick to about 1 Hz after three minutes and let one runaway source delay the outbox by up to an hour. Each source now has its own budget (default 100,000 an hour, above the 72,000 a 50 ms tick needs), counted per fire so a direct fire cannot dodge it, and a source fires at most once per pass so an inching `next()` yields to the event loop.
- **`stop()` waits for a fire in flight** (up to `STOP_GRACE_MS`, 10 s) so `Engine.close` does not close the store under a send mid-fire.
- **A host-injected `deadlines` wins.** `clock/module.ts` provides `services.deadlines` only when it is the `absent()` stand-in. A host or test that injects a registry through `EngineOptions.ports` owns it entirely, including lifecycle — the injected object may be a bare `DeadlineRegistry` with no `start`/`stop`.

## 2026-10-28 — Test mode: only on data made for the run, never in release builds

- **Release builds compile test mode out.** `scripts/build-main.mjs` defines `__APLOS_TEST_MODE__` as false when `APLOS_RELEASE_BUILD=1` (`release:build`, `release:publish`), so a distributed app ignores `APLOS_TEST` and `APLOS_DATA_DIR` and behaves normally, which is what plan 40's `verify-hardening` checks. Local `npm run package` builds and development runs keep test mode, and agents verify packaged behaviour on those (freeze R2H-4).
- **Only a directory made for the run.** With `APLOS_TEST=1`, `APLOS_DATA_DIR` must exist and resolve (symlinks followed) inside the system temp folder or an explicit `APLOS_TEST_ROOT`. It must not be that folder itself, `/`, home, or the real or preview Aplos data folder. A folder that holds a mailbox key without the `.aplos-test` marker is refused. The marker is written on first start, so a restart of the same run works (freeze R2H-1).

## 2026-10-28 — Revamp Phase 0 artifacts F1-F5 and F7 (freeze candidate)

- **zod/mini, types-only in the engine.** The contract's schemas use `zod/mini` (about 35 KB against full zod's 188 KB in a neutral bundle). The engine core imports contract *types* and the zod-free error classes only; transports validate with `parseArgs` at the host boundary (ARCH 9.3; plan 10 section 3's named fallback), because the runtime schemas plus zod cost about 158 KB of the 400,000-byte budget.
- **Engine budget measured without `legacy/`.** The portability test applies the no-Node rule to all of `packages/engine/src`, but the 400,000-byte check counts only the new engine (UTF-8 bytes, unminified) and forbids new code from importing `legacy/`, which Phase 2 deletes.
- **Contract naming.** Every method is `Type/verb` (`Mail/release`, `Mail/toggleQuotes` rather than `Mail/frame.release`), so the typed client is `api.<type>.<verb>`. Binary moves as `Blob/put` then a `blobId`, never base64. Scopes: `read`, `triage`, `draft`, `send`, `ai`, `admin`, `host`. Submissions add `scheduled`. `conflict` is a reply error type, not a governor error class (adapters return conflicts as results). No key characters (`last4`) leave the engine.
- **Ports (F3)** follow the Providers thread's proposal v2 with the changes listed in STATUS.md (one adapter per account, results for conflicts, only proof makes a send not-sent, one placements list, governor-owned token refresh, `reconnectBy` computed by the host), plus every fix from the Providers review (`packages/engine/src/ports.review-answers.md`): connect ignores auth blocks; per-account monotonic sweep generations with `SweepBound.until` and a `sweep` write in the same commit; the overdue rule applies to queued submissions only, and a submission found `sending` at start becomes `uncertain` and runs `findSent` first.
- **Store (F4).** Schema 0001 is ARCH 3.2 plus the Providers review's S1-S11 and the later additions; `store.commit` is synchronous, data-only and idempotent per required `opId`; `seq` moves once per client-visible commit; `changes` is keyed `(seq, type, id)`; rollups are computed in the store, never taken from a batch.
- **Logger (F7).** The catalog is plain data checked at runtime; its zod schemas are used in tests. Addresses are hashed with a salted synchronous SHA-256 (first 16 hex digits); free text is redacted aggressively and anything over 2,000 characters is replaced whole.
- **UI gaps.** Every gap the UI threads sent is answered in `packages/contract/GAPS.md`.

## 2026-10-27 — Revamp Phase 0 step 1: legacy folders and workspaces

- **Moved, not rewritten.** Today's engine is `packages/engine/src/legacy/` (`@aplos/engine`), the five shared files it needs (`types`, `ask`, `mailbox`, `summary`, `live`) are `packages/contract/src/legacy/` (`@aplos/contract`), and today's renderer is `apps/desktop/src/renderer/legacy/`. All three are read-only reference until Phase 2 deletes them (roadmap 1.1, plan 10 E1 item 1).
- **No re-export stubs in `src/shared`.** The plan allowed one-line stubs so importers compile unchanged. The codemod (`scripts/codemod-imports.mjs`) rewrote every importer to `@aplos/contract/legacy/*` instead, so a stub would have no importer, and leaving a file at the old path would stop git from recording the five moves as renames (`git log --follow` would lose their history). `src/shared/version.ts` stays in the app.
- **`@aplos/engine/legacy/*`, not `@aplos/engine/*`.** Imports of legacy code say so (`@aplos/engine/legacy/discover`), so the new engine's own paths stay free and Phase 2 can find every legacy importer with one search.
- **Internal source packages.** Each package's `exports` maps `./legacy/*` to TypeScript source; there is no build step. tsconfig `paths` and a Vite alias resolve the same files. `scripts/build-main.mjs` bundles `@aplos/*` and keeps every third-party package external (esbuild's `packages:'external'` would have left `@aplos/*` out of the app). `@aplos/*` are dev dependencies of `@aplos/desktop`, so electron-builder never copies their source into `app.asar`; third-party packages they use (today `fast-xml-parser`) stay declared in `apps/desktop/package.json` too, because that is the manifest electron-builder reads.
- **Provider types in the contract.** `AccountPreset`, `ProviderCapabilities`, `ProviderKind` and `ServerSettings` moved to `packages/contract/src/legacy/provider-types.ts`; the engine's `provider.ts` re-exports them. The contract never imports the engine.
- **`appVersion` through `EngineOptions`.** The engine no longer reads the app's `package.json`; the host (`src/main/service.ts`) passes it.
- **Portability test.** It moved to `packages/engine/tests/`, bundles every `.ts` file under `src/` at any depth (instead of one entry file, which nothing provides yet), and has a negative test that plants a `node:fs` import two folders deep. The orchestration run with the Node store, staged memory and Groq stays in the app as `tests/engine-host.test.ts`.
- **Engine size, measured.** The neutral bundle of everything under `packages/engine/src` (all legacy today) is about 353,500 characters unminified (the test's measure; path comments move it by a few hundred) and 201,361 bytes minified, the same as before the move, against the 400,000 budget. MOBILE.md's 146,544 minified figure is from when the engine had 11 modules. The freeze review should decide whether the budget applies to the new engine entry without `legacy/` (which Phase 2 deletes), because legacy plus zod plus new code would not fit.
- **Packaged-app proof without a window.** `scripts/verify-release.mjs` shows the app's window, which the headless rule forbids, so step 1 was proved by `npm run package` plus a content check of `app.asar` instead. The full run waits for F8's hidden test mode (`APLOS_TEST=1`).

## 2026-10-24 — OAuth clients built into releases

- **Closes the open decision on where OAuth client config lives for a public release.** The v0.2.0 draft, installed on a second Mac, could not connect Google: a packaged app read only `~/.config/aplos-mail/.env`, which exists only on the owner's machine.
- **Embedded at build time from repository secrets.** Google's "Desktop app" client type treats the client secret as non-confidential (an installed app cannot keep a secret; Google documents this, and PKCE plus the loopback redirect protect the flow), so bundling it is the standard approach for desktop mail clients. Microsoft's public client has no secret at all. Alternatives rejected: asking each user to create a Cloud project (not workable for a download), and a hosted token-exchange server (an online dependency and a service to run, for no real secrecy gain).
- **How.** `scripts/oauth-defines.mjs` gives esbuild `define` values for `src/main/oauth-config.ts`: the Google client ID and secret and `MICROSOFT_CLIENT_ID`, read from the build environment only when `APLOS_RELEASE_BUILD=1` (`release:build` / `release:publish`). Every other build, `tsx` and the tests inline nothing. Values live only in `dist/main/engine.cjs` in the asar, never in a tracked file or `package.json`; a test checks the repo tree after a placeholder build.
- **Precedence: environment, then built-in, then none.** An ID in `process.env` (dev `.env` or `~/.config/aplos-mail/.env`) wins together with its own secret, so the owner can test another client; the built-in pair is never mixed with an env value. Explicit adapter options (tests, emulator) still win over both.
- **Messages.** Without a client: "Google sign-in isn't available in this build." (Outlook likewise). Development builds add the `.env` hint; release builds don't. The renderer shows the engine's message (`capabilities.get` gained `googleUnavailable` / `microsoftUnavailable`) instead of its own hardcoded copy.
- **Release guard.** On CI a release build without the Google pair fails, unless `APLOS_ALLOW_NO_GOOGLE=1`. Local release builds warn. `MICROSOFT_CLIENT_ID` is optional until Outlook ships.
- **Still open.** Google verification for the restricted Gmail scopes. Until the consent screen is verified, only listed test users can finish sign-in, and Google shows its unverified-app warning.

## 2026-10-24 — Website: the app icon's brand, light and dark, clickable screenshots

- **Brand from the icon, not a palette.** Colors are sampled from `AppIcon.icon`'s rasters: the page opens and closes in its sky (`#9fd3ff`, dusk `#11263d` in dark), the airmail envelope's red and blue border frames each page, and the airmail blue is the primary button. Neutrals, ink and every app fragment use the app's own tokens, so page and screenshots match. This replaces the green accent and the always-dark site of 2026-10-22.
- **Light and dark follow the system, like the app.** Every screenshot exists in both appearances (`name.webp` dark, `name-light.webp` light) and `<picture>` serves the matching one.
- **Type.** Headings in Newsreader (self-hosted, OFL, optical sizes): a letter face for an airmail brand. Body text keeps the app's SF stack.
- **Screenshots are the demo.** The hero window and the calendar are app screenshots you click through, not screenshots with controls around them. Links are recorded by `scripts/capture-screenshots.mjs`, which presses every control in the real app (headless, throwaway storage, a fresh browser profile per screen, mark-as-read answered locally so the mailbox never changes) and keeps a link only if the app then matches a captured screen pixel for pixel. Screens that would mislead are left out: Settings (setup warnings in the browser preview), an event whose popover scrolls the grid, and Today.
- **Download page: built, hidden, sample data.** `/download/` detects the computer, starts the matching file, shows install steps and lists every version. It is unlinked and `noindex` until there is a public release, and never starts a download while `release.sample` is true. Its data should come from GitHub Releases at build time, with a deploy hook on publish; `VISION.md` has the plan and the reasons.
- **Nothing copied.** The inspiration sites (animations.dev, orchid.ai, aiforui.dev, akeru-bot.com, mastra.ai, email-sdk.dev, gradients.sh; aside.com, diabrowser.com and helium.computer for the download page) were looked at headlessly for feel only. Every drawing is made in code; the only image used is the app icon. No sparkle or wand glyph.

## 2026-10-23 — One app icon, from Icon Composer

The owner's `apps/desktop/assets/AppIcon.icon` is the only app icon. The Settings "App icon" picker (ink/sage/clay recolors of the old "a" mark) is removed with its `icon` setting: an illustrated icon does not recolor well, and macOS 26 lets people pick Default, Dark, Clear or Tinted for any Icon Composer icon. PNG exports for the site, README and in-app views are generated from the `.icon` (`npm run icon -w apps/desktop`), never edited by hand. Building needs Xcode 26 or later.

## 2026-10-23 — Release pipeline and auto-update

- **Artifacts.** electron-builder builds a DMG (what people download) and a ZIP (what electron-updater needs on macOS) for arm64 and x64, plus `latest-mac.yml`. Names have no spaces (`Aplos-Mail-X.Y.Z-arch.ext`), because GitHub rewrites spaces in asset names and the feed would point at files that do not exist. `npm run package` stays the fast this-Mac `dir` build; `npm run release:build` makes the full set. The asar, `extraResources` bin and `extendInfo` are unchanged.
- **Signing.** Hardened runtime with `build/entitlements.mac.plist` (allow-jit, allow-unsigned-executable-memory, disable-library-validation, audio-input), also used for helpers. The Swift symbol helper is now built universal (`lipo` of arm64 and x86_64 slices, macOS 12 minimum) so the Intel build is not shipping an arm64-only binary. It needs no `binaries` entry: electron-builder's signing pass walks the whole bundle, and the test build showed `Contents/Resources/bin/aplos-symbols` signed with the runtime flag like the app.
- **No certificate yet → ad-hoc, not unsigned.** `scripts/release-build.mjs` signs only when `CSC_LINK` (CI) or `CSC_NAME` (local) is set, and otherwise sets `CSC_IDENTITY_AUTO_DISCOVERY=false`, `mac.identity=-` and `notarize=false`. Ad-hoc signing goes a little further than "skip signing": an Apple Silicon app whose seal is broken (Info.plist edited after Electron's own ad-hoc signature) is reported as "damaged" after download, while an ad-hoc app can be opened with Open Anyway. Notarization uses electron-builder's built-in `notarize` with `APPLE_ID` / `APPLE_APP_SPECIFIC_PASSWORD` / `APPLE_TEAM_ID`; a partial set fails the build. Empty GitHub secrets are treated as absent.
- **Updater.** `electron-updater`, GitHub provider (notkainoa/aplos-mail), drafts publish as `releaseType: draft`. `src/main/updates.ts` holds the state machine (idle, checking, current, downloading, ready, error, disabled) with no Electron import, so it is tested with a fake updater. It checks 60 s after launch and every 6 h, downloads silently, and never installs on its own: `autoInstallOnAppQuit` is off, so even quitting does not swap the app. Restart runs the normal quit (drafts saved, engine closed) and only then calls `quitAndInstall`; if the owner cancels a draft prompt, the restart is cancelled too. Later hides the notice for that version until a manual check (menu or Settings) or next launch; the download stays cached.
- **When updates are off.** Development (unless `APLOS_UPDATE_DEV=1` with `dev-app-update.yml`), the browser preview, running from a mounted DMG or App Translocation, and any bundle without a Developer ID Team ID (read with `codesign --display`). Squirrel.Mac only installs an update signed by the same team as the running app, so unsigned and ad-hoc builds would fail at the swap; they say so in Settings → General and in the log instead. electron-updater is loaded only when updates can run.
- **Notice.** A persistent toast ("Aplos X.Y.Z is ready" · Restart · Later) on the existing toast axis. Toasts gained `persistent`, `key` and a quieter `secondary` action; persistent toasts keep their action when newer Undo toasts arrive. Only the main window shows it. No new icon.
- **Version.** One source: `apps/desktop/package.json`, imported through `src/shared/version.ts` (inlined by Vite and esbuild) instead of three hardcoded `0.1.0`s. The root package.json now carries the same version; `scripts/release.mjs` bumps both plus the lockfile, commits, tags and prints the push command. It never pushes.
- **CI.** `ci.yml` uses a `dorny/paths-filter` job so desktop jobs (macOS: typecheck, test, build) run for `apps/desktop/**`, `scripts/**` and shared root files, the website job (Ubuntu: typecheck, build) for `apps/web/**`, and both for root `package*.json`, `packages/**` and the workflow itself. Skipped jobs count as passing for branch protection. `release.yml` runs on `v*` tags, checks the tag matches the app version, tests, then `electron-builder --publish always`. Node 24 to match local development.
- **Google/Microsoft client IDs were runtime-only at first.** Superseded on 2026-10-24: release builds now carry the clients (see "OAuth clients built into releases").
## 2026-10-23 — AI keys in Settings and first-run setup (M40)

- **Where keys live.** Keys typed in Settings → AI go into `ai-keys.enc.json`, a vault file sealed per entry with the same keychain-protected data key (`engine-key.bin`, Electron safeStorage) as account credentials. They are never in app state, `state.json`, SQLite or logs. The engine only ever hands the renderer `{source:'saved'|'environment', last4}`, and `last4` only for keys of 12 characters or more.
- **Precedence: saved, then environment, then none.** A small resolver (`src/main/ai-keys.ts`) is passed to every provider as `apiKey:()=>keys.resolve(id)`; providers read it per request (`readKey` in `llm.ts`), so saving or removing a key works without a restart and `available` is now a getter. Without a resolver, providers still read their environment variable, so `.env`, the eval scripts and `verify-release --live-ai` are unchanged. Claude keeps one SDK client per key.
- **Paste never passes through the renderer.** In the desktop app, Paste is a main-process method (`ai.key.paste`) that reads the clipboard and forwards it straight to the engine's `ai.key.set`. Typing a key into the masked field does pass through the renderer once, at entry; the field is cleared as soon as it saves.
- **Test shows the provider's own words.** `ai.key.test` makes one cheap request: the model list for Groq, OpenAI, Gemini, Claude (one page), Ollama, LM Studio and a custom server; OpenRouter's `/key` (its model list is public); one 1-token completion for OpenCode Go (its model list also needs no key). Unlike every other AI error, a refused key shows the provider's `error.message`, with the key and anything key-shaped redacted, because that is what the owner needs to fix it. Saving runs the test automatically.
- **Errors point at Settings, not `.env`.** "Add your Groq key in Settings → AI to use Groq." and "… rejected the API key. Check it in Settings → AI." The summary card's explanations follow.
- **Browser preview.** No safeStorage there, so the store reports `secure:false` and every key field says keys can only be saved in the desktop app, naming the environment variable to use instead. Saving is refused by the engine with the same message.
- **OAuth client config stays build/environment-time.** `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`/`SECRET` and `MICROSOFT_CLIENT_ID` are not user-entered.
- **Welcome flow.** Replaces the single welcome card. Steps: Welcome (Add an account / Look around with demo mail) → the existing add-account dialog → AI (Groq preselected, a provider menu for the keyed providers, the same key field as Settings, a "Get a key" link, Skip for now) → Microphone (hold A / hold Space explained; Allow now via `systemPreferences.askForMediaAccess`, or Later; the status is read with `getMediaAccessStatus` so opening the step never prompts) → Done (Space, A, J K, ?). It runs once per data folder (`settings.onboardingDone`, already persisted) and reopens from Settings → Help & about → Show welcome. The App decides once per load whether to open it, so connecting an account (which the engine already marks as onboarded) doesn't cut the flow short.
- **Escape skips the current step**, and on the first step skips setup; the footer says which. The welcome stays mounted (hidden) under the add-account dialog so focus returns to it instead of the page underneath.
- **Choosing another provider in the welcome.** If the owner saves a key for, say, Claude while Groq has no key, Ask, Summaries, Drafts and Plans that still point at Groq move to that provider's first curated model (and Voice to OpenAI's when it's ChatGPT). Otherwise the defaults stay on Groq.
- **No AI glyph.** The AI step uses a key icon; no sparkle, wand or stars anywhere.

## 2026-10-22 — Monorepo and first website

- **Layout.** npm workspaces, no Turborepo or Nx: `apps/desktop` (the Electron app, moved with `git mv` so history follows), `apps/web` (the website) and `packages/` (reserved, empty). Docs (SPEC, TODO, DECISIONS, PROGRESS, README, API, MOBILE) and `.env.example` stay at the root. Windows and Linux will be extra electron-builder targets inside `apps/desktop`; an iOS app will be another folder under `apps/`.
- **Commands stay at the root.** Root `package.json` has proxy scripts (`npm run <script> -w @aplos/desktop`); `typecheck` and `test` fan out over every workspace. Workspace scripts run with `apps/desktop` as the working directory, so build scripts, vite, tsconfig, electron-builder config and tests needed no path changes. `node scripts/launch.mjs` is a root shim for the real launcher, which resolves paths from its own location. `.env` is read from the working directory and then the repo root (`src/main/env.ts`), so the owner's existing root `.env` keeps working. Existing environment variables still win.
- **Electron is pinned to an exact version** (`44.7.2`). electron-builder cannot read a version range when the package is hoisted to the root `node_modules`, and failed with "Cannot compute electron version"; an exact pin is its documented fix. Bump it by hand.
- **The engine stays in `apps/desktop/src/engine` for now.** It imports five modules from `src/shared` (mostly `types`) and 21 files across main, renderer and shared import it, so extracting it properly means moving `shared` too, rewriting those imports, and making Vite, esbuild and electron-builder bundle a TypeScript workspace package. That is a deep refactor with no second consumer yet. `tests/portability.test.ts` already fails if the engine gains a Node import, so the boundary stays clean. Extract `packages/engine` (and `packages/shared`) when the iOS app starts and needs it.
- **CI path awareness.** No GitHub Actions workflow exists yet. The website is built by Vercel, which skips deploys through `ignoreCommand` in `apps/web/vercel.json` on the same principle. If desktop jobs are added, filter by path: desktop jobs on `apps/desktop/**` plus root `package.json` / `package-lock.json`; website jobs on `apps/web/**`; both on `packages/**`. Docs-only changes run neither. The desktop package job needs macOS runners; the site build runs anywhere.
- **Website stack: Vite + React, multi-page.** Two HTML entries (landing, privacy) build to plain static files; no router and no server. It matches the app's toolchain and adds no new framework to learn or maintain. Astro would ship less JavaScript (the pages are static, and React adds about 70 kB gzipped), but it is a second build system for two pages. Revisit if the site grows or load weight matters. No analytics, cookies or third-party scripts; fonts are the system stack, as in the app. Deployment: Vercel's Git integration (decided 2026-10-23 at the owner's request; Cloudflare Pages was set up first and removed the same day). It gives a preview per PR and a production deploy on merge to `main` with no workflow or secrets in the repo; path filtering lives in `apps/web/vercel.json`. Previews are protected by Vercel login; production is public. Analytics and Speed Insights are off.
- **Website design (revised after owner feedback).** The first pass was clean but plain. The owner asked for the feel of animations.dev, aiforui.dev and Linear's site, and for it to always be dark. Reviewed all three: a near-black ground, big tightly tracked headlines, a framed product window that fades into the page, content that is itself interactive (animations.dev's demo cards), and a restrained tile layout for secondary features. So: dark only (no `prefers-color-scheme` switching, no light screenshots), a soft glow in the icon's green behind the hero window, a sticky translucent bar (the app's own toolbar material), a **working keyboard demo of Home** (`Triage.tsx`: Space, ←, →, Z on the app's original sample mail, with undo and a finished state; keys act only while the demo is focused so the page never hijacks Space or the arrows), a tile layout with real keycaps, provider and account chips, and scroll-in reveals (transform and opacity only, reduced motion keeps opacity only). The taste skill (`design-taste-frontend`, installed at `~/.claude/skills/taste-skill/SKILL.md`; the upstream path only provides that file, so its scripts were not used) was read and its rules were applied by hand in the first pass. This revision deliberately relaxes its bans on gradients, glows, glass and tile grids at the owner's request; it still keeps one `h1`, short headlines, no eyebrows, numbering, monospace or emoji, one typeface (the system font, as in the app) and one accent. Owner rules unchanged: never a sparkle/wand icon, no reference-app branding, the app's own icon and tokens. Emil and Apple guidance shaped the motion: press scale 0.97, ease-out curves, interruptible card exits (each action sends a ghost of the card away while the next one enters, so fast key presses never wait), hover effects gated on real pointers.
- **Screenshots are real.** The app on its original demo data, captured headlessly from its dev server on separate ports with throwaway storage (`apps/web/scripts/capture-screenshots.mjs`). Without an AI key, summaries in the reader and Ask answers are not available, so the site shows the Home focus card (whose one-line summary comes from the demo data), the week calendar and the docked Ask panel with its suggestions, not an answer. No reference-app branding, assets or copy.
- **Copy claims to verify before launch.** The feature list follows the brief and README, but Outlook, iCloud, Fastmail and IMAP have only been verified against fake servers (see PROGRESS/TODO), and Fastmail has no calendar yet. The download button and repository link are placeholders (`apps/web/src/site.ts`). The privacy page is a marked draft with bracketed to-dos (Limited Use wording, contact email) and `noindex`.

## 2026-10-20 — Second UI pass from owner feedback

- Ask returns to the docked right panel (owner preferred it over the centered overlay). The panel keeps typed mail/command matches, hold-A voice (matched on the typed character), and AI calendar event cards laid out vertically for the narrow panel.
- The focused Home card shows one big line, the AI summary that already folds in the action and deadline. No subject or separate todo line; the subject is a tooltip. Focus moves glide a single card surface between rows and keep it at a steady height in the window.
- Space runs an AI-suggested primary action; Enter/O always open the email. Actions are presets only (`SuggestedAction`: link, attachment, reply, calendar, read), never generated code, because email is untrusted input. Links must literally appear in the thread (unsubscribe/tracking links excluded), attachments must exist, replies need a prompt, calendar actions need a concrete event; anything else is dropped. Reply opens the reader with a suggestion chip that drafts through the existing AI draft path only when clicked.
- The reader puts the AI summary (same text as the Home row) big at the top above the subject, with todo and an editable due pill as a separate section of the same card. Sender and a relative date ("Friday 10:00 AM") head each message; addresses live in a hover/click popover. Never use the generic AI sparkle/wand icon anywhere; those icons were removed from the icon set.

## 2026-10-20 — Aplos's own UI direction

- The owner redirected the product from an Avec clone to their own unified mail + calendar client with AI everywhere (see SPEC.md "Product direction"). Keep Avec's overall feel and the existing palette/tokens; the owner's Excalidraw sketches are suggestions, not pixel specs.
- Home becomes a focus list: exactly one email is focused at a time and expands into a card (sender, due chip, AI summary headline, keyed actions: Bookmark B, Later ←, Open Space/↵, Done →, more). Other mail shows as a single line "Sender: summary"; on Home, rows fade with distance from the focus. Acting on the focused email advances focus to the next one. Space or Enter opens the one-at-a-time reader at that email. Mailbox views reuse the same rows without fading.
- Ask moves from a docked side panel to a centered overlay over a dimmed app ("Ask me anything", Esc). Tap A to type, hold A to record (the top-bar pill turns into a red Recording state with "Release to ask · Esc to cancel"). Typing shows Ask, matching mail and commands; answers can cite mail, open a proposed draft, or propose a calendar event that is only saved when the user confirms it.
- Development ports can be moved with `APLOS_API_PORT` / `APLOS_VITE_PORT` (CSP follows) so several checkouts can preview at once with separate `APLOS_DATA_DIR` storage.

## 2026-10-18 — Keep the newest exact inbox copy

- User-requested Aplos behavior, independent of reference research: during incoming sync, compare complete thread content within the same account and archive older exact copies. Match subject, sender/name, To/Cc, full text/HTML/quoted history, sent direction and attachment metadata/bytes; ignore delivery identifiers, timestamps and AI/workflow fields. No AI or network classification is needed for equality.
- Compare whole threads, never just their latest reply, so different conversation history cannot be archived accidentally. Unknown attachment bytes or empty bodies do not prove equality. This does not remove duplicate messages inside a provider-grouped thread; providers currently expose archive operations on whole threads. Only locally downloaded inbox threads are compared.
- Keep the latest delivery timestamp; tied timestamps prefer a newly observed copy, then a stable thread ID within a batch. Handle initial downloads and late/out-of-order batches. Flag-only syncs do not sweep existing mail. Pending local edits and edits made during sync are protected.
- Archive explicitly even when Done is configured as read/unread; preserve read state, bookmarks and todo metadata on the archived copy, which remains in All mail. Save archives and durable provider operations with the incoming mail/cursor/index transaction. Note automatic workflow edits in the same stage. Notify/classify only surviving inbox copies. Do not claim live-provider acceptance from local emulation.

## 2026-10-16 — Bookmarks leave Home

- Bookmarking is filing, not a leftover Home flag. Important or low new mail that is bookmarked leaves Home New and the low bundle, and lives in Later → Bookmarks. Status stays `new` so the provider is not archived. Unbookmarking still-new mail returns it to Home. Later todos stay on Home’s ready section even when starred.
- Stack bookmarking flings the card down and advances like triage. Skip the toast when the dock already shows undo, same as Done/Todo. The Bookmarks tab is a plain list: no second “Bookmarks” heading and no redundant row flag.
- Settings keep a side category list until the workspace is 520px wide so “Help & about” is not clipped. Collapse to wrapping pills only below that.

- Interpret “go for it” as approval for the report's three immediate recommendations: provider emulation, focused Groq evaluations and large synthetic mailbox measurements. Keep personal desktop scope; defer reusable automation rules and hosted infrastructure.
- Write all implementation and fixtures independently. Use our existing adapter injection seams rather than introducing configurable production OAuth/API origins. A development-only loopback emulator will accept only known provider origins, validate OAuth state/PKCE, and refuse external delivery. Real provider credentials and normal desktop storage must never enter this mode.
- Emulated provider results prove application integration only. Live Groq evaluations use synthetic content and the backend GROQ_API_KEY; record semantic failures honestly. M11 stays unchecked until authorized real mailbox and OS acceptance happens.
- Keep a small eight-case factual evaluation corpus with deterministic outcome assertions instead of adding another AI judge or adopting Inbox Zero code. Run it explicitly against Groq; ordinary tests never spend API quota. Reports contain case outcomes/model/timing and synthetic assertion failures, never credentials.
- Context limits must preserve complete JSON and the latest user question. Drop older history and fit complete thread excerpts to the request budget; admit citations only from excerpts actually sent. Tell the model its view is partial. Oversized composition input fails clearly instead of sending a malformed fragment. Long individual threads retain recent messages within a bounded context; this is not whole-mailbox retrieval.
- Large-mailbox evidence must include the real packaged UI. The first 10k-thread import did not finish after 210 seconds (stopped; exploratory run overlapped tests), and the first desktop attempt exhausted its 60-second Home-row deadline while the renderer used about 1 GB. Keep those failures distinct from successful measurements.
- Avoid scanning FTS for nonexistent old rows during first ingestion; reuse hot statements and skip reindexing when only flags/workflow changed. Keep the existing schema and retain indexed subject/body/people/account updates and deletion semantics.
- Triage/Undo now replace only touched thread objects. The renderer requests `compact:true` acknowledgements and follows existing change events; default state-returning calls remain compatible. This avoids cloning/transferring the entire mailbox for routine actions.
- Window sections above 200 rows using their existing fixed row height and a small overscan. Preserve scroll geometry, native wheel/trackpad scrolling and keyboard cursor movement; keep small-list presence animations. Do not add a UI framework or claim bodies are loaded on demand.
- Verification desktop processes use distinct Chromium profiles as well as temporary engine data. They can run alongside the normal installed app without sharing its instance lock or browser storage.

Hold thresholds: A 220 ms in Ask, Space 300 ms in the reader, Space 250 ms in an empty compose body; a shorter press keeps its tap meaning. Replies open inline in the reader; navigation is blocked while one is open so a draft is never hidden.

Hover moves the cursor only on real pointer movement, so scrolling under a resting pointer does not steal it.
- Pre-send checks confirm rather than block where the user may be right (no subject, mentioned attachment); they block only on things that cannot be sent (no recipients, invalid addresses). Quoted history is ignored when looking for attachment mentions.
- Calendar all-day events keep Google's exclusive end date in storage but show the inclusive last day in the editor. Date math lives in `src/renderer/calendarTime.ts` so it can be tested without the DOM.
- Layout breakpoints for content (split view, calendar, settings) are container queries on the workspace, because the space that matters is what remains after the sidebar and Ask. Window media queries only decide whether the sidebar fits; when it does not, it opens as a temporary overlay instead of vanishing, and the saved sidebar preference is left untouched.
- A thread summary that is contained in the message text is treated as a Gmail snippet, not a summary, and the reader offers to summarize instead of repeating it. Trash and Spam rows show Restore as their only quick action, since Done/Todo do not apply there. Later todos are ordered by urgency (overdue, today, upcoming, undated) using local dates.
- Every floating surface uses one material from shared tokens (`--surface`, `--surface-blur`, `--surface-shadow`, `--surface-radius`, `--surface-inset`, `--item-height`, `--item-radius`, `--item-hover`, `--surface-line`); dialogs use the opaque `--surface-solid` so long forms stay legible. Toasts follow the same light material instead of a dark bar. Native `<select>` stays for pickers so macOS draws its own menu.
- Motion animates only transform and opacity. Panels that change the layout (Ask, sidebar) change size in one step and slide with transforms, with the workspace sliding from its previous position (FLIP), so no animation frame relays out the mail list. Height animation is kept only for the short gap-closing phase of row exits/entries. Frequently changing values (countdown clock, hover, the live draft) live in the component that shows them or in refs, and panes are memoized with stable handlers, so typing or moving the pointer does not re-render the app. Performance is measured in the packaged app, not the preview browser, which uses a software GPU.
- Drag and swipe triage writes its live state to the DOM (CSS variables and `data-swipe*` attributes) instead of React state, so following the pointer costs no renders. Sides map to actions through the Triage direction setting, and every exit (rows, stack fling, arrow keys) uses that same mapping. With a mouse, the reader card only starts a drag outside message text so text selection is not lost; touch and trackpad swipes work anywhere. Content that scrolls sideways scrolls first.
- Gestures animate only on the compositor: the moving element gets its own layer while it moves, nothing painted inside it changes every frame unless it is its own layer too, and per-frame CSS variables are registered as non-inherited so a long email is not restyled on every frame. Values driven every frame never carry transitions; discrete state changes (armed, settle, release) do. Velocity counts toward a flick only if the pointer was still moving when released.

## 2026-10-15 — Multi-provider, serverless architecture

- Researched two open-source mail backends (Redakt, Superlocal) for patterns only; no code copied. Adopted: incremental provider sync from a stored cursor, a per-account rate gate, durable local operations with undo, a send hold that cannot double-send, content-hash AI caching, paged queries with change events. Rejected for Aplos: a hosted Postgres/web backend, multi-user tenancy and running our own mail server (Redakt's Stalwart approach): the user's mail host is the sync server.
- Target shape: one platform-free TypeScript engine (`src/engine`) shared by desktop now and a React Native app later; platform specifics enter through small ports (SQL database, secure storage, sockets, OAuth, background scheduling). Providers implement one contract: `sync(cursor)` returning changed threads, flag-only updates, removals and the next cursor; `apply`; `send`; `fetchBody/attachment`; capabilities. Planned adapters: Gmail API, Microsoft Graph (MSAL public client, delta per folder), JMAP (Fastmail), IMAP+SMTP (iCloud, Yahoo, AOL, generic; own threading from Message-ID/References). Proton only through Bridge on desktop. TypeScript over a Rust core because all current and planned runtimes run TypeScript; raw IMAP sockets on mobile stay behind a port.
- Storage is SQLite through Node's built-in `node:sqlite` (Node 24 and Electron 44 both ship SQLite 3.5x with FTS5), so no native module rebuild is needed for Electron. The engine owns a minimal synchronous SQL port so mobile can use op-sqlite/expo-sqlite later. The existing `state.json` is imported once and kept as `state.json.imported`.
- Aplos-only workflow state (Done vs. merely read, Later, todos, due dates, reminders) is kept locally and survives syncs; the host's labels/folders are authoritative for read, star, trash, spam and inbox membership. A thread returns to Home when a new message arrives, when Gmail moves it back to the inbox while the matching Done/Later action is "archive", or when it lands in Trash/Spam. Cross-device sync of the workflow log goes later through an encrypted hidden mailbox folder (no Aplos server).
- Gmail: the first sync fetches the newest 500 threads instead of the whole mailbox; afterwards `history.list` from the stored `historyId` drives sync. Threads that only changed labels are refreshed with `format=minimal`; added messages trigger a full thread fetch; a missing thread is removed locally; an expired cursor (404) falls back to a fresh bounded sync. Older mail is reached through Gmail search, which is limited to 100 threads per query.
- Background sync runs every 60 s and when a window gains focus, behind a per-account gate (single flight, at least 15 s between automatic syncs, exponential backoff after failures up to 15 min). "Sync now" bypasses the interval but not the single-flight rule.
- Triage is local-first. Each change is applied at once and written, in the same transaction, to an `ops` table as a provider change (remote ID, the status it started from, the patch). A worker sends ops in order per account; one failure holds back only that account's later ops, so a thread never gets an older change after a newer one. Failures retry after 30 s doubling to 15 min; "Sync now" retries at once. A 404 drops the op, since the thread is gone. Sync sends ops before pulling, and threads with an op still queued keep their local status, read, star and labels (new arrivals and removals still apply). Undo and reminders go through the same queue. Disconnecting an account drops its ops.
- Sending can never double-send. Each draft gets one Message-ID on the sender's domain when it is first sent, kept across retries. Before calling Gmail, the draft is marked with `sendAttemptAt`. A network error or 5xx is uncertain: Aplos looks for the Message-ID in Sent mail (`rfc822msgid:`) and treats a match as sent; otherwise the draft keeps an error asking the user to check Sent mail. A definite rejection (4xx) is reported as is. After a crash, drafts with `sendAttemptAt` are settled only from Sent mail and never sent again automatically.
- Live push needs a server for Gmail and Graph, so desktop polls. A later optional push relay may store only push token ↔ mailbox ID, never tokens or content. Google restricted scopes need verification; the CASA assessment applies when data passes through a third-party server, so any relay or AI proxy must be checked with Google first. Groq on mobile needs a user-entered key or a proxy; desktop keeps reading `GROQ_API_KEY` from the backend environment.

## 2026-10-16 — Outlook, IMAP (iCloud), JMAP (Fastmail), CalDAV and discovery

- One `MailProvider` contract (`src/engine/provider.ts`) with capabilities per account (`labels`, `importance`, `aliases`, `signatures`, `calendar`, `serverSearch`). Thread IDs are `${account.id}:${remoteId}`. Folder hosts report role labels (INBOX, ARCHIVE, SENT, TRASH, SPAM) so the service can describe intent the same way for every host: a patch whose `labels` lack INBOX means "archive", labels with INBOX mean "keep in the inbox". The read/unread Done and Later actions always pass explicit labels, so no host infers an archive from the status alone.
- Each adapter keeps its own index (message → thread, folder, UID/flags) in a `provider_memory` table (schema v3) that disconnect clears. Secrets (app passwords, API tokens, OAuth tokens) live in per-provider vault files, each entry encrypted with Electron safeStorage; they never enter app state, logs or the renderer.
- Microsoft Graph: public client with PKCE on a `http://localhost` loopback redirect (Entra ignores the port only for `localhost`); `Prefer: IdType="ImmutableId"` so moves keep IDs; conversations are threads; first sync is the newest 500 messages across the five role folders, then per-folder delta; sends go as MIME through `/me/sendMail` because Graph's JSON path cannot set `internetMessageId`, which the double-send guard depends on. Sends above about 3 MB are refused rather than sent through an upload session that would lose the Message-ID. Graph cannot save meetings without notifying guests; the README says so.
- IMAP/SMTP: imapflow and nodemailer behind small `ImapSession`/`SmtpSender` interfaces (also the mobile seam). Threads come from Message-ID/In-Reply-To/References, with keys derived from the first Message-ID so they survive memory loss; per-folder cursors use UIDVALIDITY/UIDNEXT and CONDSTORE when offered. iCloud does not file SMTP mail in Sent, so Aplos appends a copy; other hosts are checked first and appended only when missing. Calendar for IMAP accounts is CalDAV through a separate `CalendarBackend` with an original iCalendar parser (no library); it never writes an ORGANIZER, so servers do not send invitations, and it refuses to edit one occurrence of a repeating event.
- JMAP: Fastmail with an API token; Email/changes plus Thread/changes; sends create the draft and the EmailSubmission in one request that moves it to Sent on success, and first destroys any leftover draft with the same Message-ID. Fastmail calendar is not connected: API tokens do not grant CalDAV.
- Discovery is platform-free (`src/engine/discover.ts`): known consumer domains, then MX over DNS-over-HTTPS (Cloudflare, then dns.google), then HTTPS-only autoconfig (the domain, then Thunderbird's ISPDB), then common host names marked as a guess. Configs that point at loopback or private addresses are ignored; Proton addresses are told Bridge is not supported yet, because Bridge's self-signed certificate does not pass strict TLS. The domain checker reads MX, SPF (with the 10-lookup limit), DKIM (known selectors per host), DMARC and MTA-STS records and explains each fix.
- Review fixes adopted for the shared contract (GPT-6.1-Sol): a send whose outcome is unknown keeps its marker and Message-ID across errors and edits, and every retry looks in Sent first; real accounts whose adapter is missing keep their queued changes and never complete a send locally; threads changed while a sync is in flight are protected from that sync's answer even after their change has flushed; a late host answer cannot re-queue a change that disconnect discarded; a local sent copy without a host thread is replaced when the host's copy with the same Message-ID syncs; `'primary'` resolves to the real default calendar before saving and duplicate checks; server URLs with embedded passwords are refused.
- Not adopted from that review: replacing `Partial<Thread>` patches with explicit host operations. Each adapter maps the patch plus the labels convention above, and the tests pin that mapping; revisit if a host needs operations the patch cannot express.

## 2026-10-16 — M26 engine process, change events, paged reads

- The service runs in an Electron utility process (`src/main/engine-host.ts`, built to `dist/main/engine.cjs`). The main process keeps windows, menus, notifications, links (`openExternal` is a call back into main) and the keychain. Messages use a small typed protocol over the utility port (`src/main/engine-rpc.ts`: init, invoke/result/error, event, call/reply, close/closed); the same code runs over a `worker_threads` channel in tests. The browser preview keeps the service in-process.
- The utility process cannot reach safeStorage, so credentials are sealed with a random 256-bit data key (AES-256-GCM, `APK1` prefix) and the keychain protects only that key (`engine-key.bin`). Main unwraps it at start and passes it to the engine in memory. A key file that cannot be read (locked keychain) is never replaced, since that would orphan every saved credential; accounts then report that the keychain must be unlocked. Credentials sealed directly with safeStorage by earlier builds are re-sealed once at start; entries the keychain cannot open are left as they were.
- If the engine process stops unexpectedly, waiting requests fail with a message, main starts a new engine and sends every window a fresh snapshot. More than three stops in a minute ends the restarts with an error, so a crash loop stays visible.
- Each commit reports what the database save actually wrote (`StoreDiff`: changed and removed thread IDs, changed collections and fields), and windows receive only that as a `changes` event. Small collections (accounts, drafts, events, conversations) are sent whole when they change; threads as rows. Every change carries the engine run (`epoch`) and a commit number (`rev`). A window applies changes in order, holds those that arrive before its snapshot, ignores old ones, and fetches a new snapshot when one is missing or the epoch changed (`src/shared/live.ts`).
- The main window starts from the newest 400 threads (`state.get {threadLimit}`) and reads the rest in pages of 2,000 with a keyset cursor (`threads.page {before:{date,id}}`), so new or removed mail during paging cannot shift pages. A page never restores a thread a later change removed and never overwrites a newer copy. Pop-out windows still load everything at once.
- Not done: windows still hold every thread once paging finishes, with message bodies. Loading bodies on demand is the next step if very large mailboxes need it.

## 2026-10-16 — M29 workflow sync between devices

- What syncs: each thread's workflow fields (Done/Later/New, reply status, due date, todo, reminder and whether it fired, hidden from Home). Trash and Spam are host state and are not part of it. The log is last-writer-wins per thread (`src/engine/workflow.ts`, platform-free); a merge is commutative, associative and idempotent. Entries are keyed by the host's thread ID (Gmail, Graph, JMAP), or by the thread's root Message-ID for IMAP, whose thread IDs are built per device.
- Only real user edits enter the log (triage, undo, Home hide, fired reminders). Each edit is recorded at edit time as a snapshot of the thread's workflow fields with its edit time and a generation number, in the same transaction as the edit; that edit time, not the sync time, decides conflicts. Mail sync changes (a host archive becoming Done) do not count, or they would override another device's Later. The first device to turn sync on seeds the log from its existing Later, todos, due dates and reminders (timestamped with the thread's last message, so real edits win); a device that joins takes the shared log as it is. Each device remembers which winning entry it applied per thread, so an entry applies once, and an entry for a thread downloaded later (through search, say) still applies when it arrives. A newer message from someone else keeps the thread on Home even if another device marked it Done.
- A round reads every stored copy, merges all of them with this device's edits, writes one new copy and then removes only the copies it read. Two devices writing at once therefore never delete each other's copy; the next round folds both. An edit made while a round is in flight is not acknowledged and its thread is not patched by that round. What a round remembers is saved together with the patches it applies, so a crash in between redoes the round.
- Compaction drops plain entries older than 180 days and records a floor; a merged entry older than another log's floor is rejected, so a long-offline device cannot bring back a Later that was cleared and compacted away.
- Storage: small marked messages in a hidden "Aplos Mail Data" folder per account (a name the user is unlikely to have; a user folder named "Aplos" is ordinary mail). Only messages carrying Aplos's own marker count as records (a header for Gmail and IMAP, a named extended property for Graph, a keyword for JMAP), so user mail is never read or deleted even if it sits in that folder. Gmail uses a label with list and message visibility hidden, `messages.insert`, and trashes replaced copies, because `gmail.modify` cannot delete. Graph uses a hidden folder; IMAP an unsubscribed folder; JMAP an unsubscribed mailbox without a role. Normal sync and search never surface it.
- Encryption: a 28-symbol sync code (130 random bits plus a 2-symbol check, Crockford base32, entered in any case and spacing) → scrypt (N=2^15, random salt per write) → AES-256-GCM with the header authenticated; a separate check value tells a wrong code from damaged data. The code is kept in the credential vault (`workflow-sync.enc.json`), never in app state. Data sealed with a different code or a newer format is reported and never overwritten; damaged data is replaced; a copy too large to open is kept, never replaced, and writes trim old entries until the log fits.
- When: after each account's mail sync, at most every 5 minutes for automatic syncs unless there are edits to share, and on every manual sync. Writes happen only when the merged log differs from the host's copy. Patches from other devices change local state only; the other device already told the host about its own archive or move.
- Not done: no live mailbox verification; device clocks decide conflicts; the browser preview cannot sync devices, since it has no credential vault.

## 2026-10-16 — Second review round (GPT-6.1-Sol) for adapters, engine host and device sync

- Provider indexes are saved with the mail they describe. Writes an adapter makes to provider memory while `sync` (or a workflow round) runs are held in a stage (`src/main/memory-stage.ts`, AsyncLocalStorage) and written in the same SQLite transaction as the threads and the new cursor. A failed or interrupted sync leaves index and cursor as they were and replays. Keys carry versions: if another operation (a move during sync) rewrote a key the sync read or wrote, the sync's result is dropped and fetched again, up to three times. Adapters hold no index copies in process memory between calls.
- A send whose earlier attempt is unresolved is never resubmitted by a normal retry, for every host: not finding it in Sent proves nothing (Graph and JMAP deliver asynchronously; SMTP has no idempotency). Compose offers "Send anyway" after a warning that recipients may get two copies; the choice covers one attempt. JMAP additionally recognizes a submitted message by its submission record even when the move to Sent failed, and never destroys a draft that has a submission.
- A sync that answers after its account was disconnected or reconnected is discarded (account generation), so disconnect cannot be undone by a late answer.
- Hosts that tell Aplos where to go are held to HTTPS on every hop: JMAP session endpoints and redirects, CalDAV principal/home/event addresses and redirects, autoconfig redirects. Autoconfig results naming loopback, private, link-local or CGNAT addresses, in any spelling or after DNS resolution (system resolver on desktop, DNS-over-HTTPS in the platform-free engine), are ignored. Servers the user types by hand are not checked: self-hosted servers on a home network are legitimate.
- Calendar: repeating events and meetings with invited guests are read-only in Aplos (CalDAV), because changing them would rewrite the series or let the server email guests; guests Aplos adds are written with `SCHEDULE-AGENT=CLIENT` and no organizer. Each event keeps the ETag it was read at and saves fail rather than overwrite a newer version, also across restarts. Edited times stay in the event's own time zone; custom VTIMEZONE gaps resolve like IANA zones. Occurrences of one series have their own IDs and are all kept.
- IMAP: cached UIDs are used only while the folder's UIDVALIDITY matches; otherwise messages are found again by exact Message-ID. Without MOVE or UIDPLUS a move copies and marks the source deleted without EXPUNGE, so unrelated deleted messages are never purged. Refused STOREs and moves are errors; explicit flag changes are always sent. Thread keys come from the References root, so they survive index loss.
- Graph and JMAP: a partly deleted conversation is refetched in full; attachment lists and initial queries follow paging and the server's object limits; JMAP full fetches request body values and capture states before the first query, then replay changes so mail arriving during the first sync is not skipped.
- Domain check: SPF stops counting at `all` and ignores `redirect` when `all` exists; DMARC with duplicate tags or invalid `p`, `sp` or `pct` fails instead of passing.
- Engine host: a failed snapshot fetch is retried with backoff (0.5 s doubling to 30 s) so a window never freezes on a gap; replacement engines that fail before ready count toward the restart limit and are terminated; credential migration runs per file without withholding the data key; the data folder is created before the key is loaded.
- Accepted risks: a name that does not resolve is allowed by the autoconfig host check (otherwise offline guesses vanish), and DNS rebinding at connect time is not prevented; two devices turning sync on before either writes both seed (seeds are timestamped with message dates, so real edits still win); app data written by earlier development builds (no marker) is ignored.

## 2026-10-15 — Final release acceptance

- The user requested completion and full testing while acknowledging missing API access. Finish every independent release check; keep M11 open for genuine live mailbox and OS-permission acceptance. Do not create account credentials, borrow tokens, connect real mailboxes or send external messages for testing.
- Test the actual packaged renderer/preload/main/utility-process boundary in a fresh temporary demo mailbox, in addition to the existing fake-provider suite. Keep this as one repeatable `scripts/verify-release.mjs` runner instead of adding tests that mirror implementation details. Its loopback debugging port belongs only to the verification process; the distributed app has no test hook. Live Groq checks are opt-in and use original demo text and locally generated synthetic speech.
- Observed failure: after a genuine input event changed the popout editor and its UI showed Saving, `window.close()` reached `beforeunload` without delivering `prepare-close`; the old draft remained in SQLite. Native-close checks alone had missed this path. Guard dirty renderer unloads as well, save through the existing close handler, and accept that renderer's save acknowledgement in main even when no native close is pending. Failed saves keep the editor open.
- Terminal launch must remove an inherited `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` flag, which otherwise makes the desktop executable behave as Node and reject Chromium arguments. Preserve the rest of the process environment, including backend-only API configuration, and forward explicit launch arguments.

## 2026-10-15 — Completion audit: finish the M26 platform boundary

- The audit found that M26's checkbox was too broad: utility-process isolation, paging and change events were implemented, but the service still imported desktop modules and MOBILE.md still described extraction as future work. Reopened M26 and finished that work instead of treating the checkbox as evidence.
- Move the original orchestration and demo into `src/engine`, leaving `src/main/service.ts` as the desktop composition root and keeping its public API compatible. Inject the SQL store, staged memory, provider adapters, Groq, encrypted workflow transport, UUIDs and native symbol rendering through typed interfaces. Keep AsyncLocalStorage, filesystem/vault access, Node crypto/DNS, sockets and environment secrets in the host. Preserve staged-memory atomicity rather than replacing async scopes with a global variable.
- Provider environment ciphertext defaults to portable Uint8Array; desktop adapters explicitly specialize it to Buffer. The core only measures validated base64 payloads, so it needs no Node decoder. Browser-style numeric timers must work without `.unref()`.
- Verify the entire engine with a neutral bundle, a browser-libraries-only TypeScript compilation, and actual mail operations in a restricted runtime with Node globals absent. A mobile host still needs real storage, credential, crypto, networking and async-context implementations; this remains a desktop app plus a portability spike, not a completed mobile app.

## 2026-10-21 — One way to open mail
- Split view is removed (owner: "we don't want it anymore"). Emails always open in the one-at-a-time stack; the toolbar mode picker, the setting and the split styles are gone, and a stored `openMode` is dropped on load.
- Ask closes with an X at the right end of its header instead of a chevron on the left.
- Home's distance fade is symmetric and softer (neighbours .84, floor .46); the low-importance line no longer fades and sits on its own soft well so it never reads as another email.
## 2026-10-21 — Top bar navigation (experiment)
- New `layout` setting (General → Navigation): "Top bar" (default) or "Sidebar". The top bar layout has no sidebar or sidebar button; the toolbar's left side holds a compact account switcher ("All" with avatars and ⇅), Home / Later / All mail / Drafts as one segmented tab group, a Calendar icon and a ⋯ menu with Sent, Trash and Spam. Settings and Keyboard shortcuts are icons in the window's bottom-left corner, with a sync dot when offline, syncing or failing.
- Needs reply and Awaiting reply are no longer mailboxes in either layout; Home carries that work. Needs-reply mail already reached Home (New if new, "Waiting on you" if done, Later if snoozed; bookmarks stay in Bookmarks by design). Awaiting-reply mail now shows on Home under "No reply yet" once 3 days pass without an answer, not right away, so every sent email doesn't crowd Home. Done on mail that is already done takes it off Home (`hiddenFromHome`); a new send or a reply brings it back.
## 2026-10-21 — Top bar navigation removed
- Owner disliked the top bar layout; it is gone along with the Navigation setting, and a stored `layout` is dropped on load. The sidebar is the only layout again. The reply changes above stay: no Needs reply/Awaiting reply mailboxes, "No reply yet" on Home, Done on already-done mail hides it from Home.
## 2026-10-21 — Minimized sidebar
- New `sidebarStyle` setting (General → Sidebar): Normal or Minimized. Minimized removes the sidebar entirely; the top-left button (a ☰ icon instead of the sidebar glyph, `[` opens it) shows one small menu with accounts, every mailbox with its count, Refresh mail, Settings and Keyboard shortcuts. Back/forward stay on the toolbar's back arrow and history shortcuts.
## 2026-10-21 — M38 unified Calendar page
- Layout and behaviour follow observed Notion Calendar patterns only (week time grid, day and month views, mini month and calendar list on the left, popover beside an event, drag to create/move/resize, now line, single-key navigation). No Notion code, icons, assets, colours or wording are used; the look is Aplos's own tokens: one card on the canvas, the calm warm palette, SF-style tracking, blue for today and red for now.
- One combined list: `calendar.list` with no account (or `'all'`) returns every account's calendars, skipping a host that cannot answer; the renderer adds calendars it only knows from events (offline, or an unlisted host calendar) so every event can be toggled, and maps the `'primary'` alias to the account's default calendar. Colours: the host's colour when it has one (Google `backgroundColor`, Graph `hexColor`, CalDAV Apple `calendar-color` with the alpha byte dropped); otherwise the default calendar takes the account colour and the rest take distinct colours from a small calm palette.
- Hidden calendars are a setting (`hiddenCalendars`, `accountId/calendarId` keys), so they persist and reach every window. "Only" (or ⌥-click) isolates one calendar among those in view and leaves hidden calendars of other accounts alone; pressing it again shows them all. The account selector filters the page; choosing an account while on Calendar no longer jumps to Home.
- Event blocks: calendar colour as a tint with a solid left edge, text tinted toward ink, past plans faded. Overlaps pack into columns per overlapping run; in week view an event reaches 1.7 columns into its neighbour (cascade) and later ones sit on top, in day view columns stay side by side. Snap is 15 minutes; a click on the grid drafts an hour, a drag drafts what was drawn. Only the quick popover's Save (or the full editor) writes anything. Moves and resizes save at once with an Undo toast. Meetings the host marks as scheduling stay read-only (no drag, edit or delete).
- Keys: T today, D/W/M views, J/→ next and K/← previous period, N new event, ↵/E edit the open event, ⌫ delete it, Esc closes or cancels a drag. C keeps meaning Compose everywhere, so New event is N rather than Notion's C. Weeks start on Sunday like the earlier month grid; a week-start preference is left for the owner to decide.
- The rail (mini month, calendars, "From your mail") sits beside the grid when the page is at least 880px wide; narrower (Ask open, small window) it moves into a popover behind a calendar button. View choice is remembered per device (localStorage); the date always opens on today.
- "From your mail" lists emails whose suggested action is a calendar event that is not on a calendar yet; choosing one runs the existing Groq extraction and opens the editor. Nothing is saved without the user.
- The demo now has three calendars per account (Work: Work, Team, Focus time; Personal: Personal, Family, Fitness) with original plans across the current week, including overlaps, all-day and multi-day events. Existing demo databases keep their old events; new demos and reset data get the full week.
## 2026-10-21 — Calendar motion, gestures and Today in the dock
- Motion uses transform and opacity only, Aplos's own tokens (`--dur-*`, `--ease-*`, read once for Web Animations) and `reducedMotion()`. Every animation can be taken over midway: a new one starts from where the element is drawn (computed transform), never from its resting place. With reduced motion, slides, cross-fades, FLIP, lifts and smooth scrolls are skipped; views and plans change in place.
- Trackpad: a two-finger horizontal swipe (wheel `deltaX`) pages the current view. The day heads, all-day bars and columns follow the fingers inside a clip (hour labels stay); past the threshold (22% of the width, 60–160 px) the grid resists. When the wheel goes quiet (120 ms) it turns the page if it passed the threshold or was a flick (>0.45 px/ms over a third of it, same direction), otherwise it springs back. A gesture locks to its starting axis, so vertical scrolling never pages; momentum after a page turn is swallowed. Horizontal wheel events in the calendar are taken (`preventDefault`) and the page sets `overscroll-behavior-x: none`, so the browser never reads them as back/forward; Electron does not navigate on swipes. The reader's swipe (`useSwipe`) is a different element and unaffected. `useSwipe` was not reused because it flings a single row off-screen, while paging needs the new period to come in from the other side; the new hook follows the same quiet/flick rules.
- Pinch (⌘/ctrl + wheel, which a trackpad pinch sends) changes the hour height between 28 and 112 px and keeps the time under the pointer in place; the height is remembered per place (page, Today).
- Period changes (T, J/K, arrows, mini month, swipe) slide the tracks in from the side of travel (48 px and a fade for keys, from the swipe's side for gestures). Day/Week/Month cross-fade: the view left fades out on top while the new one scales up from 98.5%. The time grid glides on first open to now, or to the first plan if it is earlier; later visits keep their scroll; T glides back to now.
- Drag: a move or resize lifts one block that follows the pointer across days, snapping to 15 minutes and gliding 90 ms between slots, with a 3% scale and a shadow on a fading layer; the original stays faint. On drop the real event takes over from where the lifted block is drawn and settles with the spring easing (FLIP). Esc (or a cancelled pointer) glides the block back and lets go. Events whose overlap layout changes (a drop, a calendar shown or hidden, Undo, a save) glide to their new places; new plans fade in.
- The dock now holds Ask (default) and Today behind a small switch, with the close X at the right of the switch row. Ask stays mounted so tap/hold A keeps working from anywhere and opens Ask. ⇧A opens the dock on Today (or closes it there); A and ⌘J open Ask. Today reuses the page's model (combined calendars, colours, hidden calendars, popovers, quick create, drag and Undo) and the same time grid in a compact form.
- Today follows the email that is open, or the one the list cursor is on. A plan Groq already found for that email this session (Space on a suggestion, "Find plans in this email", Calendar's "From your mail", or Today's own "Find it") shows as a dashed outline beside, never over, what it clashes with; clashing events get a red ring and the strip says "Clashes with …". A plan already on a calendar is shown as that event, ringed. Today never calls Groq by itself: it offers "Find it" when the email's suggested action is a calendar event. One click on the outline or "Add to calendar…" opens the editor prefilled; nothing is saved without Save.

## 2026-10-21 — Adding a found plan goes through Today
- Space on an email whose suggested step is "Add to calendar", the reader's "Add to calendar" on a found plan, and the Calendar's "From your mail" no longer open the editor straight away. Groq finds the plan, the dock opens on Today at that day, and the plan is drawn as the dashed outline beside what it clashes with, with a confirm card above the grid: an editable title, the time, a calendar picker (the email's account's default calendar first), clashes or "You're free then", and **Not now · Edit… · Add**. Focus moves to Add, so Space then Enter adds; Esc on the card is Not now. Focus goes back where it was once the plan is added or put aside.
- The outline can be dragged to another time, or its lower edge pulled, with the same lifted drag as events; this only changes the time it would be added at. Nothing is saved until Add (or Save in the editor). Add shows "Added … to <calendar>" with Undo, which deletes the event and brings the plan back.
- "Not now" hides that email's plans from Today for the session ("This email's plan is hidden · Show"); asking again (Space or Add to calendar) shows them. Plans added from Today are remembered so one whose title or time changed before adding is not offered again; the email's next plan, if any, follows.
- Today stays on the email under review until the user moves to another email or closes the dock, so a slow extraction or the Calendar page (which has no open email) still lands on the right plan. If the user moved on or is writing, a toast offers "Review" instead of opening the dock over their work. A popped-out window has no dock, so it still opens the editor.
- With Settings → "Automatically add plans" on, extraction still saves as before. The review then finds the plan already on the calendar, so Today opens on the saved event, ringed. This path follows from `emailContext` and was not driven separately.
- A plan with guests lists them on the card ("Invites …") so a one-click Add never sends invitations the user did not see.
- In the reader, found plans live in the summary card at the top, in place of the "Add to calendar" suggestion: a date tile, the title, the day and time, the place and guests, and **Add to calendar** (or **On your calendar** once saved; both open Today on it). The old "Plans found in this conversation" list under the messages is gone; "Find plans in this email" fills the same card, or says nothing definite was found. Other suggestions (Reply, a link) stay beside the due date, and found plans still show under them.
- Event extraction gives each message its own week (the weekday it was sent and the next seven dates, in the user's zone), and the prompt says to look weekdays up there rather than count. Before, the model only had the 14 days after *now* and resolved "Friday" in a Monday email to the wrong day about one run in four.
- A found plan matches a saved event by the same moment as well as the same text: Groq writes local offsets (`14:00-07:00`) and hosts write UTC (`21:00Z`), so a string comparison treated an already-saved plan as new (and as clashing with itself).
- The reader's due pill shows only when the email has a due date (found by triage or set by the user). "Add due date" no longer sits in every summary card; ⋯ → "Due date & reminder" shows the pill while picking. A summary card with no to-do, due date, reminder or suggestion has no second row at all.

## 2026-10-21 — Ask as a tool-using agent
- Ask runs a small agent loop of our own (`src/engine/agent.ts`, platform-free) over Groq's OpenAI-compatible tool calling, streamed. No framework. Bounds: 6 model calls (the last one with `tool_choice:none` and an "answer now" note), 4 tool calls per step, 30 s per call, 120 s per answer, a 48 K-character context budget (older tool results are trimmed first, then older history), and the existing `ai.cancel`/requestId abort. Bad tool JSON, unknown tools, unknown refs and tool errors go back to the model as `{error}`; Groq's `tool_use_failed` (it validates arguments server-side and fails the whole stream) is retried, then answered without tools.
- Tools (`src/engine/ask.ts`): read-only `search_mail` (the existing FTS/operator search, now `searchMail()` in the service, with a some-of-the-words fallback), `read_thread`, `list_events` (state events), `find_contact`, `current_context`; proposal-only `propose_draft`, `propose_event`, `propose_triage`. No tool can send, save, archive or schedule. The model sees mail only by short refs (m1, m2…), so citations and triage can only point at mail a tool showed it; recipients and guests must appear in the mail or be typed by the user. Tool schemas stay permissive (no enum/min/max/required) because a schema violation makes Groq drop the whole turn; our own checks give the model a usable error instead.
- The open email (or Home focus), selection, draft, accounts, view and clock are seeded in the system prompt, so "summarize this" needs no tool. The old "first 100 threads" stuffing and contacts dump are gone, as is `groq.ask`.
- Citations are written by the model as `[m2]` (also `(m2)`/`【m2】`) and stored as `[^n]` against `citations[n-1]`; the panel shows numbered inline chips plus source chips. Steps are stored on the message; rate-limit waits are shown live but not saved.
- Streaming: engine events `ai.progress` (text batched every 40 ms). Groq answers short questions in a few hundred milliseconds, so short answers mostly appear at once; long ones visibly stream.
- Ask model setting (`askModel`, General → AI): `openai/gpt-oss-120b` (default), `openai/gpt-oss-20b`, `qwen/qwen3.8-27b` — the only tool-capable chat models this key can reach on 2026-10-21 (Kimi K2, Llama 4 and Qwen3-32B are retired). All three passed the 5-question live check (`npm run eval:ask`); 20B put a "Friday" lunch on Tuesday and skipped the contact lookup once, so its description says to review its cards. `GROQ_MODEL` still applies to the other AI features only. (Superseded 2026-10-21: per-feature models in Settings → AI; `GROQ_MODEL` now only seeds Summaries, Drafts and Plans for a setup saved before those settings existed, and scripts that call Groq directly.)
- Groq's free tier allows 8 K tokens/minute and 200 K tokens/day per model; one Ask answer is 1.6–8 K prompt tokens. Ask waits out short 429s (up to 6 waits within its 2-minute deadline, shown as "Waiting for Groq · about N s") and explains long ones (daily quota) with a suggestion to switch model. For daily use with Ask, Groq's Developer tier is the practical fix.
- Open for the owner: a provider abstraction was not added (Groq only per AGENTS.md). If wanted later, `Chat` in `agent.ts` is already the seam: any OpenAI-compatible streaming endpoint with tools fits it without touching the loop or tools.

## 2026-10-21 — Ask providers and parallel tools
- Owner decision: Ask may run on OpenRouter or the owner's OpenCode Go plan as well as Groq. Every other AI feature (summaries, polish, drafts, calendar extraction, transcription) stays on Groq. AGENTS.md updated accordingly.
- One OpenAI-compatible client (`src/main/llm.ts`) serves all three: same streaming parser, error mapping (401/403 key, 402 credits, 429 with retry-after, 404 model) and no provider bodies in errors. Keys come only from the backend environment (`OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, `OPENCODE_API_KEY`); the renderer only sees whether one is set.
- The setting is `askModel = provider:model` (`groq:openai/gpt-oss-120b` default); bare Groq IDs from the first Ask build migrate. Settings → General → AI has Ask provider and Ask model. `ai.models` lists each provider with live models where it publishes a catalog: OpenRouter's public list filtered to models that accept `tools` (about 400), curated picks first; OpenCode Go's list intersected with the models it serves over chat/completions (Kimi K3, GLM 5.3, GLM 5.3 Flash, DeepSeek V4 Pro, DeepSeek V4.1 Flash). Go's Anthropic- and Responses-style models (Claude, Qwen3.8 Max, GPT) are left out rather than adding two more wire formats.
- OpenCode Go: OpenCode says Go "is designed for OpenCode and other coding agents" and asks clients to send their own user agent and a stable `x-opencode-session`; Aplos sends `aplos-mail/<version>` and the conversation ID. Mail Q&A is not coding-agent traffic, so it may be throttled or flagged; that risk is the owner's call. OpenCode Zen (pay per token, same key format) would be the sanctioned alternative if it becomes a problem.
- Non-Groq providers get a 100 K-character context budget and 4,096 output tokens (reasoning models need room); Groq stays at 48 K / 2,048 for its free-tier per-minute limit.
- Parallel tools: all tool calls from one model step now run concurrently and return in the order asked (up to 6 per step), and the system prompt asks models to batch independent lookups. GPT-OSS on Groq still emits one call at a time; Qwen, Claude, Kimi, GLM and similar batch.
- Not verified live: OpenRouter and OpenCode Go (no keys configured for Aplos; the opencode CLI's own login was deliberately not reused). Fake-server tests cover request shape, headers, streaming quirks, catalogs and routing.

## 2026-10-21 — Settings → AI
- AI moved out of General into its own Settings tab. Ask's provider is three cards (Groq, OpenRouter, OpenCode Go) with a selection ring that glides between them and a Ready / Needs a key status; picking one without a key shows how to add it (copyable variable name, link to the provider's key page) instead of hiding the choice.
