- The model is a searchable picker (Recommended, then All models with a count) instead of a native select, because OpenRouter offers about 400 tool-capable models. Arrows, Enter and Esc work; keys typed in it never reach the app's shortcuts; short lists jump by first letter.
- "Test Ask" (`ai.test`) sends one tiny request asking the chosen model to call a `ping` tool and reports the time, or warns when the model replied without using the tool. It runs only on click and includes no mail.
- An "Also on Groq" group shows that summaries, drafts, plans and voice still use Groq and whether its key is set.

## 2026-10-21 — More Ask providers: ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini, custom
- Owner request. Ask's providers are now Groq, ChatGPT (OpenAI API, `OPENAI_API_KEY`), Claude (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`), Gemini (`GEMINI_API_KEY`), OpenRouter, OpenCode Go and Custom. Everything else stays on Groq.
- OpenAI and Gemini use the shared OpenAI-compatible client (Gemini through Google's `/v1beta/openai` endpoint). OpenAI's reasoning models reject `temperature`, so none is sent; both get `reasoning_effort: low` for quick mail answers. Model lists come from each provider's `/models` (key required), filtered to chat models, curated picks first.
- Claude uses the official `@anthropic-ai/sdk` and the Messages API rather than an OpenAI-compatible shim (`src/main/anthropic.ts`). Ask's OpenAI-shaped history is mapped to `tool_use` / `tool_result` blocks; each Claude turn's own content (thinking blocks included) is kept on the assistant message as `raw` and replayed unchanged, as the API requires, and stripped before any OpenAI-compatible request. `effort: low`, refusals reported plainly, a tool call cut off by `max_tokens` is not run. Curated: Claude Opus 5.5, Sonnet 5.5, Haiku 5.5.
- Custom: any OpenAI-compatible server (Ollama, LM Studio, vLLM). Its address is a setting (`askCustomUrl`, http or https, no credentials in the URL), its key optional (`CUSTOM_AI_API_KEY`); with no key no Authorization header is sent. Its model can be picked from the server's list or typed. Settings warns that the mail you ask about is sent to that server.
- Not verified live: none of these keys are configured for Aplos. Fake-server tests cover endpoints, headers, OpenAI's missing temperature, Gemini's model names, a Claude streaming round trip with tool calls and block replay, refusals, and the custom address rules and routing.

## 2026-10-21 — Ollama and LM Studio as first-class Ask providers
- Owner request. Both get their own card, default local address (Ollama `http://localhost:11434`, LM Studio `http://localhost:1234`; changeable per app in Settings → AI, e.g. another Mac on the network) and no key; the optional `OLLAMA_API_KEY` / `LM_STUDIO_API_KEY` cover ollama.com and an LM Studio server with auth on. The card shows Running / Not running from a live probe; when an app isn't answering, Settings says what to start, offers a command to copy (`ollama pull qwen3`, `lms server start`), Try again and a download link.
- Ollama uses its native `/api/chat` (`src/main/ollama.ts`), not its OpenAI shim, because only the native API can raise the context window per request: Ollama's default can be 4K tokens, which would silently cut Ask's prompt, so Ask asks for 16K (`options.num_ctx`), `think:false` for quick answers and `keep_alive` 10 minutes. Tool calls come back as object arguments without IDs (Aplos assigns them) and results go back with `tool_name`. There is no tool_choice, so the final step sends no tools. Models: `/api/tags`, kept only when `/api/show` lists the `tools` capability (older versions without capabilities keep everything), with size and quantization shown.
- LM Studio chats through its OpenAI-compatible `/v1/chat/completions` (its native chat API has no custom tools) and lists models from its REST `/api/v1/models`: language models with `trained_for_tool_use`, with size, quantization and whether loaded; older versions fall back to `/v1/models`. Unloaded models load on demand (LM Studio's just-in-time loading). Note: `options.num_ctx` on Ollama and LM Studio's JIT context length aren't confirmed in their current docs.
- Not verified against real apps (neither is installed here). Fake-server tests cover both adapters; the Settings screens and an Ask round trip were checked against a temporary stand-in Ollama on loopback, stopped afterwards.

## 2026-10-21 — Ask panel refinement
- Working state: no dots. Finished tool steps sit as quiet lines above one live line with a soft blue pulse and a light sweeping the words: "Thinking" before any tool, the running step ("Searching mail for “invoice”") while one runs, "Writing the answer" after. Once text streams, the steps fold into one summary line ("Searched mail, checked your calendar ›") that expands on click.
- Cards share one shape: a small kind label, the preview, a hairline, then actions on the right, and a green done state. The event card is now a preview of the calendar entry (date tile, title, day and time, place, guests, "You’re free then" or the clash) with Add to calendar, Edit (the full event editor) and a calendar picker when there is more than one; once added it reads "Added to your calendar" with Undo (removes it) and View. It recognises an event already added, also after a restart. The inline field-by-field editor and mini day grid were removed for a calmer card.
- The footnote's model name opens Settings → AI.

## 2026-10-21 — Ask empty state and opening slide
- Opening Ask no longer shifts the whole app: focusing the field while the panel was still off the right edge scrolled the `overflow: hidden` app shell 360 px, which then slid back as the panel arrived. The shell now uses `overflow: clip` (cannot be scrolled at all) and the field focuses with `preventScroll`.
- "Hold A to talk" sits right-aligned inside the field instead of the mic button and the line above the list; clicking it starts recording like the mic did. The History button and its view are gone.
- The dock now animates its width with the panel pinned to its right edge. The earlier approach (width in one step, panel sliding over it with a transform) left an empty strip showing the window background for the length of the slide. Animating width relayouts the mail column each frame, but the list is virtualized so that stays small.
- Talking: the chip starts recording on click (Done finishes) and records while held, asking on release. Holding A also works inside Ask's own field: the A is held back for 220 ms, a tap types it, and if another key arrives first the A is typed before it so fast typing keeps its order. Shift+A, IME composition and other fields type normally. Speech held in the field follows whatever was already typed.
- Recent rows drop the history icon and gain an X that deletes the chat. In a chat the field moves to the bottom of the panel, under the conversation. Recent chats (a clock that opens a menu of every chat, newest first, the open one ticked) and then New chat (a plus, ⌘ N) sit just right of the Ask/Calendar tabs; Ask renders them there through a portal into a slot App leaves in the dock header.
- Empty panel: a first-time user sees only "Try asking". Afterwards the examples go and it shows "Recent" (last three chats) and "Last searched": emails opened from Ask, newest first, whether picked from its matches or clicked in an answer (sources, inline citations, triage rows). Only clicks count; mail an answer merely mentions does not. Kept in local storage (`aplos.askOpened`, 20 ids), shown for the selected account, never trash or spam.

## 2026-10-21 — Providers and a model per feature
- Settings → AI has two sections. Providers lists every provider with its status (Ready / Needs a key / Running / Not running / Needs an address); opening a row shows what it offers or what it needs (key variable to copy, server address, how to start a local app). Models has one picker per feature: Ask, Summaries (summarize/classify/suggested action), Drafts (draft + polish), Plans (calendar extraction) and Voice (transcription), plus Ask's connection check.
- Owner's decision supersedes "Groq for every AI feature": Groq stays the default for everything, but any feature can use any set-up provider. Stored as `summaryModel`, `draftModel`, `planModel`, `voiceModel` (`provider:model`) beside `askModel`; unreadable values fall back to the Groq defaults on load.
- Summaries, drafts and plans moved to a platform-free `engine/writer.ts` that takes a JSON call. Groq keeps its own non-streamed JSON-mode call; other providers go through their chat transport with `json:true` (OpenAI sends `response_format: json_object`, Ollama `format: json`; the rest, including Gemini whose OpenAI endpoint documents only schema-based structured output, rely on the prompt) and the first JSON object in the reply is parsed, so a model that wraps it in prose or a code fence still works.
- Voice: only Groq and OpenAI have a transcription API here. OpenAI's speech models come from its live model list (transcribe/whisper, excluding realtime and diarize variants) with a curated fallback. Not verified live against OpenAI.
- Pickers show models from providers that are set up, grouped by provider, plus the current choice even if its provider isn't ready (marked, and the row says so in amber). Catalogs over 12 models (OpenRouter) show recommended ones until you search. Background sorting of new mail skips quietly when the Summaries provider isn't set up, or is a local app (Ollama, LM Studio) that isn't running; Summaries and Plans are gated separately since they can use different providers.
- Polish refuses emails over 40,000 characters of HTML (and replies far longer than the input) instead of truncating, because its result replaces the whole body. The Reader's refresh tooltip names the provider the next summary will use, not the one that wrote the current summary (that isn't stored).
- Pre-merge review (2026-10-22): replies are read leniently (whole reply, then a fenced block, then each balanced `{…}` from the last, after dropping `<think>` blocks), so reasoning models on custom servers work. Polish keeps inline `data:` images out of the request (placeholders, restored after; a reply that drops one is refused) and sizes its reply budget to the email: 4096 tokens for emails that fit before (per-minute plans such as Groq's free tier count the budget against their limit; a 413 now says so), about a token per 4 characters beyond that. Ollama grows its context only for such JSON requests; Ask keeps the fixed 16K. A reply that is already JSON is read untouched before any `<think>` stripping. Each feature only accepts its kind of model (Whisper/transcribe IDs for Voice only). Existing setups keep `GROQ_MODEL` for Summaries, Drafts and Plans. The Settings note now says new mail goes to the Summaries (and Plans) model during sync.
- Reader summary: an email without a real summary is summarized when opened, once per session per email (`src/renderer/summaries.ts`); a failure stays on the card with its reason until Retry, so reopening doesn't ask again. Reasons come from matching the engine's provider error messages and name the provider chosen for Summaries in Settings → AI, with its key variable (`src/renderer/summaryText.ts`); unknown errors fall back to "Something went wrong" with the raw message in the popover.
- `ai.summarize` (reader) applies the summary and suggested action every time, fills to-do and due date only when empty, and sets importance and reply state only while the email has no real summary (`src/shared/summary.ts`, the same rule the card uses). Fields changed while the model works keep the owner's value, as in sync classification.
- Mail list rows without a real summary show the subject (dimmed), not the snippet; rows on screen are summarized in the background, two at a time, once per session, pausing for a minute after a failure. Failures are not shown in the list; the reader explains them.
- Composer (2026-10-22): Space-hold dictation works from any field in the composer, not just an empty body; the held Space is replayed as a typed space (or a button press) on a quick tap, and flushed before the next key so fast typing keeps its order. Ask's panel, menus, dialogs and native selects keep Space. The AI "Write" instruction strip was removed with its button; suggested replies still draft through `ai('draft')`. Polish uses a spell-check glyph, never a sparkle.
- Leaving the composer (2026-10-22): App routes navigation through `leaveCompose`, which calls the open Compose's exit (`exitRef`) and continues only if it closed. The exit asks Save / Delete / Keep editing only for a draft started in this session that has content; a draft reopened from Drafts is already saved there (edits included) and closes quietly, so Delete there can't remove a long-lived draft by surprise. Clicks made while it decides replace each other; only the last navigation runs. Dictated text goes above a trailing quoted message (a forward's original), otherwise at the end. Native window close of a popout keeps the old save-silently path, since it can't wait on a dialog.
- eng-store (2026-10-28): `laneFor` input building forgives only a body decode failure (missing codec, corrupt row: the lane runs without text); every SQL read in `laneInput` still fails the commit loudly, and only the `laneFor` call itself is guarded. A draft sent in place under the same remote id counts its recipients as correspondents once the draft bit clears. Follow-ups, not done: the replay property test defaults to 80 sequences (`APLOS_REPLAY_RUNS=1000` runs the full depth, about 22 s); the rollup folder-totals check compares both sides through the same helper; the replay test never installs `laneFor` or ingest extensions; a delete then resync counts a correspondent twice; `compactChanges` age floor trusts `commits.at` if the wall clock moves backwards (clients only re-snapshot).

## 2026-10-28 — Q2 Electron hardening: debug guard on the release define, no E2E variant yet (plan 40 Q2 item 6, partly)

- **The debug guard is keyed on the release define (ADR 40-6).** `src/main/security.ts` refuses `--remote-debugging-*`, `--inspect*`, `--debug`, `--debug-brk` and `--debug-port` (one or two dashes, or as Chromium parsed them) with exit 78 only in a bundle built with `APLOS_RELEASE_BUILD=1`, and never consults `app.isPackaged`, which Electron derives from the executable's name. This follows the Engine thread's rule that test-only behaviour is compiled out only when `APLOS_RELEASE_BUILD=1`. The updater's `APLOS_UPDATE_DEV` local feed is never used in a release build. DevTools follow the same define: `webPreferences.devTools: !releaseBuild` in `windows.ts` and the Toggle Developer Tools menu item gated on `!releaseBuild` (closed 2026-10-28 in #40).
- **Deviation: no `APLOS_E2E` define and no E2E build variant.** Plan 40 Q2 item 6 moves `verify-release.mjs` to an E2E variant. The reconciliation of `q2-guard` and `q2-fuses` dropped the `APLOS_E2E` define that `q2-fuses` had added, because nothing needed it once the guard keys on the release define: `npm run package` builds (and `electron .`) have no release define, so they keep `--remote-debugging-port`, and `verify-release.mjs` keeps running against them and stops with a clear message when it is pointed at a release build (exit 78). Those `npm run package` builds carry the same `electronFuses` as a release, since electron-builder applies `build.electronFuses` to every run; `verify-release.mjs` drives the app over Chromium's remote debugging (CDP), which the Node inspector fuse does not affect. So for now the "variant" is the local package: fused like a release, ad-hoc signed with `entitlements.adhoc.plist`, without the release define, never uploaded (`release-build.mjs` refuses a bundle without the define and `release:publish` refuses an ad-hoc build). It is not `show: false` and does not hide the Dock icon, so it still shows a window: `verify-release.mjs` remains a run for a person at the screen, not for headless agents, until F8's test mode exists.
- **Closed: a release build ignores `APLOS_TEST` and `APLOS_DATA_DIR`.** `test-mode.ts` compiles both out when `APLOS_RELEASE_BUILD=1` (the Engine thread's rule), so a release build never reads them; the guard half (exit 78) and this half are both in code. `verify-hardening` is the place for a launch row proving normal behaviour with them set. (The browser-preview `server.ts` still reads `APLOS_DATA_DIR`; it is not part of the desktop release.)
- **Only a Developer ID release is uploaded.** `release-build.mjs --publish always` refuses before building without `CSC_LINK`/`CSC_NAME` or with `--expect adhoc`, and `--upload-only` verifies every `.app` under `release/` with `--expect release` before it uploads. The Release workflow's checkout sets `persist-credentials: false`, so only the upload step's environment holds a GitHub token.
## 2026-10-27 — Revamp UI foundation (F9): Paper tokens, design lint, skill, frozen props

- **"Match the reference" is retired.** The earlier entries "Visual parity (M14)" and "Interaction parity (M16)" earlier in this file told agents to reproduce the reference app's measurable look and timings. Both are superseded by `plans/DESIGN.md` (Paper), section 13. The entry that says interface phrases matching the reference word for word were rewritten was not true: DESIGN §13 lists 17 Avec-derived layouts and phrases still in today's screens. The new screens drop all of them, and the design lint's banned-string and banned-class rules keep them out. The reference apps are ideas only.
- **One token source.** `apps/desktop/src/renderer/ui/tokens.json` (W3C design-token format) is the only file with raw design values. `apps/desktop/scripts/design/build-tokens.mjs` generates `ui/generated/tokens.css`, `ui/generated/motion.ts` and `ui/generated/contrast-pairs.json`. Its `--check` mode fails when a generated file was edited by hand. The generated files live in `generated/` so they never collided with today's `ui/tokens.css` during Phase 0.
- **Colour checks without a dependency.** WCAG contrast, Lab and CIEDE2000 are computed in `scripts/design/color.mjs` and checked against the identity audit's `de2000.py`. `check-contrast.mjs` checks every declared pair in light, dark and Increase Contrast. It also holds the accent and status colours at least ΔE00 8.5 from the reference brand colours (10 from Avec's), and the shell's Lab b* below zero. The resting field ring (about 1.33:1) is a declared exception (DESIGN decision 20).
- **Design lint.** Each DESIGN §12.2 rule is one core check over a PostCSS or TypeScript AST in `tools/lint/design/rules/`. There is a standalone runner (`node tools/lint/design/run.mjs`) and thin ESLint and stylelint adapters, so the rules ran before ESLint and stylelint were installed, and there is one implementation for both tools.
- **Components.** The props of every DESIGN §6 component are frozen in `renderer/ui/props.ts`. Changes after the freeze are additive and announced with HANDOFF-READY.
- **Commands.** Every action is declared once in `renderer/commands/catalog.ts`. A pure resolver applies DESIGN §5 rule 16 (IME, Caps Lock, single keys off in text fields) and decision 7 (arrow triage only on Home, one by one and the reader). The shortcut sheet is generated from the registry, so it can no longer miss ⌃5, ⌃7, ⌃8, ⌘K or ⌘/.
- **The `aplos-design` skill** (`.claude/skills/aplos-design/SKILL.md`) is the rulebook every UI agent loads, per AGENTS.md. A check compares the token names it lists with `tokens.json`.
- **Design lint inside ESLint and stylelint.** `tools/lint/design/index.mjs` (ESLint, scoped by `files` and `ignores`) and `tools/lint/design/stylelint.mjs` (each rule skips files outside the new renderer tree) wire the same core rules into `npm run check`. Component classes are kebab-case blocks with BEM elements and modifiers (`ui-list-row__date`).
- **Headless timing is valid.** A never-shown Electron window reports a 60 Hz frame clock, long-animation-frame and Event Timing entries, and real `capturePage` pixels, so `test:perf` and the visual harness run headless (`tests/perf/timing-spike/RESULT.md`). Sandboxed agent shells refuse a second renderer process, so harness runs reuse one window.
- **Holds.** A hold releases 450 ms after the last key, so a burst of any length closes once. A pointer still on the list then holds at most 1200 ms past the last key. A pointer resting with no key holds until it leaves, and hiding the window releases. One controller in `renderer/data/holds.ts` serves the data layer and the motion runtime.

## 2026-10-28 contract-v1 freeze sign-off (integrator)

- **Engine size.** The neutral engine bundle the budget test counts (new engine without `legacy/`, no zod) measures 165,845 bytes at 9544e97, against the 400,000-byte budget.
- **Round-2 leftovers deferred to Phase 1, additively within `v:1`.** FR-34 / R2G-2 (stand-in reports `mailFrame: true` while the `srcdoc` rule says "only while `mailFrame` is false"), FR-35 (`Folder.role` does not list `category.*`; the enum is open), FR-7's additive points, R2B-7, R2G-5 to R2G-7 and R2H-5 to R2H-10. None changes a frozen shape. The owning lane fixes each in a contract-change PR the integrator merges.

## 2026-10-28 Google OAuth client secret (providers, #59)

- **A bundled Desktop-client secret is allowed; keys are not.** Google treats the client secret of an installed (Desktop) app as non-confidential, and the token exchange needs it. `oauth/client.ts` may therefore resolve an embedded client id and secret for official builds. This is an exemption from the AGENTS.md "no bundled keys" rule for this one value only; API keys, user tokens and `.env` stay out of the bundle. The secret is never printed or logged.
- **Reconnect always asks for consent.** Google returns a refresh token only on a consent grant, so `reconnectAuthParams` sends `prompt=consent` and `access_type=offline` with the login hint. A reconnect that returns no refresh token is a failed reconnect and keeps the old state.
- **`reconnectBy` carries a margin.** It is the refresh-token expiry less 18 hours (at most half the lifetime), so the weekly reconnect is asked for before the token dies.
- eng-views (2026-10-28): the engine-bundle budget in `packages/engine/tests/portability.test.ts` is measured on the minified neutral bundle (what ships) and stays 400,000 bytes; the test prints the figure. Today: 253,443 bytes minified, 426,360 unminified (store, runtime and views 1 to 3). The unminified figure is a growth signal only. (An earlier draft raised the unminified limit to 450,000; the Integrator asked for the minified measurement instead.)

## 2026-10-29 eng-time: the timer loop survives a hung source and a sleeping Mac (Integrator review of #89)

- **A hung `fire` or `next` no longer stalls the loop.** Each call is raced against `clock.sleep`: `FIRE_TIMEOUT_MS` 30 s for `fire`, `NEXT_TIMEOUT_MS` 5 s for `next` (a read is a local query). On timeout the loop logs `engine.watchdog` with `kind: 'timer_drift'` (the catalog has no dedicated timer-stall event; this is the nearest, and `lagMs` carries the timeout), backs the slot off 1 s and carries on with the other sources, so one stuck network call in `sync:*`, ops, drafts or jobs cannot hold back the outbox deadline.
- **A slot never runs concurrently with itself.** An abandoned call leaves the slot `inflight`; `earliest()` and the read step skip it. When the real promise settles (resolve or reject, both handled, so no unhandled rejection) the slot is marked dirty and the loop kicked, which re-reads its deadline. `stop()` is unchanged (10 s grace).
- **The system clock caps every wait at 60 s (`MAX_SYSTEM_WAIT_MS`).** libuv timers use the monotonic clock, which pauses while the Mac sleeps, so an 8 h deadline with 7 h of sleep fired about 7 h late and the send-later rule (suspend a send more than 5 min past `not_before`) could suspend a message due just before wake. `systemTimer` re-checks `Date.now()` every minute (it already re-arms when early), and `sleep` goes through the same timer, so lateness is bounded to about a minute with no host help. The cost is one timer turn a minute while something is armed. The fake clock is unchanged.
- **`Engine.wake()` is added (additive).** It forwards the host's power-resume, network-return or clock-change hook to the loop's `wake()` (a no-op after `close`, or with a host-injected registry that has no `wake`). The host (eng-host) should call it on `powerMonitor` `resume`; with the cap it is an improvement from about a minute to immediate, not a requirement.
- **Small fixes in the same change.** A source marked dirty while another fires is re-read instead of fired on its stale deadline. The 1 s backoff is set before the `timer_drift` log line and every loop log call is wrapped, so a throwing log port cannot cause an immediate re-fire. `register()` with an id already registered still replaces the first source; the replaced slot is orphaned (its running fire is not read again and the new source can overlap it), now documented on `register`.
- **Note for time-2 (outbox): send-level idempotency.** `STOP_GRACE_MS` (10 s) lets a send that outlives it be abandoned at quit, so the same message may be sent again at the next launch. The loop cannot prevent that. The outbox must make a send idempotent (a per-message send key checked before the provider call and recorded in the same commit as the sent state) so a re-fire after an abandoned send is harmless; the same holds for a send abandoned by `FIRE_TIMEOUT_MS`, which keeps running in the background.

## eng-time time-2: the submission state machine (2026-10-29)

- **No new store columns.** The WIP's `0002_outbox` migration collided with the store's own 0002 and 0003 and the store is not this lane's file, so it is dropped. Everything the outbox needs beyond 0001 and `submissions.revision` (when it was queued, the store rev, the `findSent` check count and next check, the sent time, subject and recipient names, the envelope, the `Submission/set` opId) lives as one JSON object in `submissions.error`, next to the problem; `error_until` stays the problem's `until`. `outbox/store.ts` is the only reader and writer, so a later migration that gives these real columns is mechanical. Request to eng-store, not blocking: a partial index on `(state) WHERE state IN ('sending','uncertain')` (the outbox reads those rows on every recompute).
- **The start rule runs on every fire, not only at launch or wake.** A `sending` row that this engine instance is not awaiting (`inflight`) is a lost outcome: it becomes `uncertain` with a `findSent` due now, never `suspended`, re-queued or re-sent. Doing it on every fire needs no wake signal (the loop's `wake()` re-reads `next()`, and `next()` reports a lost row as due now), and it also recovers a row whose settle commit failed in this process. `module.wake()` and `module.start()` remain. Two live engines on one store would let the second turn the first's in-flight send `uncertain`; it still never sends it, the first still settles it `sent`, and the single-instance lock is the real guard.
- **Nothing that can fail runs between the marker and `send`.** The payload (draft read, remote-draft precondition) is built before the marker, and `inflight` is set before it. A send that is never re-sent needs exactly one rule: state `sending` is committed (compare-and-set on the waiting state and `not_before`) before the first byte, and nothing but a human (`sendAnyway`) moves `uncertain` or `failed` back to `queued`.
- **`SendPort.admission` refusing with `send_limit` fails the waiting row** (`failed`, problem with `until`) instead of waiting, the same outcome as a `PausedError` with `cls:'send_limit'` from `send`: never auto-retried (D7), Retry is the user's. Any other refusal waits until its `until` (or polls every 30 s) and stays under the overdue rule.
- **A hung send does not stop the overdue rule.** The account is busy while its job runs, but `next()` still returns `not_before + grace + 1` for its waiting rows, so they are suspended on time ("Send now or Reschedule") instead of waiting silently behind a send that never returns. `SendPort.send` has no abort signal, so adapters must bound their own calls.
- **`findSent` that throws counts as a check that found nothing**, so the owner gets the prompt after the second check even when offline. At launch every `uncertain` row without a due check gets one more round (the Sent folder or the search index may have caught up). `since` is `min(attempt_at, now)`, so a wall clock set back after the marker cannot push the search window past the message.
- **The same revision is never sent twice under a new Message-ID.** `create` refuses a draft whose revision already has a `sent` submission (`stateMismatch`, "already sent"). `sendAnyway` is the one deliberate second delivery of one Message-ID, and only after the prompt; the counting fake's "never two deliveries" invariant excludes it by design.
- **Backward clock jump:** `not_before` is absolute, so a row queued before a jump back waits the difference; nothing sends early and nothing is lost. Forward: waiting rows more than 5 minutes late are suspended, a `sending` row never.
- **The send is built once.** `buildMime` runs at `send` with a random boundary and the bytes are stored in `submissions.mime`; a retry, `sendAnyway` or a restart reuses them byte for byte and the Message-ID is never regenerated. `Message-ID` is `<128 random bits@sender-domain>` (not `aplos.local`), which spam filters prefer.
- **MIME hardening (defects in the WIP).** The SMTP envelope (including Bcc, which was never validated) goes through the same `checkAddress` as the headers, so a CR/LF or a command in an address is refused before anything is queued; non-ASCII domains become punycode, a non-ASCII local part is refused (no SMTPUTF8). `htmlToText` was quadratic on `<`/`<li ` input (a 1 MB draft could hang the engine) and mis-indexed after `toLowerCase`; it is now one linear `indexOf` pass with ASCII lower-casing, and a lone `<` is text. Inline images with a Content-ID go in `multipart/related` so `cid:` resolves. Encoded words are 36 bytes so a folded header stays near 76 columns.
- **`alsoDone` is not handled here** (it needs views' `Thread/act` and op journal), and the outbox does not delete the local draft or its remote copy when a submission is `sent` (time-3's `Draft/*`). `Draft/set|resolve|destroy` must refuse while `liveSubmissionOf(draftId)` exists and call `Submission/set undo` before a destroy.

## eng-time time-2: fixes after the Opus review of the send state machine (2026-10-29)

- **`sendAnyway` waits for the checks.** It is refused unless `checkAt` is absent and the problem is `send.uncertain` (the prompt is showing), with "We are still checking whether this was sent". The same demand (`ifChecksDone`) is part of the commit's compare-and-set, so a check scheduled between the act's read and its commit makes `sendAnyway` lose. Before, a send that reached the server while the search index lagged could be re-sent a second after it went `uncertain`.
- **One `now` per fire.** `fireOutbox` passes its `now` to the start rule, so a recovered row's `checkAt` is never later than the `now` `startChecks` filters with. On a real clock the recovered row's `findSent` now always runs before any other send of the account.
- **Header hardening.** `htmlToText` trims line ends right to left (it was quadratic on long runs of spaces or `&nbsp;`). A Message-ID taken from inbound mail (In-Reply-To, References) must be `<` + printable ASCII except `<>"\` + `>`; anything else is dropped, never thrown. A long attachment name is written as RFC 2231 sections (`name*0=`, `filename*0*=UTF-8''`), a display name or subject word longer than 64 characters is RFC 2047 encoded, a Content-ID over 255 characters is not written, and `buildMime` throws `MimeError` rather than emit a header line over 998 octets.
- **A failed-without-bytes row says Cancel.** `send.noBytes` rows are only ever cleared by Cancel (Retry would fail again, and `send` refuses while the row is live). The copy now says "Cancel it, then open the draft and send it again", and Retry on such a row is refused with that sentence. No new send path: nothing was sent, so Cancel then `send` (new Message-ID) is already safe.
- **`settleFailed` from `uncertain` is intended.** A `NotSentError` for the attempt in flight (same `attempt_at`) proves the bytes did not leave, so it settles `failed` (Retry) even if another engine's start rule had made the row `uncertain`. Pinned by a test.
- **`cancel` of an `uncertain` row waits for the checks too.** It needs the same `ifChecksDone` guard as `sendAnyway` (checks over, problem `send.uncertain`), enforced inside the commit; while a check is pending it is refused with "We are still checking whether this message went out." Cancelling earlier unlocked the draft, a second send got a new Message-ID, and the pending `findSent` result for the first (now `cancelled`) row was dropped: two deliveries. A new `send` for the draft is already refused while any live row (`uncertain` included) exists, checked inside the create commit.
- **Mime limits, round two.** `htmlToText` tracks `atLineStart` instead of reading `out.endsWith` (which flattened the string per tag). `headerSafe` collapses any run of CR, LF, NUL, spaces and tabs to one space, and `foldHeader` folds by index and never emits a whitespace-only line. `References` keeps the first id and the last 20; the top header block may not pass 10,000 octets (`MimeError`, after trimming).

### Gaps for other lanes (found by the review, not fixable in the outbox)

- **eng-contract / eng-host: services.** The production module has no `blobs` or `settings` services (`EngineServices` lacks them): attachments are refused in the real engine ("no longer available") and `undoSendSeconds` is fixed at 10 s until the host provides them.
- **Providers: Bcc on the wire.** The outbox keeps Bcc out of the MIME and puts it in `SubmissionPayload.envelope.rcpt`. An adapter whose API reads recipients from the MIME headers (Gmail `messages.send` raw) must add the Bcc recipients itself (for Gmail, a transient Bcc header on the bytes it uploads, never stored), or Bcc recipients get nothing.
- **Adapters / governor: a hung `SendPort.send` has no engine-side limit.** There is no abort signal; the account stays busy until the adapter returns. Adapters or the governor must bound every send call (the overdue rule still suspends the waiting rows behind it).
- **eng-store: account removal.** Removing an account cascades and silently deletes its queued, scheduled, suspended, failed and uncertain submissions. The store should refuse the removal, or the host should confirm and list what would be lost, before it runs.

## eng-time time-2: fixes after the SEND-GUARD review of PR #105 (2026-10-29)

- **One Message-ID per message, including the remote draft's (HIGH).** The send used to mint a fresh Message-ID while the adapter could send the synced Gmail draft (`drafts.send`), which carries the per-revision `drafts.remote_internet_id`. After a timeout `findSent` searched for an id that never left, found nothing and the owner was offered Send anyway: a second copy. Now `prepareSend` reuses `remote_internet_id` as the row's Message-ID (and in the pinned MIME) when `synced_revision` equals the pinned revision and the stored id is well formed; otherwise it makes a fresh one. `payloadOf` names `remoteDraftId` and `baseRemoteMessageId` only when `drafts.remote_internet_id === submissions.internet_id`, so the remote draft can leave only under the id `findSent` will look for. A draft synced at another revision sends the pinned MIME without the remote draft; its stale remote copy is left for time-3's draft cleanup after `sent` (before this, `remoteDraftId` was passed for it).
- **The sender is the account address (MEDIUM).** The engine stores no send-as aliases yet (`account-read.ts` lists the account address as the only identity), so `Submission/set send` refuses any `from.address` that is not the account's own (trimmed, case-insensitive) with a validation sentence; the address is written as stored and the draft's display name is kept. Gap for eng-store/providers: when send-as identities are stored (Gmail `sendAs.list`, Graph aliases), `senderOf` in `outbox/submissions.ts` should accept them.
- **Not-sent classes keep their own reason (MEDIUM).** `permanent`, `permission`, `anti_abuse` and `config` are `send.permanent`, `send.permission`, `send.anti_abuse` and `send.config` (the contract has no send-specific codes for them) with plain copy that says what to do. They fail the same way every time, so `Retry` on one is refused with "Sending again would fail the same way. Cancel this message, fix the cause, then send the draft again." Only `net.offline` (transient and unknown classes), `auth.expired` and `quota.sendLimit` stay retryable. The contract's `Submission` has no `canRetry` flag, so the UI must hide Retry for those four codes (the engine refuses it regardless); a flag in the contract would be cleaner. `anti_abuse` says "try again later" yet refuses Retry, as the review asked: a later send is a new one from the draft.
- **Date is the send time (recommended).** `Date:` is the row's `not_before` (the end of the undo window, or the chosen time), not the compose time. Reschedule and Send now re-date the stored bytes inside the same commit that moves the row: only the first header line is replaced (`withDate`), so the Message-ID, headers, body and attachments are byte for byte as pinned and no draft or blob is read again. The move is a compare-and-set on the waiting state, so once the `sending` marker is committed it loses and the bytes are never touched. Retry and Send anyway keep the bytes exactly.
- **Domains the URL parser cuts short are refused (recommended).** The punycode step uses `new URL`, which drops everything after `/ ? # \ :` and strips tab and LF (`a@evil.com/bänk.com` became `evil.com`). A non-ASCII domain containing `/ ? # \ : @ %`, whitespace or a control character is now refused before the conversion.
- **`findSent` looks 5 minutes earlier (low).** `since` is the attempt time minus `CLOCK_SKEW_MARGIN_MS` (5 minutes), because the provider stamps the message with its own clock, which can run behind ours; a wider window only costs a few more candidates in the search.

## eng-time time-4: reminders and Later (2026-10-29)

- **Taken from the WIP (`rv/eng-time/reminders-later`), and what changed.** Only the two sources and the handlers. The WIP's own `system.ts`, `loop.ts`, `module.ts` and `zone.ts` are dropped (the merged clock has the first three; `calendar/recurrence/zone.ts` already has the DST-safe `toWall`/`fromWall`, so `clock/zone.ts` only adds the local day/weekday/hour reading and the "was due" text). Defects fixed: (1) the Later fire moved a thread to `status: 'new'` and cleared `later_until`, which contradicts the views lane (Home's "Back from Later" and its count are derived from `status = 'later'` with `later_until <= now`; `clearFromHome` hides it); (2) the fire's opId named `now`, so two engines firing at different instants both notified; (3) one batch commit for the whole due set, so one vanished thread decided the rest, and the opId depended on the set; (4) the presets used 9:00, other keys and other weekend rules than the picker (`renderer/screens/later/laterPresets.ts`) and ignored `calendar.weekStart`; (5) events went through an injected `emit` of a private shape instead of the contract's `reminder` host event with a `NotificationItem`; (6) nothing re-read the sources when a deadline was set while the loop slept.
- **Both sources live in the clock module** (`clock/module.ts`, lane `eng-time`, first in the root's order): it builds the loop, registers `reminders` and `later`, and provides `services.reminders` and `services.later`. The zone is read lazily from `services.views.zone` (the views lane's zone, the host's by default), falling back to the host zone: there is no time-zone setting in the contract yet.
- **Reminders.** `next()` is the `timers_wf` minimum; `fire(now)` marks each due reminder with `reminder_fired_at = now` in its own `system` commit whose opId names thread, `reminder_at` and `edited_at`, then sends the host event only if the commit was not a replay and changed something. A crash between commit and event loses that one event (the reminder stays in `Reminder/list` until acked), never repeats it. `Reminder/ack` writes `reminderAt: null` for fired reminders only (an upcoming reminder in the list is left alone) and keeps `reminder_fired_at`; `Thread/act`'s `clear` writes both null. Neither touches `edited_at`: Counts reads it as "handled today". `ifNoReply` is always false in the list: there is no column.
- **A late fire says so.** `wasDueAt` and the body "Was due 9:00 AM" (or "Was due Mon, Oct 26, 9:00 AM" on another local day, in the owner's zone) are set when the fire is 2 minutes or more after the deadline (`LATE_AFTER_MS`); on time, neither. The host event is `{type: 'reminder', item}` with `item.kind` `reminder` or `later` (the contract has no `later` event type).
- **Later never changes the thread.** The `later` source announces each return once and moves a watermark (`meta` `later.fired_through`) past it; one empty `system` commit per thread (opId `later:returned:<thread>:<date>`) is the dedupe ledger, so a rewound watermark or a second engine stays silent. A watermark ahead of the clock is ignored. The first run starts the watermark at launch (old dates stay quiet); after that a Later that fell due while the app was closed is announced at the first pass with its was-due time. At most 20 returns are announced per fire (Home shows all of them).
- **Presets mirror the picker**: Today evening 18:00 (only before 17:00), Tomorrow 8:00, This weekend Saturday 8:00 (Monday to Thursday), Next week the first weekday 8:00 (never today; `calendar.weekStart` from the settings document, Monday when unset; the macOS-locale default is the host's job), Someday. Keys are the picker's ids (`evening`, `tomorrow`, `weekend`, `nextWeek`, `someday`). Wall times go through `fromWall`: a repeated hour is the first, a skipped one lands after the gap.
- **Both sources re-read every minute (`RECHECK_MS`).** `Thread/act` commits a reminder or a Later and nothing calls `deadlines.changed('reminders' | 'later')`, so without this a reminder set while the loop sleeps would wait for the next wake. It costs one cheap fire a minute per source while the engine runs, and it should go as soon as the views lane (or the runtime's `observeCommits`) calls `changed` after a commit that touches `reminder_at`, `later_until` or `status`.

### eng-time time-4: fixes after review (2026-10-29)

- **Idle Later fires write nothing.** The `later` source used to commit `later:through:<now>` on every fire (about 1,440 permanent `commits` rows a day, since opIds are never pruned). Now the fire keeps the watermark in memory and commits it only when it announced something, or when the stored one is missing, ahead of the clock or more than a day old. A restart re-reads at most a day of dates, and the per-thread opIds keep any of them from being announced twice. Test: 1,000 idle fires add no commit rows.
- **`Reminder/list` is two bounded reads.** Upcoming (the partial index `timers_wf`, no SCAN, `LIMIT 200`) and fired-not-acked (newest first, `LIMIT 200`). No index covers fired reminders, so that read still scans `thread_workflow`; its LIMIT bounds the result, not the read. `Reminder/ack` matches by id, so it reads all fired reminders without a LIMIT. **REQUEST to eng-store:** an index `ON thread_workflow(reminder_fired_at) WHERE reminder_fired_at IS NOT NULL AND reminder_at IS NOT NULL` (or `reminder_at WHERE reminder_at IS NOT NULL`) so the fired read and `ack` stop scanning at 100k threads.
- **A reminder fire is capped at 50.** `next()` is still the earliest unfired `reminder_at`, which stays at or before `now` while due rows remain, so the loop fires again after its 1 s backoff (one `timer_drift` note per extra pass). 200 overdue reminders take four passes and each fires once.
- **`Reminder/list` titles are clamped** with the same helper as the notice (one line, 500 characters; the contract allows 2,000).
- **A reminder due at launch cannot be lost.** The loop starts inside `createEngine` (the module's `start`), before the host can `connect()` and subscribe, so a reminder that is due at launch is marked fired and its `reminder` host event can reach nobody. The fired mark is kept (it is what stops a repeat) and `Reminder/list` returns fired, unacked reminders (`firedAt` set, `at` is the was-due time), so the host reads them at connect. Hosts must call `Reminder/list` after subscribing. Test: a reminder due before any connection is listed with `at` and `firedAt`.
- **Re-setting the same time notifies again.** The fire's opId names thread, `reminder_at` and `edited_at`; `Thread/act` stamps `edited_at`, so the same `at` set again after a fire is a new notification. Test: removing `editedAt` from the opId fails it.
### eng-time time-4: follow-ups after PR #113 (2026-10-30)

- **`Thread/act` and `Operation/undo` call `deadlines.changed('reminders' | 'later')` after the commit.** `views/ops.ts` `deadlinesChanged(deps, writes)` looks at the workflow patches of the commit and calls it for `reminders` when `reminderAt` or `reminderFiredAt` is in a patch, for `later` when `laterUntil` or `status` is; `commitBuilt` (so `Thread/act` and the sender rules) and `undoOperation` call it after the commit landed (not on a replay), guarded by `isAbsent(deadlines)`, with the registry passed in from `views/module.ts` like the window engine's. A reminder or Later set while the loop sleeps now fires at its time. The sources keep their 60 s re-read (`RECHECK_MS`) as the fallback for a write that bypasses the views lane; it was not lowered or removed. Test: `tests/views/deadlines.test.ts`.
- **The host announces what it missed.** `apps/desktop/src/host/reminders.ts` `announceMissedReminders(session, notify)` subscribes first, then calls `Reminder/list` once and notifies each fired, unacked reminder not yet announced, and forwards live `reminder` host events to `notify`; one set of notification ids (`reminder:<threadId>:<at>`, the engine's own) keeps a reminder from notifying twice. A failing list call is swallowed. Not wired into `src/main` (it still runs the legacy engine): the new engine's host calls it once after `engine.connect()`.

## eng-time time-2: send follow-ups after the Integrator's notice on PR #105 (2026-10-29)

- **REQUEST to Providers (binding before drafts sync / the Gmail draft send path is switched on): run the `findSent` contract.** Reusing `remote_internet_id` as the submission's Message-ID means the unsent Gmail draft copy carries the same id as the message that leaves. The port says `findSent` searches Sent only, but both legacy adapters search everything (google.ts has no `in:sent`, microsoft.ts falls back to `/me/messages`); an adapter built the same way finds the UNSENT draft after a `drafts.send` timeout and the outbox marks the row `sent` when nothing left. `sendPortContract(name, makeHarness)` in `packages/engine/tests/ports/send-port-contract.ts` (node:test; header explains the harness) asserts: a message in Sent is found; a message that exists only as a draft with the same id is NOT; another id does not match; `since` is honoured. The Gmail adapter, the Microsoft adapter and the Gmail emulator-backed fake server must each run it (Gmail: `in:sent` / label SENT, never DRAFT; Graph: the sentitems folder, never `/me/messages`). The outbox's counting fake models Drafts and Sent separately, its `findSent` ignores drafts, and it passes the contract (`tests/outbox/send-port-fake.test.ts`).
- **Message-IDs are per revision.** `prepareSend` never reuses `remote_internet_id` for a `microsoft` account (Graph keeps a draft's Message-ID across edits and `payloadOf` never sends a Microsoft remote draft or `baseRemoteMessageId`, so revision R+1 could share R's id after R went uncertain, was cancelled or actually left), and for any provider it skips a synced id already used by a submission of another revision of the draft (a fresh id is minted). **When draft sync (time-3) is written it must keep Message-IDs per revision**: each revision's upload gets its own Message-ID, never one per draft. `assertIdsPerRevision` (`tests/outbox/helpers.ts`) is the assertion to call from the draft-sync tests.
- **A row that has had an attempt is never re-dated.** Reschedule and Send now rewrite the `Date` header only on a row whose `sending` marker was never committed (`submission.attempted`, plus `attempt_at` as a fallback). After Send anyway or Retry the row is queued again, and a retry of an uncertain send must go out as byte-identical MIME under the same id. A send that was provably not sent and only paused (`PausedError`) clears the marker, so its Date may still be refreshed.
- **`auth` not-sent copy** no longer says "check your connection": "Aplos needs you to sign in to this account again. Reconnect it in Settings." The reason code stays `auth.expired` and the row stays retryable (Retry works once the account is reconnected).
- eng-store (2026-10-30): migration 0004 added `reminders_wf` on `thread_workflow(reminder_at)` for every reminder. The reminders source now names its indexes: due, next and the upcoming list use `timers_wf` (unfired only, `INDEXED BY`), the fired-unacked list and ack use `reminders_wf` (newest reminder first, no table scan, no sort); this closes the REQUEST recorded for time-4. The plan tests assert each index and no `SCAN` or `TEMP B-TREE`.
- eng-time drafts (time-3, 2026-10-30): **The local draft is the truth, and the sync state rides in `drafts.data`.** The store has no columns for the fingerprint, the next upload time, the in-flight Message-ID, the problem or the conflict, so `drafts.data` is the fields plus one `_sync` object (`outbox/draft-state.ts` is its only reader and writer; `prepareSend` ignores the key). A later migration can move them to columns. The eng-time extension (`outbox/extension.ts`) now also owns the `draft`, `draftDelete` and `remoteDraft` write kinds; the submission writer moved to `applySubmission` and `outboxExtension` is exported from `outbox/extension.ts` (and `module.ts`) so the two writers share one value without an import cycle.
- **Saves are compare-and-set inside the commit.** `Draft/set` with `baseRevision` throws `DraftConflict` from the extension when `revision` moved, so of two saves on one base (two windows, two engines on one store) exactly one commit lands and the other is `stateMismatch` "This draft changed in another window. Reopen it before editing." Saving the same content again is not a new revision. A draft with a live submission refuses `Draft/set` and `Draft/resolve` (`stateMismatch`; `conflict` while `sending`), and `Draft/destroy` cancels a send that has not started (`undo` for queued and scheduled, `cancel` for failed and suspended) but refuses `sending` and `uncertain` (`conflict`). Creates take a deterministic id from the opId, so a replayed create answers with the draft it made.
- **Sync schedule.** A save sets `dueAt` to 5 s later (the idle wait; every change moves it) or to now on close. `dueAt` is stored, so a restart resumes a pending upload once (the instance that starts finds it past due). `drafts` is a deadline source: it fires uploads detached, one per draft at a time, and skips a draft with a live send (a send pins its revision; the upload waits for the send to end, and `outbox` changes wake `drafts`). An empty new draft is never uploaded. The fingerprint (sha-256 of the sorted-key JSON of the fields) is compared with the one the remote copy holds, so an edit put back before the upload, or a close, uploads nothing.
- **Message-IDs per revision, kept across retries.** Each revision's upload gets its own Message-ID (`uploading: {internetId, revision}` is committed before the call); a retry of the same revision (paused, lost reply, restart mid-upload) reuses it so an uncertain create is recognised, a new revision mints a new one. A skipped upload (same fingerprint) does not move `synced_revision`, so a later send of that revision uses its own fresh id and the pinned MIME rather than a copy under an older revision's id; `assertIdsPerRevision` holds through sync and send (tests).
- **Refusals never drop the draft.** `PausedError` moves `dueAt` to `until` (or 30 s); a lost reply or any unclassified error retries after 30 s; `permanent`, `permission`, `config` and `anti_abuse` leave the draft local with `problem` (`Draft.sync = 'failed'`, reason `draft.sync`) and logs `draft.sync_failed`; the next edit or a close asks again; a wake brings every wait forward. The draft copy is built leniently: half-typed addresses are left out of the copy, attachments whose bytes are gone are skipped, and Bcc stays out of the draft copy (see the third review round below: a provider that sends the copy itself would show the header to every recipient).
- **Remote edits: replace only what nobody changed, ask otherwise.** A `remoteDraft` fact whose message id or Message-ID this device wrote (`wrote`, the in-flight `uploading` id, `remote_message_id`) is our own echo and changes nothing, even when history reports it before the save returned (no phantom Drafts row). A draft the provider has and we do not appears with `origin: 'remote'` (id `account:remoteDraftId`, `sync: synced`), never as a message or thread row; editing it makes it ours, uploading to the same remote draft. An edit of a local draft that nothing changed here since the last sync replaces it only when the fact carries the body; the fact is metadata only today, so it raises the conflict state instead. An edit while there are unsynced local changes, an upload answered `conflict`, or a copy deleted or sent elsewhere while edited here (`gone`) raise the conflict state: uploads stop, the local text is untouched, `Draft.conflict` is set and `Draft/changed {conflict: true}` goes out. `Draft/resolve`: keep mine uploads over their copy (against the message id that conflicted; for `gone` it creates a new remote draft), take theirs replaces the fields (for `gone` it discards the draft), keep both keeps mine and makes their version a second local draft (`copyId`). Take theirs and keep both need their version; when only an upload conflict has been seen they answer `unavailable` "The other version has not arrived yet." until history delivers it. A copy deleted elsewhere is deleted here when nothing was changed here. `Draft/changed` is announced from a microtask after the commit that carries the `_sync.announce` marker (so a rolled-back commit announces nothing), once per marker.
- **After `sent` or a discard the draft and its copy go.** The `drafts` source deletes a draft whose current revision has a `sent` submission (no live one) and queues the removal of its remote copy; `Draft/destroy` does the same. The removal queue is in memory (3 refusals, then logged and dropped): a crash between the local delete and the provider delete leaves the copy, which then returns as another device's draft. Removal needs `DraftSyncPort.remove` to treat an absent draft as done (`drafts.send` already consumed it); the module without a `DraftSyncPort` (`createOutboxModule` in the older tests) keeps the old behaviour of leaving a sent draft in place.
- **Gaps for other lanes, not done here.** Contract: `Draft/set` has no close flag, so close is `OutboxModule.closeDraft(id)` / the service's `close: true` until the contract adds one (suggest `closing?: boolean`); `Draft.conflict` carries no `gone` marker. Store/views: no column for the sync state (see above) and no tombstone for a discarded draft whose remote copy is not yet deleted. Ports/providers: `RemoteDraftWrite.message` has no body, so a remote draft shows its snippet and take theirs from a metadata-only fact copies the snippet (the extension reads an optional `html` on the message and replaces silently only when it is there); providers should deliver the body (or add `DraftSyncPort.load`). `DraftSaveResult.conflict` should name the conflicting copy's content or the provider should report it promptly through `remoteDraft`.
- eng-time drafts review fixes (time-3, 2026-10-30). **Resolving a conflict leaves the row honest.** Keep mine clears `synced_revision`, `remote_internet_id` and `remote_change_key` (the copy is theirs until our upload lands), so a send right after it leaves the pinned MIME under a fresh Message-ID, never their copy via `drafts.send`. The conflict now stores `theirsInternetId` and `complete`; take theirs sets `remote_internet_id` from `theirsInternetId` (or clears it together with `synced_revision` when history did not report one), and the same holds for the silent replace and the refresh of a phone draft. An incomplete other version (snippet only) is refused for take theirs and keep both with `unavailable` ("Their version can't be read here. Keep yours, or open it on the other device."); only keep mine works.
- **A discarded or sent draft keeps a hidden row until its copy is confirmed gone.** `draftDelete` marks `_sync.removing` (plus `removeAt`, `removeTries`) instead of deleting whenever a remote copy exists or a first upload has no result yet; every reader (`Draft/get`, `save`, `resolve`, `prepareSend`, the frames) treats the row as missing, `remoteDraft` facts for it are ignored (a `removed` fact ends the wait), and `draftDelete {final}` deletes it once `DraftSyncPort.remove` succeeds (an absent draft counts as success). The `drafts` source retries with a backoff (30 s doubling to 1 h; a permanent refusal logs and asks again after 24 h) and never abandons the removal; it survives a restart. A late ack of an upload for a discarded draft records the copy's ids on the hidden row, and a copy that history reports under the upload's Message-ID is adopted the same way; a row with no copy and no result waits 10 min, then goes. The in-memory queue (3 refusals) stays only for a copy whose row is gone entirely. **REQUEST to eng-views (`views/lists.ts` `draftsPlan`, `views/counts.ts`):** add `json_extract(d.data, '$._sync.removing') IS NULL` to the Drafts list predicate so a hidden row is not listed or counted while its removal is pending (offline, that can be long); until then the id is listed and `Draft/get` answers `notFound` for it.
- **Schedule fixes.** `synced` keeps `dueAt` null while a conflict is set and `syncDraft` clears a stale `dueAt` on a conflicted row (it used to re-fire the source at 1 Hz); `Draft/resolve` sets it again. An upload whose reply may hide a write (a timeout or a 5xx, not a refusal) for a revision that has since changed clears the stored fingerprint and the `uploading` mark and makes the current revision due (the `retry` write, unguarded by revision), so a draft the owner reverted is never shown as synced while the copy holds the newer content; the re-upload then meets the provider's changed-copy answer and the owner chooses. A refusal that wrote nothing for an old revision needs nothing.
- **The deadline scan is cached (`outbox/draft-due.ts`).** The due times live inside each row's JSON, so every save used to scan the whole table (twice). The source now keeps the due uploads, the removals and the sent drafts in memory: a full scan fills them, a write this engine makes (or a provider fact, via the announce hook) re-reads only that row, a send state change re-reads the sent drafts, and a full scan repeats when the source is asked and the last one is 60 s old (another process's writes are found then; `fire` always scans fully). Test: with 2,000 drafts, 20 saves run no multi-row query on `drafts`. REQUEST to eng-store, not blocking: a `due_at` column (or partial index) on `drafts` would remove the scan and the 60 s fallback.
- **For Providers.** `cleanUpSent` must not delete a remote draft that was sent as the Email itself (JMAP `EmailSubmission` with `onSuccessDestroyEmail` consumes it): `DraftSyncPort.remove` must treat an absent draft as success. Facts for a draft's copy should carry the body and the Message-ID: without the body a conflict cannot be resolved in favour of theirs, and without the id a taken or refreshed copy has no id of our own to send under. A remote-origin draft's snippet-only first sight still shows the snippet as its body (editing it uploads that); the provider delivering the body closes it.
- **Not done (recorded only).** The module-global announce listeners (`onDraftAnnounced`) are shared by every module in a process; each listener re-reads the row it is told about, so extra listeners only cost a read, but two engines in one process both announce.
- eng-time drafts second review round (time-3, 2026-10-30). **An ack the row no longer expects is not a sync.** `synced` records the result only when its Message-ID is the row's `uploading` one; a conflict, a choice or a newer attempt clears `uploading`, so an old upload landing late (after keep mine, say) only adds its ids to `wrote` and leaves `synced_revision`, the fingerprint and `dueAt` alone (it used to mark the draft synced and cancel the keep-mine upload). The uncertain `retry` keeps the old attempt's Message-ID in `wrote`, and `ownUpload` matches `wrote` as well as `uploading`; history reporting that copy is now adopted by a live draft that has none (it used to become a phantom remote draft, and the next upload made a second copy). The upload job's `touchDue` calls never throw (a store closed under a late upload no longer hides the original error or becomes an unhandled rejection; no registered log event exists for it).
- **A consumed remote draft is not deleted.** `SendReceipt.draftConsumed` is kept on the submission (`draftConsumed` in its JSON) and the hidden row of that draft is finalised without calling `remove`. Only the receipt counts: after an uncertain send that `findSent` found, whether the draft was used is unknown and the removal still runs. The Drafts list and the drafts count leave out hidden `_sync.removing` rows (views).
- **For Providers (JMAP exposure).** On JMAP the sent message can be the same `Email` as the draft (id kept, `$draft` removed). `deleteDraft` is now documented to do nothing when the target is no longer a draft (no `$draft` keyword, not in Drafts); the outbox skips it after a consumed send, but a removal that runs for another reason (a later retry, an uncertain send) would delete the sent message unless the adapter checks.
- eng-time drafts third review round (time-3, 2026-10-30). **A conflicted draft is not sent, and a draft whose text never arrived is neither edited nor sent.** `prepareSend` and the `create` write (inside the commit) refuse `stateMismatch` while `_sync.conflict` is set (a send would clean up by deleting the copy that holds the other device's version) and `unavailable` while `_sync.incomplete` is set; `create` also treats a hidden (`removing`) draft as missing. `incomplete` is set when a draft from another device is stored from metadata only (`insertRemote`, `refreshRemote`: the fields hold the snippet); `Draft/set` refuses it with `unavailable` until a version with its body is reported (`refreshRemote` takes the same message again when it now brings the body). **A copy that is gone is forgotten before it is hidden.** A `gone` conflict (from history or from an upload result) clears `remote_draft_id` and the other remote columns, so discarding the draft never asks the provider to delete that id (on JMAP and Graph with immutable ids it can now be the sent message); the `saveDraft` doc says a target that is no longer a draft is `gone`, never replaced. **A late upload result cannot undo a choice.** The upload's `conflict`/`gone` answer is the `conflict` draft write: it applies only when the row's `uploading.internetId` is that attempt's (as `synced` does), a refused-for-good answer likewise (`ifUploading`), and a conflict history already described is completed by the same copy again instead of replaced or skipped. **Keep mine after a conflict that named no copy** clears `remote_message_id` (no stale base, so the upload does not conflict again). **A copy that omits anything is not this revision.** An upload that left out an attachment it could not read (`partial`) keeps the remote ids but clears the fingerprint and `synced_revision`; taking or refreshing from their version keeps my attachments and sign-off in the fields, so `synced_revision` stays unset then too (a Gmail `drafts.send` of such a copy would send without them). **Bcc stays out of draft copies** (reverses the time-3 first-round choice): a provider that sends the copy itself would show the Bcc header to every recipient. A draft with Bcc recipients gets a Message-ID of its own at send (`messageIdOf` does not reuse the copy's), so it leaves as the pinned MIME with Bcc in the envelope; the fingerprint still covers Bcc, so a Bcc-only change is still saved and uploaded. An unanswered create followed by an edit keeps the first attempt's Message-ID in `wrote` (history reporting that copy is not a phantom draft); the orphan copy it made on the server is not deleted yet. `Draft/destroy` requires the `send` scope when the draft has a live send it would cancel (scopeRule `originating`); a discard with no send needs only `draft`.
- eng-time drafts fourth review round (time-3, 2026-10-30; the Integrator's Opus re-check N1 to N4). **A copy that may hold the other device's version, or that is a sent message, is never deleted.** N2: a `removed` fact is always news, even for the copy a conflict already names: the "same version again is known" early return no longer applies to it, so a phone that sends a draft it had edited forgets the id (`gone`) and discarding asks the provider for nothing. N3: a fact for the copy that arrives while a send is live or sent (not an echo, not a removal) cannot be asked about, so it sets `_sync.changedElsewhere`; when a draft whose current revision was sent is cleaned up, a copy with an open conflict or that marker is forgotten (remote ids cleared, row deleted) instead of deleted; an `ack` of our own upload clears the marker, as do keep mine and take theirs. That covers a send created while an upload is in flight whose upload answers `conflict`, and a phone edit during the undo or send-later hold. A plain discard by the owner still deletes the copy. N4: an upload result `gone` for a draft that already holds a conflict marks that conflict `gone` too (keep mine then has no base). **N1: a draft whose text never arrived (the provider's metadata only; nothing delivers the body yet) is honest about it.** `Draft/set` with a `baseRevision` of such a draft saves the edit as a NEW local draft (`dr_...` id from the opId, origin `local`, no remote ids) and leaves the other device's draft as it is; the answer is the new draft, so **the client must switch to the returned id** (the same opId replays to the same new draft; a stale base is still `stateMismatch`). Sending it is refused `unavailable`: "This draft was written on another device and its text isn't available here yet. Open it there to send it, or write a new one." `Draft/destroy` of such a draft deletes only the local row (no hidden row) and leaves the provider's copy alone, because the owner has not seen its text and there is no confirm parameter; the draft can come back from history when the other device changes it again. **Contract gap (REQUEST to eng-contract, not blocking):** `Draft` has no `incomplete` field and I did not edit the contract; until it has one, `toDraft` reports such a draft as `sync: 'failed'` (never `synced`). When the body is delivered (`refreshRemote` with `html`) the draft is `synced` again.
- eng-time drafts N3 follow-up (time-3, 2026-10-30). **`_sync.changedElsewhere` alone forgets the copy, sent or not.** A phone edit during the undo or send-later hold sets the marker without a conflict; if the owner then discards the draft (which cancels the send), `Draft/destroy` forgets the remote ids and drops the row instead of queueing a remove, so the phone's only complete version survives. No conflict is raised on that cancel: the owner is discarding the draft, so there is nothing to resolve, and the other device's copy is simply left alone. A removal reported for the copy while a send is live or sent (the phone sent it, so the id may name its sent message) also sets the marker (it was dropped silently), so our send's cleanup does not call `remove` for it either.

## 2026-10-30 — eng-contract: `Draft.incomplete`, the answer to eng-time's request

- the contract `Draft` gains an optional `incomplete: boolean` (additive within v:1): a draft written on another device whose text this device cannot read yet. Sending it is refused, and `Draft/set` saves the edit as a NEW local draft whose reply carries the new id, leaving the other device's draft untouched; `Draft/destroy` forgets only the local row. Until #119 lands and eng-time's follow-up wires the field into `toDraft`, such drafts keep reporting `sync: 'failed'`; the client should treat `incomplete === true` as the honest signal once present. The stand-in engine needs no change (the field is optional).

## 2026-10-30 — eng-time: `toDraft` reports `Draft.incomplete`

- with the contract's optional `Draft.incomplete` (#120), `toDraft` reports `incomplete: true` for a draft whose text another device wrote and this one never received, and its `sync` is no longer forced to `failed` (it matches the provider: `synced`). This closes the REQUEST to eng-contract recorded with the #119 work. A replay of the first incomplete-edit save (same opId) already returns the same forked draft: the fork id derives from the opId, covered by the N1 fork test.

## prov-gmail-write: the Gmail send transport (2026-10-30)

- **Bcc is the one header the adapter adds.** The outbox never writes Bcc into a sent message (the envelope carries it), but Gmail's API has no envelope: it reads recipients from To, Cc and Bcc and removes Bcc from delivered copies. `send.ts` therefore inserts one folded `Bcc:` line, just before the blank line, for envelope recipients the headers do not name; every other byte is the pinned MIME. A To or Cc address the envelope does not hold is refused, never sent.
- **Header safety is an allow-list, not a fix-up.** The top header block may hold only what the outbox writer emits (Date, From, To, Cc, Subject, Message-ID, In-Reply-To, References, MIME-Version, Content-Type, Content-Transfer-Encoding), each at most once, CRLF only, no control bytes; From must be exactly the account; Message-ID must equal `internetId` (or `findSent` could never settle it). Anything else is `NotSentError` (`permanent`) with no request. A header the engine starts writing must be added to `PINNED_HEADERS` on purpose.
- **`drafts.send` carries the pinned bytes as its media** (upload endpoint, as Google's drafts guide allows), so a phone edit landing between the `drafts.get` precondition and the send cannot be what leaves. Needs confirming on the live gate.
- **A remote draft that is gone (404) is never "failed".** Before sending the pinned MIME instead, the adapter searches Sent for the Message-ID; a hit resolves the send with that copy's ids (an earlier attempt or another device already sent it), so no second copy goes. A `drafts.send` 404 after a successful read is uncertain (the draft may have just been sent elsewhere), settled by `findSent`.
- **Proof rules.** Not sent: anything before the sending request is written (draft read, resumable session open, even a dropped connection there), a refused connection, and only a 400, 401, 403, 413, 429 or a `messages.send` 404 answer. Uncertain: everything after the request was written (5xx, reset, timeout, abort; 408, 409, 499, a `drafts.send` 404 and any other status). A 2xx is accepted mail only when its body is Gmail's Message with a string `id`; any other 2xx is uncertain and `findSent` settles it. A send times out after 60 s plus 1 s per 50 KB so it cannot hold the account's one slot forever.
- **`findSent`** queries `rfc822msgid:<id> in:sent -in:drafts after:<since minus three days>` with spam and trash included, then re-reads each hit's labels and Message-ID: found only for SENT without DRAFT. A failed search, a hit that vanished before it was read, or a second page is thrown, never `absent`.
- **Review follow-up (supersedes the bullets above where they differ).** `drafts.send` is OFF by default (`GoogleProviderOptions.useDraftsSend`): every send is the pinned MIME through `messages.send` and the engine removes the remote draft itself. The route stays behind the flag with its tests; TODO(live) in `send.ts` names what the live gate must prove before it is switched on (uploaded bytes honoured by `drafts.send`, the client Message-ID kept, Bcc stripped from delivered copies). With the flag on, a draft that reads 404 with no Sent copy is uncertain (nothing sent; `findSent` decides), never a pinned send that could be a second copy. A 2xx without a string Message `id` (a proxy or captive portal page) is uncertain, not sent. Only 400, 401, 403, 413, 429 and a `messages.send` 404 prove not sent; 408, 409, 499 and any other status are uncertain. `findSent` bounds `after:` three days before `since` and no longer filters on internalDate (a sent draft keeps its date): the Message-ID, SENT and no DRAFT decide. (Follow-up 2026-10-30, corrected: the SENT-listing fallback added after this tests every listed candidate for the copy (SENT, no DRAFT, this Message-ID) and uses the date only to see where the window ends, so a search miss, a lagging index included, is covered by the fallback within its page cap, a sent draft that keeps its old date too (within the search's own 3-day `after:` bound). Only a miss beyond the cap, 30 newer sends with no older candidate, throws `sentListingPartial`. See the send-guard follow-ups below for when it answers absent.)
- **Send-guard follow-ups (2026-10-30, the Integrator's review of #129).** (1) Every upload request (multipart POST, resumable session open and PUT) is sent with `redirect: 'manual'`, so a 307/308 can never re-send the pinned bytes to wherever it points; a 3xx on the sending request is uncertain like any unlisted status, and on the session open it proves nothing left. Tested over real sockets with Node's `fetch`: the redirect target receives nothing. (2) The `sync.gmail.request_refused` line for `messages.send` and `drafts.send` leaves out `providerMessage` (Gmail's text can quote a header it rejected, such as an address); class, reason and status stay. The thrown error keeps it for diagnostics, where the Logger's redactor applies. (3) **`findSent` falls back to the newest SENT messages by label** when the `rfc822msgid:` search finds nothing (complete answer, nothing vanished, no second page): one `messages.list` with `labelIds=SENT`, `maxResults=10` (5 units) with `nextPageToken` requested, then a metadata get (`internalDate` included) of every id listed. The same check as a search hit decides (SENT, never DRAFT, this exact Message-ID). The fallback can only turn `absent` into `found`: a failed listing, a failed get or a candidate that vanished is thrown, never `absent`. **It answers `absent` only when the listing is provably complete for the window** (the Integrator's Opus findings on #139, a duplicate-send path): some candidate is older than `since` minus 10 minutes, or a page is short (under 10 ids) with no `nextPageToken`. While no older candidate has shown and a `nextPageToken` exists, up to two more pages are read (at most 3 pages, 30 gets). A listing that is still full or continues after that cap may hide the copy behind 30 or more newer sends, so it is thrown as `incomplete('sentListingPartial')` (transient, retryable, never `absent`). **Correction to an earlier sentence:** the owner IS still offered Send anyway on it: the engine's `findSent` wrapper (`outbox/start.ts`) catches every throw and treats it as found-nothing, so after `MAX_CHECKS` = 2 a thrown `sentListingPartial` shows the same "Check Sent, or Send anyway" prompt as `absent`. The throw only keeps the adapter honest (never `absent`); an engine follow-up could stop offering Send anyway on an unknown answer. Every listed candidate is read, and an older one no longer ends the read, so a copy listed after an out-of-order older candidate is still found. **The copy test runs on every candidate first; the date only decides whether the window was reached** (the Integrator's finding: testing the date first threw away a matching copy dated before `since` minus 10 minutes, which needs a device clock about 15 minutes ahead of Gmail's, or a sent draft that keeps its old date, and answered `absent` where the search path would have found it). The one date limit on a match is the search's own `after:` bound (`AFTER_SLACK_MS`, 3 days before `since`), so both paths agree and the shared `sendPortContract` case "a copy 7 days before `since` is not returned" stays green (the contract's fake and the Gmail emulator list it in the label listing; only this bound keeps it `absent`). This supersedes the earlier "a candidate older than the window is never a match". Remaining assumption, unverified until the live gate: the listing is newest-first in practice. Unknown until the live gate: whether the label listing really shows a send sooner than the search. The `drafts.send` gone-draft check (`chooseRoute`) still uses the search alone. (4) The send rig's governor now carries the `oauth` budget, as the engine's defaults do, so a 401's forced refresh and single re-send run for real (tested: one refresh, two requests, one copy; a second 401 is `NotSentError` `auth`).

## 2026-10-31: release builds minify syntax only, so test-only code leaves no trace (plat-release, answering eng-time's REQUEST on PR #124)

- **Question:** the outbox kill-point harness (time-6) compiles its call sites out of release builds with `__APLOS_TEST_MODE__` false, but esbuild keeps the dead text (`if (false) killpoint(KP.X)`) unless it minifies, so a release bundle can still contain the *call sites*. Must release builds be minified so there is literally no trace?
- **Decision: yes for syntax, no for names.** `APLOS_RELEASE_BUILD=1` builds of `electron.cjs`, `engine.cjs` and `preload.cjs` pass esbuild `minifySyntax: true` (new `apps/desktop/scripts/build-options.mjs`, used by `build-main.mjs`). That folds `if (false)` branches away and drops every declaration only they used. It renames nothing and keeps whitespace, so crash stack traces and a reader of `dist/main` stay as legible as before. Full minification is not used: it saves size we do not need and costs legibility.
- **Evidence:** on the kill-point branch's two bundle entries with the release define, the unminified engine entry still contained `killpoint` and `killableSend` text; with `minifySyntax` neither bundle contains any of `killpoint`, `killableSend`, `installKillHook`, the six point names or the harness env name, and the bundles are about 4% smaller. A real `APLOS_RELEASE_BUILD=1 node scripts/build-main.mjs` now produces bundles with zero `if (false)` occurrences. `apps/desktop/tests/plat-release/release-syntax.test.ts` pins the options and proves, with a positive control, that a test-only call site disappears from a release bundle and stays in a dev bundle.
