- **For eng-time:** the "dead text stays unminified" caveats in `killpoints.ts` and `release-grep.test.ts` describe the dev/unminified case; the real release build is now syntax-minified, so the grep may require NONE of the canaries to appear in a release bundle built with `minifySyntax: true` (import `syntaxOptions` from `apps/desktop/scripts/build-options.mjs`). The `outbox/killpoints.ts` design (inline define test at each call site) stays correct and is what makes the folding work.

## eng-time time-6: the kill-point harness and matrix (2026-10-30)

- **Kill points leave the release bundle by construction, minified or not.** `outbox/killpoints.ts` names six points (`KP`; the four of the plan plus `during-find-sent` and `during-commit`). The release build (`apps/desktop/scripts/build-main.mjs`) defines `__APLOS_TEST_MODE__` false and does NOT minify, so the first design, a `const KILLPOINTS` exported from `killpoints.ts` and tested at each call site, left the whole kill-point module and its strings in the bundle (esbuild folds a cross-module const only when minifying). What works unminified: each call site tests the define inline (`typeof __APLOS_TEST_MODE__ === 'undefined' || __APLOS_TEST_MODE__`), which esbuild turns into `if (false) killpoint(KP.X)`; it keeps that dead text but drops every declaration only dead text uses (`killpoint`, `killableSend`, the hook, `KP`). The name strings live only in `KP`, and call sites pass `KP.SOME_CONSTANT`, never a string literal (a literal at a dead call site stays in the output); `KILL_POINTS` is `/* @__PURE__ */ Object.values(KP)` so it does not keep `KP` alive. The environment variable that picks the point is read only by the test child (`tests/outbox/killpoint-child.ts`); no file under `src/` knows its name. Without the define (tsx tests, a dev build) the guard is live and, with no hook installed, every point does nothing. `tests/outbox/release-grep.test.ts` bundles `src/index.ts` with the build-main settings (workspace packages bundled, `__APLOS_TEST_MODE__` false), minified and not, greps every point name and the variable name, and has a positive control per bundle (the same entry as a test build holds every name) plus a check that `build-main.mjs` still sets that define. The desktop's `engine-process.ts` still bundles today's engine, so its two cases skip until it bundles the new one (they then assert by themselves). Optional hardening for Platform, not needed: esbuild's `drop: ['labels']` would remove call sites entirely.
- **Where the points sit.** `before-marker` and `after-marker` bracket `commitMarker` in `runner.ts sendOne`; `after-accept` follows a successful `deliver` and precedes the `sent` commit; `during-commit` is inside the commit transaction of any move to `sent` (`store.ts move`), so a kill there leaves an open SQLite transaction that must roll back. `after-bytes` and `during-find-sent` are inside the provider call, which the engine cannot reach, so `module.ts` wraps the `SendPort` in `killableSend` in test builds: `after-bytes` once `send` has finished (the bytes left, or the call failed) and before the engine hears; `during-find-sent` with a request in flight. The wrapper works for any `SendPort`, so adapters carry no kill-point code.
- **The harness** (`tests/outbox/killpoint-harness.ts`, `runKillScenario`) seeds a temp `mail.db`, runs one child process per entry of `killAt` (a real engine, SIGKILL at the point, fake clock), then restarts a last engine in the test process and drives the clock past the 60 s recheck. `assertSendInvariant`: at most one delivery and one `send` call per Message-ID; the row ends `sent`, `failed`, `uncertain` (with the prompt) or `suspended` (with the overdue sentence), never `queued`/`sending`/missing; `sent` only with a delivery, `failed` and `suspended` only without one. The counting fake keeps its record in a file (`killpoint-fake.ts`) so a dead engine's deliveries are counted by the next one. **Plugging in the Gmail emulator (Providers/prov-emu):** `port: { module, config }` where `module` exports `createSendPort(env)`; the emulator is an HTTP server and must live in the test process (a child dies with its server), so its `createSendPort` builds the Gmail send adapter over the emulator's URL and the scenario passes `countDeliveries`. There is no Gmail `SendPort` adapter in the repo yet (`prov-gmail-write`), which is the only missing piece; nothing in the engine needs to change.
- **Matrix result** (`tests/outbox/killpoint.test.ts`; 39 rows at first, 47 after the review round below): kill points x server accepts/drops x `findSent` finds / finds only after the index lags past both checks / throws, plus kills during `findSent` itself (first life and restart), during the restart's own `sent` commit, a quit after the prompt followed by a kill in the launch recheck, and a ten-minute outage before the marker. No row delivers twice or calls `send` twice; nothing ends silently. A delivered message the engine cannot confirm (`lags`, `throws`) ends `uncertain` with the prompt, never `failed`: the owner decides, and only Send anyway can make a second copy. Mutating the start rule (a lost `sending` row re-queued) fails 31 of the 39.
- **time-6 review round (Opus findings, 2026-10-30).** (1) **Lost-mail defect fixed: a wall clock set back between the two `findSent` checks no longer postpones the second.** The launch recheck skipped any `uncertain` row whose `checkAt` was set, and the timer waited for that `checkAt`, so with the clock an hour earlier the owner was not asked for an hour while the message might be lost. `pullInChecks` (`outbox/start.ts`) now pulls a `checkAt` more than `RECHECK_MS` ahead back to `now + 60 s`, keeping `checkCount`; it runs at launch (`recheckUncertain`), on `wake()` and on every fire, and never touches a legitimate due time (always at most 60 s ahead) or one in the past (clock moved forward: the check is due at once, runs once). Matrix rows: quit 40 s in (first check done), relaunch an hour earlier (send dropped; answer lost with the index lagging): `uncertain` with `send.uncertain` after exactly two `findSent`; relaunch a day later: the second check runs at once, once (`uncertain`, or `sent` when the index has caught up). A unit test covers the running process (clock set back, `wake()`). (2) **The suite can no longer hang.** Every life, including the restarted one, runs in a child process; a life has a 15 s wall-clock budget and a step cap, a scenario 20 s, and a timeout kills the children and fails with `did not settle: state=<row states>`; after the first hung scenario the others fail at once (`not run: an earlier scenario hung`). Removing the marker commit now fails the suite in about 20 s instead of spinning. (3) **Two mutations the matrix missed are caught:** `MAX_CHECKS = 1` fails two rows (`indexLagMs: 30000`, so the message shows only at the 60 s recheck) and `CLOCK_SKEW_MARGIN_MS = 0` fails one (`serverClockOffsetMs: -120000`, the server stamps arrival two minutes behind our clock); both verified by mutating the source and rerunning. (4) The invariant also checks `reasonCode === 'send.uncertain'`, and a control row has the provider refuse with a `NotSentError` (`permanent`): `failed` with `send.permanent`, no delivery, never searched. (5) **What the release really ships:** `build-main.mjs` does NOT minify, so the shipped bundle still holds the dead call-site text (`if (false) killpoint(KP.X)`, `false ? killableSend(...)`): no point name, no environment variable, no declaration, nothing executable, but the identifiers `killpoint`/`killableSend` do appear. `release-grep.test.ts` therefore asserts on the unminified (shipped) bundle that every name string is absent and every declaration is gone, and on the minified bundle that the identifiers `killableSend`, `installKillHook` and `killpoint` are absent too (canaries, with a positive control on the test child). **REQUEST to plat-release (not blocking):** if "no trace at all" must be literal, set `minify: true` for the main and engine bundles when `APLOS_RELEASE_BUILD=1` in `apps/desktop/scripts/build-main.mjs`; the test already covers that bundle.
- **time-6 follow-ups (Integrator's notice on #124, 2026-10-30).** (1) **`during-commit` now fires inside the commit of EVERY state move** (`store.ts move`), not only the move to `sent`. The hook gets a second argument, the move as `from>to:why` (`killpoints.ts`; `transition` passes its `why` in the write, which changes nothing else), and a matrix row names the ONE move it kills with `onMove`: `marker`, `sent`, `uncertain` (a `send` of unknown outcome), `startup-uncertain` (the launch's start rule), `check-uncertain` (a `findSent` that found nothing), `failed`, `cancelled`, `queued-after-sendAnyway`. There is no default move, a `during-commit` step without one throws. Whatever the move, the commit rolls back whole and the restart derives the state again: no duplicate, no lost row (a refusal whose `failed` commit died ends `uncertain` with the prompt, because the restart cannot know it was provably not sent). `tests/outbox/killpoint-moves.test.ts` proves in-process that the point fires for each move, so a lost call site fails in a second. (2) **The owner's buttons are part of the harness** (`ActionSpec`): Undo, Cancel and Send anyway, at fake ms into a life, at the prompt (retried until the machine accepts it, as the UI would), or synchronously AT a kill point (the click lands between two steps of the send); every call is journaled before and after, so a death inside it shows as `killed`. What the machine does, asserted by the rows: **Undo** is a compare-and-set on `queued`/`scheduled`; if it commits first the row is `cancelled`, the marker loses, nothing is ever sent (also after a restart); if the process dies inside the Undo commit it rolls back and the owner never got an answer, so the message goes out once on restart (`sent`, 1 delivery, never `cancelled` with a delivery); with the send in flight Undo is refused ("This message is sending now.") and the send finishes. **Cancel** of an `uncertain` row is accepted only after the checks and the prompt, so a `cancelled` row with a delivery is one the owner cancelled after "Check Sent, or Send anyway" (`assertSendInvariant` demands the journal show that); during a check it is refused; killed inside its commit it rolls back and the prompt returns. **Send anyway** is the owner's deliberate second delivery: after it (committed, or died inside it) at most ONE new delivery and ONE new `send` call on top of the one an earlier life may have made, never a lost row; a Send anyway killed in its own commit leaves the prompt and nothing new. The counting fake gained `script` (what the k-th `send` of a Message-ID does: `drop`, `lose`, `ok`). Matrix: 45 rows before, 83 now (38 new: 12 moves, 6 Undo, 4 Cancel, 16 Send anyway), plus a check that every move is exercised. (3) **`release-grep.test.ts`**: the skip now applies ONLY to the engine process, and only when its release bundle holds no outbox text (`UNCERTAIN_COPY`, which no define removes) and its test build no kill-point name; the engine entry (`src/index.ts`) ASSERTS it reaches the outbox and holds every name in the test build, so the test fails if the composition root stops reaching it. Every release bundle is built with the define read from `build-main.mjs` itself and a test asserts each was built `false`; a bundle with NO define keeps every name (the `typeof __APLOS_TEST_MODE__ === 'undefined'` guard defaults ON), so any new bundle of the engine must define it (comments in `killpoints.ts`, `store.ts` and the test). (4) **TODO (Platform / whoever owns `apps/desktop/src/main/engine-process.ts`): the two engine-process release-grep cases ("the desktop app's engine process bundled for release has no kill point once it holds the outbox", minify false and true) turn on when that file bundles the new engine.** They are not a switch to flip: they decide by whether the RELEASE bundle holds outbox text, so the moment the outbox is in it they stop skipping and FAIL unless the names are stripped (`__APLOS_TEST_MODE__` false in `build-main.mjs` for release, which it already is). A unit test (`the verdict fails, not skips, for a bundle that holds the outbox but not the strip`) pins that. Mutations checked: firing `during-commit` only for `to` other than `uncertain`/`cancelled` fails 16 tests (13 matrix rows, 3 in-process); pointing the engine entry at a bundle without the outbox fails the new assertion (the old skip would have passed it); pointing the engine-process case at an unstripped outbox bundle fails instead of skipping.
- prov-gmail-write drafts (2026-10-30; **PORT GAP 3 closed by #153, see "prov-gmail: Gmail drafts adapter on the additive draft ports" below**): **Gmail draft removal is off** (`GoogleProviderOptions.deleteDrafts`, default false; `deleteDraft` then resolves without a request). `DraftSyncPort.remove` and `ProviderAdapter.deleteDraft` carry no expected message id, so a delete would destroy a phone edit made after the last history poll (PORT GAP 3, `drafts.ts` header). Keep it off until the port carries the expected message id and the adapter refuses on a mismatch (an additive change through eng-contract). Provider draft sync as a whole: PORT GAP 2 is closed on the Gmail side (see "prov-gmail: PORT GAP 2" below); a duplicate remote draft from a lagging search is still not cleaned up. A create looks for its Message-ID in drafts (search, then the newest drafts without `q`), then in all mail: a SENT or TRASH hit answers `gone`; a hard-deleted draft cannot be caught.
- prov-gmail-write drafts, Integrator re-check (2026-10-31): **a create-lookup hit is compared with our bytes, never trusted by its Message-ID header.** On a hit in `drafts.list` (search or newest) the adapter reads the draft with `drafts.get` format=raw (60 units) and compares the decoded bytes with `draft.mime` (line endings normalised, otherwise exact): equal is `saved` with the current message id; different is `{kind:'conflict'}` with its message id and nothing written (a phone edit that kept our Message-ID is never adopted as the base of the next replace); a failed raw read, or a draft that vanished or stopped being a draft between the reads, throws transient and is never `saved`. Known limit: the comparison is exact, so a Gmail rewrite of our own bytes shows as a conflict about our own text (safe; "keep mine" recovers it); TODO(live) checks what Gmail keeps. **A 404 on a hit of the all-mail lookup throws `draftHitVanished`** (as lookups (a) and (b) do), so a copy sent or discarded elsewhere whose id changed is not created again. Lookup (c) is a search too and lags like a hard delete (documented in the header). The header's cost note is corrected: the lookup is at least 15 units, about 215 with 10 drafts, at most about 615.
- prov-gmail-write drafts, second Integrator re-check (2026-10-31): **the byte comparison blanks multipart boundaries.** The engine rebuilds the draft MIME on every attempt with fresh random boundaries, so the same revision and Message-ID give different bytes and the first comparison would have reported a conflict on every unedited copy. `google/draft-compare.ts` normalises line endings and replaces each boundary declared in a part's own Content-Type header, and the matching `--<boundary>` / `--<boundary>--` delimiter lines, with a fixed token per nesting depth (text that only looks like a boundary is untouched); everything else is byte for byte. Tests build the retry with the engine's real `buildMime` (new boundaries, plain and nested mixed) and expect `saved`; a changed body or header expects `conflict`. **PORT GAP 4 (closed by #153, see the section below): a conflict on the create path carried no `remoteDraftId`**, and the engine records no Message-ID in `wrote` for it, so the next upload creates a second Gmail draft under a new Message-ID, history later imports the first as another device's draft, and take theirs / keep both fail with noTheirs. Needed: an additive `remoteDraftId?` on the conflict `DraftSaveResult` (through eng-contract) that the engine stores. A test pins today's result shape so the change is visible when the port moves. Provider draft sync stays OFF until then.


## prov-gov: the lease's follow-ups after #127 (2026-10-30)

- **A clock set back never freezes the lease.** Every wait in `sync/lease.ts` is kept with the clock reading it started at (`Wait`: the retry backoff, the probe interval); a reading earlier than that ends it at once, as `deadDue` and the cool-down already did with `publishedAt`. A jump of hours costs one measuring write. Before, a 3-hour jump during a write backoff or the slow 30-minute wait left a lone Mac secondary, with no renewal or probe, for the length of the jump.
- **A 'restart' retires the old lease controller for good.** Its round or owner's claim still in flight finishes against a channel that refuses it, and its reports are ignored (`LeaseEntry.stale`). Before, a claim held across a restart wrote its pin and reported primary on the restarted actor's context while the new controller's gate (the gate as it stood before the owner's lift) still blocked, and nothing reset the role. The claim in flight is rejected; the owner asks again on the new controller, where one fresh ok reading still suffices.
- **Own copies past the digest memory are trashed too.** The Gmail channel keeps the ids of own copies its bounded digests forgot. On a floor readers honour the id is enough; otherwise an own-only compaction downloads at most `OWN_REREADS` (3) of them again per call, through the governor (60 units each), once per copy: covered, it is trashed; not covered (it holds a record the new copy lacks), it stays on the label for good, where a later full compaction may still trash it.

- **Payload after an attempt (eng-time, Integrator BINDING).** `payloadOf` (`runner.ts`) names `remoteDraftId` / `baseRemoteMessageId` only on the FIRST attempt: it is called with the row read before the `sending` marker, so `hadAttempt(row)` (`attempt_at` set or `extra.attempted`) is true only for Send anyway, Retry after failed, or a requeue after an attempt, and then the payload is the pinned MIME + envelope under the same Message-ID, because the first attempt may already have consumed the remote draft (`drafts.send`). A pause before the bytes left (`settlePaused` restores the pre-marker values) stays a first attempt. Tests in `tests/outbox/send-guard.test.ts` (1b).


## eng-html image proxy: Opus security review round (2026-10-30)

The reviewer found no SSRF bypass; the round closed test gaps (each mutation below was run against the source and now fails the suite) and added hardening.

- **Tests that now bite.** (1) TLS verification: `tests/eng-html/tls-fixtures.ts` makes throwaway certificates with the `openssl` command line (a CA, a leaf for `img.example`, a leaf for another name, a self-signed one, a leaf from an untrusted CA) and `tls.setDefaultCACertificates` trusts the test CA for one test. The good certificate is fetched; self-signed, unknown CA, wrong name and an IP literal against a name-only certificate are each refused as `http` with the exact TLS error code and the server sees no request. Adding `rejectUnauthorized: false` fails five tests. The tests skip, with a reason, when `openssl` is missing. (2) The header tests hard-code the expected names and values (`Host`, `User-Agent`, `Accept`, `Accept-Encoding: identity`, `Connection: close`) and compare the raw headers the server received, so an added `Cookie` or `Referer`, or gzip, fails. (3) `maxHeaderSize`: Node's default is 16 KiB, the same as the old constant, so the option did nothing; it is now 8 KiB and a 12 KiB header block is refused (`HPE_HEADER_OVERFLOW`) while a 2 KiB one is not. (4) The classifier already refused 192.52.193.0/24 (AMT) and 192.175.48.0/24 (direct delegation AS112); only the table lacked them. Added with both edges and the mapped forms.
- **Hardening.** (a) At most `MAX_IN_FLIGHT` = 6 fetches in flight across all tokens (`limits.ts`, a semaphore around each hop); the rest wait, and the fetch's own time limit and abort signal cover the wait (an aborted waiter leaves the queue). Together with `maxBytes` that caps buffered bodies at 60 MB. Per token, `PER_TOKEN_BYTES` = 50 MB of body bytes across all fetches and hops; past it a fetch is refused as `budget`. Both can be tightened by tests, never loosened. (b) Names with no dot and the suffixes `.local`, `.internal`, `.lan`, `.home.arpa` (and `home.arpa` itself) are refused as `address` before resolving, since split-horizon DNS can point them inward. (c) `systemResolver` runs at most `MAX_LOOKUPS` = 2 `dns.lookup` calls at once (`createSystemResolver`); a slot frees when the lookup settles, not when its caller gives up, so a slow attacker DNS cannot fill libuv's four-thread pool. A queued lookup whose fetch was aborted leaves the line. (d) An https redirect to http is refused as `redirects` (one condition in `follow`); http to https and http to http are still followed.
- **Follow-ups, not done.** NAT64 with a network-specific prefix needs RFC 7050 discovery (today only `64:ff9b::/96` is treated as NAT64). The GIF frame-size walk (the header's logical screen size is trusted, per-frame descriptors are not read). The suffix list is short on purpose; `.corp`, `.intranet` and `.localdomain` are not refused by name (their addresses are still checked).
- **Reuse contract for the Gmail List-Unsubscribe POST executor.** `requestOptions` (`fetch.ts`) hard-codes `method: 'GET'` and the headers (`REQUEST_HEADERS` plus `Host`, `setHost: false`). An executor that needs POST or other headers must build its own options from the same pieces, not mutate these: keep the pinned `host` (the checked address), `family`, `port`, `lookup` (`pinnedLookup`), and `servername` (the name for SNI, empty for IP literals); re-add `Host` from `target.hostHeader` whenever it replaces `headers` (with `setHost: false` Node adds none); keep `agent: false`, `maxHeaderSize` and the abort `signal`; never set `rejectUnauthorized`, `ca` or `checkServerIdentity`; send no cookies or `Referer`, and follow no redirect without pinning the new URL again from scratch (`pinTarget`) and refusing an https to http downgrade. Take the target from `pinTarget`, run it through the same in-flight semaphore and the system resolver's lookup cap, and cap the response body.
## eng-html image proxy: SSRF review follow-up (2026-10-30)

Supersedes the earlier bullets above where they differ. The classifier and pinning are reused by the Gmail List-Unsubscribe POST executor.

- **Empty labels.** A non-literal host with an empty label after its one legitimate trailing root dot (`a..b.example`, `.example.com`, `printer.local..`, `a.internal.%2e`, `img.example..`) is refused as `address` before resolving (`hasEmptyLabel` in `resolve.ts`). `localName` and `privateName` now strip every trailing dot (`/\.+$/`), so `localhost..`, `intranet..` and `x.home.arpa..` are refused by name too. A single trailing dot (`example.com.`) is still accepted and resolved as written; SNI drops that dot.
- **Fair slots.** `createSemaphore(max).acquire(signal, group)` queues waiters per group (the image token) and hands a freed slot to the waiting group holding the fewest slots (the one waiting longest on a tie), so one mail with twenty slow fetches cannot keep another mail's single image out of the six slots. A fetch's own limit (`timeoutMs`, 10 s) is a work clock that stops while the fetch queues and restarts at the grant; the wait has its own bound (`waitMs`, `WAIT_MS` 30 s in total per fetch across its hops, tightenable like the others, never loosened) after which it is refused as `timeout`. Lookups before the slot (DNS) run on the work clock.
- **The semaphore is exported** from `limits.ts` (`createSemaphore`, with `Release`) for the unsubscribe executor, which must run its request through the same instance as the image fetches or a second one it creates would double the cap. `createImageProxy` owns the instance today; the executor's wiring (how it receives it) is for the executor's PR. A waiter's late abort after it was granted or removed no longer splices index -1 (it used to drop an unrelated waiter).
- **Schemes.** `parseTarget` and `pinTarget` take an optional `schemes` allow-list (`'http' | 'https'`, default both). The https-only executor passes `['https']` and gets `scheme` for `http:` before anything is resolved, instead of carrying its own check.
- **DNS cannot be cancelled and is shared.** A started `dns.lookup` (getaddrinfo on libuv's pool) cannot be cancelled; abandoning its caller only stops waiting, the slot frees when the lookup settles. There are two lookup slots (`MAX_LOOKUPS`) in one shared resolver (`systemResolver`) for the whole process, FIFO and not per token, so slow attacker DNS can still delay other mails' lookups by up to the time it holds both slots. The unsubscribe executor shares this resolver and these two slots (pass `systemResolver` to `pinTarget`, never build a second one, which would double the pool use). Per-token fairness for lookups is not done: it would need the token passed through `Resolver`.
- **TLS SNI test.** The count it asserted (server `tlsClientError` events) could pick up a stray connection to the test's ephemeral port on a busy machine. It now attributes connections by the client's local port (a `mock` over `tls.connect`), waits for both handshakes, and a deliberate stray connection proves it is ignored.

## 2026-10-30 — eng-contract: the UnsubscribePort seam and the outcome names

- **`UnsubscribePort` is a host-injected port, like `images`.** `ports/content.ts` declares `UnsubscribePort` (`describe(headers)`: pure and offline; `run({ from, headers, signal })`: the one-click POST, mailto or link planning in the host with Node networking, honours `signal`, never throws for an ordinary refusal and resolves `failed` or `unsure`; the SSRF rules are the host's) and `UnsubscribeKind` (`done | unsure | failed | open | compose | none`). `EngineServices.unsubscribe` is required, `'unsubscribe'` joins `HostPorts` and the host-port list in `runtime/engine.ts`, and a host that does not inject it gets the standard `unavailable` stand-in. Covered by `tests/runtime.unsubscribe.test.ts`.
- **For prov-gmail-write:** the `declare module '../runtime/services'` augmentation of `unsubscribe?:` in your `methods/unsubscribe.ts` must be removed when you rebase, because `EngineServices` now declares `unsubscribe` as required (a second, optional declaration is a type error). Import `UnsubscribePort` and `UnsubscribeKind` from `../ports` and drop your local `UnsubscribeExecutor`/`UnsubscribeKind` (the shape is identical). The "not available in this version" check on an absent executor goes: an absent port throws the standard `unavailable` itself (use `isAbsent` if you want your own sentence). I did not touch your file.
- **`Unsubscribe/run` names its outcomes** (additive within v:1): `outcome` is still an open enum, now `done | openLink | unavailable | unsure | failed | compose`. `done`: the request was accepted (a one-click 2xx) or the unsubscribe email was queued. `openLink`: show `url` for the owner to review. `unavailable`: the sender offers no usable way. `unsure`: the request was sent but the sender's answer did not confirm it (say so honestly, never claim success). `failed`: the attempt was refused or errored, nothing changed. `compose`: a mailto needs a message the owner must review and send; open the composer with `url`. It stays open so older clients that only know the first three remain valid and treat an unknown value as a message to show. `generated/openapi.json` regenerated; the stand-in engine needs no change (it never enumerates outcomes).

- eng-contract (2026-11-01), review notes on the unsubscribe seam: `Unsubscribe/run` `done` means the request was accepted (a one-click 2xx) or the unsubscribe email was queued, not that the sender confirmed it; `failed` means the sender refused or the request could not be made (the owner is not unsubscribed). For prov-gmail-write: their `methods.test.ts` builds services by hand with no `unsubscribe` (`unsubCtx(store)`) and expects `unavailable`: when they drop the `!executor` guard, that test must inject an absent port (the standard stub) or keep the guard, otherwise it throws a TypeError on `undefined.run`. `UnsubscribePort.run` rejects with `signal.reason` on abort.

## 2026-10-30 — eng-time time-5: the notification outbox

- **Silent baseline is `accounts.baseline_done_at` plus the commit's step.** The ingest hook (`outbox/notifications.ts`, registered as `outboxExtension.ingest`) writes a `pending` row only for `origin: 'sync'`, `step: 'incremental'` batches, for a message that is `added`, unread, not a draft or own mail, in a folder with role `inbox` and none with role `spam` or `trash`, on an account whose `baseline_done_at` is set and whose `notifications` switch is on. The baseline step commits `baseline_done_at` in the same commit as its messages, so the step check is what keeps the first window silent; backfill, resync, prefetch, workflow, search and local writes never notify. No new marker was needed. Not done: no age limit on a message (mail that arrives after a long sleep still notifies) and no sender-rule or muted-thread check; the host or a later package can add them at claim time. (Superseded: "no age limit" is replaced by a 24 h age cap and a 20-row burst cap in the review-fix section "eng-time time-5: review fixes on the notification outbox"; the sender-rule and muted-thread note still stands.)
- **The hook never throws.** It wraps the whole batch and each message (a throw rolls back the sync commit and blocks the account on every retry), uses `INSERT OR IGNORE` keyed by `message_id`, and treats missing `roles` or `flags` as "does not qualify". A message it cannot read is skipped, the rest of the batch still notifies.
- **Leases live in `claimed_by`.** The table has no lease column, so a claim writes `claimed_by = '<token>@<lease end ms>'` (60 s, `CLAIM_LEASE_MS`). `claim` first returns expired claims to `pending` (same transaction), drops rows whose message was read or left the Inbox meanwhile, then claims up to `max` oldest-first. The `notifications` deadline source fires at the earliest lease end, resets expired claims and sends a `notify` host event so main claims again. A migration can give the lease its own column; `leaseOf` and `LEASE_END` are the only readers.
- **Ack is idempotent and ends the notification from any state.** `Notification/ack` accepts ids of the form `mail:<accountId>:<remoteId>` (anything else is ignored) and moves `pending` or `claimed` rows to `posted` or `dropped`; a finished row is never changed, so a late ack from a claimant whose lease expired also ends it. Delivery to main is at least once (a crash between post and ack re-offers after the lease); the item id is stable so the OS banner can replace itself. `posted` and `dropped` rows older than 30 days are deleted at the next claim or lease expiry.
- **Item text is sender and subject only**, control characters and line breaks flattened, clamped to the contract's 500 and 1,000 characters. Never the snippet or body. (Superseded: the body is now capped at 200 characters, and format characters (bidi, zero-width, BOM, soft hyphen) are stripped as well as control characters; see "eng-time/eng-host: the host claim loop" below. The title keeps its 500.)
- **`notify` and `badge` host events.** The hook cannot emit inside the commit (it may roll back), so it asks for a look in a microtask; the service then reads the table and sends `notify { pending }` only when rows exist. `start()` sends one for rows a crash left behind. The badge is `readCounts(...).all.unread` (views' own number, debounced 250 ms, sent only when it changes).
- **(Superseded by the review-fix section below.) Gap for the runtime: the badge cannot hear plain reads.** `FrameBus` is frozen and has no commit listener, and the store given to lanes has no subscription, so a read that lowers the unread count does not reach this lane. `NotificationService.refreshBadge()` exists for that call; until a runtime seam calls it after relevant commits (for example from `observeCommits`), the badge follows new mail, claim, ack, start and wake only. A 10 s poll was tried and removed: it adds timer fires that the existing "no spin" tests count, and it wakes the process for nothing. (Superseded: the views lane now calls `deadlines.changed('notifications')` after a flags, op or placements commit and the badge follows reads; see the review-fix section "eng-time time-5: review fixes on the notification outbox".)
## 2026-10-30 — eng-time: a cancelled uncertain row re-sent as a fresh row of the same revision

- **Known, accepted for now.** `messageIdOf` (`outbox/submissions.ts`) excludes the same `(draft, revision)` from `usedByOtherRevision`, so when an `uncertain` row is cancelled and the owner sends the SAME revision again, the fresh row reuses the same Message-ID (the synced draft's `remote_internet_id`). A fresh row counts as a first attempt, so `payloadOf` would name the synced draft (`rd-1`) again even though the first attempt may already have consumed it.
- **Why it is covered.** The Gmail adapter's own checks apply (`sendsRemoteDraft`; a missing draft is `uncertain`, never a blind second send), `findSent` still searches for the same id, and `drafts.send` is OFF until the draft path is switched on. Switching it on must first re-test this case (cancel an uncertain row, send the same revision again) in `send-guard.test.ts`.
- **The restart guard.** `send-guard.test.ts` now holds a restart test (a hung send, `kill()`, `open()`, the row is `uncertain`, Send anyway): the second delivery carries no `remoteDraftId` or `baseRemoteMessageId`, the same bytes and the same Message-ID, so a later change that keeps the attempt flag in memory instead of in the row fails it.

For prov-gmail-write: their `methods.test.ts` builds services by hand with no `unsubscribe` (`unsubCtx(store)`) and expects `unavailable`: when they drop the `!executor` guard, that test must inject an absent port (the standard stub) or keep the guard, otherwise it throws a TypeError on `undefined.run`. `UnsubscribePort.run` rejects with `signal.reason` on abort.

## 2026-10-30 — eng-contract: `Notification/claim` is at-least-once (wording)

- **Docs only, no schema change.** The `Notification/claim` summary (`api/admin.ts`) and the `NotificationItem` comment (`schemas/session.ts`) said a claimed item is "never handed out twice" / "claimed once, never duplicated by a crash". The engine's outbox (time-5, #147) leases a claim for about a minute and offers the item again if it is not acked, with the same stable `id`, so delivery is at-least-once and a repost replaces the banner. Both comments now say that; the host must treat it that way.

## 2026-10-30 — eng-time time-5: review fixes on the notification outbox

- **Delivery is at least once, with a stable id.** The claim lease hands a row to one claim at a time, but a host that posted and died before its ack gets the same item again once the lease ends. The id (`mail:<account>:<message>`) is the same each time, so the repost replaces the banner instead of stacking. Tests record every post of a simulated host and check: each qualifying message is posted at least once, always under its own id, never inside a live lease and never after its ack. **Contract wording to fix (not edited here):** `Notification/claim` in `packages/contract/src/api/admin.ts` says "a claimed item is never handed out twice", and the `NotificationItem` comment in `schemas/session.ts` says "claimed once, never duplicated by a crash"; both should say "at least once, stable id".
- **A late ack after a reclaim is accepted.** The ack carries ids only, no claim token, so it cannot tell that the row was claimed again. It marks any `pending` or `claimed` row `posted`/`dropped`; the other host may still post once more (same id, replaces the banner). Refusing it would only guarantee a duplicate.
- **Clock set back.** A lease more than `CLAIM_LEASE_MS` ahead of `now` counts as expired (in `expire` and in the source's `next()`, which then reports "due now").
- **The badge follows the mailbox.** The views lane's `deadlinesChanged` (`views/ops.ts`, the only edit outside `outbox/`; trailer `Lane-Approved: eng-views`) now also calls `deadlines.changed('notifications')` after a commit with an `op`, `flags` or `placements` write (read, unread, archive, trash, spam, labels, restore, undo). The `notifications` source reads `store.seq()` in `next()` and `fire()`, and a moved seq schedules the 250 ms debounced badge look, which emits only on a changed count. The ingest hook asks for a look on every batch that carries messages, any origin. Sync commits that only write `flags`/`placements`/deletes (no message) reach neither, so the source also wakes every `BADGE_POLL_MS` (60 s) to compare `seq`: a remote read shows in the Dock within a minute.
- **One query for the badge.** `UNREAD_INBOX_SQL` counts `thread_folders.unread > 0` over each account's first Inbox folder, forced onto the partial `tf_unread` index (the planner prefers the primary key, which walks the whole Inbox). A test holds it equal to `readCounts(...).all.unread` on a seeded two-account world through reads, moves and Done, and another reads the plan.
- **Ack lookup** starts at `notifications` and probes `messages` by primary key (`EXISTS`), not a scan of every message; a test reads the plan and a 5k-message world acks correctly.
- **Age cap and burst cap.** Mail whose `internalDate` is more than 24 h older than the commit is not announced (it still counts in the list and badge), and one ingest batch writes at most 20 rows (the newest 20; the rest are dropped silently, not counted anywhere). This bounds what a Mac that was off for days posts when it syncs again.
- **Skipped, on purpose:** Promotions and other low-importance mail still notifies (no category check); mail that arrives in Spam and is moved to the Inbox by the server is a new placement with `added=false`, so it never notifies while a message first seen in the Inbox does (inconsistent, accepted); no sender-rule or mute check at ingest. Each is a product decision for the notification settings work.

## 2026-10-30 — eng-contract/eng-time: draft port gaps 3 and 4, body delivery, supersedes

All four are additive and optional inside `v:1`: an adapter that implements none of them keeps working exactly as before (the engine only passes or reads the new members when it has them). Providers' files (`apps/desktop/src/host/providers/**`) are untouched; this section says what to implement.

- **Gap 3, `remove` / `deleteDraft` carry the expected message id.** `DraftSyncPort.remove(account, remoteDraftId, expectedMessageId?)` and `ProviderAdapter.deleteDraft?(remoteDraftId, ctx, expectedMessageId?)`. *Providers:* when `expectedMessageId` is given, delete ONLY IF the draft still holds it (Gmail: the draft's `message.id` equals it; JMAP: the `Email` id; Graph: the message `id`). If it holds another (edited or replaced on another device, or it became a sent message) do nothing and resolve; a missing draft is success. Without the parameter, behave as before. Gmail's `deleteDrafts` switch can be turned on once the adapter implements the guard. *Engine:* `removeHidden` (a discarded or sent draft's hidden row) passes the row's `remote_message_id` whenever it is known and calls the old two-argument form when it is null; the rowless removal (an upload that landed after its draft was gone) passes the message id that upload returned. A refused-by-guard call resolves like a delete, so the hidden row still finishes: the owner's discard never destroys the phone's newer version or a sent mail. Not covered: the engine's `remote_message_id` can be older than the provider's (a phone edit history has not reported); that is exactly the case the guard catches, and the phone's version then comes back through history as a draft of its own.
- **Gap 4, `remoteDraftId?` on the create-path conflict.** `DraftSaveResult` conflict is `{ kind: 'conflict'; remoteMessageId?; remoteDraftId? }`. *Providers:* on a create (the upload had no `remoteDraftId`) that finds an existing copy of the upload's Message-ID whose bytes differ, name that copy (`remoteDraftId`, and `remoteMessageId` when known). *Engine:* only when the upload was a create and the row has no remote draft, it stores `remote_draft_id` (and `remote_message_id` when named) on the row and adds the upload's Message-ID and the conflict's message id to `wrote`; the "keep mine, take theirs, or keep both" choice is raised as before, so keep mine updates that copy (base = the named message id) instead of creating a second draft under a new Message-ID, and history reporting the copy is an echo, not a phantom draft. A discarded draft adopts the copy only to delete it (removal due at once, guarded by the message id). On the update path, or when `gone`, the field is ignored. A conflict that names no `remoteDraftId` is today's behaviour.
- **Body delivery.** `RemoteDraftFact` / `RemoteDraftWrite` gain `html?` and `text?` (`sync/facts.ts` passes them through). *Providers:* fill them with the draft's body when you have it (Gmail `format=full`, JMAP `bodyValues`, IMAP fetch, Graph `body`). *Engine:* the body is the top-level `html`, else `text` (escaped, paragraphs on blank lines), else an `html` already on `message` (the old way). With a body the draft is COMPLETE (`_sync.incomplete` cleared, also when the same copy is reported again with its body), so Draft/set edits it in place, and take theirs / keep both use the full body; without one it stays snippet-only and incomplete, and an edit forks. Attachments are still not delivered.
- **`supersedes` on a remote fact.** `RemoteDraftFact` / `RemoteDraftWrite` gain `supersedes?: string[]`, the remote message ids the fact replaces (JMAP and IMAP update by create plus destroy; Gmail `drafts.update` gives the draft a new message id). *Providers:* fill it with the previous message id(s) of the same draft. *Engine, two cases only:* (1) the fact supersedes the id the row holds (`remote_message_id`) or any id in `wrote`, and its `internetId` is one of the row's own (`wrote`, the running upload, or the synced copy's `remote_internet_id`): it is the echo of OUR upload under its new message id; the row's `remote_message_id` advances to it and is remembered in `wrote`, with no conflict and no new revision. (2) The fact supersedes our base but its `internetId` is not ours (or absent): it is another device's edit that replaced our base, and goes the ordinary way (replaces a clean draft, raises the choice on a dirty one). Not handled, by design: a `removed` fact with `supersedes` is a removal (it is not matched to a later fact that supersedes it), and a fact with a NEW `remoteDraftId` is matched by its Message-ID as before (the later `saved` result sets the ids). This is the fact-side `supersedes`; the upload-side `DraftUpload.supersedes` (Providers' PORT GAP 2: earlier Message-IDs a create should look up) is NOT part of this change and stays open. Providers' PORT GAP 1 proposed `body?: { text, html }`; the binding request fixed top-level `html` / `text`.
- **Tests.** `packages/engine/tests/outbox/drafts.ports.test.ts` (19). The fake `fakeDrafts` in `tests/outbox/helpers.ts` honours the guard (`removeExpected`, `removeGuarded`) and gained the `conflictExisting` step.
- **Review fixes (supersede the bullets above where they differ; rule: the other device's only copy, or a sent message, is never deleted or silently overwritten).**
  - *`supersedes` is our echo only with proof.* A provider may keep a draft's Message-ID across edits (Graph does; JMAP and IMAP clients often do), so the Message-ID alone no longer makes a superseding fact ours. The row's base advances to the new message id only when (a) an upload under the fact's Message-ID is still running (`uploading.internetId`), or (b) the fact carries a usable body and the fields it describes hash to `meta.fingerprint`, the content we last uploaded. Otherwise the fact takes the ordinary path: our base stays, and the next upload is checked against it, so the provider answers `conflict` rather than accepting an overwrite of the phone's edit. Accepted cost: a provider that delivers no body (or a draft with attachments or Bcc, whose copy differs from the stored fields) gets a conflict on the next upload after its own echo, never a silent overwrite.
  - *`DraftSaveResult` conflict gains `remoteInternetId?`*, the Message-ID of the copy it names. The engine adopts the named copy (create path, row without a remote draft, row not being discarded) only when it equals the upload's Message-ID. Without it, or with another one, the conflict is the ordinary one and nothing is adopted (`conflictForeign` and `conflictUnproven` in the fake). The fake now matches the copy by Message-ID.
  - *An adopted copy is "theirs", never ours.* Its bytes differ from ours, which is the case where another device edited it. The row stores only `remote_draft_id` (so keep mine updates it) and remembers the upload's Message-ID; `remote_message_id` stays null and the copy's message id is NOT stored or put in `wrote`. Its history fact (our Message-ID, the conflict's message id) therefore completes the open conflict with their body instead of being dropped as an echo, so take theirs and keep both work. Keep mine sets the base from `conflict.theirsMessageId` as before (unguarded when none was named, as the owner chose).
  - *Discard forgets, never deletes, a copy we cannot call ours.* A discarded row never adopts a conflict's copy; with no remote draft known its hidden row ends at once and the copy stays (history may show it later as a draft of its own). Discarding a row whose open conflict has no message id of ours for its copy (`remote_message_id` null: an adoption) forgets the copy like the changed-elsewhere and sent cases.
  - *Completeness.* A delivered body makes a draft complete only when the fact does not say the copy has attachments (`message.hasAttachments`): attachments are not delivered, so such a draft stays incomplete (forks on edit, no take theirs or keep both) instead of uploading `attachments: []` over the phone's copy. A body is also left out (snippet only, incomplete) when the stored body, after escaping and wrapping a `text`, is over 1,000,000 characters, the local save limit; and an empty `html` or `text` next to a non-empty snippet is not a body (`html` then `text` then the message's `html`, first non-empty).
  - *Not covered:* `isEcho` still treats any fact carrying a Message-ID we wrote as ours (before this branch too), so a phone edit under our Message-ID is not shown as a conflict; it is only caught by the provider's check on our next upload. The adoption guard "no adoption over a row that has a remote draft" also covers the update path (an update's row always has one), so there is one guard, tested through an update that names a draft claiming our Message-ID.
- **Second review of #153 (an upload explains ONE move of the base).** `uploading` now stores `base`, the remote message id the attempt was made against (`syncDraft`). A fact that supersedes the row's base under the running upload's Message-ID counts as that upload's echo only while `row.remote_message_id` is still that base and the fact supersedes it (`explainedByAttempt`). After the base has moved the attempt has used its move: a further hop under the same Message-ID (a phone edit that keeps it, as JMAP and IMAP clients do) needs `holdsOurContent`. Without this, a lost-reply retry could be followed by a phone edit that moved the base to the phone's version, and the retry would then overwrite it or a discard would delete it. A row written before the field has no `base` and explains no move (safe). A retry that starts later stores its own base, so a hop during that attempt is again one move.
- **Remote body cap is checked before any work.** A source over 1,000,000 characters is dropped before `paragraphs()` escapes it (escaping only grows a text); `bodyOf` is worked out once per fact; `sync/facts.ts` does not carry an `html` or `text` over the limit. An over-limit source still counts as the body on offer, so the draft stays snippet-only rather than falling back to another source.
- **An old provider that ignores the expected message id** is pinned in tests (`FakeDrafts.state.ignoreExpected`): the engine still passes the id, an unreported phone edit is then deleted by a discard (the limit of such a provider; the Gmail adapter must forward the third argument), and the rules that need no provider help hold (a copy adopted from a conflict is forgotten, never deleted).
- **Third review of #153: the running-upload proof is by content, not by hops.** The "one move per attempt" rule (stored `base`) is dropped: a same-revision retry reuses the Message-ID and a fresh base, and one coalesced fact can supersede both hops, so counting moves was unsafe. `uploading` now stores the upload's `fingerprint` instead of `base`. A fact supersedes-and-moves the base only when its Message-ID is the running upload's AND its delivered content (a body, no attachments) hashes to that fingerprint (`explainedByAttempt`), or when it hashes to the last synced fingerprint (`holdsOurContent`). A fact with other content, with no body, or from a row written before the field gets no proof and goes the ordinary way: a conflict, which is always the safe direction. Tests: the lost-reply retry then a phone edit under the same Message-ID, a phone edit while the retry is running, one fact superseding the base and C, a fact with no body; the Message-ID-only proof makes all four fail.
- **Fourth review of #153: a content proof only where the fact can show everything.** `theirsFields` fills in the local draft's attachments, sign-off and `from` because a fact cannot show them, so a matching fingerprint says nothing about those. `hasFingerprint` (both the running-upload proof and the last-synced proof) now gives NO proof when the draft keeps attachments or a sign-off (`keepsMine`), when the copy says `hasAttachments`, or when the copy's `from` is missing or is not the account's own address (every upload goes out under it). Without a proof the fact is an ordinary remote edit: a conflict. The cost: a draft with an attachment or sign-off never advances its base by echo; its next upload meets the provider's own check and raises a conflict at worst (never an overwrite). Facts the tests build carry `from`.
- **Fifth review of #153: the content proof is closed-world.** Four rounds each found one more field a fact cannot show (attachments, sign-off, From, Bcc). `DRAFT_CONTENT_FIELDS` (`draft-state.ts`) now classifies EVERY draft field once, typed against `DraftFields` so a new field does not compile until classified: `shown` (to, cc, bcc, subject, html, from), `carried` (account, mode, thread, reply target: identity, a provider edit cannot change it), `notShown` (attachments, signature, signatureSource). `showsEveryField` gives a fingerprint proof (running-upload and last-synced alike) only when the copy carries every `shown` field (a Bcc the provider cannot show is not an empty Bcc: `MessageFacts.bcc?` is new, optional, passed through `messageWrite`), the sender is the account's own address, and every `notShown` field is empty here and not reported by the copy. Otherwise no proof, which is a conflict. A test pins the list against the contract's `DraftFields` schema and the exact `notShown` and `shown` sets, so a field cannot silently become provable. The draft copy deliberately carries no Bcc (an earlier decision, pinned by `drafts.third` test 7: a draft with Bcc is sent as the pinned MIME, never as the copy), so `draftMime` is unchanged; a copy then shows `bcc: []` and any Bcc a phone adds shows as a difference. Body selection moved to `draft-body.ts` (line limit). Not compared (accepted): the From display name; an html-only formatting change when a provider delivers only text (phone drafts carry html). Providers: set `MessageFacts.bcc` on draft copies when the provider can show it (Gmail can, from the draft message's Bcc header), or echoes of our own uploads get no proof.
- **Sixth review of #153: a copy never silently replaces what only this device holds.** The draft copy carries no Bcc by design (and no sign-off source), so once a fact delivers a body, replacing a clean draft with the copy (`theirsFields` sets `bcc: message.bcc ?? []`) would drop a local Bcc recipient or `signatureSource` with no conflict. A clean draft with a Bcc or a `signatureSource` is now handed to the owner as a conflict (`holdsOnlyHere`); the copy's own Bcc is not merged in (a phone that removed one it added would be hidden). Take theirs, if chosen, is then an explicit owner decision. Tests: Bcc with a copy that shows an empty Bcc and with one that cannot show it, a sign-off source, and a control that a draft with nothing only-local is still replaced silently. The closed-world test now pins the `carried` set too.
- **Seventh review of #153: attachments and the sender join the no-silent-replace rule.** Our uploaded copy carries readable attachments, so a phone can remove them; replacing a clean draft with the copy would write the phone's text plus OUR attachments back (and a send would put the removed file in the mail). `holdsOnlyHere` now includes `attachments.length > 0` (a draft with attachments conflicts on a phone edit; take theirs is explicit, as before). `theirsFields` takes the account address and uses `message.from` when it names another address (else it keeps the draft's own `from`, which keeps fingerprints stable); a clean draft whose phone edit changes the sender is a conflict, not a replace (`changesSender`), and the conflict's `theirs` shows the sender. A copy sent from the account's own address (any case) is still replaced silently. Take theirs on a sender conflict gives the draft the other address, which the send path then refuses with its usual message ("Send from this account's own address") instead of mailing from a wrong alias. Tests: `drafts.third` 6 flipped to expect a conflict, and attachments, alias sender, own-address control, keep-mine on a Bcc conflict in `drafts.supersedes.test.ts`. Not changed (accepted): a phone removal of a clean draft deletes it with no conflict (deleting or sending on the phone is the owner's choice).
- **Eighth review of #153.** (1) `theirsFields` no longer copies OUR attachments into a copy that says it has none (`hasAttachments !== true`): after the attachment-removal conflict, take theirs and the second draft of keep both give the phone's text without the removed file (`drafts.third` 6 flipped; the copy then is exactly this revision). A copy that says it HAS attachments still keeps ours, and is incomplete anyway. (2) `insertRemote` and `refreshRemote` pass the account address, so a draft started on the phone from an alias keeps that sender and a send from here refuses loudly (`senderOf`) instead of leaving from the main address.
- **BINDING, to fix before provider draft sync is switched on (nothing wires it yet):** (3) `theirsFields` should use `message.from` whenever present and compare it with `base.from?.address ?? own`: today a draft that took alias A conflicts on every phone edit, take theirs keeps A after the phone switched back, and a display-name change is dropped (sending still fails loudly). (4) `sync/facts.ts` `hasAttachments` ignores inline parts, so a pasted inline image on a clean no-attachment draft makes the copy "complete" and replaces our draft with `cid:` references and no image (pre-existing): an inline part or a `cid:` in the body must make the copy incomplete.
- **Test files.** The `supersedes` and body-cap tests moved to `drafts.supersedes.test.ts`; the shared helpers are in `drafts.ports.kit.ts` (the old test file passed the 800-line lint limit).
- **Over-limit bodies are never replaced by another source.** `sync/facts.ts` no longer drops an `html` or `text` over 1,000,000: it cuts it to 1,000,001 characters so it stays bounded and the engine still sees that a body was offered; `bodyOf` returns nothing when ANY offered source (top-level html, text, the message's html) is over the limit, so the draft stays snippet-only and is never built from a shorter text over the phone's rich html.

## 2026-10-30 — eng-contract/eng-time: #157 follow-ups

- **A create refused for good is given up like a replaced one.** `problemFor` (draft-sync.ts) cleared `uploading` without keeping its Message-ID. It now keeps the attempt's and any older unanswered attempt's id through `abandoned()` (draft-state.ts, shared with the replace and uncertain-retry paths): into `wrote` always, and into `earlier` (offered as `supersedes`) while the draft has no remote copy. A refusal is not proof that nothing landed. Test in `drafts.supersedes.test.ts`.
- **Engine bundle after this change: 399,992 of 400,000 bytes.** Made room by sharing `abandoned()`, simplifying the body selection in `draft-body.ts` (the over-limit check is one pass over the offered sources) and dropping the separate `earlierWith`. The budget holds as decided; there is still no headroom.
- **BINDING with (3) and (4), before provider draft sync is switched on:** (c) `adoptCopy` (draft-writes.ts) adopts a copy by Message-ID alone. With a provider that keeps the Message-ID across edits, a phone-edited copy under our Message-ID would be adopted as ours, and the next upload would replace it. Adoption should also need the content proof (`hasFingerprint` against the upload's fingerprint) or the provider's `remoteInternetId` plus bytes proof, like the create-path conflict.
- **The `notifications: backfill pages` todo (`gov-stack-attack-blocks.test.ts`, prov-gov-attack-10): eng-time decides NOT to build a per-message new-arrival fact; providers may switch the test to `skip`.** The test's second half asserts that no `incremental` commit carries a message older than the first sync. That cannot be a provider invariant: an old message moved back to the Inbox elsewhere is a real Inbox change and commits with the history page that reports it. What the notification rule needs is only that such mail is not ANNOUNCED, and it already is not: the ingest hook skips mail whose `internalDate` is more than 24 h older than the commit (and caps a burst), so regained old mail updates the Inbox and the badge without a banner. A new fact would be a contract change for a case the age cap already covers, and the engine bundle has no room (399,992 of 400,000 bytes). The backfill half of the test (every backfill page commits coarse) still holds and is worth keeping as its own test; the regained-mail half should assert on notifications (none queued for the old message), not on commits.

Almost everything left is used. The 400,000-byte budget stands; the 450,000 question is with the owner.

It re-reads the row. Test: a refusal that arrives after a fact adopted the copy.
