## 2026-10-30 — eng-contract/eng-time: BINDING (4) inline parts and `cid:` make a copy incomplete

- **A remote draft copy with an inline part is not complete.** Inline parts (a pasted image) are not delivered, and `hasAttachments` of a message counts only visible attachments, so a clean draft whose phone copy brought a `cid:` image was replaced by a body with a broken reference, dropping this device's own inline image. Two rules now: (a) `sync/facts.ts` (`draftWrite`) sets the draft copy's `message.hasAttachments` when any attachment fact is inline (the field then means "parts this device cannot carry"; it is only read for draft copies here, a message's list display is unchanged); (b) a body that points at an inline part (`cid:`, `hasInlineRef` in draft-state.ts) is not complete, for a provider that says nothing about parts. An incomplete copy never replaces a clean draft silently (the owner is asked), and take theirs / keep both are not offered for it. The pattern is narrow on purpose (Opus review: a broad `cid:` match made ordinary prose or a link's query string an incomplete copy, whose only way out was keep mine, which overwrites the other device's text) but spelling-proof (second Opus review): after one pass of character-reference decoding (`&quot;`, `&#39;`, `&#99;`, `&#x63;`, as a browser does) it matches `cid:` as a `src`, `srcset` (any candidate), `href`, `background` or `data` value or inside `url()`, with or without quotes and in any case, and Outlook's `[cid:` placeholder anywhere in a text body (the body text counts too). "CID: 4471" in prose, `?cid:1` in a link, `data:` URIs and `[cid]` do not. Both rules fail their tests when removed: the facts.ts flag in `provider-additions.test.ts`, the body rule in `drafts.inline.test.ts`, and the `completes()` half (a copy whose body points at `cid:` gets no content proof for an echo even when the provider lists no part) in its own test. **Linear time (third Opus review, HIGH).** The first spelling-proof pattern had two `\s*` around an optional quote, which backtracks quadratically on a run of spaces (10k spaces 0.2 s, 100k 22 s; 1 MB would block sync for about half an hour, and `&#32;` / no-break spaces are decoded first, so a reply quoting hostile HTML could trigger it). It is now `(?:\b(?:src|href|background|data)\s*=|url\()\s*(?:["']\s*)?cid:` with one `\s*` before the quote and one inside the optional quote group. Timing tests run eleven hostile shapes (whitespace, quotes, equals signs, entities, repeated `src=` / `url(`) at 30,000 characters first (the old pattern fails that one in about 2 s, so a regression fails fast instead of hanging the run) and at 1 MB, each under 200 ms. **Known gaps, recorded, none is a spelling any editor writes:** `&#99id:` (no semicolon, browsers still decode it), `&quotcid:`, unquoted `srcset=cid:a`, `poster=`, `image-set('cid:a')`, CSS escapes (`\63 id`), and a tab or newline inside `cid:` pass as complete. **Known false positives,** costing a conflict that keep mine clears: `srcset="b.png?x=cid:1 1x"`, and a reply quoting an Outlook `[cid:image001...]` placeholder makes every phone edit of a clean draft a conflict. An incomplete copy is stored with its body but marked incomplete (an edit forks it), not snippet-only. Tests: `drafts.inline.test.ts` and the `stepWrites` test in `provider-additions.test.ts`; both rules fail their tests when removed.

## 2026-10-30 — eng-contract/eng-time: BINDING (3) the sender of a draft from another device

- **The copy's sender is compared with the one the draft goes out under.** `theirsFields` used to take `message.from` only when it differed from the account's address and otherwise kept the draft's `from`. A draft that took an alias A therefore conflicted on every phone edit (A differs from the account address), take theirs kept A after the phone switched back, and display-name edits of the sender were dropped. Now (`copySender`, draft-state.ts) the copy's sender is compared with `base.from ?? own`: the same address keeps the draft's `from` and takes the copy's display name when it has one (for a draft with NO explicit `from` the name is not kept: it goes out from the account address under the account's own display name, and only the address matters); another address is taken from the copy, except the account's own address, which is the absence of an explicit `from`. `changesSender` (draft-writes.ts, the no-silent-replace guard) compares the effective senders (`from ?? own`) of the copy and the local draft, so a clean draft conflicts exactly when the sender really changed (alias to own, own to alias, alias to another alias), not on every edit. The proof paths are unchanged (`showsEveryField` still wants the account's own address, so an alias draft gets no echo proof and conflicts, the safe side). Alias drafts can only exist as drafts from another device today (the engine stores no send-as identity, and `senderOf` refuses another address on upload and send), so the tests (`drafts.sender.test.ts`) run on remote-origin drafts.

## 2026-10-30 — eng-contract: the `cid:` scan's known gaps are deferred, on purpose

- **Deferred, not fixed:** `&#99id:` without a semicolon, `&quotcid:`, unquoted `srcset=cid:a`, `poster=`, `image-set('cid:a')`, CSS escapes (`\63 id`), and a tab or newline inside `cid:` (URL parsing strips them) all pass `hasInlineRef` as complete. None is a spelling any editor writes. **Why this is safe to defer:** the scan does not protect against loss of this device's data. If OUR draft holds an inline image it has attachments, and `holdsOnlyHere` (`attachments.length > 0`) already turns any phone edit into a conflict whatever the body says; the `cid:` scan only matters for a clean draft with none, where a missed reference means the draft shows the broken image the phone's own copy already had, and nothing of ours is overwritten. So the gaps cost a cosmetic broken image in a rare hand-written case, and each fix (entity decoding without `;`, CSS escape decoding) adds scanning code that has already needed three review rounds for linear time. **Trigger to revisit:** a provider that serialises one of these forms, or a report of a broken inline image after a phone edit; then add the form with its timing test. The engine draft-sync wiring stays queued behind providers' #156.

## 2026-10-31 — eng-contract/eng-time: BINDING (c) a copy is adopted only when proven ours

- **Rule (the Integrator's decision, 03:40).** `adoptCopy` used to adopt a copy reported under the Message-ID of the running upload (or any id in `wrote`) by that id alone. A provider keeps a draft's Message-ID across edits (Gmail does, per Providers' probe), so a lost-reply copy edited on the phone was adopted as ours; a later update then overwrote it, or a discard deleted it inside the removal grace window. Now a copy is adopted only when `provenOurs`: its Message-ID is the running upload's (or a superseded attempt's) AND its content has the fingerprint of that upload, by the same closed-world proof as the running-upload echo (`hasFingerprint` / `showsEveryField`: it must show every field, own-address From, no attachments or sign-off). Anything else, including a snippet-only copy with no body (Gmail without a body read), is shown as a remote draft (`insertRemote`): a visible duplicate the owner can discard, never replaced or deleted from here. The removal-grace deletion of an adopted copy therefore applies only to proven-ours copies.
- **Fingerprints of superseded attempts.** `_sync.prints` maps the Message-ID of each given-up attempt to the fingerprint of what it uploaded (newest 5, the bound of `earlier`), written by `abandoned()` in the same setMeta that moves the id into `wrote`, and by the unexpected-ack branch of `synced`. The running upload's fingerprint is in `uploading`.
- **Two rows never share a remote draft (Opus review of this change).** History may show an unproven copy as a remote draft before the upload's reply arrives. The reply (`synced`) then names that remote draft: the early row is the same copy, and is dropped, ONLY when it is untouched (`origin: 'remote'`), holds the very message id the reply names, and has no send queued (`sameCopy`). Any other early row (a newer, phone-edited version; a draft the owner edited or is discarding; one with a send in its undo window) is kept and this draft is NOT bound to it: the upload is treated as done (`wrote` and `prints`, NOT `earlier`: the next create must not point back at the copy the other row holds, or an adapter would replace it and loop), `dueAt` stays so the next upload makes its own copy, a discarded draft just ends. A create-path conflict (`adoptedByConflict`) likewise refuses to bind to a copy another row already shows. Nothing is lost either way: the other row keeps the phone's content in front of the owner. The cost is a visible duplicate, as decided.
- **Known and left:** on Graph a draft's message id does not change when it is edited, so `remote_message_id` equality in `sameCopy` does not prove the very same version there; the Graph adapter must supply a change key comparison before its draft sync is wired. `adoptCopy` still returns early when the matching row already has its own remote draft, so a copy of a superseded attempt that is a different remote draft is not shown (nothing is deleted server-side; the adapter's `supersedes` replace normally makes it the same draft). `prints` keeps the newest 5 (the bound of `earlier`; `wrote` keeps 20): an id evicted from `prints` but still in `wrote` gives a visible duplicate instead of an adoption, the safe side.
- **Cost.** More copies show up as remote drafts when the provider cannot show a body or the content differs; with Providers' #156 (body and `message` together) a normal lost-reply copy is proven and adopted. Tests: `drafts.adopt.test.ts` (phone-edited copy not adopted and survives an update and a discard; unedited copy adopted without a duplicate; snippet-only shown; a superseded attempt only on its own fingerprint; the early row dropped), and the existing adoption tests now report the full copy (`copyWrite`).
- `draft-proof.ts` holds the proof helpers (split out of `draft-writes.ts`, which passed the 800-line limit).

## 2026-10-31 — eng-contract/eng-time: follow-ups for the draft-sync wiring (#164 and #156 reviews)

- **A reply written elsewhere keeps its reply target.** `insertRemote` built a foreign draft with `theirsFields` and no base, so a phone reply kept its `threadId` but lost `replyToMessageId`, and an edit uploaded from here could fall out of its Gmail thread (the reply headers are rebuilt from `replyToMessageId`). It now looks the copy's In-Reply-To up among the stored messages of the account (`repliedTo`, `draft-proof.ts`) and sets `mode: 'reply'` and `replyToMessageId` (reply-all cannot be known from a copy). A reply to a message we do not hold stays a plain draft with its thread.
- **`synced` looks for other holders whenever this row does not already hold the replied remote draft** (it was only when the row had none): a draft that adopted an earlier attempt's copy is no longer re-bound to a later attempt's copy that another row shows.
- **An unproven copy shown for an attempt leaves `earlier`.** The next create no longer offers its id as `supersedes`; the adapter's byte comparison was the only thing stopping a replace.
- **A copy proves nothing unless it says it has no attachments.** `showsEveryField` requires `hasAttachments === false` (it only refused `true`); adapters must always report it (Gmail does, from the parts list). Test helpers report `false` by default.
- Not done, as the Integrator's last list no longer asks for it: `sameCopy` ignoring a shown copy that is already `sent` (very narrow). **Draft-sync wiring itself** is the host composition root (`apps/desktop/src/host`), because the engine already takes a host `DraftSyncPort` (`services.drafts`); `src/main` still runs the legacy engine, so there is nothing to switch on in the engine.

## 2026-11-03 — eng-time: the end-to-end review's HIGH 1 (isEcho) and its follow-ups

- **A new message id under our Message-ID is no echo without our content (HIGH 1, probe P4a).** `isEcho` took any fact whose `internetId` was the running upload's or in `wrote` as our own echo. Gmail is believed to keep the Message-ID header across a phone edit, so the edit was dropped, the owner's later edit met a conflict that carried no body, and `keepMine` then updated over a version nobody had seen. Now a fact is an echo when it carries a message id this row holds or wrote; a NEW message id under one of our Message-IDs is an echo only with our content: the running upload's fingerprint, a superseded attempt's `prints` entry, or the last synced fingerprint (`provenOurs` / `holdsOurContent`). Otherwise the ordinary remote-edit rules apply: a clean draft takes the edit, a draft with unsent changes gets a conflict with both bodies. Cost: our own copy reported with no body, under a message id the reply has not told us yet, raises a conflict (the Gmail adapter delivers the body with the report, so this is the fallback). Tests: `drafts.echo.test.ts`; five older `supersedes` tests encoded the dropped-as-echo behaviour and now assert that the edit is shown.
- **Keep mine on a conflict whose other version was never shown whole forgets the remote ids and makes a NEW copy.** `keepMine` used to update over whatever the copy held (the upload's base was the conflict's message id, or none). Now, when `conflict.complete !== true` (no body arrived) it behaves like a gone copy: `FORGET_REMOTE`, the next upload is a create, the other version stays where it is and is shown if history reports it again. When the owner saw the other version whole, keep mine still replaces it knowingly. The cost is a possible duplicate remote draft (a create-path conflict on our own byte-changed copy), the safe side. Seven older tests that expected an update over the copy now expect a create.
- **LOW 1: `Submission/set` with `type: 'send'` answers `unavailable` when the host passed no send port** (the real engine path before sending is wired), instead of queueing a message that locks its draft for about ten minutes and never leaves. **LOW 2:** `wakeDrafts` retries drafts with an upload or removal pending; a draft parked by a refusal that will not change (`permission`, `config`) tries again with its next edit; the comment now says so (no new retry loop). **LOW 3:** a removal with no draft row that keeps failing logs `draft: r<16 hex>` (a digest of account and provider draft id), not the provider's draft id.
- **#190 MEDIUM: the newer phone version held under a send is not lost with the draft.** `applyDraftDelete` forgot a held full report when the sent or discarded draft went away, so the phone's newer version stayed on the provider unseen. The held report (not a removal) is now inserted as the other device's own draft after the row is gone (`resurface`). Two N3 tests that expected nothing left now expect that draft. **LOW:** the sent-at-current-revision half of `sendPins` has a behaviour test (a send that is done but whose clean-up has not run still holds a phone edit). **LOW, recorded:** the held report keeps a full HTML body in the row's data (one report, cleared on release).
- **Opus review of this change (FIX, 3 MEDIUM + 1 LOW, all fixed).** (1) Our own update reported with no body BEFORE its ack opened a conflict that nothing ended (the ack arrived in the "not expected" branch), and keep mine then left a duplicate of our own copy: `synced` now ends a conflict whose "theirs" is the very message id and Message-ID the ack names (`ownReport`) and takes the ack normally. (2) After keep mine made a new copy, a later report of the OLD copy under our Message-ID was swallowed by `adoptCopy` (the row already had a copy): now a report of another remote draft under one of our Message-IDs is dropped only if it is proven ours, otherwise shown. (3) `resurface` reused the usual id `acct:remoteDraftId`, which the sent phone draft itself carries (its sent submission would then pin the new row, or the insert hit the primary key): a fresh id (`…~<seq>`) is used when the usual one is taken by a draft or a submission. LOW: a held report is resurfaced only if it still names the row's own copy (the `releaseHeld` rule). Consequence recorded: an orphan first copy of an attempt that the adapter did not replace shows as a visible duplicate when its report carries no body.
- **Not done here:** the #189 follow-ups (preload exposure, `messageBytes`, in-flight byte budget, `trusted()` tests, the data lock before engine exit) are Electron main-process code in `apps/desktop/src/main`; they go in their own PR. The Mac live probes need a signed-in Gmail account on a Mac.

## 2026-10-30 — eng-time/eng-host: the host claim loop

- **Banner text is plain and short.** `flat()` in `outbox/notifications.ts` now also removes Unicode format characters (`\p{Cf}`: bidi overrides and isolates, zero-width characters, BOM, soft hyphen), not only control characters, for the sender and the subject alike, so a subject cannot reorder or hide text in an OS banner. The body (subject) is capped at 200 characters (was 1,000); the title keeps 500. The clamp counts code points, so a surrogate pair is never split, and trailing whitespace is trimmed after the clamp. Removing ZWJ also breaks joined emoji sequences into their parts; accepted.
- **The host claims on connect.** `notify` is not replayed on attach, so rows that were pending before the host connected would wait for the next new mail. `runNotificationLoop(session, post, options?)` (`src/host/notifications.ts`, no electron import) subscribes first, then claims once, then claims again on every `notify` host event. Same convention as `announceMissedReminders`; the host calls it once right after `engine.connect()`. Not wired into `src/main` yet (main still runs the legacy engine).
- **Run shape.** A run claims `{ max: 50 }` until a batch is short or empty (at most 100 batches), posts each item, and acks the posted ids and the dropped ids in separate calls of at most 50 ids. Runs never overlap; any number of notifies during a run schedule exactly one more. A failing claim is swallowed and tried again at the next notify, never on a timer, so a sick engine cannot cause a hot loop.
- **A throwing post leaves the item unacked.** It is not `dropped`: the engine offers it again after the 60 s lease, which is the retry. Only a returned `'dropped'` (notifications off, Focus) finishes an item without a banner. Logs carry a fixed message and the error name, never notification text.
- **At least once, without reposting.** The loop remembers the ids it posted and acked (the newest 500) and does not post them again if the engine offers them; an id posted but whose ack failed is acked again at the start of the next run and not reposted. The ack for an item already posted is still sent after `stop()`; nothing new is claimed or posted.

## 2026-10-30 — eng-html: proxy-transport "six slow fetches" flake

- **Cause: a foreign request, not load.** The slow test server held and counted every request. A local tool probing the newly opened port (a `GET /` with `user-agent: node` and `Host: localhost:<port>`) was held like a fetch, so the seventh "connected", the request count was 15, and no one ever released it, which is the 10 s timeout. Found by logging the Host and user agent of each request under CPU load.
- **Fix, test only.** `slowServer()` answers any request whose Host is not `img.example` with 404 at once and leaves it out of `held`, `peak` and `requests`. The release loop now keeps releasing until every fetch has finished (it used to stop after three rounds, which can leave late connections held when they connect slowly). The test timeout is 30 s and `until` waits up to 5 s. 40 full-file runs under a 24-process CPU burn pass; before the fix a failure showed up in 1 of about 15 loaded runs.

## 2026-10-30 — prov-gmail: Gmail drafts adapter on the additive draft ports (#153)

Rule kept: another device's edit, or a sent message, is never deleted or silently overwritten.

- **PORT GAP 3 closed.** `deleteDraft(remoteDraftId, ctx, expectedMessageId?)` forwards the third argument. With an expected id: `drafts.get` (metadata); a missing draft is success; a draft that is no longer a draft (sent, trashed) is left alone; a draft whose CURRENT message id differs (another device edited it) is left alone and the call resolves; only a match gets `drafts.delete` (a 404 on it is success). **Without an expected id nothing is deleted and no request goes out**, whatever the option says: a caller that cannot prove the copy never deletes (there is no unguarded delete any more). `GoogleProviderOptions.deleteDrafts` now defaults to TRUE (guarded removal only); `false` turns every removal off. Known limit: Gmail has no precondition on `drafts.delete`, so an edit in the milliseconds between the `get` and the `delete` is still lost (the same window as the replace).
- **PORT GAP 4 closed.** A create whose lookup hits a copy with other bytes returns `{ kind: 'conflict', remoteMessageId, remoteDraftId, remoteInternetId }`; `remoteInternetId` is the Message-ID header read from the raw bytes just compared (unfolded, any case); it is left out when the raw has none, and the engine then adopts nothing. Nothing is written; the engine's adoption rule (only when it equals the upload's Message-ID, the copy stays "theirs") is its own.
- **Body delivery.** For a foreign DRAFT message the history pass reads the draft with `drafts.get` format=full (60 units) and delivers `html` / `text` (parts found with `walkPayload` of `mime.ts`, decoded strictly) only when the draft has no attachment, the parts carry their data inline (no body stored by attachment id), the part is not over 1,000,000 characters, and the text is not blank. A part is decoded strictly in its declared charset (UTF-8 when none): an unknown charset label or bytes invalid in it give NO body (never a UTF-8 guess), and a part whose declared `size` or base64 length cannot fit the cap (more than 4 bytes per allowed character) is skipped before it is decoded. "No attachment" is judged on EVERY part, leaf or container: a filename, an attachment or inline disposition, or a type that is neither `text/*` nor `multipart/*` (`message/rfc822`, `application/*`, `image/*`) makes the draft an attachment draft, and the children of such a container are never read as the draft's body (a forwarded `.eml` must not become the draft's html). Otherwise the fact is reported as before (snippet only). At most 5 reads per history PASS (`BODY_FETCHES`), counted across all its pages (`drafts.bodyReads` in `provider_memory` while the pass is not at its head; a page of a pass that already used its 5 reads none); the rest wait in `provider_memory` (`drafts.bodyPending`, message id to `[draft id, failed reads]`, newest 200 kept) and go first on the next pass, reported again with the body (the engine completes a draft that is re-reported with one). A waiter is dropped when the pass reports something newer for its draft or when the read finds another message id or a non-draft, or on a 404; a pause or refusal keeps the rest waiting; a transient failure (5xx, network, timeout) keeps it waiting for `MAX_TRIES` = 3 failed reads, then it is dropped and the classified error is logged as `sync.gmail.request_refused` with `attempt: 3` (no content; the F7 catalog is closed, so no new event id); any other failure drops it. Content is never logged. **Attachments stay undelivered** (such drafts stay incomplete in the engine and fork on edit). The same `drafts.get` format=full read gives the draft's Message-ID header, which the fact carries as `internetId` (no extra request), also when no body is delivered. Trade-off: the engine treats a fact with one of our Message-IDs as our echo, so a phone edit that kept our Message-ID is not raised at once; it is caught by the provider's base check on our next upload (conflict, never an overwrite).
- **`supersedes`.** A foreign draft fact lists the earlier message ids this adapter itself recorded for the same draft id (`drafts.byMessage`), never the page's deleted messages and never another draft's. A first sighting names none. The engine decides whether the fact is our echo, on its own proof.
- **PORT GAP 2: closed on the Gmail side, see "prov-gmail: PORT GAP 2" below** (it was open here; `DraftUpload.supersedes` arrived with #157).
- **Tests.** `tests/prov-gmail/drafts.test.ts` (guarded delete: matching, mismatching, absent id, sent, trashed, 404, option off), `drafts-lookup.test.ts` (conflict fields), `draft-history.test.ts` (bodies, bounds, pending, supersedes).

## 2026-10-30 — eng-html: proxy-transport "six slow fetches" flake

- **Cause: a foreign request, not load.** The slow test server held and counted every request. A local tool probing the newly opened port (a `GET /` with `user-agent: node` and `Host: localhost:<port>`) was held like a fetch, so the seventh "connected", the request count was 15, and no one ever released it, which is the 10 s timeout. Found by logging the Host and user agent of each request under CPU load.
- **Fix, test only.** `slowServer()` answers any request whose Host is not `img.example` with 404 at once and leaves it out of `held`, `peak` and `requests`. The release loop now keeps releasing until every fetch has finished (it used to stop after three rounds, which can leave late connections held when they connect slowly). The test timeout is 30 s and `until` waits up to 5 s. 40 full-file runs under a 24-process CPU burn pass; before the fix a failure showed up in 1 of about 15 loaded runs.

## 2026-10-30 — eng-time/eng-host: notification follow-ups, claim retry and banner text

- **A failed claim retries on a timer.** `runNotificationLoop` arms one unref'd timer when `Notification/claim` fails: 5 s, doubling per consecutive failure, capped at 5 min (`retryDelay`). A claim that works resets the delay; any new run (a notify or the timer itself) cancels the pending timer first; the stop function clears it, and a timer that fires after stop claims nothing. The `timers` option lets tests drive it without waiting. A failed ack is still retried only at the next run (it has an engine lease behind it, and the claim retry covers a dead engine).
- **Banner text strips a named set, not `\p{Cf}`.** `flat()` removes U+00AD, U+200B, U+200E/F, U+202A-E, U+2060-2069, U+FEFF and U+E0000-E007F (soft hyphen, zero-width space, LRM/RLM, bidi embeddings and overrides, word joiner and isolates, BOM, tag characters). ZWNJ/ZWJ (U+200C/D) and the Arabic number signs (U+0600-0605) stay: Persian, Indic and Arabic subjects and emoji sequences need them. Other format characters (for example U+061C Arabic letter mark) are kept; the list is the owner's named set, extend it if one proves abusable.

## 2026-10-30 — prov-gmail: foreign-draft facts carry `message` (#156)

- **Foreign-draft facts carry `message`, and a body never goes without it (Opus CRITICAL on #156).** Without `RemoteDraftFact.message` the engine's `theirsFields` would build `to: []`, `cc: []`, `subject: ''` with `complete: true` from a delivered html/text and wipe a phone draft's recipients and subject. `draft-body.ts` therefore builds `message` (the existing `messageFacts`, `partial: true`) from the same `format=full` read: `from` (address lower-cased), `to`, `cc`, `bcc` (every Bcc header of the read, lower-cased, `[]` when none: the full read shows all headers, and the engine's closed-world proof `showsEveryField` needs it), `subject`, `snippet` (`snippet` joined the fields mask), and `attachments` (a draft holding an attachment of any kind lists it non-inline, so `hasAttachments` reads true). **html/text are delivered only together with `message`**: no From header, no thread id or no date means no body at all (snippet-only, as before). The same read still gives `internetId`. Tests: `draft-history.test.ts` "fact metadata" (including the engine's own `messageWrite` conversion) and `draft-echo.test.ts`, which commits the adapter's facts into the engine's world: a phone draft is inserted with its recipients and subject and complete, a clean draft replaced by a phone edit keeps the phone's recipients and subject, the copy of our own upload is our echo, the same copy with a Bcc is not.
- **Final review of #156: headers the fact cannot show whole mean no body.** `messageFacts` reads the first of each header, so a draft with two `To`, `Cc`, `From` or `Subject` headers (a raw API or IMAP-appended draft; Gmail's own apps never write one) would be installed as "complete" with the second header's recipients lost. `draft-body.ts` now delivers NO body (the `message` is still reported, snippet-only) when any of those four headers appears more than once; Bcc already reads every header. Same for a `Reply-To` header (conservative: the engine's `theirsFields` copies only `replyToMessageId` from the local base and has no Reply-To field, so a phone reply draft's Reply-To would be dropped silently; an `In-Reply-To` alone does not block the body). Engine side, not changed here: `theirsFields` has nowhere to put the copy's `inReplyTo` / `replyTo`; if it should, that is an eng-contract change. Also no body when the decoded subject or a display name holds U+FFFD (`facts.ts` decodes encoded words without `fatal`, which the ordinary sync path keeps; the check is local to `draft-body.ts`).
- **The tests go through the engine's own conversion.** `draft-echo.test.ts` no longer rebuilds the `remoteDraft` write by hand: the adapter's facts pass through the engine's `stepWrites` (`draftWrite`, the actor's conversion) into one `store.commit` of the engine's world; only the sync actor's loop is skipped (the engine world has no Gmail provider). The phone copy now differs from the local draft in recipients, cc, subject and body, so a kept local field fails. Finding recorded: a fact that carries one of OUR Message-IDs is ignored by the engine (`isEcho`) even when the copy added a Bcc, so the Bcc cases use another Message-ID: a clean draft takes the copy with its Bcc, a draft with unsent local edits raises the conflict. A Bcc added to a copy that keeps our Message-ID is still caught only by the provider's base check on our next upload (conflict, never an overwrite).

## 2026-10-30 — eng-contract/eng-time: PORT GAP 2, `DraftUpload.supersedes`, and the omitted-id wording

- **`DraftUpload.supersedes?: string[]` (additive, optional, v:1), create path only.** An unanswered create may have landed. When a newer revision replaces such an attempt, its Message-ID goes into `_sync.earlier` (new, at most the last 5, each once, only while the row has no `remote_draft_id`). The next create offers them as `supersedes`; the adapter looks for a draft carrying one of those Message-IDs and REPLACES it (the result names that `remoteDraftId`) instead of creating a second remote draft. An update (the row has a remote draft id) carries none. A retry of the same revision offers the same list again (its own Message-ID is never in it). `earlier` is cleared when a create is answered. An adapter that ignores the field keeps today's behaviour: a duplicate remote draft can remain, never a loss (history reports it as another device's draft). The adapter replaces a found copy ONLY when it is still a draft, its Message-ID is listed AND its canonical bytes equal an upload the adapter recorded under that Message-ID; otherwise it answers `conflict` naming the copy, writes nothing and never deletes (a Message-ID alone proves nothing: providers keep it across edits). The engine clears `earlier` when the create is answered in any way, `conflict` and `gone` included, or when a fact adopts the copy, so a kept draft cannot offer dead ids forever.
- **Omitted `expectedMessageId` wording (Providers' spec note on #156).** "Behave as before" was wrong: before was an unguarded delete. The docs on `DraftSyncPort.remove` and `ProviderAdapter.deleteDraft` now say the engine's call is the old two-argument one and cannot be guarded; an adapter may and should leave the copy alone rather than delete blind (Gmail does: the copy stays, history reports it as another device's draft, an orphan and never a loss). Either way the call resolves.
- **Decision on Providers' question (#158): no persistence channel on the draft port yet.** The Gmail adapter keeps the hashes of the bytes it uploaded in process memory, so after a restart a create that names `supersedes` finds the earlier copy but cannot prove the bytes are ours and answers `conflict`, never a replace. We accept that for now. It needs an unanswered create, then an edit, then a restart, and it ends in the owner's ordinary choice (keep mine, take theirs, keep both) with nothing written, overwritten or deleted. A channel is not free: the reply of an unanswered create never reaches the engine, so a result-carried receipt cannot work, and the engine cannot store the bytes (size) or hash them the way an adapter canonicalises them (boundaries and line endings are provider-specific). If live evidence says restarts matter, the additive shape is `DraftUpload.supersedes` entries gaining the engine's own hash of what that attempt sent, with the normalisation (each multipart boundary blanked, CRLF) defined in the port doc and implemented once in `mime.ts` (eng-time) for the adapters to compare; that is a contract change for Opus review, not part of #157. Until then the adapter keeps the in-memory record and the conflict fallback.
- **Review of #157 (VERDICT FIX, two HIGH).** (1) `earlier` is cleared when the create is answered in any way: `uploadConflict` (a conflict or a gone copy is an answer, kept drafts included) and when a fact adopts the copy (`adoptCopy`), not only in `synced`. Before, keep mine after a `gone` create offered the same dead ids on every later create, for ever. Tests: a create answered `gone` and one answered with a foreign conflict, then keep mine; adoption by a fact; the 5-id limit; a restart; an edit made while the first create runs whose reply is lost. (2) The `DraftUpload.supersedes` contract text no longer lets an adapter replace a copy just because its Message-ID is listed (providers keep it across edits): replace only when it is still a draft, the Message-ID is listed AND its canonical bytes equal an upload the adapter recorded under it; otherwise `conflict` naming the copy, nothing written, never a delete. The conflict doc covers this case. To make room in the engine bundle budget the field classification `DRAFT_CONTENT_FIELDS` is now three lists (`carried`, `shown`, `notShown`) with a compiler check that every `DraftFields` key is in exactly one (verified both ways), and the LOW comment moves in `draft-state.ts` are done.
- **Engine bundle budget: no headroom left (2026-10-30).** With #157 the minified neutral engine measured 399,951 bytes against the 400,000-byte budget (the test prints the figure); the first version measured 400,029 and failed the portability test, so redundant code was removed (the `supersedes` list is not re-filtered, because a revision's Message-ID is never in the list of earlier ones, and not de-duplicated, for the same reason). Any further engine code, including the BINDING items (3) and (4) of the #153 follow-ups, needs either a decision to raise the budget (spec 4.1 sets 400 KB) or an equal cut elsewhere. This is a NOTICE to the Integrator and the owner, not something a lane can settle alone.
- **Tests.** `drafts.supersedes.test.ts`, "gap 2" (5) and "earlier" (7, including the refused create below), 12 in all: the second create offers the first's Message-ID; a fake that honours it leaves one remote draft; one that ignores it leaves two (old behaviour); no `supersedes` on an update; a same-revision retry offers the same list; the list ends when a create is answered (synced, conflict, gone, adoption), survives a restart and keeps the newest 5. The fake has `state.honourSupersedes`.

## 2026-10-30 — prov-gmail: PORT GAP 2 closed on the Gmail side (`DraftUpload.supersedes`)

- **Rule.** A create whose own Message-ID finds nothing looks up each Message-ID in `supersedes` (at most the last five, never its own, only searchable ones): the drafts search per id, the newest drafts once, then all mail. A draft found is REPLACED (`drafts.update`; the result names its `remoteDraftId`, new message id) only when all hold: still a DRAFT (not SENT or TRASH) with the message id the search showed; its raw Message-ID header is one of `supersedes`; its thread is the upload's `remoteThreadId` when that is set; and its raw bytes canonicalise (`draft-compare.ts`, boundaries blanked, line endings normalised) to an upload this adapter recorded for the same local draft under that same Message-ID (`DraftAttempts`, SHA-256 of the canonical form, last five per draft, 100 drafts). The record is made BEFORE every create or replace request goes out, so a reply lost after the copy landed still leaves the proof.
- **Otherwise nothing is written.** A phone edit of the earlier copy (even one that keeps our Message-ID), a copy of another local draft, a Gmail rewrite, or no record: `{kind:'conflict'}` with `remoteDraftId`, `remoteMessageId`, `remoteInternetId` (as for gap 4). A SENT or TRASH message holding a listed Message-ID (no draft found): `{kind:'gone'}` as today, never recreated. A DRAFT-only hit the lookups missed: thrown as index lag. A draft with any other Message-ID is never read as ours. The adapter deletes nothing. Absent or empty `supersedes`: today's lookups, unchanged.
- **Deviation from the spec text: in-process memory, not `provider_memory`.** `saveDraft` returns only a `DraftSaveResult`; `provider_memory` is written through a sync step's result, and `ctx.memory` is read-only, so the adapter cannot persist the hashes from a save. The record lives in a per-adapter `WeakMap` keyed by the deps object. After a restart nothing is provable, so the create answers `conflict` (the owner's "keep mine" recovers) and never replaces. Safe, but an uncertain create that spans an app restart still ends in a conflict instead of a replace. Closing that needs a persistence channel on the draft port (an additive `memory` write on `DraftSaveResult`, or a store handle on `AdapterContext`), an eng-contract change; not requested here.
- **Costs.** Each listed id adds one search (5) and 20 per hit read; a replace adds one raw read (60) and an update (15). The replace has the same millisecond window as any update (Gmail has no etag), and the governor may re-send an `idempotentWrite` update after a transient failure.
- **Still true.** A second earlier copy beyond the one replaced (two earlier creates both landed) stays as a duplicate the owner removes; nothing deletes it. The caveat "an uncertain create followed by an edit can leave two remote copies" is removed for the normal case. TODO(live): Gmail rewriting headers or wrapping of an uploaded draft would show our own copy as a conflict (safe).
- **Tests.** `tests/prov-gmail/drafts-supersedes.test.ts` (14): replace with one draft total and a retry that writes nothing; found via the newest drafts when the search lags; two edits after a lost create and a lost replace; phone edit (conflict, nothing written); no record (restart); another local draft's id; a copy in another thread than the upload names (conflict); SENT and TRASH (gone); absent and empty (today's duplicate); a foreign draft untouched; the upload's own id in the list ignored; a lookup that finds nothing reads ten drafts once each (not twice, about 200 quota units saved per create); a draft already read by the create lookup that carries a superseded Message-ID is still replaced. `findEarlier` takes the create's `DraftReads` map, so a draft `findByInternetId` read is matched against `supersedes` from that read and never read again.

## 2026-10-28 — eng-html html-1: the mail sanitizer's parser and CSS tokenizer
- `packages/mail-html` parses mail with **htmlparser2 12.0.0** (with domhandler) and tokenizes CSS with **@csstools/css-tokenizer 4.0.2**. Measured by `node packages/mail-html/spike/measure.mjs` (esbuild, platform neutral, ESM): the pair bundles to 122,471 bytes, 68,648 minified, under the 150 KB target. Licences: MIT and BSD-2-Clause throughout (htmlparser2, domhandler, domelementtype, domutils, dom-serializer MIT/BSD-2; entities BSD-2; the tokenizer MIT, no dependencies). On the first 20 corpus messages both parsers rebuild the spec tree on 19 of 20 (the same message, 014, differs for parse5 too); the tokenizer round-trips all 20 losslessly.
- parse5 (spec-exact HTML tree construction) was the other candidate: 153,365 bytes minified on its own, over the target, and measured much slower on hostile and long mail (best of 5 on a loaded machine: 1 MB of words 1,095-1,739 ms against 24-133 ms; 2,000 nested divs 266-474 ms against 5 ms, growing quadratically, so 100,000 nested divs did not finish in 5 minutes). postcss and css-tree were ruled out: neither bundles for platform neutral (they import `path`, `fs`, `url` or `module`), and css-tree pulls in mdn-data (CC0, outside the MIT/BSD/Apache rule).
- Why a non-spec parser is safe here: the sanitizer never copies input markup. It writes a fresh document from an element and attribute allowlist with every text and attribute value escaped (`<` and `>` too), drops SVG and MathML whole, and emits no raw-text element except `<style>`, whose CSS is re-tokenized and has every `</` escaped. Where htmlparser2 and a browser would disagree about the input, the output still means one thing to the browser. parse5 stays a devDependency, as the spec-parser oracle the tests read the sanitizer's output with.

## 2026-10-31 eng-html html-1/html-2: review of the revived spike and corpus branches

- **One Sonnet review ("corpus gap" and "size and licence").** Fixed: ten class labels the message source did not support (e.g. `css-import` on a file with no `@import`, `ping-attr` on two files with no `ping=`) were removed from the manifest, a test now checks six evidence-bearing classes against the source, and two new messages (129 open-redirect image parameter names, 130 `ping` on links, areas and SVG) keep `open-redirect-image` and `ping-attr` above one handwritten message each. Licences are clean (htmlparser2 and `@csstools/css-tokenizer` MIT; domhandler, domutils, domelementtype, entities BSD-2-Clause); the size numbers reproduce exactly.
- **Known gaps, left on purpose.** (1) `loadCorpus` reads each message as a UTF-8 string, so byte-level encodings (invalid UTF-8, UTF-16, ISO-2022-JP, charset header versus `<meta charset>`) and MIME structure (multipart/related, duplicate `Content-ID`, quoted-printable) are not represented; they belong to the `Mail/frame` input path (html-4, with the store's raw bodies) and get a byte-level corpus there. (2) `mhtml:`, `jar:`, `wyciwyg:`, `resource:` and `ms-its:` schemes have no entry; the sanitizer's rule is an allowlist of schemes, so one scheme-allowlist test covers them when html-3 lands. (3) The leak harness (`leak.e2e.ts`, `canary.ts`) is the second half of html-2 and is not in this PR. (4) The html-1 entry's parse5 timings (1,095 to 1,739 ms) did not reproduce on this machine (55 ms for 1 MB of words); the conclusion that htmlparser2 is faster holds (7 ms), the magnitudes are inflated. (5) The root `node_modules` hoists htmlparser2 10.1.0; the 12.0.0 pin resolves inside `packages/mail-html` and the lockfile is correct, so nothing outside the package may import it.

## 2026-10-31 eng-contract: `ListHeaders.authenticated` gates List-Unsubscribe one-click (RFC 8058 section 4)

- **Contract (additive, inside `v:1`).** `ListHeaders.authenticated?: boolean` (`packages/engine/src/ports/provider.ts`). `true` only when the provider verified a signature on the message that covers both List-Unsubscribe and List-Unsubscribe-Post and aligns with the From domain: a passing DKIM signature whose `h=` lists both headers and whose `d=` aligns with From in DMARC's sense (RFC 7489 section 3.1.1, strict or relaxed), or an equivalent verdict (a passing ARC chain vouching for such a DKIM result, or the provider's own verdict that says exactly that). `false`: checked and it does not hold. Absent: unknown. Absent, `false` and anything that is not the boolean `true` are all "not authenticated". No wire method or generated schema carries `ListHeaders`, so nothing frozen changed shape.
- **Store.** Migration 0005 adds `messages.list_auth INTEGER CHECK (list_auth IN (0, 1))`; rows from before it are NULL (unknown, fails safe). `MessageWrite.listAuthenticated` is kept only as a strict boolean and only beside `listUnsubscribe` (`'true'`, `1`, an object, or a verdict without a stored List-Unsubscribe are NULL), and every message write replaces it, like `list_unsub`. `listHeaders()` returns `authenticated` for 0 and 1 and leaves it out for NULL, so `LaneMessage.list` and the unsubscribe target both see it.
- **Engine behaviour (`methods/unsubscribe.ts`).** The target is the thread's newest authenticated inbound message with List-Unsubscribe, else its newest one: a spoofed newer message cannot replace an authenticated target. For a target that is not authenticated, the headers reach `UnsubscribePort` without `listUnsubscribePost`, so the host plans no POST; the owner-visible ways stay (the mailto the owner reviews and sends in the composer, `compose`; the https page the owner opens, `openLink`, with its URL shown), and a header with neither is `unavailable` as before. No new outcome or method value. Consequence until Providers deliver the verdict: no one-click POST happens at all; one-click senders show as a page link.
- **What Providers must supply (`prov-gmail`, `apps/desktop/src/host/providers/google/facts.ts`, and every later adapter).** On every `MessageFacts` that carries List-Unsubscribe, set `list.authenticated` from a verdict the receiving server produced, never from text a sender could write: in Gmail, the `Authentication-Results` header added by `mx.google.com` (the topmost one whose authserv-id is Google's; ignore every other `Authentication-Results` and any `ARC-*`/`DKIM-Signature` content the engine would have to verify itself). `true` only when that header has `dkim=pass` with a `header.d` (or `header.i` domain) that aligns with the From domain and the passing signature's `h=` covers both `List-Unsubscribe` and `List-Unsubscribe-Post` (Gmail's results do not list `h=`, so the adapter must fetch the `DKIM-Signature` headers, pick the one the passing result names (its `header.b` prefix of `b=`, and its `d=`/`s=`) and read its `h=`; when that cannot be done, send `false` or leave it out). `dmarc=pass` alone or `spf=pass` alone is not enough. Ask for `Authentication-Results` and `DKIM-Signature` in the metadata header list. Send it on every fetch of the message (a write without it resets the stored value to unknown). Also in the executor (`google/unsubscribe.ts`, its `TODO(providers)`): plan one-click only when `headers.authenticated === true`, as defence in depth.



## 2026-10-31 providers (prov-gov): the data key and migrated credential files are written durably

- **What.** `engine-key.bin` and the `migrateCredentials` rewrite used a fixed `<file>.tmp` name and no fsync, so a power cut between write and rename could leave an empty key file (every saved credential unreadable) or a torn credential file. Both now go through `writeFileDurably` in the new `main/durable-file.ts`: unique `<file>.<uuid>.tmp`, create with `wx` and mode 0600, fsync the file, rename, fsync the folder (`EISDIR`/`EINVAL`/`EPERM`/`ENOTSUP` ignored as in `vault.ts`). A failed write removes its temporary copy and keeps the old file. `loadDataKey` removes stale `engine-key.bin.tmp` / `.<uuid>.tmp` copies at start.
- **Why a second copy of the primitives.** `vault.ts` keeps its own because it is bound to its `VaultFs` seam, timeouts and the in-flight temporary-name claims. Folding it onto `durable-file.ts` is a separate cleanup, not done here so the vault, which is tested heavily, is not touched by a key change.
- **Key presence check.** `loadDataKey` now treats only ENOENT as "no key" (was `existsSync`, which is false on any stat error such as EIO and would have created a second key over the real one) (Opus review finding).
- **Integrator Opus review of #176 (test gaps and one leak).** The `DurableFs` seam now has `open` (returns write/sync/close), `rename`, `syncDir`, `remove` and `stat`, so the tests assert open(0600) -> write -> sync -> close -> rename -> folder sync, that the temp is created with `wx` (an existing file is refused), that only EISDIR/EINVAL/EPERM/ENOTSUP are ignored on the folder flush, and that a failing `stat` (EIO, folder still writable) never reaches a write. Mutations run: no file sync, `w` for `wx`, ignoring EIO, any stat error = no key; each now fails a test. `loadDataKey` throws a plain `The credential key could not be checked` with the original as `cause`, because `engine-host.ts` puts `error.message` into `keyError` and an EACCES message carries the data path.
- **Not done (optional item).** Creating the first key with `link(temp, path)` so two racing first starts cannot overwrite each other: unreachable under the single-instance lock.
- **Left on purpose.** `migrateCredentials` does not clear stale `*.enc.json` temporaries: the vault does that at its own load, and doing it from migration could remove a live vault write. The first-key create is not no-replace (a second process racing between `existsSync` and rename could win); the app holds the single-instance lock, so it is not added.

## 2026-10-31 — eng-time: three LOW follow-ups of the draft engine (#169 review)

- **A discarded draft that holds a copy still deletes it when its running upload names a copy another row shows (`synced`).** The failed holder check used to end the hidden row at once, which left an adopted copy A on the provider for good (nobody would delete it later). Now a `removing` row with no copy of its own (`remote_draft_id === null`) still ends at once (the other row's copy is not ours), but one that holds a copy clears `uploading`, remembers the attempt (`wrote`, `prints`, never `earlier`) and sets `removeAt` to now, so the hidden row deletes A with its expected message id and then ends. The other row's copy is not touched. Tests: `drafts.lows.test.ts` (an update answered with a new remote draft id: the fake gained a `renameTo` step, the shape of JMAP/IMAP updates).
- **`sameCopy` also refuses a holder whose send is done.** It checked for a live send but not a `sent` one, so an early row the owner had already sent (its clean-up not yet committed) could be dropped as the same copy and the reply would bind the draft to what on some providers is now the sent message. `hasSend` (live or sent) is shared with `editedElsewhere`. Test holds the clean-up commit back with a fault so the row is `sent` and not yet hidden.
- **`repliedTo` is deterministic.** A Message-ID is not unique (the same mail can be stored in two threads), and `LIMIT 1` without `ORDER BY` picked whichever row the index returned. It now prefers the message in the draft's own thread (`remoteThreadId`), then the oldest stored (lowest `messages.id`).
- **A phone reply whose parent syncs later is re-linked (not deferred).** `refreshRemote` now runs `repliedTo` for a remote-origin draft that is still `mode: 'new'` with no reply target and, when the parent is stored, makes it a reply (`mode`, `replyToMessageId`; the revision moves by one like any refresh). The same version reported again used to return early and now does not while the parent is newly found. Limit: this runs when the provider reports that draft again (a history change or a full read); nothing re-checks a phone reply when only its parent arrives, so until the draft is next reported it stays plain. Trigger to revisit: a message ingest hook that looks for `mode: 'new'` remote drafts with that In-Reply-To, if reports turn out to be too rare in practice.
- **Not done (older, outside #169):** a row that held an adopted copy A and then binds to another copy B leaves A as a duplicate only the phone sees.
- **Review fix: a late parent adds only the reply target (`relink`).** When the same version of a phone reply is reported again and only its parent is newly found, `refreshRemote` writes just `mode` and `replyToMessageId` (revision +1, fingerprint recomputed) and keeps every other stored field; it no longer rebuilds the draft from a report that may carry no body (which cut the body to its snippet and marked the draft incomplete). A report that brings the text a snippet-only draft was waiting for still takes the full path. `repliedTo`'s last tiebreak is the earliest-dated stored message (`messages.id` orders by date), not "oldest stored". `holdersOf`, `sameCopy` and `hasSend` moved to `draft-proof.ts` (the line limit).
- **Review fix (Opus on #174): no late parent while a send of the draft is queued or done.** `refreshRemote` found a late parent (and called `relink`, which bumps the revision) even on a same-version re-report while a send was queued; `sentDrafts` hides a draft only when the sent revision equals the current one, so the draft stayed in Drafts and could be mailed twice (FR-7). `withParent` is now skipped while `hasSend(tx, row.id)` (live or sent): the reply target is added on a later report once no send exists. Test: queue a send of a phone reply, sync the parent, re-report the same version, run the send, and the draft leaves Drafts (it fails without the guard); a control shows the parent is still added when no send is queued. `withParent` has one caller, so the guard sits there.

## 2026-11-02 — eng-host: the composition root of the new engine (`apps/desktop/src/host/compose.ts`)

- **What it is.** `composeEngineHost(options)` builds the object graph the prov-gmail rigs wire by hand, with every environment decision injected (database opener and data folder, fetch, clock, log sink, power source, token vault, browser launcher, Google client inputs, banner function, planning environment, optional data key and extra ports). It is not called from `src/main`, which still runs the legacy engine. Graph: lock hook, log sink, `mail.db` through `openStore({extensions: laneExtensions()})`, the install-salted logger, the engine's own timer loop, `Governor` over a `GoogleTokenSource`, `ActorHost` with the Google factory (`createGoogleProvider` with `createGoogleHostConnect`), every stored account started, then `createEngine` with `accounts`, `drafts` (both the one `ActorHost`) and `deadlines`, then the loop, the host session (`Reminder` catch-up, the notification claim loop) and `watchPowerResume`.
- **Built beyond the rigs, on purpose.** (1) The real Google sign-in replaces the rigs' hand-written `connectGoogle`; `knownSubject` is `boundedKnownSubject` over `accounts.remote_subject` and `accountIsCommitted` reads `accounts.id`. (2) The timer loop is created by the host, not by the clock module, because `ActorHost` needs a `DeadlineRegistry` before the engine exists; the host starts it after the engine and stops it before the actors. (3) `ActorHost` commits through a late-bound store: until the engine exists it commits to the raw store, then to `engine.services.store`. Without this, an actor's sync commits never queue a `state` frame and no window hears of new mail (the rigs use the raw store because they have no windows; a test fails when the switch is removed). (4) Accounts are started before the engine is created, so the first due draft upload never meets `accountNotStarted` (permanent, parks the draft).
- **Close order.** One list of undo steps, run newest first and once (a second `close()` returns the first one's promise): power listeners, notification loop and reminder catch-up, the host session, the engine, the timer loop, the actors (`dispose` publishes the expired lease claim), the database, the log sink, the lock. A step that throws does not stop the rest. A failure during start unwinds the same way.
- **Lock.** `apps/desktop/src/host/lock.ts` is not on this base (host-2 is parked), so the root takes an optional `lock` hook (acquire first, release last) and does not invent a lock of its own. Wiring `lock.ts` is one line once it exists. Tests use a fake lock; a second composition is refused by it before anything is opened.
- **Left out on purpose.** `send`, `html`, `images`, `unsubscribe`, `calendar`, `rules` stay the engine's `unavailable` stand-ins unless the caller passes them in `ports`. The Gmail workflow channel (the lease) is on only when `keys` is given. The playground launcher, `--selftest` and the doorway are other host steps.
- **`plans/OWNERS.md`** gains `apps/desktop/src/host/compose.ts` under eng-host (the Integrator's file; flagged in the report).
- **Review fixes (Opus review of 92868a6b, VERDICT FIX).** (1) `close()` now calls `disposeConnectSessions(engine.services)` first, before the engine closes: no lane module does it yet (connect-sessions.ts says the engine's stop must). Probe test: `Account/connect`, wait for the browser, close; the loopback listener is gone, a late callback is refused, the vault holds nothing. (2) A reminder banner that rejects is caught and logged as a fixed `rpc.failed` line (no error text) instead of becoming an unhandled rejection in main. (3) Default `HostEnv` is now `windowVisible: false` (no window, unfocused, on mains), so a host with no window layer does not poll every 60 s; the tests pass their own env. (4) Tests added: restart with a draft already due uploads it, the workflow channel and lease with a data key (construction, lease round, close, no timer left armed), the rejecting banner, the waiting sign-in at close. Mutation results: removing the dispose, the catch, or `startAccounts` each fails exactly its test. Moving `startAccounts` to just after `loop.start()` is NOT caught and cannot be: `ActorHost.start` has no await before it registers the actor, so with one account the call completes in the same synchronous run as `loop.start()`, before the loop can fire anything. The order is kept as a rule (accounts, then engine, then loop) and noted in the code. Seen and left: a token refresh still in flight when `close()` runs (a sync not yet finished) keeps the http client's 30 s timeout armed until it settles; with the baseline done no timer is left.

## 2026-10-31 eng-host host-2: resident mode, menu bar, lock, wake, parked

- **Base and merge.** `rv/eng-host/resident-mode-2` is `origin/revamp` with the unreviewed WIP branch (`origin/rv/eng-host/resident-mode`, last 2a26befe) merged in. Every conflicting file kept host-1's merged code (`windows.ts`, `native-methods.ts`, `test-mode.ts`, `host/lifecycle.ts`, `windows.test.ts`, `power.test.ts`); only the host-2 parts of the WIP were taken, after reading each line. The WIP's older copies of the host-1 pieces (no `hasPainted`, the first `lockNavigation`, its own `silenceSystem`) were dropped.
- **Login launch is windowless with or without accounts.** The spec says `wasOpenedAtLogin` starts windowless. host-1's `startupWindow` only did that with no accounts, so it now returns `none` for every login launch (test updated), and `startWindows` in `windows.ts` returns `none` first, which also covers the default where the welcome window is off. The WIP's separate `startsWindowless` was dropped as a duplicate.
- **Resident mode.** `main/lifecycle.ts` switches the activation policy from the window count (`activationPolicy`: regular with a window, accessory with none, always accessory in test mode) and does not switch while quitting. macOS already keeps main alive when the last window closes (`window-all-closed` only quits elsewhere). A login launch applies accessory at once. The menu-bar item's Open Aplos focuses or creates the window through `focusMain`.
- **Menu-bar item.** `host/menu-bar.ts` is the entry model (status line, Open Aplos, Pause sync for 1 hour or Resume sync, Quit Aplos completely) and `main/menu-bar.ts` is the `Tray`. The glyph is the letter `A` as the tray title with a 1x1 transparent image; a test checks no sparkle, star or wand code point appears anywhere in the item. Not created in test mode. The WIP's extra `click` handler that popped the menu up a second time was removed (macOS opens the context menu itself).
- **Pause sync is wake-only today.** The legacy engine has no pause and runs its own 60 s poll in the utility process, which is outside this lane. "Pause" stops the host forwarding wakes (`sync {auto: true}`) for an hour and the status line says "Paused until HH:MM"; the legacy poll keeps going. The new engine's governor makes it a real pause.
- **Quit.** Every quit (⌘Q, the menu-bar item, the Dock menu) goes through `before-quit`, then `quitWarning(pendingWork(latestState))` (the `Outbox/pending` shape, computed from today's drafts and thread reminders), then the window close, then a 3 s `drain` of the engine close, then the lock release. Skipped for an update install, "Try again", and a system shutdown. In test mode the warning text goes to stderr and the quit continues. A send with an attempt still unconfirmed counts as `uncertain`, never also as scheduled.
- **Wake.** `watchPowerResume(power, engine, clock?)` keeps its host-1 shape; with a clock it adds a 1 s tick that wakes when wall time moved over 30 s between ticks, and one dedupe window (5 s) so `resume`, `unlock-screen` and the fallback make one `wake()`. The plan's check says a 31 s jump triggers one `engine.wake()`; the lane card's "zero or two" reads as a typo, and one is what is tested. Today `wake` is `sync {auto: true}`, skipped while paused or parked.
- **Lock.** `run/aplos.lock` holds `{pid, startedAt}`, created with `wx`. A dead pid, garbage or an empty file is reclaimed by renaming it aside. Review fix to the WIP: a launch that moved the file aside re-reads what it moved, and if that is a different launch's fresh lock (it reclaimed and took the lock between our read and our rename), puts it back and refuses, instead of silently stealing it. A small window remains in which a third launch could create the file between the rename and the put-back; Electron's single-instance lock keeps two app launches apart anyway, so this guards a second engine host. Pid reuse by an unrelated live process makes a stale lock look live (the error says to quit the other copy); the start time is stored but not compared, because there is no portable way to read another process's start time without a native call.
- **Parked.** `createProcessState` is `starting | ready | parked`; the third engine exit within 60 s parks and shows "Export diagnostics / Try again" (diagnostics reveals the log folder until E7's report exists; Try again relaunches). The exit signal is `app.on('child-process-gone')` filtered to the engine's `serviceName`, because `engine-host.ts` is another lane's file. That file keeps its own restart loop (a dialog after the fourth restart); both run until the new engine's host replaces it. `ready` is set once at boot; a restarted engine is not reported back as ready, because that needs a hook in `engine-host.ts`.
- **Crash and watchdogs.** `crashReporter.start({uploadToServer: false})` runs after test mode sets `userData`. The event-loop monitor (p99 over 500 ms, one numbers-only line) is started. The actor-heartbeat supervisor (`host/crash.ts`, restart a silent actor after 2 minutes) is built and tested but not started: the legacy engine has no actors, so there is nothing to supervise until the new engine's host passes `AccountActorHost.lastHeartbeat`.
- **Left out on purpose.** (1) The `--selftest` launcher: the WIP stub printed `{ok:false}` and exited, which could be mistaken for a result; host-3's function is in place and the launcher belongs with the composition root that forks the new engine. (2) Wiring `engine.wake()`, `Outbox/pending` and the heartbeat to the new engine (composition root, out of scope). (3) A packaged check with fuses: not possible headlessly here.
- **`verify-resident.mjs`.** Runs a hidden built app (test mode, fresh temp data dir, Playwright `_electron`, never `open`): closes the only window, checks main is alive and the Dock icon hidden, waits for a reminder due in 60 s to notify with no window, checks `run/aplos.lock` is held, quits gracefully and checks the lock is released, and removes its temp directory on every exit path. Note test mode is always accessory, so "dock hidden" there proves nothing about the policy switch; that is an owner hand-check.
- **Tests.** `lifecycle` (park guard, pending work, quit warning, policy, drain), `lock` (reclaim, live refusal, garbage, race, the put-back, kill at each of four startup steps, second engine refused then admitted), `power` (jump, dedupe either order, timer cancelled), `menu-bar`, `crash`. Mutation checks were run for lock reclaim, the 3-exits guard and the wake dedupe: each turned the suite red and was restored.
- **Review fixes (Opus, VERDICT FIX), same day.** (1) HIGH: `crashReporter.start()` ran before `app.setName`, and starting the reporter fixes `userData` from the app name at that moment. A hidden Electron probe (package name `@aplos/desktop`, a throwaway product name) showed start-then-name gives `~/Library/Application Support/@aplos/desktop` and name-then-start gives `.../<product name>`; the probe's folders were removed afterwards. `startCrashReporting(app, reporter, name)` in `host/crash.ts` now names the app first and is the only place either call is made (a test fakes both and checks the order, another reads `main/lifecycle.ts` to keep the direct calls out). (2) MEDIUM: the lock now stores `boot` (`Date.now() - os.uptime()*1000`); a lock whose boot differs by more than 60 s is stale even if its pid is alive again (a reboot reuses pids, and EPERM counts as alive). The tolerance covers clock adjustments within a boot; a clock step over 60 s during a session would make a live lock look stale, which is why the tolerance is not tighter. A lock with no boot field (an earlier build) falls back to the pid check. (3) LOW: `nodeLockFs.create` writes a private temp file and `linkSync`s it into place, so a launch never reads a half-written lock. A mutation back to a plain exclusive write is not detectable from one process (the result is the same on a quiet disk), so only the all-or-nothing, EEXIST and no-leftover-temp behaviour is tested. (4) LOW: wake dedupe only applies when the clock has not moved backwards. (5) LOW: the ⌘Q question is a `createQuitGate` in `host/lifecycle.ts`; a confirm that throws or rejects counts as yes and never leaves the gate stuck. (7) LOW: the kill-at-startup test no longer claims a rollback its fake database did itself; it now checks only what the real code decides (the lock is reclaimed, the data written before the kill is still there, the new launch opens it).
- **Re-check items (same day).** The temp-file write in `nodeLockFs.create` is now inside the `try`, so a write that fails half way (a full disk) removes its temp file (a test injects a failing write; moving it out of the `try` turns that test red). The source-text guard also checks `startCrashReporting` comes before `requestSingleInstanceLock` in `main/lifecycle.ts`. A `begin` that throws in `createQuitGate` is caught, so there is no unhandled rejection and the gate asks again.
- **Known gap: boot identity is a time check, not an id.** The lock's boot time comes from `Date.now() - os.uptime()`, so a wall-clock step of more than 60 s during a session could make a live lock look stale. Electron's single-instance lock covers normal launches, but not two differently named instances sharing one `APLOS_DATA_DIR`, nor the future forked engine. Trigger: when the forked engine is wired, compare `/usr/sbin/sysctl -n kern.bootsessionuuid` (exact boot identity) first and keep the time check as the fallback. Not implemented now.

## 2026-10-31 eng-html html-3: the mail sanitizer (`packages/mail-html`, `MailHtml` port)

- **Design.** `mailHtml: MailHtml` (`version: 'mail-html/1'`, pure, synchronous, no DOM or Node imports; a test reads every `src/` import) in `packages/mail-html/src`: `parse.ts` builds a tree over htmlparser2 12's exported `Tokenizer`; `sanitize.ts` walks it iteratively (a stack of frames, depth cap 256) and writes a fresh document from the allowlist in `policy.ts`, every text and attribute value escaped (`escape.ts`); `css.ts` re-tokenizes `<style>` and `style=""` with `@csstools/css-tokenizer` and writes them back token by token. Nothing is copied from the input as markup, so the parser's view of hostile input only affects which text survives, never what the output means; the browser's reading of the output is checked by a parse5 oracle (`tests/oracle.ts`). The earlier unreviewed branch (`rv/eng-html/sanitizer`, `wip-pause-1028`) was read line by line; its allowlist, CSS writer and image book were kept and corrected, the rest rewritten.
- **Why not htmlparser2's `Parser`.** Measured: 100,000 nested `<div>`s take 4.4 s to parse (8.8 s to render with the earlier branch), because its open-element stack uses `unshift`/`shift`/`indexOf` and every unmatched end tag scans it. The tree builder keeps htmlparser2's rules (implied end tags, void elements, foreign content, the ignored second `<form>`) on a push/pop stack with a count per open name, so an unmatched end tag is O(1). The walker applies the same implied end tags to what it writes, which makes the output a fixpoint (`sanitize(sanitize(x)) === sanitize(x)`, checked for every corpus message). `domhandler` stays in `package.json` only as htmlparser2's own dependency.
- **Rules.** Elements: an allowlist (text formatting, tables, lists, `img`, `a`, `details`), everything else unwrapped (text kept) or dropped with its content (`script`, `meta`, `link`, `base`, form fields, `iframe`, `frame`, `object`, `embed`, `svg`, `math`, `template`, `noscript`, media). `<noframes>` fallback text is re-parsed once and sanitized, since the frames it stands for are dropped. Attributes: an allowlist; `href` keeps http, https, mailto and tel (WHATWG-parsed, written back normalised) with `target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"`, and fragments; `src`, `background` and CSS URLs become `cid/<n>`, `img/<n>` (images on) or nothing, plus raster `data:` in `<img src>` only (the CSP allows `img-src data:`). Ids, anchor names, `headers`, ARIA id references and `#id` selectors move into an `m-` namespace (DOM clobbering: a hyphenated name is no JavaScript identifier). CSS: at-rules other than `@media`, `@supports`, `@keyframes`, `@layer` and `@container` go with their blocks; strings inside `image-set()` and `image()` are URLs; address-like strings elsewhere are emptied; `attr()` and `expression()` go, and so do `var()` and `env()` inside image functions; comments become `/**/`; `</` never appears outside a CSS string. Trackers: remote images that are hidden (attribute, inline style or a hidden ancestor), 1 px or less in a dimension, at most 2x2, or unsized with a beacon-like path, removed and counted. Quotes: Gmail, Yahoo, Proton, Apple (`blockquote type=cite`), Thunderbird and Outlook markers and "Original/Forwarded message" lines, found with a bounded look-ahead (the earlier branch's look-ahead was quadratic on deep mail). The document declares `<meta charset="utf-8">` within its first 1,024 bytes, and ESC and NUL become U+FFFD everywhere.
- **Limits.** 512 KiB of markup (style text not counted), 20,000 elements, 256 KiB of `<style>` text per message (a block over the budget is dropped; one that alone is over it is cut before tokenizing, so a 5 MB stylesheet costs 0.8 ms and the text after it survives), and `style` attributes over 32 KiB are dropped. A clipped message ends with "This message is very long, so Aplos shows only its beginning." Gmail clips at about 100 KB; 20,000 elements is about four times a large newsletter.
- **Measured** on this Mac with a load average of 9 to 16 on 8 cores from other agents, best of 5 runs per message after a warm-up: the 186-message corpus (132 handwritten, 54 synthetic) renders in a **median 0.11 ms and p99 29.3 ms** (slowest: g32 mixed line endings 30.5 ms, g13 5 MB tag line 29.3 ms, g04 100,000 nested fonts 23.1 ms). 27 hostile shapes (1 MB lines, 100,000-deep nesting, whitespace runs in text, attributes and styles, entity floods, 100,000 `url(` and `image-set(` openers, 20,000 distinct styles, 100,000 attributes, a 5 MB stylesheet) take 0.1 to 65 ms each. Bundle (esbuild, platform neutral, ESM, minified): **79,836 bytes**, 32,039 gzipped. Tests: 68 in `packages/mail-html/tests` and 18 in `apps/desktop/tests/eng-html/corpus.test.ts`, all passing. Unloaded numbers were not measured (no quiet machine).
- **Corpus classes and the tests that prove them.** `S` = `tests/sanitize.test.ts`, `C` = `tests/css.test.ts`, `P` = `tests/perf.test.ts`; "corpus-off/on" = S "renders every message with images off/on: nothing can run, load or clobber" (the parse5 oracle over every message); "expect" = S "meets every expectation in the manifest".

| Class | Proven by | Not proven here, and why |
|---|---|---|
| script-inline, event-handler, base-tag, meta-refresh, meta-misc, iframe-nested, object-embed, audio-video, svg-script, svg-foreignobject, svg-other | S "removes scripts, handlers and every element that can run or load something", S "keeps attributes only from the allowlist and never with a namespace prefix", corpus-off/on | |
| javascript-url, link-scheme, data-url | S "opens web, mail and phone links outside the frame and strips every other scheme", S "maps inline parts to cid/<n>; unknown cids, non-raster data and CSS data URLs go", C "drops every remote or unknown url() when images are blocked…" | |
| css-url, css-legacy | C "drops every remote or unknown url()…", C "rewrites allowed remote images to img/<n> and known cids to cid/<n>", corpus-off/on | the CSS half of the oracle uses the sanitizer's tokenizer |
| css-import, css-font-face, remote-font | C "drops @import, @font-face and every at-rule outside the layout set, in any spelling", C "keeps layout at-rules and sanitizes inside them…" | |
| css-image-set | C "treats strings in image-set() as URLs and empties address-like strings elsewhere", corpus 132 | |
| css-exfil | corpus-off (with images off nothing loads) | with images on, selectors (`[href^=…]`, `:hover`, `:has(mark)`, `details[open]`) choose which listed `img/<n>` loads (gap 1) |
| css-layout-abuse | expect (text kept) | overlays inside the frame are allowed; not a sanitizer property |
| form-autosubmit | S "unwraps forms: their text stays, fields and actions go", corpus-off | |
| mxss | S "turns mXSS shapes into inert output, as parse5 reads it", S "stops a </style> in CSS from closing the style element", the S and C idempotence tests, corpus 131 | Chromium never read the output (parse5 stands in) |
| dom-clobbering | S "moves ids, anchor names and id references into the m- namespace (DOM clobbering)", the oracle's `m-` check in corpus-off/on | the html-4 reporter must still not read globals by bare name |
| deep-nesting, huge-line, resource-exhaustion | S "flattens 100,000 nested elements past the depth cap…", S "cuts very long HTML at the limit…", P corpus timing and the 27 shapes | |
| malformed-encoding | S "escapes control characters, lone surrogates and NUL…", S "declares UTF-8 within the first 1,024 bytes and lets no ISO-2022-JP shift through", corpus 131 | byte-level encodings and MIME belong to html-4 (gap recorded by html-1/2) |
| tracking-pixel | S "removes and counts tracking pixels, never listing them for the proxy", expect (`trackersRemovedMin`) | pixels sized by `<style>` classes, placed off screen or set as backgrounds (gap 2) |
| remote-image, benign-newsletter | S "keeps blocked remote images as quiet sized placeholders…", S "rewrites allowed remote images, backgrounds and CSS images to img/<n>…", expect (`remoteImages` exact, kept text) | |
| cid-inline, relative-url | S "maps inline parts to cid/<n>…", the oracle's index bounds, corpus 084, 102, 104, 126, 132 | that the handler answers 404 for paths it did not issue (html-4) |
| quoted-history, find-text, plain-text | S "wraps quoted history in a closed <details>…", S "marks find matches in text…", S "returns plain-text mail as text…", expect (`quotedCountMin`, `marksMin`, `bodyKind`) | |
| hidden-content, unicode-bidi | expect (kept as text) | bidi spoofing in display is unchanged; hidden text is the AI features' concern |
| ping-attr, popup-navigation | S links test (`ping` and `target=_top` dropped), the oracle's target/rel check | the window-open and navigation handlers (html-5) |
| open-redirect-image, ssrf-image | expect (they appear only as `img/<n>`) | refusing them is the proxy's (html-6 `ssrf.test.ts`) |
| storage-cookie, postmessage-spoof, link-prefetch | corpus-off/on (no script, form, frame or link element survives) | needs the leak harness (html-2 second half, not built here): Chromium's own anchor DNS prefetch or hover preconnect, and the parent's message checks |

- **Bypass review** (time-boxed, given only the allowlist and the ARCH 10 headers). Tried and blocked: `noscript`, `template` and declarative-shadow-DOM mXSS; SVG and MathML namespace confusion (`<svg><style>`, `mglyph`, `</p>` breakouts); `</style>` in attributes, blocks, CSS strings and as `\3c /style`; CSS escapes `\75rl(`, `u\72l(`, `\000075rl(`, `@\69mport`; comments, CDATA and processing instructions; attribute entities (`&#106;`, `&Tab;`, `&colon;`, zero-padded references); `srcset` and `<picture><source>`; `<a ping>`; `background=` on tables and on a `div`; `<img src=data:>` with SVG and HTML; `<svg><use href>`; `<input type=image>` and `formaction`; `xlink:href` and MathML `href`; Unicode case folding (`ſ`, `İ`, the Kelvin sign); NUL in tag names, schemes and properties; `meta` refresh and CSP; `base`, `link` prefetch, preconnect and stylesheet; speculation rules; auto-submitting forms; `iframe srcdoc`, `frame`, `object`, `embed`, `applet`, `portal`, `fencedframe`, `permission`; `details ontoggle`, popover, `dialog`; `cid:` traversal and quote injection; relative `img/0`, `cid/0` and `aplos-mail:` links; `target`/`rel` overrides and `<base target>`; `@font-face`, `@import`, `@namespace`, `@page`, `@property`, unknown at-rules and nested CSS; clobbering `body`, `fonts`, `addEventListener`, `ready`, `parent`. **Two successes, both fixed and added to the corpus:** (131) ESC survived in text and attributes and the document declared no charset, so a frame sniffed as ISO-2022-JP could swallow a closing quote and read the next attribute value as attributes (`onerror`); ESC now becomes U+FFFD, `<meta charset="utf-8">` comes first, and `lang`/`dir` on the root are validated so it stays within 1,024 bytes. (132) `image-set(var(--x))` with `--x:"img/0"` put a string the sanitizer never classified in URL position (a same-origin `img/<n>` or `cid/<n>` request; outbound only through the proxy); `var()`, `env()` and `attr()` are now replaced inside image functions. Not fixed: the conditional-load channel with images on (gap 1). `tel:javascript:alert(1)` stays a `tel:` link (the system phone handler gets it), accepted.
- **Known gaps.** (1) With images on, CSS selectors decide which listed images load, so a sender can learn hover, quote-open and find-match state; the fix belongs to the pipeline or proxy (fetch every listed `img/<n>` at render, before the frame asks). (2) Tracker detection reads attributes and inline styles only. (3) A find match split across elements is not marked. (4) `[id="x"]` attribute selectors in mail CSS no longer match the moved ids. (5) The CSS half of the oracle uses the same tokenizer as the sanitizer; the HTML half (parse5) is independent.
