(6) Nothing here ran in Chromium; the leak harness is the end-to-end check. **For html-4:** serve `text/html; charset=utf-8`, answer 404 for every path not issued for the token (and for every `img/<n>` while images are blocked), and keep the reporter free of bare global reads.

## 2026-10-31 eng-html html-3: fixes from the Opus security review (VERDICT FIX)

- **MEDIUM, fixed: nested `<noframes>` re-parsing was quadratic.** The fallback text was re-parsed with fresh limits after one regex pass removed nested `<noframes>` tags, and `<nofr<noframes>ames>` turns back into `<noframes>` when its inner tag is removed, so every level re-read everything below it. The reviewer's shape (`<noframes>` + O(0) + … + O(239) + 250,000 filler characters, O(0) = `<noframes>`, O(k) = `<nofr` + O(k-1) + `ames>`, 0.51 MB) took 464 ms in the perf test on the old code (565 ms in a single run here, 3.2 s in the review) and now takes 6.4 ms (load average about 22 on 8 cores). The cleanup regex is gone: a fallback is re-parsed only at the top level (a `fallback` flag in the walk context), a `<noframes>` inside re-parsed text is dropped, and all re-parses share one element budget (`LIMITS.maxElements`), so each input character is parsed at most twice. Corpus 133 (a 110-level version, 82 KB) and the P test "finishes 240 levels of a noframes fallback that rebuilds itself" (fails on the old `sanitize.ts`, checked by swapping it back) cover it.
- **LOW, fixed: mail could pose as or hide Aplos's own elements.** A mail `class` name starting with `aplos-` (any case, as quirks-mode matching is) becomes `m-aplos-…`, in element and `<body>` attributes and in `.aplos-…` class selectors in its CSS; a mail `<details class="aplos-quote">` no longer counts as a quote (only the sanitizer's own output, read back in the idempotence check, keeps them). Test: S "keeps the mail from posing as, or hiding, what Aplos adds (aplos- classes)". Left: element and structural selectors (`details{display:none}`, `p:last-child`, `[class*=aplos]`) can still hide the wrappers and the clipped note; mail CSS is allowed, so this is cosmetic (no load, no script), recorded rather than fought.
- **LOW, fixed: timing tests that flaked under load.** `packages/mail-html/tests/perf.test.ts` takes the best of 7 runs (15 more for messages over 10 ms); when the p99 is over 40 ms it is judged against an ordinary 100 KB newsletter timed in the same run (must be under 12 of them; measured 7.0 to 7.4 under load) with a 250 ms ceiling. Mutation check: making the walk's `depth()` cost O(frames) gave p99 105.9 ms = 23.4 newsletters, which fails. `packages/engine/tests/outbox/drafts.inline.test.ts` (eng-contract's file, same thread): the 30,000-character gate keeps a 500 ms line; the 1 MB size is judged by its ratio to a 50,000-character run of the same shape (linear about 20x, measured 21 to 40x, limit 80x; quadratic 400x), and the 50k run fails fast first. Mutation check: the old quadratic pattern (`\s*["']?\s*cid:`) fails both tests in 13 s instead of hanging.
- **LOW, recorded: the CSS half of the oracle shares the sanitizer's tokenizer** (`@csstools/css-tokenizer`), so a tokenizer bug would hide from both. Trigger to close it: when the leak harness (html-2 second half) gives us a headless Chromium, add an offline check that loads every sanitized corpus document with images off and on and asserts zero network requests and no parsed `url()` outside `cid/` and `img/` from Chromium's own CSSOM. Not built now.

## 2026-10-31 eng-contract: independent Opus security review of the authenticated List-Unsubscribe fact (VERDICT MERGE)

(Date corrected from 2026-11-02, which was written in error; the machine date is 2026-10-31.)

- **Open, LOW, for providers' side.** (1) The host executor (`google/unsubscribe.ts`, `planUnsubscribe`) still plans a POST for any headers carrying `List-Unsubscribe-Post`; the engine-side strip (`gated()` in `methods/unsubscribe.ts`) is the only gate today. Providers: add `headers.authenticated === true &&` to the one-click condition as defence in depth (about 16 host test call sites). (2) "Authenticated" means aligned with that message's own From, so an attacker who DKIM-signs their own domain and sets References to a real newsletter's Message-ID can become the newest authenticated message in the thread and be chosen as the target. The impact is the same as before the change (the POST reveals the IP address and that the inbox reads mail). Cheap hardening when needed: if a thread's authenticated List-Unsubscribe messages come from more than one From domain, strip the POST. Trigger: the providers lane starts supplying the verdict (until then no message is authenticated, so no POST is ever planned).
- **Behaviour notes.** While no provider supplies the verdict there is no one-click anywhere and demo one-click senders show as page links; an older authenticated message beats newer ones, so its tokenized URL may have expired; the open-page fallback may be the one-click endpoint opened with GET in the owner's browser, so the new renderer should keep the confirm dialog that shows the host.

## 2026-10-31 providers (prov-gmail): the Gmail verdict for ListHeaders.authenticated

- **Where.** `apps/desktop/src/host/providers/google/list-auth.ts` (`listAuthenticated(headers, from)`, pure, no I/O or clock), called from `messageFacts` in `facts.ts`; `list.authenticated` is set only when the message has a List-Unsubscribe header, so the engine's `messageWrite` stores it beside the header on every fetch. `METADATA_HEADERS` now asks for `Authentication-Results` and `DKIM-Signature`.
- **Rules (all must hold for `true`).** (a) Only the topmost `Authentication-Results` (payload headers are in message order), and only when its authserv-id is exactly `mx.google.com` (case-insensitive, optional numeric version, comments ignored); a foreign topmost one is `false`. Every lower A-R is ignored because a sender can write one (Gmail's own is added on top at receipt), and `ARC-*` headers are ignored entirely: an ARC chain would have to be verified by us, and its first set was written by a hop we cannot trust without that. (b) The A-R is parsed by one bounded scan (no backtracking regex): at most 4,096 characters, 512 tokens, 20 results and comment depth 8, else `false`; comments are stripped, quoted values unquoted, a result that repeats a property is dropped. (c) A `dkim=pass` whose signing domain (`header.d`, else the domain of `header.i`) aligns with the From domain; exactly one From header with one address (the same `parseAddresses` result the owner is shown). (d) Gmail's results do not list `h=`, so the passing result is matched to its `DKIM-Signature` by `d=`, `s=` (when `header.s` is given) and `header.b` (at least 6 characters, case-sensitive) as a prefix of `b=` without whitespace; zero or several matches (a decoy can copy `d=`, `s=` and the prefix) do not count, and any signature over 8,192 characters, with a repeated tag, or more than 8 signatures makes the message `false` (an unreadable one could be the real match). Tag names are case-sensitive (RFC 6376), so `H=` is not `h=`. The signature's `h=` must list `from`, `list-unsubscribe` and `list-unsubscribe-post`. (e) Exactly one List-Unsubscribe and exactly one List-Unsubscribe-Post (an `h=` entry covers one instance); a missing Post is `false`. (f) `spf=pass` or `dmarc=pass` alone is never enough. Absent (unknown) only when there is no A-R at all or no headers; otherwise `false`.
- **Alignment without a public-suffix list.** Equal, or From ends with `.` + d, with d holding at least two non-empty labels. This rejects a bare `d=com` and never accepts d below From (DMARC relaxed would accept `d=news.example.com` for From `example.com`; we report `false`, failing safe). It would accept `d=co.uk` for any `*.co.uk` From, but nobody can produce a passing signature for a public suffix itself, so the gap is theoretical. A signature whose `i=` names a subdomain of `d=` and that Gmail reports only by `header.i` finds no match and is `false` (fails safe).
- **Executor.** `planUnsubscribe` plans `oneClick` only when `headers.authenticated === true` (strict boolean) and List-Unsubscribe-Post is the RFC 8058 value; the `TODO(providers)` is gone. The host tests' `headers()` fixture now carries `authenticated: true`; a new test checks `false`, absent, `'true'` and `1` fall back to mailto or the page with no lookup or request.
- **Mutation checks** (each turned `list-auth.test.ts` or `unsubscribe.test.ts` red, then restored): topmost A-R (`findLast`), authserv-id check removed, alignment always true, dotless d allowed, From count check removed, ambiguity (`matches[0]`), min prefix removed, `h=` without `list-unsubscribe-post`, `h=` without `from`, Post count (allow absent, and removed), List-Unsubscribe count removed, executor gate removed, executor gate as `Boolean(...)`.
- **Left.** (1) The ARC chain is not used, so mailing-list mail that breaks the author's DKIM (subject tags, footers) shows no one-click, only the mailto or page. (2) A message that reaches the mailbox without Gmail adding its own A-R (`messages.insert`/`import`, some internal deliveries) would make a sender-written `mx.google.com` A-R the topmost one; the signature lookup still applies but the attacker can write that too. Impact as recorded by the review (a POST to a sender's URL reveals the IP and that mail is read); worth closing if such messages are found in practice (for example by requiring Gmail's `Received` hop directly above). (3) The multi-From-domain thread hardening (strip the POST when a thread's authenticated List-Unsubscribe messages come from more than one From domain) belongs to the engine's `gated()`/target choice and is requested from eng-contract separately.

## 2026-10-31 eng-contract: one-click is stripped when a thread's authenticated List-Unsubscribe messages span several From domains (closes the LOW of the 2026-11-02 review, item 2)

- **Rule (`methods/unsubscribe.ts`, `mixedDomains` / `gated`).** When the target is authenticated, the engine also reads the From address of the target itself and of the thread's newest `DOMAIN_WINDOW` (100) inbound, non-draft messages, **whether they carry List-Unsubscribe or not and whether authenticated or not** (the first version compared 50 List-Unsubscribe messages; see the #183 review bullet below) (review fix: with only authenticated messages compared, a lone authenticated attacker message over unauthenticated real ones was the target, was alone in the set and kept the POST). The target choice stays authenticated-first. If they do not all share one From domain, the target's `listUnsubscribePost` is stripped before the headers reach `UnsubscribePort`, exactly as for a non-authenticated target: no POST is planned, the mailto the owner reviews and the page the owner opens stay offered, no new result kind or method value. A target that is not authenticated was already stripped (it is only chosen when the thread has no authenticated message), so the check only runs for an authenticated target. The change can only add strips.
- **Where the domain comes from.** Stored fields only: `messages.from_addr` (no new column, no migration). The domain is the text after the last `@`, trimmed and lower-cased (`fromDomain`), compared as an exact string. No public-suffix list and no "registrable domain" guess: `news.example.com` and `mail.example.com` count as two domains. That is the safe side (a sender that mails from two subdomains gets the page link instead of one-click), and a list we invented would be wrong in both directions.
- **Missing or unparseable From.** A null From, an address with no `@`, an empty domain, or a domain containing a character outside printable ASCII (whitespace, controls, anything non-ASCII; checked before lower-casing, which would fold the Kelvin sign U+212A into an ASCII `k`), `<`, `>`, `,` or another `@` is an unknown domain, and an unknown domain is distinct from every domain and from every other unknown: any such message among the compared ones strips the POST, even if it is the only one. (A provider should not call such a message authenticated, since the verdict means aligned with From; this covers a provider that does.) Internationalised domains therefore strip until a provider supplies a punycode form; the safe side.
- **Contract on `ListHeaders.authenticated` (comment in `ports/provider.ts`).** `true` is allowed only when the From header holds exactly one mailbox and that mailbox's domain is the one the DKIM alignment was computed on: the engine compares domains from the stored first From address, which must be the very address the verdict covers. A provider that cannot guarantee this (several From mailboxes, an unparseable From) must leave it absent.
- **Window.** The newest 100 (`DOMAIN_WINDOW`, Gmail's cap on messages in a thread; first written as 50), ordered by `internal_date DESC, id DESC`, one query over the thread's own rows (index `messages_thread`) with `LIMIT`; it runs only for an authenticated target. Known limit: an attacker who adds more than 100 newer messages to the thread pushes the real sender out of the window and is then alone in it, so the POST is kept for them; the impact is what it was before the authenticated fact existed (the POST reveals the owner's IP address and that the mail is read). A wider scan would not cost real newsletter threads their one-click; only an "overflow strips" rule would (threads that grow past the bound), and that rule is not adopted, so a thread beyond 100 messages is a Gmail-cap edge case.
- **Trade, on purpose.** An unauthenticated message from another domain now strips the POST of an authenticated target: a spoofed, unvouched reply downgrades a real thread to the page link, the safe side. Unauthenticated messages from the same domain change nothing, and an unauthenticated message still never becomes the target while an authenticated one exists. Also unchanged: the host executor's own `authenticated === true` gate (Providers' item 1) is still open.
- **Tests (`tests/unsubscribe-auth.test.ts`).** One domain keeps the POST; the attacker case in both orders strips it with the page shown; an unauthenticated message from another domain strips; unauthenticated messages from the same domain keep it; a lone authenticated attacker message over unauthenticated real ones (the review's scenario) strips; case differences keep it; subdomains strip; missing, unparseable or non-ASCII (Kelvin sign) From strips alone and beside a known domain; mailto stays offered when stripped; the window bound. Each was mutation-checked (no domain check, no lower-casing, unknown ignored, unbounded query, comparing only authenticated messages, non-ASCII check after lower-casing).
- **Integrator's Opus security review of #183 (VERDICT FIX), both applied.** (1) MEDIUM: `readTarget` sorts authenticated messages first, so the target can be older than every row `mixedDomains` reads, and the check never saw the target's own From. Probe: unauthenticated real issues, one signed attacker message in the thread via References, then 50 more real issues: the window held one domain and the attacker stayed the target. `readTarget` now selects the target's `from_addr` and `mixedDomains(q, rowid, own)` compares `[own, ...window]`. (2) LOW: a thread where only the attacker's message carried List-Unsubscribe (a personal conversation) showed one-click to the attacker. The window now holds every inbound, non-draft, non-fromMe message, with List-Unsubscribe or without. Trade, stated exactly: one-click exists only when the target carries List-Unsubscribe-Post, and a thread loses it only when some other inbound message in the window has a different From domain than the target (a spoofed reply, or a second correspondent beside a bulk sender); such a thread shows the page link, the safe side. The owner's own messages and drafts are not compared. Tests: the probe (window of real issues, old signed attacker target), a lone signed header in a personal conversation, plain messages from the target domain keep the POST, own replies and drafts are not compared; each mutation-checked (target's own From dropped; List-Unsubscribe filter back; fromMe and drafts included).

## 2026-10-31 eng-html html-3: `aplos-` selectors in every position, and the noframes test gap (#171 round-2 LOWs)

- **LOW, fixed: mail CSS could still hide Aplos's own wrappers through selector spellings the first fix missed.** Mail class names starting `aplos-` already became `m-aplos-` in markup and after a `.` directly before `{`; Aplos's wrappers (`details.aplos-quote`, `mark.aplos-find`, `img.aplos-blocked`, `p.aplos-clipped`) each carry exactly one such class. The rename now happens wherever a selector can name a class, and no longer depends on a `{` following (`packages/mail-html/src/css.ts`, new `class-selectors.ts`):
  - **Class selectors:** a `.` and an `aplos-` name become `.m-aplos-…` with comments between them (a browser drops comments, so a dot, a comment and a name are one class), through escapes (`.\61plos-quote`), in any case, inside `:is()`, `:where()`, `:not()`, `:has()`, nested rules and malformed argument lists (`:is(.aplos-quote, ;)`, `:where(.aplos-quote, })`, an unclosed `:is(`). A dot followed by whitespace, `animation-name:aplos-fade`, strings and `.aplosx` are left alone.
  - **Attribute selectors (`[class…]`, any case, with or without a namespace such as `*|`): REWRITTEN, not dropped.** Rewriting keeps the mail's own renamed elements matching what the sender meant, and Aplos's no longer match. `=`, `~=`, `$=`: each name that starts `aplos-` (at the start of the value or after whitespace) becomes `m-aplos-…`. `^=`, `*=`, `|=`: the same, and a bare `aplos` stem too (`[class|=aplos]` becomes `[class|=m-aplos]`, as does `^=aplos`). Quoted or not, `i` and `s` flags untouched, comments (and, generously, whitespace) between the operator characters tolerated. Malformed (no `]`) is rewritten too. A scan ends at the next `[`, so it is linear (tested on 100,000 `[`).
  - **Ids:** unchanged and re-verified. Every mail id and anchor name becomes `m-<id>` (`ids.ts`), `#aplos-x` before `{` becomes `#m-aplos-x`, and Aplos writes no id at all, so no id selector can hit an Aplos element. Test: `#aplos-quote`.
  - **Tests:** css.test.ts "aplos- class names, in every selector position" (8 tests); sanitize.test.ts "keeps selectors of every spelling from reaching Aplos wrappers"; the oracle (`tests/oracle.ts`) now fails any sanitized mail `<style>` (not the reading stylesheet in `<head>`) that still names an `aplos-` class by a dot or by a `[class…]` selector, so every corpus message is checked; new corpus message **134-css-hides-aplos-wrappers** (none of the earlier 133 had a class bypass). Mutation checks, each red then restored: old `ident()` condition (6 failures, including corpus 134 through the oracle); attribute rewrite off (5); comment skipping off (5); stem operators off (4); id rewrite off (2).
  - **Cost of rewriting, accepted:** a mail's own `[class*=aplos-]` no longer matches a class such as `foo-aplos-bar` (it matches only names that start `aplos-`, now `m-aplos-`), and `[class|=aplos]` no longer matches a class named exactly `aplos`. Mail that styles by such names is vanishingly rare.
  - **Still possible, cosmetic (no load, no script):** selectors that do not name an `aplos-` class can still hide the wrappers: tag and structural ones (`details{display:none}`, `p:last-child`, `:not(.x){…}`), generic attribute selectors (`[class]`), and fragments that are not recognisably ours (`[class^=ap]`, `[class*=quote]`, `[class$=-find]`, `[class*=os-q]`). Rewriting those would change selectors for unrelated classes. Recorded, not fought.
- **LOW, fixed (coverage): nested `<noframes>` re-parsing could come back without a test noticing.** With the old regex gone, corpus 133 and the p99 test stay green if fallbacks inside fallbacks are re-parsed again (p99 31 to 34 ms either way). `perf.test.ts` now has `'<noframes>'.repeat(2000)` plus 250,000 characters of filler next to the 240-level self-rebuilding shape, both held to the existing 300 ms line (about 8 ms and 19 ms on the current code). Mutation checks on `fallback()` in `sanitize.ts`, each red then restored: dropping the top-level-only flag (re-parse inside re-parsed text) fails the 2,000-level shape at 8.4 s; re-parsing after stripping nested `<noframes>` (the old regex behaviour) fails the 240-level self-rebuilding shape at 1.8 s. The two shapes cover the two ways it came back; neither alone does.
- **MEDIUM, fixed (Integrator's Opus review of #177): a rewritten `[class="…"]` value skipped the string checks.** `string()` in `css.ts` returned the rewritten value straight away, before the image-function and address checks every other string gets, so `a{--x:[class="https://evil.example/p.png aplos-q"]}` kept the address, and inside `image-set()`, `-webkit-image-set()` or `image()` the value was never classified. Now the rewritten value (what is written, not what came in) goes through the same two checks: in an image function it goes to the image book; elsewhere an address-like string becomes `""`; only then is it written with `cssString`. Tests (css.test.ts "judges a rewritten [class] value like any other string"): the reported shape, `[class="aplos-q:x"]` (the rewrite keeps it address-like), an image-set holding one value with the address before the name and one after it (the first is classified and recorded as the image proxy would, the second is `none` because a name in front makes it relative), and an address after the name in a plain string (not a URL, kept as before the rewrite). Corpus **135-css-class-selector-value-as-address** (custom properties, `image-set`, `-webkit-image-set`, `image`; zero remote images). Mutation check: writing the rewritten value before the checks again fails that test, the idempotence test and both corpus renders (images off and on) through 135.
- **LOW, fixed (test): a value holding several names.** `[class="foo aplos-x"]` for `=`, `~=`, `$=`, `^=` and `|=`, a tab and a newline between names, and a name that merely ends in `aplos-x` (`xaplos-x`) are now cases. Mutation checks, each red then restored: `WHOLE_NAME` matching only at the start of the value (the survivor from the review) fails 1 test; `NAME_OR_STEM` likewise fails 2.
- **Whitespace in a rewritten value is kept as written (the choice).** `=` compares the whole value, so collapsing or trimming runs of spaces would change which elements the mail's own selector matches (a value with leading or trailing space never matches a class list, and still does not). Only the `aplos-` prefix changes. An unquoted `[class=foo aplos-x]` is an invalid selector (the value is `foo`, the rest is junk, and the browser drops the whole rule), so it is written as it came.

## 2026-10-31 providers (prov-gov): #173 review fixes, an unstarted account is a transient refusal

- **Integrator Opus review, MEDIUM.** `host-drafts.ts` threw a `permanent` `accountNotStarted` when the account had no actor. That is true for a moment during every `stop(account, 'restart')` and possibly at boot, and the draft clock does not wait for accounts to start; the engine's `refused()` would park the draft in a problem state with no next attempt. It now throws `NotSentError` with class `transient` (nothing was written, so the attempt is not marked uncertain): a save retries after `DRAFT_RETRY_MS`, a removal after the same delay (up to its three attempts). `draftsUnsupported` and `accountMismatch` stay permanent.
- **LOW.** The fake adapter now records `arguments.length`, so "omits expectedMessageId when absent" fails if the two-argument branch is removed (checked by deleting it).

## 2026-10-31 providers (prov-gmail): #180 Opus review fixes, the verdict is not believed when its own header contradicts it

- **MEDIUM, fixed.** Google's `Authentication-Results` echoes sender-chosen text (the envelope sender in an SPF comment), so a quote and `;` there could make the parser read a `dkim=pass` segment that Google never wrote, next to Google's real `dkim=neutral`/`fail` for the same forged signature. Two changes in `list-auth.ts`: (1) a pass is not believed when another non-pass `dkim` result may name the same signature (same signing domain, compatible `header.s`, agreeing `header.b` prefixes; a detail the other result leaves out counts as agreeing), because Google always reports its own failure for a forged or replayed signature; a failed `dkim` for another domain or signature does not hide a good pass; (2) one segment that does not parse fails the whole header instead of being dropped (an empty segment from a trailing `;` is still skipped). Both repro inputs of the review are tests.
- **LOW, fixed (tests).** Lookalike authserv-ids `evil-mx.google.com`, `mx.google.com.evil.test`, `xmx.google.com`, `mx.google.com.`, `google.com` and `mx.google.comx` are false; replacing the exact id match with `endsWith` or `startsWith` now turns a test red (the earlier claim of that mutation check was not backed by a test).

## 2026-11-02 — eng-time: a draft held by a send does not take a newer phone version (Integrator's #174 delta review)

- **Probe (worst case, before the fix).** A phone reply (remote-origin row, revision 1) with a send queued at revision 1, then the phone copy reported with a NEWER Message-ID: `refreshRemote` rebuilt the draft (revision 2, the new subject) while the send was queued. Then: (a) the send still mailed the pinned content of revision 1, but `payloadOf` named the remote draft WITHOUT `baseRemoteMessageId` (it is sent only when `synced_revision === row.revision`, and the refresh had moved both), so a provider that sends by draft id could not tell that the copy it consumes is the phone's newer one; (b) `sentDrafts` hides a draft only when the sent revision equals the current one, so the draft stayed in Drafts at revision 2 with ids that name a consumed copy; (c) nothing deleted the copy, but a second Send of that row would have mailed the newer text again.
- **Fix.** `sendPins` (draft-proof.ts): a live send of any revision, or a sent one of the row's CURRENT revision, holds the draft. `refreshRemote` returns at once for the same version, and for another version only remembers it (`changedElsewhere`, as `editedElsewhere` does for a local draft): the row keeps its revision and fields, the send keeps naming the version it was queued on (`baseRemoteMessageId`), and a sent draft leaves Drafts as before.
- **Fate of the phone's newer version (closed-world rule).** It is never overwritten or deleted from here: the row is not rebuilt, `changedElsewhere` makes a discard forget the copy instead of deleting it, the hidden-row removal after a send passes the expected message id, and an upload from the stale row is base-guarded, so the provider answers with a conflict. It is NOT queued: if the send is undone or fails, the row shows the older version until the provider reports the copy again (history reports changes only), and then takes it in the ordinary way (tested). If the send goes out, the provider decides what happens to a copy that is not the one named (Gmail: refuses on the base; the fake in the tests consumes the draft regardless, which is a limit of the fake). A removal of the copy (`w.removed`) still deletes the row under a live send: the other device sent or deleted it, nothing is left to hold, and the queued send carries its own pinned bytes.
- **`hasSend` was too broad for the late-parent gate (#174).** It counted a send of an older revision, so a draft still in Drafts with a newer edit never got its reply target. `refreshRemote` now uses `sendPins`. A remote-origin row with a sent older revision and a newer revision cannot be reached any more (the gate prevents it), so the narrowing is covered by a test of the predicate itself. `hasSend` stays, deliberately broad, for `editedElsewhere` and `sameCopy` (the safe side there).
- **Review fix: taking the newer version clears the memory.** `changedElsewhere` stays set while the row shows the OLD version (a discard then forgets the copy, correct), but the rebuild path of `refreshRemote` now clears it: the row names the reported message id again, so a discard deletes the copy guarded on that id. Before this, a discard after an undo and a re-report forgot the copy and left it on the provider, to come back on the phone's next edit. Test: queue a send at pm-1, phone pm-2, undo, pm-2 again, discard.
- **Known gap (recorded, not built).** After an undo or a failed send the newer phone version stays silent: only a boolean is stored (not pm-2's id or content) and history reports changes only, so the row shows the older version until the phone edits again. Trigger to build: store the held message id with the flag, and when the send ends without going out turn it into a conflict or ask the provider for the copy again.

## 2026-10-31 — eng-host: the five LOWs from the composition-root review (#178)

- **Correction (append-only) to the 2026-11-02 compose section, item (4) of "Built beyond the rigs".** It says `accountNotStarted` is "permanent, parks the draft". Since the providers' #173 it is a transient refusal that is retried (see the entry above). Starting accounts before the engine is still the rule, because it avoids a pointless first-upload retry, but a miss no longer loses or parks anything.
- **(1) A failed account start is visible and retried once per wake.** `createAccountStarts` in `compose.ts` (a separate file would be unowned in `plans/OWNERS.md`; move it to `host/start-accounts.ts` once the Integrator lists it) runs `ActorHost.start` for each stored account. A failure logs one `rpc.failed` line (`method: Host/startAccount`, `reason` = the error class) under the context `account` = the install-salted hash of the account id (`hashAddress`, the salting the host logger uses; never the address, never the raw id, never message text). The class is a provider error's class, else the error's `name` (a plain `Error` for "no provider for x" and "supplies no budget", so those two are not told apart; they would need distinct error types in `sync/host.ts`, which is the providers' file). The composed host gains `startFailures(): readonly {accountId, errorClass}[]` (salted id, class), empty when every stored account runs; the future UI or menu reads it. On each power wake (after the 5 s dedupe) `retryFailed()` tries the failed accounts once more; one attempt at a time, a start that succeeds leaves the list, one that fails again logs again (once per wake), and `close()` stops retries and waits for one in flight before the actors are disposed. Not done: a timer-based retry (a stored account that cannot start stays stopped until the next wake or restart). Trigger: when the menu shows `startFailures`, add a "Try again" that calls the same retry.
- **(3) One module instance of the connect sessions.** The engine package `exports` gained explicit entries `./clock/loop`, `./sync/{account-read,connect-sessions,governor,host,lookup}` (no wildcard), and `compose.ts` imports them through `@aplos/engine/...` instead of `packages/engine/src/...`, so a bundler or loader cannot give it a second copy of `connect-sessions` whose `disposeConnectSessions` finds nothing. The bundle is built from `src/index.ts`, so exports do not change it. Test: the engine's own `connectSessionsOf(services)` (imported through the package) returns the same map twice, and after `close()` returns a new one, which only happens if compose's dispose reached the same WeakMap; removing the dispose step turns this and the waiting-sign-in test red. Under tsx both import forms resolve to one file, so the test cannot fail on the old relative import by itself; it guards the dispose step and the one-instance assumption, and the import form is the fix.
- **(4) The wake fallback is on in production.** `ComposeOptions.wakeClock` (a `WakeClock`: `now`, `every`, `cancel`) is passed to `watchPowerResume`; the default is the real clock (`Date.now`, an unref'd `setInterval`), never a fake. A 31 s wall-clock jump is one `engine.wake()` and a resume right after adds none; 29 s is none. A test with no `wakeClock` checks a real 1 s `setInterval` is armed and cleared on close.
- **(5) Temp folders.** `world(t)` registers its folder removal with `t.after`, so a failing test still removes it (checked with a forced failure: no `aplos-compose-*` folder left).
- **Mutations checked (each red, then restored).** Removing the connect-sessions dispose (2 tests red; the run hangs on the leaked listener, as it would in production); passing no clock to `watchPowerResume` (the jump test and the real-clock test red); removing the retry on wake, and logging the raw account id (the start-failure test red). `packages/engine/package.json` is eng-contract's file; the same Engine thread owns both lanes.

## 2026-10-31 providers (prov-gov): the credential key's errors reach the window only as plain messages (#176 delta re-check, three LOWs)

- **Raw file errors no longer reach the window.** `loadDataKey` threw the raw `mkdir` and `writeFileDurably` errors (EACCES/ENOSPC/EIO carry `/Users/<name>/.../engine-key.bin.<uuid>.tmp`), and `engine-host.ts` forwarded `error.message` as `keyError` (shown through `engine-process.ts`). Now every failure `loadDataKey` raises is a `KeyringError` (plain words, original as `cause`): folder not prepared, key not saved, key not checked, locked keychain, damaged key. `engine-host.ts` forwards the message only for a `KeyringError`, any other error becomes the generic unavailable message, and the raw error goes to the log (`console.error`), which stays on the machine.
- **Tests.** The key file is written with mode 0600 (the 0644 mutation now fails). The folder flush goes through `syncFolder(openFolderAt, path)`, so a test hands it a handle whose `sync()` throws EIO (thrown, handle closed) or EPERM/EISDIR/EINVAL/ENOTSUP (ignored, handle closed); swallowing the flush error now fails. A failed rename and an uncreatable folder give a `KeyringError` with no path and the cause kept.

## 2026-10-31 eng-html html-3: class whitespace kept as written in markup too (correction to #177)

- **Correction.** The last bullet of the #177 section ("Whitespace in a rewritten value is kept as written (the choice)") gave the wrong reason and described only half of the code. It said selector values keep their whitespace because `=` compares the whole value. True, but the markup side did not: `scopeClassList` in `ids.ts` split a class attribute on whitespace and joined it with single spaces whenever it held `aplos-`, so an element with `class=" foo  aplos-x "` became `class="foo m-aplos-x"` while its own selector `[class=" foo  aplos-x "]` became `[class=" foo  m-aplos-x "]`. The two no longer matched, and `^=`/`$=` values with spaces broke the same way. The history above stays as written; this entry is the correction.
- **The real reason, and the fix.** Collapsing whitespace in markup is not needed for security: the rename only has to move a name that starts `aplos-` (at the start of the value or after whitespace, any case) to `m-aplos-`, and Chromium splits a class list on the same five ASCII whitespace characters the rename looks at. So markup and selector values now go through one function, `moveOwnNames` in `ids.ts`, which changes nothing but the prefix; `scopeClassList` and the `=`, `~=`, `$=` selector path both use it (`^=`, `*=`, `|=` use the same rule plus the bare `aplos` stem). Whitespace stays as written in both places, so an element and its own selector match exactly as they did before the rename. A class value with no `aplos-` was already returned untouched.
- **Test.** sanitize.test.ts "moves aplos- names in markup and selectors alike, so =, ^= and $= still match": a class with a leading space, a double space, a tab, a space-newline-space run and a trailing space, with `=`, `^=` and `$=` selectors written for it. The markup value must equal the `=` value byte for byte, and the `^=` and `$=` values must be its prefix and suffix (no DOM selector matcher is in the dependencies). Mutation check: putting the old split-and-join back into `scopeClassList` fails it, then restored.

## 2026-11-03 — eng-time: a newer phone copy held under a send is applied when the send ends (Integrator's #186 gap)

- **Probe (before).** Pin a send at pm-1, the phone saves pm-2 (held: `changedElsewhere`), the owner undoes: the row still shows "Version one" at the same revision, so Send again was accepted and went out with the OLD content (base pm-1); the sent draft's row ended, the copy was forgotten (0 removals), and pm-2 stayed on the provider as a draft the desktop never showed. Nothing was destroyed, but before #186 the owner saw pm-2 after the undo.
- **Decision: hold the report, not just a flag.** The Integrator proposed storing the held message id and refusing a new send. An id alone cannot be turned into anything the owner can see (history reports changes only, and the port cannot fetch a draft), so `changedElsewhere` now holds the newest `RemoteDraftWrite` for the copy (`true | RemoteDraftWrite`; a string or object is truthy, so every boolean reader keeps working, and rows from before keep `true` and behave as before). `releaseHeld` (submissions.ts) applies that report through the normal path, as if it had just arrived, in the same commit that clears the flag, once `sendPins` says nothing holds the draft: a clean phone draft takes it (`refreshRemote`), a local draft with edits gets the ordinary conflict question (`editedElsewhere`/`raise`), a removal becomes the usual "gone" conflict. It runs (1) right after a send is undone or cancelled, so the owner sees the version at once (the pre-#186 behaviour), and (2) at the start of every new send check, so a release that failed (or a race) is retried there. A send check that applies it then answers with the existing sentences: the revision moved ("This draft changed in another window. Reopen it before sending."), or the conflict (UNRESOLVED). A pinned send (live, or sent at the current revision) is unaffected: nothing is released while `sendPins` holds.
- **Reader audit.** `changedElsewhere` is read in `applyDraftDelete` (truthy: a draft that holds a held copy forgets it on discard, unchanged), `markChangedElsewhere` (now keeps the NEWEST report; the same message id again changes nothing), `keepMine`, `takeTheirs`, `refreshRemote`'s rebuild and `releaseHeld` (all clear it). The discard rule stays as #186 left it: taking the version clears the flag, so a discard after that deletes the copy guarded on its id; a discard while a copy is still held (the stale view) forgets it, which cannot destroy the phone's version.
- **Tests.** `drafts.pinned.test.ts` (undo shows the version and the next send carries it; a failed release is retried by the send check, which refuses the old revision and sends nothing; the newest report is the one held; a local draft's held edit becomes a conflict at the undo). Each goes red when its piece is reverted (no release after cancel, no release in the send check, the flag only, the first report kept).
- **Known, not built.** (1) A removal of the copy (`w.removed`) still deletes the row while a send is live (`refreshRemote`): a draft sent from the phone plus the queued desktop send mails twice; both are the owner's explicit actions on two devices. (2) A provider that keeps the same message id when a draft is edited (Graph) is ignored while pinned, because the version is told apart by message id only: the trigger is Graph's change-key comparison in `refreshRemote` and `markChangedElsewhere`. (3) A held report is the provider's last word at that time; if the owner discards the stale view while it is held, the copy is forgotten, not deleted (it may come back as a draft of its own when the phone edits it).
- **A discard does not release.** The send a discard cancels goes through `setSubmission(..., release = false)` (module.ts): releasing there would turn the held phone edit into a conflict and the discard would then queue a (provider-guarded) delete of the copy. The existing test `N3: a phone edit during the undo hold, then the owner discards` pins this (red when the discard path releases).


## 2026-10-31 eng-html html-2 (second half): the leak harness (`apps/desktop/tests/eng-html/{canary,leak.e2e}.ts`)

- **What it is.** One Playwright spec, `npx playwright test -c apps/desktop/tests/eng-html/support/playwright.config.ts` (its own config, because plat-ci's only matches `*.browser.spec.ts` and `*.electron.spec.ts`; the plan's `leak.e2e.ts` path works once that config matches `*.e2e.ts`). It launches a **bare hidden Electron** (`support/harness-main.cjs`: `show:false`, accessory policy, Dock hidden, temp `--user-data-dir`, `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` removed, AI keys blanked), not the Aplos app: the frame pipeline (html-4, html-5) does not exist, so a local stand-in (`support/frame-server.ts`) plays the `aplos-mail://` relay. Electron, not plain Chromium, because only Electron's Chromium issued the link hints (see below) and its `session.webRequest` is the same listener the real backstop will use. Every corpus message (135 files and 54 synthetic, 189) is sanitized by `mailHtml.render` and served into `<iframe sandbox="allow-scripts allow-popups">` (no `allow-same-origin`; scripts only for the harness reporter, as ARCHITECTURE 10 plans) under four conditions: frame headers or a bare document with no policy (the sanitizer alone), each with images off and on. A scripted pass focuses the frame, hovers up to 30 links, summaries and images (resting 150 ms on up to 12 links in the first condition) and presses nine keys; it never clicks. Images on is served only as the rewritten `img/<n>` and `cid/<n>`, answered from canned PNG bytes (the remote URL is never fetched, and an `img/<n>` beyond the sanitizer's `remoteImages` is a 404 and a finding). The frame headers are built in one function, `frameHeaders()` in `support/frame-server.ts`, for html-4 to replace: ARCHITECTURE 10's list, with `img-src` narrowed to this token's `img/` and `cid/` paths and `font-src data:`. An images-on load whose document equals the images-off one is skipped (228 of 756).
- **Canaries.** `canary.ts`: an HTTP canary on a random loopback port recording method, URL, headers and the message id (first path segment, or the `<tag>` of the Host header); Node's own instrumentation probes every listening port with `user-agent: node` and a `traceparent` header, so only requests with a Chromium user agent count (the rest are counted and printed as noise). The DNS canary maps each `<tag>.canary-dns.example` to `<tag>.localhost:<canary port>` and every other name to `~NOTFOUND` through `--host-resolver-rules` (so nothing ever reaches a real resolver, and the harness runs offline), and `--log-net-log` is parsed event by event: a lookup, preconnect or request for a canary host appears under the rewritten name, which keeps the tag. Chromium logs the rewritten name, not the original; that is why the tag travels in the target.
- **Positive controls** (a second Electron, its own net log, documents loaded raw with no sanitizer and no policy): a direct canary image, an image on a canary host (canary Host header and net log), `<link rel=dns-prefetch>` (net log, `is_speculative`), and `<script>window.__pwned=1</script>` (reporter sees `pwned` and a new global). All asserted. Observed and **not** asserted: `<link rel=preconnect>`, a link inside the sandboxed frame, and a link in the host page produced no net-log event in this harness (a probe with a top-level link in a hidden window did show repeated speculative lookups, so Chromium's link hints exist; why the harness window did not trigger them is open).
- **Assertions** (soft, in one test with a step each, so every failure prints): the harness loaded everything; positive controls; zero canary requests per message and condition; every request the page and Electron's `webRequest` saw is the document's own route (images off: none); zero net-log events naming a canary host or the canary port; no top-frame navigation, popup (Electron's window-open handler logs and denies), dialog or host-page error; no storage write (the reporter tries `localStorage`, `sessionStorage`, `document.cookie`, IndexedDB; each must throw `SecurityError`, and `window.origin` must be `null`); no script of a message ran (`window.__pwned` stays undefined and no new property on `window`; the corpus has no marker convention of its own, its scripts call `fetch` or `new Image()` on the canary); **no CSP violation in a sanitized document** (the sanitizer must not lean on the policy; a refusal is a finding even when the policy stopped it); and the CSSOM check below.
- **CSSOM check (closes the "CSS oracle shares the tokenizer" LOW, in part).** In every load the reporter walks `document.styleSheets` and `getComputedStyle` for up to 6,000 elements (`background-image`, `list-style-image`, `border-image-source`, `mask-image`, `shape-outside`, `content`, `cursor`, `filter`, `clip-path`, and `::before/::after/::marker/::first-line/::first-letter/::selection/::placeholder` for the first 300) and every `cssText`, collects each `url()`, and counts `@import` and `@font-face` rules and `document.fonts`. Each URL must resolve to this token's own `img/<n>` or `cid/<n>` below the sanitizer's counts, and the counts must be 0. This is Chromium's parser agreeing with the sanitizer's claim; it does not replace the oracle (a `url()` Chromium never parses is also not loaded, which is the point).
- **Results on the corpus: no leak found.** Zero canary requests in 189 messages x 4 conditions; zero unexpected requests (24,935 `webRequest` records, 5,000-image and 4,000-image stress messages included); zero canary names in the net log; no navigation, popup, dialog, storage write, script marker or new global; zero CSP violations; zero CSSOM `url()` outside `img/<n>` and `cid/<n>`; no `@import` or `@font-face`. Per class: every class in `ATTACK_CLASSES` has at least one message and none leaked. EXPECTED_FAILS is empty.
- **Mutation checks (restored after each).** (1) `ImageBook.source` returning the remote URL as an inline image: canary requests for 001 and 008 in the bare conditions, "unexpected requests" and net-log events in all conditions, CSP `img-src` violations in the frame conditions. (2) The same for CSS URLs only (`context !== 'img'`): messages 027, 028, 029 hit the canary (13, 8 and 4 requests), and the CSSOM check names `background-image|…/027-css-url-style-attribute/background`, `list-style-image` and the rest. Each channel (canary, own-route, net log, CSP violation, CSSOM) caught both.
- **Observations that are not leaks.** (a) Messages built to exhaust a renderer do exhaust it: 089 (popup click overlay) and 090 (CSS layout abuse) never answered the harness within 10 s in the frame and bare conditions, and g37 (5,000 images) and g38 (entity bombs) were slow enough to hit the same limit under load; those loads have no reporter data, so their storage, script, policy and CSSOM checks are blind (requests, canary and net log still cover them). They are listed in the log line "renderer exhausted by stress messages" and, by design, not retried. A denial of service inside one frame is the sandbox's job to contain (own renderer), and 090 is already recorded as "overlays are allowed, not a sanitizer property"; whether the frame should cap layout work is a html-4/5 question. (b) The first condition rests on links, but Chromium's link hints (anchor DNS prefetch, hover preconnect) were seen only for a `<link rel=dns-prefetch>`; see the controls.
- **Not covered.** The real `aplos-mail://` protocol handler, the real reporter and its hash, the main-side `webRequest` backstop, `setWindowOpenHandler` and `will-frame-navigate` of the app (the harness installs its own observers, which deny popups), the image proxy and its SSRF cases, the keyboard relay, and a packaged build. Link hover/anchor preconnect from a real pointer in a visible window (no positive control fired, so silence is weak evidence; hand-check in the packaged app, and consider `X-DNS-Prefetch-Control: off` in `frameHeaders()` for html-4, since an `https`-only default may not extend to a custom scheme). Hover-dependent images with images on (known gap 1 from html-3): the harness allows `img/<n>` and cannot tell which selector chose it. `postMessage` spoofing of the parent (needs the real host check). Cookies set by a response header on `img/<n>` (the stand-in sends none). Timing: 226 s at load average 24 to 30 on this 8-core Mac (481 s before skipping identical images-on loads); an idle machine should take about a third, not measured. Debug knobs: `LEAK_LIMIT`, `LEAK_ONLY`, `LEAK_CONDITIONS`, `LEAK_DUMP`.
- **For html-4 / html-5.** Replace `frameHeaders()` and the stand-in server with the real `Mail/serve` and protocol relay and keep every assertion; swap `probe-reporter.ts` for the real reporter (its fields are a superset of `{token,h,w,bg,ready}`); run the same spec through the Aplos app once `main/protocol.ts` exists, using `tests/e2e/support/electron.ts`.

## 2026-10-31 providers (prov-gmail): #180 Opus delta re-check, sameness by header.b alone and a stricter header shape

- **MEDIUM, fixed.** `mayNameSameSignature` ruled a failed `dkim` out when its signing domain or selector differed, but DKIM allows `d=bank.example; i=@sub.bank.example` and Google echoes `header.i`, so the real fail for a forged signature (`@sub.bank.example`) was not seen against an injected pass (`@bank.example`). Sameness is now decided by the `header.b` prefixes alone (one a prefix of the other); a non-pass `dkim` result with no `header.b` counts as contradicting. The domain and selector tests were removed (their mutations had failed no test).
- **LOW hardening, done.** (1) A `(` or `"` directly after word characters is refused in the tokenizer (RFC 8601 puts comments and quotes only where whitespace or `=` precedes them): `header.s=k( ...` would otherwise open a comment that hides Google's real `dkim=fail`. (2) A `dkim` result after the first `spf` or `dmarc` result fails the header: Google writes its `dkim` results first, and text echoed into an SPF comment lands after them. Not done: believing only signatures Gmail reported (no way to know offline that Gmail reports every signature).
- **Tests and mutations.** New: the `i=` below `d=` attack, a different selector, a result with no `b=`, the `k(` comment attack (isolated so only the tokenizer rule stops it), `"`, a `dkim` after spf and after dmarc, and a legitimate `arc=pass` first header still true. Mutations red: domain test re-added, `(` rule removed, order rule removed, missing-`b` treated as agreeing.

## 2026-10-31 providers (prov-gmail): #180 round 3, no `;` inside a comment or quoted string

- **MEDIUM, fixed.** The glued-opener rule missed an opener right after `=`: a forged `s=(` or `s="` plus a crafted envelope sender in the SPF comment made the comment or quote run across the `;` between results, swallowing Google's own `header.b` and `spf=pass`, so the real `dkim=fail` named a different signature and the injected `dkim=pass` came before any spf result (reproduced as `true` for the quote case and a comment variant). A comment or a quoted string that contains a `;` (also an escaped `\;`) now fails the whole header: Google writes neither with one, a sender can, and with that rule no comment or quote can reach a result boundary. The synthetic test that used `;` inside a comment and a `reason="ok; fine"` was changed to commas.
- **Tests.** The comment, quote and escaped-`;` attacks; mutations red: no `;` check in comments, none in quoted strings, none for the escaped form. Disabling the verdict (the other option offered) was not needed; the live-probe question (does Gmail ever write `(`, `"` or `;` into a value) stays on the Mac probe list.

## 2026-10-31 providers (prov-gmail): #180 round 4, one dkim run after the authserv-id, base64-only b=

- **HIGH under the raw-echo assumption, fixed.** Google writes `arc=pass (i=1 ... dkdomain=<value>)` before spf and the value comes from the attacker's own ARC hop, so `dkdomain=x); dkim=pass header.i=@bank.example header.b="()AAAA"; x.y=z (` puts a `;` outside any comment and a forged `dkim=pass` after the ARC result; and the tokenizer drops comments and quotes from Google's `header.b` while the signature's `b=` stayed raw, so `b=()AAAAAAzz` read back as `AAAAAA` against the injected `()AAAA` and the real `dkim=fail` no longer named the same signature (the reviewer's concrete input returned true). Two checks: (1) `dkim` results are accepted only as the unbroken run straight after the authserv-id (a `dkim` result after any other result fails the header, which covers the earlier spf/dmarc-only ordering rule; real Gmail order is dkim, arc, spf, dmarc, so the earlier test that put arc first was wrong and now expects false); (2) `header.b` and the signature `b=` must be base64 (`[A-Za-z0-9+/=]+`) before they are compared: a signature with another `b=` is unreadable (the verdict is false), and a failed `dkim` result whose `header.b` is not base64 counts as naming the same signature.
- **Tests and mutations.** The reviewer's input with `b=()AAAAAAzz` and `b=""AAAAAAzz`; each rule alone (a pass after an arc result, a junk-b signature whose prefix matches, a failed result with a junk `header.b`); an escaped `\;` in a quoted string. Mutations red: the old spf/dmarc-only order rule, no signature base64 check, no fail-junk rule, no escape check in quotes. The check on Google's own `header.b` inside `signatureFor` was dropped as redundant (a base64 signature cannot start with a non-base64 prefix).
- **Accepted residual risk (reviewer's wording).** Google writes a non-pass `dkim` result with the real `b=` prefix for every signature it fails. **Mac live probes to run:** does Gmail echo raw `(`, `)`, `"` or `;` into `header.s`, the ARC comment and the SPF comment; does it write one `dkim` result per signature, in one run before `arc`/`spf`/`dmarc`.

## 2026-10-31 eng-contract: correction to the one-click window size (Integrator's #183 LOW; the window excludes the target's own row)

- **Correction (append-only; the 2026-10-31 "one-click is stripped when a thread's authenticated List-Unsubscribe messages span several From domains" section above is left as written).** It says an attacker needs "more than 100 newer messages" to push a real sender out of the window. That was wrong: the window query did not leave out the target's own row, so the target took one of the 100 places. Derived exactly: one real unauthenticated message R, the attacker's signed target T, and e newer messages from the attacker's domain; the window holds the newest 100 inbound non-draft messages, T included, so R is in it only while e + 1 < 100, and **99 newer messages were enough to keep the POST (98 stripped)**.
- **Fix (`methods/unsubscribe.ts`, `mixedDomains`).** The window query now has `AND id <> ?` with the target's id, because the target's From is already compared separately as `[own, ...window]`. The window holds `DOMAIN_WINDOW` (100) OTHER messages. Precisely: a real sender's message is compared while fewer than 100 other messages (the target not counted) are newer than it; with e newer messages besides T it is compared for e up to 99 (R is the 100th other message) and out of the window at e = 100 (the 101st). The limit that remains is the documented one: past that the effect is what it was before the authenticated fact (the POST reveals the owner's IP address and that the mail is read). Code comments say the same.
- **Tests (`tests/unsubscribe-auth.test.ts`).** "DOMAIN_WINDOW is 100" pins the constant. "the window holds 100 OTHER messages" uses literal counts: R, T and 99 newer attacker messages strip the POST (link), 100 keep it. It replaces the old window test, which counted the target in the window. Mutation checks, each red: `DOMAIN_WINDOW` 50, 150 or 101 (the probe and the constant test); the `AND id <> ?` exclusion dropped (the probe), in both the SQL-only and the parameter-removed forms.

## 2026-11-03 — eng-time: Opus review of the held copy (e200cf5f)

- **A removal is always kept (HIGH).** Gmail reports a removal under the LAST KNOWN message id, so the equal-id shortcut in `markChangedElsewhere` dropped a removal that followed a held edit, and the undo then replayed the edit onto a draft whose copy no longer existed (a second Send would have mailed it again). Rules now: a held removal stays held and is never replaced (a late report of an older version cannot bring the copy back); a removal always replaces a held non-removal; the same id again is ignored only for a non-removal. On release a held removal takes the normal removal path: a clean local draft ends, an edited one gets the "gone" conflict (keep mine uploads as a new draft, never over a vanished copy). A remote-origin row still ends at the report itself, as before (known gap below).
- **A report applied while unpinned drops what was held (MEDIUM).** `dropHeld` turns a held object back into `true` whenever `editedElsewhere` or `refreshRemote` apply a report with nothing pinning the draft (clean replace, conflict, rebuild, or an ignorable repeat). The copy stays marked, so a discard still forgets it, but the older report can never be replayed over the newer one if a release failed (disk error, app killed between the cancel and the release). Tests: a clean draft that took B after A was held, and a conflict that keeps naming B.
- **The release is guarded (LOW).** It rebuilds `{...held, kind: 'remoteDraft'}`, and replays only a report whose account is the draft's and whose copy id the row still names; otherwise it only turns the hold into `true` (no stray draft is inserted). It uses the same pin test as the path that applies the report: `sendPins` for a remote-origin row, any send for a local one (`editedElsewhere` holds on any send), so a local draft with only an older sent revision is not re-held in a loop by its own release. A refused send check while a send still pins the draft commits nothing (tested through the store sequence).
- **Known, not built.** (1) "Newest" is decided by arrival, comparing message ids, not `changedAt`: a late older report can replace a newer held one, and the same holds on the unpinned path (not new). (2) A local draft with a newer edit and only an OLDER sent revision is held by any send (the broad `hasSend` in `editedElsewhere`), so its phone edit is held and not asked about until the older send is out of the way: the narrowing done for remote-origin rows (`sendPins`) was not applied to local ones, on the safe side. (3) A remote-origin row still ends when the copy's removal is reported under a live send (see above).

## 2026-11-03 — eng-time: second Opus review of the held copy (30b42872)

- **`dropHeld` only where a newer version is written (MEDIUM).** It is now a pure function applied to the meta handed to `replaceWithTheirs` and `raise` (and the removal that ends or questions the row), after the echo, the pin and the known-conflict returns. An echo or a repeat of a known version changes nothing and no longer discards the held report, so the release (or the next send check) still applies it. `refreshRemote` needs no call: its rebuild already clears the mark, and a reply-target relink is not a newer version.
- **Local rows use `sendPins` too (MEDIUM).** The simplest safe fix: `editedElsewhere` and `releaseHeld` ask the same question for every row (`sendPins`: a live send of any revision, or a sent one of the CURRENT revision). A local draft whose older revision was sent and that the owner edited since is not pinned, so the phone's edit is asked about in the ordinary way instead of being held behind a sent submission that never goes away; and a hold only ever exists while the pin that made it exists, so no hold can become unreleasable (no refusal in the send check was needed, which also keeps every way out open: reopen, discard). `hasSend` (any send, ever) stays only in `sameCopy`, on the safe side. Test: send revision 1, edit to 'After', phone edits: a conflict naming the phone version, 'After' never mailed over it.
- **A removal is never an echo (HIGH, pre-existing).** `isEcho` returns false for a removal: Gmail reports it under the last known message id, which equals the row's own, so a phone-sent or phone-deleted draft stayed in Drafts and could be mailed again. Now it takes the removal path (a clean draft ends; an edited one gets the "gone" conflict; under a live send it is held and released at the undo). Our own removals are unaffected: the hidden `removing` row meets the report first and just ends. Tests: no send, a queued send then undo (second Send refused `notFound`), and our own removal reported back.
- **A replaced draft deletes its copy again (LOW).** `replaceWithTheirs` clears the mark (the row names the reported version, so a discard deletes the copy guarded on its id), as `refreshRemote` and `takeTheirs` already did. Test: held A, failed release, B replaces the clean draft, discard removes the copy guarded on B.

## 2026-11-03 — eng-time: third Opus re-check of the held copy (b679b71b)

- **Known behaviour change (LOW), recorded.** A send that used up the Gmail copy (`drafts.send`), followed by an owner edit of the draft before the sent draft is cleaned up, now ends with a "copy gone" conflict when Gmail reports the removal of our OWN sent draft: the removal is no longer taken for an echo (`isEcho` returns false for a removal), and with a sent submission of an OLDER revision the row is not pinned (`sendPins`), so the report reaches the ordinary path. The text is kept and "keep mine" uploads a fresh copy; the question is unneeded, but nothing is lost. Suggested follow-up: when a send of this draft used up the copy (`draftConsumed`), forget the copy silently instead of asking. Trigger: seen in practice, or a UI complaint.
- **Test.** The phone removes a draft whose older revision was sent and which was edited here since: 'After' is kept, the conflict says the copy is gone, nothing is held, and 'After' is not mailed. It goes red when a removal is ignored for a row that has a sent send, or taken for an echo.

## 2026-10-31 eng-contract contract-4: the real engine behind `APLOS_ENGINE=real`, direct MessagePorts, main out of the data path

- **Switch.** `APLOS_ENGINE=real` starts the new engine; anything else (the default) is today's legacy engine, unchanged. A release build ignores the variable (`realEngineRequested(env, releaseBuild)`), because the real path is not complete until the switch on days 11 to 13; it is a development and test flag only.
- **Processes.** Main forks the new engine in its own Electron `utilityProcess` (`engine.cjs` with `--aplos-engine=real`, service name `Aplos Mail Engine (new)`, so its stops are counted apart from the legacy engine's). `engine-process.ts` stays the one entry and imports `main/engine-utility.ts`, starting it on that argument, so `build-main.mjs` (plat-ci) did not change. The import is static on purpose: a lazy `import()` made esbuild wrap every engine module in an `__esm` initializer whose names (`init_killpoints`) kept the kill points' module name in a release bundle. With the new engine in `engine.cjs`, eng-time's `release-grep` test for the engine process turned on as planned (it skipped until now) and passes for all three shapes; its declaration check now matches whole declarations only, because the store's `let hooks` (store/ingest.ts) matched `let hook` (Lane-Approved: eng-time). The utility process runs `composeEngineHost` (host/compose.ts) and serves the ports; main keeps the keychain, the window layer, the native methods and the host channel.
- **Ports.** One `MessageChannelMain` per window, made only when the window's preload asks (`aplos:engine-connect`) and only for the window's trusted main frame (`trustedCaller` in `host/transports/port.ts`: the sender frame must be the webContents' main frame, not detached, of type `window`, showing the app's own renderer URL, with a `BrowserWindow`; mail frames, any subframe, DevTools, webviews and other origins are refused). One end goes to the utility process (`attach`), the other to the main frame with `WebFrameMain.postMessage`. A window that closes or starts a new document has its port revoked (`detach`) at once, so it receives nothing more; a reload asks again. A second window gets its own port and its own engine session.
- **Preload.** `window.aplosEngine` exposes only `call`, `subscribe` and `position`; the page never sees a port or an Electron object. Engine methods must be in `portMethods` (the generated renderer table's engine methods, never a `host` or desktop method, never an unknown name); desktop methods go to main (`aplos:desktop`). The engine side checks the same list again before dispatch, and the engine's own `checkAccess` a third time. Binary moves as transferable `ArrayBuffer` (the transfer list keeps only ArrayBuffers). Note: `contextBridge` copies a page's buffer into preload's world, so the page's own buffer is copied once; from preload on it moves.
- **Payload cap.** No engine-to-window message over 256 KB (`messageBytes`: JSON text as UTF-8 plus binary bytes) except a blob route's reply (`Blob/get`, 26 MB). An oversized reply becomes `tooLarge`; an oversized frame becomes `reset {reason: 'cannotCalculateChanges'}` at the same position, so the window re-reads instead of missing it.
- **No mirror.** On the real path main holds no `latestState` and no copy of mail state. `window.compose` (and the legacy `window.compose` name) validates through main's own control connection (`Draft/get`); the ⌘Q warning asks `Outbox/pending`. The control connection is a `host` caller limited to `Draft/get`, `Outbox/pending`, `Session/get`, `Counts/get`. A grep test covers the real-path modules (`port.ts`, `engine-real.ts`, `engine-utility.ts`); it covers all of `src/main` once Phase 2 deletes the legacy code.
- **Host channel.** The utility process forwards `host` frames from the control session; main validates each with the contract's `HostEvent` schema and acts: `badge` (Dock count), `appearance` (theme), `openExternal` (only `https://accounts.google.com`, `signInURL`), `reminder` (banner), `parked` (desktop frame). `notify {pending}` needs nothing from main, because compose's claim loop runs beside the engine and posts each item through a `notify` request, which main validates with `NotificationItem` and shows. Compose's `openExternal` is a request with the same sign-in check.
- **Restarts.** `createEngineSupervisor` restarts a stopped engine after 500 ms and counts each stop with lifecycle.ts's own `createProcessState` (3 stops in 60 s park; the existing "stopped 3 times" dialog and Try again). A restarted engine gets every port-holding window a new port; its `hello` carries `{dbEpoch, seq}`. On the same `dbEpoch` the window keeps its state token and nothing is re-sent (no snapshot); a different `dbEpoch` gives the window a `reset {reason: 'dbEpoch'}`. Windows see `desktop` frames `engine: connecting | ready | parked`. Quit asks the engine to close its database and exit (killed after 3 s); main's single-instance data lock (`host/lock.ts`, taken in lifecycle.ts) covers the utility process, so compose's own `lock` option stays unset.
- **Smallest honest ports (recorded, not faked).** Real: SQLite (`openSqlite`), the network (`fetch`), `systemClock()` (new package export `@aplos/engine/clock/system`), the rotating log, the Google sign-in, and a keychain-sealed token vault in a new file `google-tokens.v2.enc.json` (the legacy engine's `google-tokens.enc.json` holds another format). Without a data key the vault refuses every write. The device id is a random UUID in `<dataDir>/device-id` (0600). Left out, so they answer `unavailable`: the engine's `KeyProvider` (the Gmail workflow channel stays off), `send`, `html`, `images`, `unsubscribe`, `calendar`, `rules`, `laneFor`, and the window layer's focus facts (`env` stays "no window, unfocused, on mains"). Settings' Google client is not read (`Settings/get` is still a stub).
- **Today's renderer on the real path.** It still loads and still calls `aplos:invoke`; main answers its native methods (close, quit, pop-outs) and refuses every engine call with "This screen is not on the new engine yet." Making the new renderer use `window.aplosEngine` is the UI lanes' work (ui-shell, days 11 to 13).
- **Not done here (open).** `Session/get`, `Session/changes` and `Blob/*` are still contract-3 stubs: over the port `Session/get` answers `notImplemented`, and the resume after a restart has no `Session/changes` catch-up yet. Blob handles: FR-6 makes every window and main one principal (`app`), so a second window that knows a handle id may read it; the transport only guarantees that a window never receives another window's replies or frames. The owner or contract-3 decides whether windows should be separate principals. Main does not yet re-check a window's trust on every message (only when a port is issued); a navigation revokes the port. Main modules outside the real path still read the legacy mirror and simply see nothing here: the startup window choice (`sessionFacts`, only with `APLOS_ONBOARDING_WINDOW=1`), the menu-bar sync line, the banner's send sound (silent), and the welcome window's `Setup/end`; each moves to `Session/get`, `Settings/get` or a host event when those exist.
- **Tests.** `apps/desktop/tests/eng-contract/{port,payload,real-path}.test.ts` (19 tests, real Node MessagePorts and the real composed engine, no Electron). Mutations, each red then restored: the subframe, DevTools-type and origin checks of `trustedCaller`, the engine-side and preload-side allowlists, a closed port that keeps its subscription and send path (against an engine session that keeps pushing after close), a revoke that does not detach, the 256 KB cap, the blob-route exemption, the UTF-8 count. The closed-port guards are redundant on purpose (session close, unsubscribe, closed flag, port close), so removing one alone stays green; the mutant removes both of the port's own guards. One headless `APLOS_TEST=1 APLOS_ENGINE=real` launch of the built app: a hidden window got its port, `Session/get` answered `notImplemented` over it, `Counts/get` and `Demo/load` answered, `Blob/put` with a transferred buffer reached the engine's validator, `Window/compose` was refused through `Draft/get`, a `SIGKILL` of the engine process reconnected the window on the same `dbEpoch` and seq with frames `state, desktop:connecting, desktop:ready` (no reset), the quit warning came from `Outbox/pending`, and the quit exited 0 in under a second with the data lock released.
- **OWNERS.** `apps/desktop/src/main/{engine-real,engine-utility}.ts` and `apps/desktop/tests/eng-contract/**` are added to eng-contract's row (Integrator approval on the commit); `main/lifecycle.ts` (eng-host) changed in five small places: wake, park count, quit warning, quit drain, boot hook.

## 2026-11-03 eng-contract contract-4: fixes from the independent Opus security review (VERDICT FIX)

The review found no port theft, allowlist bypass or leak. One MEDIUM and four LOWs are fixed here; two items are recorded, not built.

- **MEDIUM, fixed: a cancelled navigation revoked the port.** Main revoked a window's port on `did-start-navigation`, which Electron fires before `will-navigate`. A top-level link that windows.ts cancels and opens in the browser instead (any plain `<a href>`) still revoked the port: the page stayed loaded without one, preload had already asked, and every later call waited forever. Now `revokeOnNewDocument` (port.ts) revokes only on a committed main-frame navigation (`did-navigate`), `render-process-gone` and `destroyed`, plus the window's `closed`. A committed document runs a fresh preload, which asks again. The window side also notices a port closing under it (the DOM `MessagePort` `close` event, `WindowPortEnd.onClose`): its open calls fail `unavailable` at once and preload's `onLost` forgets its earlier ask, so the next call asks main for a new port. Tests: "a prevented navigation keeps the port; a committed one revokes it and the new document gets a fresh one" (red when revoking on `did-start-navigation`), and "a port that closes under the window fails its open calls at once and lets preload ask again".
- **LOW, fixed: release-grep suffix.** The whole-declaration match from contract-4 missed a declaration esbuild renamed on a name clash (`let hook2`, `function killpoint2`). It now allows a number suffix (`\d*(?![\w$])`), with a unit test (red without the suffix). Lane-Approved: eng-time.
- **LOW, fixed: window-to-engine caps.** A port now refuses a window's call while `MAX_IN_FLIGHT` (256) of its calls are open (`unavailable`, "Too many requests at once"), and arguments over `MAX_ARGS_BYTES` (4 MB; a draft's HTML is at most 1M characters) with `tooLarge` before they reach the engine. `Blob/put` (`uploadRoutes`) may carry up to 26 MB.
- **LOW, fixed: start timeout.** An engine that never answers `ready` and never exits is killed after `readyTimeoutMs` (default 60 s; a large migration may be slow but never silent forever). The kill is a stop like any other: it counts toward the 3-in-60-s park, and a restart follows otherwise. Test with manual timers.
- **LOW, fixed: sign-in URL.** `signInURL` now pins the path too: only `https://accounts.google.com/o/oauth2/v2/auth` (default port, no user info, any query) may be opened for the engine.
- **RECORDED, must fix before `send` is wired on the real path: the data lock can be released before the engine has exited.** lifecycle.ts's quit drain (`QUIT_DRAIN_MS`, 3 s) and the supervisor's `quitMs` (3 s) race: when the engine is slow to close, main can release `run/aplos.lock` while the utility process still has `mail.db` open, and a second launch could open it too. Harmless while the real path sends nothing; with the submission or send port wired, a second engine could double-send. Fix then: release the lock only after the engine's `exit` event, or after the kill plus a short wait. Trigger: wiring the submission/send port on this path.
- **RECORDED, accepted for now: blob principals.** Every window and main are one principal, `app` (FR-6, `principalOf`), so a window that knows another window's blob id may read it. Acceptable now because every window runs the same trusted bundle and mail frames get neither preload nor a port. Three conditions stay attached: blob and frame-token ids are unguessable (at least 128 random bits); listener and MCP callers never get the `app` principal (they are `client:<id>`); revisit before any lower-trust window (a different bundle, a plugin view, anything a mail frame can drive) gets a port.
- **Confirmed (my open question 2): secret and UI-only methods.** By design the window principal reaches the 6 secret methods the engine serves (`Account/connect`, `Account/updateSecret`, `Ai/removeKey`, `Ai/setKey`, `Workflow/code`, `Workflow/enable`) and the 14 UI-only methods; listeners never do (`checkAccess`). The seventh secret method, `Ai/pasteKey`, is a desktop method: main reads the clipboard, and the key never crosses a window's port.
- **Mutations (each red, then restored), round 2: 16 of 16 caught**: the 10 from contract-4 plus revoking on `did-start-navigation`, a client that ignores its port closing, no in-flight cap, no argument cap, no ready timeout, and a host-only sign-in check.

## 2026-11-03 prov-gmail: `drafts.list` is paged, and a draft is `removed` only on proof (Integrator end-to-end HIGH 2; #192)

- **Bug.** `listDrafts` read one page (500) and `settleDrafts` marked every tracked draft missing from it `removed`; with more than 500 drafts a draft that still exists made the engine delete a clean local draft (orphaning the Gmail copy) or raise a false "deleted on another device" conflict.
- **Fix (google/history.ts).** (1) `listDrafts` follows every `nextPageToken`, at most `DRAFT_LIST_PAGES` (20 pages = 10,000 drafts, 100 units); a repeated token or the bound leaves the listing `complete: false`. (2) `retainedDrafts`: an incomplete listing reports no removal at all (every tracked draft stays tracked; new drafts from the pages read are still reported). A complete listing still checks each missing draft with `drafts.get` (`fields=id,message(labelIds)`, 20 units), because a listing paged while drafts change can skip an item, and because real Gmail's `drafts.list` HIDES drafts in Trash while `drafts.get` still answers 200. A draft is gone only when the check shows a 404 with Gmail's `notFound` reason, or a message labelled TRASH or SENT or without DRAFT; alive when it is a live draft; anything else (another 404 reason, 403, 5xx, no labels) proves nothing and keeps it tracked. A pause (429) stops the step with the state untouched as before.
- **Check order and bounds.** Missing drafts whose message this very history page deleted are checked first (at most `DRAFT_DELETED_CONFIRMS` = 50), then the rest, least recently checked first, at most `DRAFT_CONFIRMS` = 10 per pass. A draft seen alive moves to the back of the tracked map (`DRAFTS_KEY` keeps insertion order), so a crowd of live drafts the listing hides can never starve the checks behind it; the unchecked ones are checked on the next pass that touches a draft. More than 50 drafts deleted on one page leave the rest tracked until such a pass.
- **Trade-off, accepted.** In an account with more than 10,000 drafts a new phone draft beyond the listed pages is not reported until the listing fits; nothing is ever deleted on that basis. Such an account is far outside the product's targets.
- **Confirmed (Integrator's question).** `deleteDrafts` defaults to ON (adapter.ts `deleteDrafts !== false`), guarded by the expected message id (`deleteDraft(id, expectedMessageId)`; without one nothing is deleted). The older HANDOFF line "OFF" was stale since #153 closed PORT GAP 3.
- **Emulator.** `drafts.list` now hides drafts in Trash unless `includeSpamTrash=true`, like Gmail (prov-emu, Lane-Approved).
- **Tests.** `tests/prov-gmail/draft-paging.test.ts` (11): 505 drafts (no false removal, second page read), a real deletion among 505, a listing that skips a live draft, an endless listing, a repeated token, a Trash-hidden draft, 12 hidden live drafts not starving a later deletion (at most 10 checks a pass), 13 drafts deleted on one page all removed, and a non-`notFound` 404 / 403 / 500 on the check keeping the draft. On the old one-page code only the 505-draft test and the two incomplete-listing tests are red; the others guard the later layers and are red under these mutations, each caught: any 404 gone, no TRASH check, no rotation, no deleted-first order, no cap, incomplete flag ignored, repeated-token check removed.

## 2026-11-03 — eng-contract: real-path LOW follow-ups

From the Integrator's review of #189 (REQUEST 11:31). The real path stays default OFF behind `APLOS_ENGINE=real`.

- **LOW-1, fixed: default-off exposes nothing new.** Preload used to expose `window.aplosEngine` and register its `aplos:engine-port` and `aplos:desktop` listeners in every window. Now main adds `--aplos-engine-port` (`ENGINE_PORT_ARG`) to `webPreferences.additionalArguments` only on the real path (`engineWindowArguments(realPath)` in windows.ts, eng-host, Lane-Approved), and preload calls `exposeEngineBridge({ contextBridge, ipcRenderer }, process.argv)`, which does nothing without it. The bridge moved from preload.ts into `host/transports/port.ts` (Electron injected), unchanged in behaviour, so it runs under node:test.
