Checked on the built app with a temporary headless Playwright Electron spec (`APLOS_TEST=1`, fresh temp dirs, not committed): without the variable `window.aplosEngine` is `undefined`; with `APLOS_ENGINE=real` it exists and `Counts/get` answers over the port.
- **LOW-2, fixed: `messageBytes` measures what the structured clone carries.** The replacer reads the raw value (`this[key]`), not the one `toJSON` returned: Map and Set entries count (they were `{}`), a RegExp counts its source, an Error its name, message and stack, and binary (`ArrayBuffer`, any view, a Node `Buffer`) counts its `byteLength` (a `Buffer` was measured as `{"type":"Buffer","data":[...]}`, about 3.5 times too large).
- **LOW-3, fixed: a byte budget per port, and a size check in preload.** A port refuses a call while the arguments of its open calls plus this one would pass `MAX_IN_FLIGHT_BYTES` (64 MB), with the in-flight refusal's shape (`unavailable`, "Too many requests at once"); a reply or a failure releases its bytes. Each port has its own budget. Preload (`createPortClient.call`) now refuses arguments over the method's cap (`MAX_ARGS_BYTES`, or `MAX_BLOB_MESSAGE_BYTES` for an upload route) with `tooLarge` before posting, so an honest page never copies them into the engine process. The engine side still measures only after the message is deserialised; a compromised renderer can still make one message that large arrive (the transport has no pre-deserialisation hook), but no longer hold more than 64 MB of open calls on its port.
- **LOW-4, fixed: tests for the broker's trust re-checks.** "connect refuses a window whose main frame no longer shows the app" and "an engine restart re-issues no port to a window that no longer shows the app" each go red when `!win.trusted()` is removed from `connect` or `reissue`.
- **RECORDED, corrected: the data lock released before the engine exited.** The 2026-11-03 entry above overstates it. The race is real (lifecycle.ts releases `run/aplos.lock` after the 3 s quit drain, possibly before the engine's utility process has closed `mail.db`), but outside test mode main also holds `app.requestSingleInstanceLock()` until the main process exits, and Chromium kills utility processes with main, so a second normal launch cannot start a second engine on the same data. Two writers are possible mainly under `APLOS_TEST=1` (no single-instance lock; a second test instance on the same `APLOS_DATA_DIR`) or with an orphaned engine that outlived a crashed main. Still to fix before `send` is wired on this path (release after the engine's `exit`, or after the kill plus a short wait); not built here.
- **Mutations (each red, then restored): 11 of 11 caught.** Bridge ungated; no `additionalArguments`; measuring the `toJSON` value; no Map/Set case; a `Buffer` measured through `toJSON`; no byte budget; bytes never released; one global budget; no preload size check; no trust check in `connect`; no trust check in `reissue`.
- **MEDIUM, fixed (independent Opus review of 0706bcf0): `messageBytes` could be made to exhaust memory.** The LOW-2 version replaced each Map or Set with a new array inside `JSON.stringify`, so a Map that contains itself never looked like a cycle. A raw envelope with a 100,000-entry self-containing Map (it clones in about 8 ms) ran the engine process out of heap for every window. A nested array that reuses its children (`m = [m, m]`, 30 deep) hung it. `messageBytes(value, limit)` is now an iterative walker: an explicit stack, no recursion, no `JSON.stringify` of containers. It counts each object once, as the structured clone copies a shared object once (a later reference counts one byte). An ancestor met again is a cycle and counts as infinite, as before. It stops with infinity once the total passes `limit` (dispatch, replies and preload all pass their cap) or more than `MAX_MEASURED_NODES` (1,000,000) values were seen. It never throws. A normal message measures exactly as before (its JSON text in UTF-8 plus binary bytes); Map, Set, RegExp, Error and binary keep LOW-2's convention. Tests (payload.test.ts): "a self-containing Map or Set of 100,000 entries is refused at once, without exhausting memory", "shared children are counted once, so a deep DAG returns at once", "cycles in plain objects and arrays are refused without hanging", "a huge sparse array or too many nodes is refused at once", "stops reading as soon as the total passes the limit", "a normal message measures exactly its JSON text in UTF-8, as before", "every caller stops measuring at its own cap", "a self-containing Map posted raw is refused tooLarge and the engine keeps serving". Against the old version the first and last ran out of heap, the DAG test timed out and the budget test failed; the cycle and exact-size tests are regression guards and passed on both. Mutations, 10 of 10 caught: no cycle check, shared objects walked again, no list budget, no Map budget, no early stop, no limit passed at dispatch, at replies or in preload, Map content ignored, binary not counted by bytes.
- **LOW, recorded, not built: reconnecting resets a window's in-flight budget.** A page that calls `aplos:engine-connect` in a loop gets a new port each time, and with it a fresh `MAX_IN_FLIGHT_BYTES`. Keeping the total per window id in `createPortHost` would not help on its own: closing a port closes its engine session, which aborts its running calls (`connection.ts`, `untilAborted`). Their promises reject at once, so the bytes are released while the method may still be running and holding its arguments. A real fix needs the session to report when the aborted work has actually finished, or main to rate-limit `aplos:engine-connect` per window. Revisit with the per-message trust re-check; it needs a compromised renderer (the honest preload asks again only after its port closes).
- **Fixed (Opus re-check of bdd527dd): three gaps in the walker.** (1) A boxed String (`new String('x'.repeat(8e6))`, cloned in about 8 ms) fell through to the object path, whose `Object.keys` listed every character before the budget check: 2.3 s and 660 MB on the engine's event loop. Boxed String, Number and Boolean are now leaves measured by their value (JSON's convention); a boxed BigInt is unmeasurable (infinite). Own keys are now gathered with a loop that stops once it passes the node budget, and a Set's size is checked before it is spread. (2) A typed-array view over a large buffer (`new Uint8Array(new ArrayBuffer(5e7), 0, 1)`) measured 2 bytes, but the clone copies the whole buffer: a view now counts its whole `buffer.byteLength`, and each buffer is counted once however many views (or bare references) share it. (3) Named properties on an array (`a.foo = ...`) were ignored, but the clone copies them; they now count like an object's keys. Tests (payload.test.ts): "a boxed String, Number or Boolean is a leaf measured by its value, at once", "a view counts its whole buffer, and a buffer shared by two views counts once", "named properties on an array count". All three were red on bdd527dd (2.3 s; 12 bytes for a 50 MB buffer; 14 bytes for a 4 MB named property). Mutations, each red: boxed values not leaves, a shared buffer counted twice, a view counting only its slice, named array keys ignored; the earlier walker mutations were re-run on the new code and all stayed red. The early-stopping key loop and the Set size pre-check only save allocation (the list budget would refuse the same input later), so no test can tell them apart; they are not mutation-tested.
- **Moved: the measurer is now `packages/contract/src/measure.ts` (`@aplos/contract/measure`).** port.ts passed the 800-line lint limit (`max-lines`, code lines only). The new file is inside eng-contract's `packages/contract/**`, has no zod and no Node import (the contract's portability test bundles it for a neutral platform), and port.ts re-exports `messageBytes` and `MAX_MEASURED_NODES`, so callers did not change. The built preload bundles it. A headless real-path launch (temporary spec, not committed) still answered `Counts/get` over the port and refused a 5 MB `Draft/set` in preload with `tooLarge`.
- **Fixed: a long string is refused before it is scanned.** The full `npm test` run on b19b1e8e failed the new boxed-String test by time (152 ms against 100 ms under full-suite load): measuring an 8M-character string still copied it through `JSON.stringify` and scanned it. A string's UTF-8 size is at least its UTF-16 length, so the walker now refuses a string (boxed or not) longer than the room left under `limit` before copying or scanning it. Test: the same boxed-String test now also requires a 100M-character plain string to be refused in under 50 ms; it is red when that check is removed.
## 2026-11-03 — eng-time: the 17:00 review of #194 (our own MIME through the real adapter)

- **MEDIUM: trailing line breaks no longer change a fingerprint.** The engine's MIME writer leaves the html part as `<p>x</p>\r\n` before the boundary (the line break belongs to the boundary), and the Gmail adapter's parser hands it back as part of the body, so our own byte-identical copy never passed the content proof and an update with a lost reply ended in a conflict about the owner's own text and then an orphan duplicate. `fingerprintOf` now hashes `html` without trailing CR/LF (a scan from the end, not a regex), on both sides. Existing stored fingerprints of drafts whose html ends in a line break would differ; the new engine path is not released, so nothing real is stored. Tests: the engine's real uploaded MIME (`draft-echo.test.ts`, the Gmail emulator and the adapter's own history pass): the copy is recognised as our echo, and a lost-reply create is adopted without a duplicate; both fail without the normalisation.
- **LOW: the version keep mine steps away from is shown.** After keep mine on a version that was never shown whole, the old copy stays on the provider and nothing showed it until another phone edit. It is now inserted as an incomplete remote draft (`showUnseen`, from the conflict's snippet fields, under a fresh id if the usual one is taken), so it is visible, refreshed by later reports, and a discard never deletes an incomplete draft's copy. **Test:** a real conflict survives an ack that names another copy (only an ack for the conflicting copy ends it); the `ownReport` mutation "clears any conflict" is now caught directly.
- Row-level helpers moved to `outbox/draft-rows.ts` (`draft-writes.ts` was past the 800-line limit).

## 2026-11-03 prov-gmail: #192 follow-ups (restored drafts, unknown checks, label rules tested)

- **A draft restored from Trash is news again.** `drafts.list` hides a trashed draft, so it is reported `removed` while in Trash and leaves the tracked map. A history record that removes TRASH from a message that is, or may be (no labels on the ref), a draft now runs the listing and reports it as a draft fact like a new one (`PageChanges.untrashed`; it is not a fetch candidate, so restored ordinary mail costs nothing extra). Before, it stayed invisible until edited elsewhere, and a dirty local copy kept by the owner left two copies in Gmail.
- **`unknown` check results rotate too.** A draft whose `drafts.get` keeps failing (403, 5xx, a 404 without `notFound`) goes to the back of the tracked map like a live one, so ten of them cannot hold the front and starve the checks behind them.
- **Label rules are tested:** a 200 without `labelIds` keeps the draft; labels without DRAFT, or with SENT, mean gone; TRASH was already covered.

## 2026-11-03 — eng-time: #194 round 2, the fingerprint hashes what the writer produces

- **MEDIUM:** the writer changes our text in two more ways than the last line break, and the adapter hands the changed version back: every CRLF and lone CR in the html becomes a line break of its own (`<p>a</p>\n<p>b</p>` returns as `<p>a</p>\r\n<p>b</p>\r\n`), and `headerSafe` squeezes runs of spaces in the subject (a trailing space does not survive either). A multi-line html body or a double-space subject therefore left a duplicate draft in the lost-reply case and, on a clean draft, replaced the owner's own text. `fingerprintOf` now hashes `html` with CRLF and lone CR turned into LF and the trailing breaks stripped, and the subject through `headerSafe(...).trim()`; every comparison goes through that one function, so no site is out of step. Tests: a unit test of what hashes the same and what must still differ (an inner break, a space in the html, other text or subject), plus two end-to-end tests through the real uploaded MIME, the Gmail emulator and the adapter's history pass (multi-line html with a lone CR and a double-space subject: recognised as our echo; a lost reply with the same content is adopted, no duplicate); all three fail with the previous fingerprint. Engine bundle 398,960 of 400,000 bytes (the fix fit).
- **LOW, noted:** a draft whose html is empty is never recognised as ours: the adapter reports no html for it, so nothing proves the copy and our own recipient-and-subject-only copy opens a conflict. That predates this PR and is the safe direction (a conflict, never a replace); proving it by subject and recipients alone is not done because a phone edit of those fields would then pass.

## 2026-11-03 — eng-contract: last Opus check of the message measurer (MERGE), one LOW recorded

- The final Opus re-check of the measurer (boxed values, whole view buffers, named array keys, the long-string refusal, the move to `@aplos/contract/measure`) found nothing blocking. **LOW, left:** an object KEY longer than the room left is still JSON-stringified before the total is compared with the cap (`{ ['x'.repeat(1e8)]: 0 }` costs about 100-200 MB of short-lived memory and some CPU before it is refused). The cost is linear in a value the process has already received and cloned, so it cannot hang or grow without bound; the fix is to pass the bytes still allowed down to `keyedContents` and refuse a longer key first. Not built: the real path is default off and the plumbing touches four functions for a bounded cost. **Comment note:** `ownKeys` builds `for...in`'s own key list before the room check, so its comment claims a little more than the loop does; on plain objects that list is no bigger than input already held.

## 2026-11-03 — eng-time: two LOW follow-ups of #194 (a correction and look-alike encoded words)

- **Correction to the empty-html note above.** A draft whose html is empty comes back from the Gmail adapter as `<p></p>`, not as "no html", so the copy never matches the empty body we uploaded and is never proven ours. In the lost-reply case that leaves a DUPLICATE draft (our own copy shown as another device's), and with unsent edits it can open a conflict about our own text; the earlier sentence said only "a conflict". It is still the safe direction (nothing is replaced or deleted) and predates #194; proving it by subject and recipients alone is still not done, because a phone edit of those fields would then pass. A fix would hash an empty body and `<p></p>` the same, with the reviewed proof otherwise unchanged; not built.
- **A subject, display name or other header text that looks like an encoded word is encoded.** `mime.ts` wrote plain printable ASCII raw, so a subject such as `=?UTF-8?B?SGk=?=` reached Gmail as typed and Gmail decoded it ("Hi"): the owner's characters were changed, and a header could be made to read as something else. `unstructured` and `displayName` now encode any value containing `=?` (`needsEncoding`, with the existing non-ASCII and long-word rules), so a reader decodes it back to exactly what was typed. An ordinary subject with `=` or `?` (`Is x=1 ok?`) is still written plain. Test: subject, To name and From name; it fails without the rule.

## 2026-11-03 — eng-host: the data lock is released only after the engine confirmed it closed

- `finishQuit` (`main/lifecycle.ts`) gave the engine at most 3 s (`QUIT_DRAIN_MS`) to close its database and then released the data lock whether or not it had: a stuck or failed close let the lock go while the engine process could still be writing, so a second instance could take the lock and open the same database. `closeThenRelease` (`host/lifecycle.ts`) now releases the lock only when the close resolved within the wait; a stuck or rejected close, or one that finishes after the wait, leaves the lock held and the quit goes ahead anyway. A lock that is not released names this process and is reclaimed as stale the moment the process is gone (the reclaim checks the pid and the boot time), and outside test mode `requestSingleInstanceLock` also stays held until main exits and Chromium takes the utility process with it, so the held lock costs nothing on a normal quit. This was the "before `send` is wired on this path" gate. Test: closed in time releases once; stuck, failed and late closes do not (the always-release mutation is red).


## 2026-11-03 — eng-host: the data lock waits for the engine process to exit (#203 Opus MEDIUM + LOW)

- **MEDIUM:** `supervisor.close()` resolved right after it killed a stuck engine, without waiting for the exit, so `closeThenRelease` saw a "close" and let the lock go about 3 s after the quit while the process could still be writing. `close()` now returns `'exited'` or `'stuck'`: it asks the engine to quit, kills it after `ENGINE_QUIT_MS` (3 s) and reports `'exited'` only when the process really went away, `'stuck'` if it is still there `ENGINE_EXIT_AFTER_KILL_MS` (2 s) after the kill. `realEngine.close()` rejects unless the result is `'exited'`, so `closeThenRelease` keeps the lock. `QUIT_DRAIN_MS` is 6 s, longer than both waits together, so a slow but real exit is still seen before the drain gives up (a test pins that inequality).
- **LOW:** `realEngine.close()` sets `closeRequested`, and `bootRealEngine` returns before it creates the supervisor if that is set (a quit that landed while `engineInit` was still running); the supervisor itself already refuses `start()` once closing (tested; the guard's removal is red).
- Tests (`tests/eng-contract/real-path.test.ts`): the supervisor wired into `closeThenRelease` as `finishQuit` does it, with a fake engine that ignores quit and kill: lock held (kill happened, no exit); exits just after the kill: released; exits when asked: released at once. The fake `manualTimers` now fires timers in time order, including ones set while advancing. Mutations red: `'stuck'` → `'exited'` after the kill, `QUIT_DRAIN_MS` back to 3 s, `start()` allowed while closing.

## 2026-11-03 prov-gmail / prov-emu: live probe `gmail.auth-results-shape` (tier R) and what its answers mean for #180

- **What it is.** A read-only probe of up to 200 recent Inbox messages (apps/desktop/scripts/live/probes/gmail-auth-results.ts). Metadata only, five named headers; the report is counts of structure from a fixed allowlist (new names added to `COUNT_KEYS` on purpose), so the runner's content-free check still holds. The verdict counts come from the real `list-auth.ts` fed the headers the sync would see; the order, quote, parenthesis and `header.b` counts come from a looser reading of the same value so an order the strict parser refuses is still seen.
- **`arcBeforeDkim > 0` or `shapeUnexpected > 0` on Google's own header:** `dkimRunFirst` (a dkim result only in the unbroken run straight after the authserv-id) would refuse real mail, and every such list message would be `authenticated: false` (safe: the one-click POST is not offered, the `open`/`compose` plan is). Fix the parser's order rule before the owner relies on one-click; a spoofed order would not be believed either way.
- **`headerBNotEight > 0`, `dkimNoHeaderB > 0`:** `signatureFor` matches on the `header.b` prefix and needs at least `minBodyPrefix` (6) characters; a shorter or absent value means no signature is matched, so the message is `false`. Change `minBodyPrefix` only with a security review.
- **`withQuote`, `withNestedParens > 0`, `notTokenizable > 0`:** Google echoes sender text into comments, so these are expected on a minority of mail and fail closed (`false`); a large share means the tokenizer's refusals cost real one-click offers, which is a product question, not a safety one.
- **`severalAuthResults > 0`:** only the topmost header is believed; confirms the first-header rule is the one that matters in real mail.
- **`withDara`, `withUnknownMethod`:** a new method kind is tolerated by the parser (it only reads dkim); counted so a format change shows up.
- **`falseNoListPair` high:** many newsletters send `List-Unsubscribe` without `-Post`; that is why `authenticated` is false for them and one-click is not offered (by design, RFC 8058).
- Usage and the meaning of each count are in plans/LIVE-RUNS.md.

## 2026-11-03 prov-gmail / prov-emu: live probe `gmail.draft-message-id-after-edit` (tier M) and what its answers mean for `isEcho`

- **What it is.** Two phases of one probe through the guarded runner (`--phase create` with `--confirm-mutating`, then the owner edits the one draft, then `--phase inspect`). The draft has no recipient, a fixed subject and a known Message-ID; the runner can only update a draft its own run made, so the API re-save happens inside `create`. The evidence holds 0/1 flags and counts only. The draft is left in Drafts because the guard refuses every draft delete in every tier.
- **`updatedKeptMessageIdHeader = 1`, `updatedMessageIdChanged = 1`, `updatedDraftIdKept = 1`:** Gmail keeps our Message-ID across our own API saves while giving the draft a NEW message id each time and keeping the draft id. This is what the engine already assumes for its own uploads (`drafts.ts` precondition on the message id; a Message-ID match is never proof by itself).
- **`messageIdIsOurs = 1` with `bodyEdited = 1` after the owner's edit** (a phone or web edit keeps our Message-ID): the engine's `isEcho` must NOT treat "internetId is ours" as an echo of our own upload. This is the case the end-to-end review HIGH 1 was about, and the engine now requires content proof (`holdsOurContent` / the fingerprint); the probe turns that from a safe assumption into a measured fact. Nothing changes in the adapter: it already reports the new message id and lets the engine decide.
- **`messageIdIsOurs = 0` after the edit** (the other client replaces the header): our Message-ID can no longer find a copy that was edited elsewhere, so the Message-ID lookup that adopts a lost-reply create (`drafts.ts`) must not rely on it after a foreign edit; the `supersedes`/fingerprint path already covers it. The `TODO(live)` comment at `google/drafts.ts` is then resolved as "Gmail clients rewrite it".
- **`draftFound = 0`:** the draft was not among the newest 25 (a busy account) or was removed; run `create` again.
- **#180:** unrelated to this probe; see the `gmail.auth-results-shape` note.
## 2026-11-03 — eng-contract: measurer LOW-A and LOW-B, and a limit on port asks (before the real path defaults on)

- **LOW-A: window arguments are measured with a bound of `MAX_WINDOW_NODES` (100,000) values, not a million.** `messageBytes(value, limit, maxNodes)` takes the bound; `port.ts` passes `MAX_WINDOW_NODES` for what a window sends (the engine side and the preload check). A million empty objects (3 MB on the wire, under the 4 MB cap) took 0.5 s to measure and a sparse array `a[999_990] = 0` about 100 ms, enough for a compromised renderer to stall the engine that serves every window; both are now refused in a few milliseconds, while a legitimate call (hundreds of values) is untouched and a big reply from the engine itself keeps the one-million bound.
- **LOW-B: a key longer than the bytes still allowed is refused before it is stringified.** `keyedContents` gets the bytes left (`limit - total`) and subtracts each key as it goes, so `{['x'.repeat(1e8)]: 0}` and 100 keys of a million characters each cost nothing (about 500 ms and 100 MB before).
- **A window may ask for a port at most 5 times in 10 seconds (`CONNECT_LIMIT`).** `aplos:engine-connect` had no limit, and each ask gave a fresh 64 MB in-flight budget; a reload asks once, so a loop of asks is not a page of ours. A sixth ask answers `unavailable`, issues nothing, and does not touch the port the window holds. Per window id, with an injectable clock for the test. The in-flight budget still resets with each real port (closing a port aborts that session's calls); with at most 5 ports in 10 s the exposure is bounded.
- Tests: `payload.test.ts` (nodes, sparse array, long keys, a raw 100,001-value message refused at the port, each red against the previous code) and `port.test.ts` (the connect limit).


## 2026-11-03 — eng-contract / eng-host: the open LOWs of #201 and #203 (before APLOS_ENGINE=real may be turned on)

- **#201 LOW: a hole in an array costs 4 bytes and no entry.** `arrayContents` (packages/contract/src/measure.ts) lists an array's own keys instead of reading every slot by index: only the slots that exist become entries, and the holes are counted from the length (`null`, 4 bytes each) so they still count against the byte limit. `a[99_990] = 0` is one entry (6-99 ms and 10 MB of heap before, under 1 ms now); a length past `MAX_MEASURED_NODES` is refused before any key is read; named properties on a sparse array are still found. A dense array costs what it did (the key pass was already there for the named properties). `new Array(1e9)` is refused by its length; the key listing stops at the room left (`ownKeys`), so a huge object or array never has all its keys listed.
- **#201 LOW: `Blob/put`'s 26 MB allowance is for its bytes only.** `messageBytes(value, limit, maxNodes, textLimit = limit)` takes a fourth bound for everything except binary payloads (the text of strings, keys, numbers; a buffer's `byteLength` is the part it does not count). `port.ts` passes `MAX_ARGS_BYTES` as the text bound for every window call (`argsBytes`, used by the served port and the preload check), so an upload keeps 26 MB for one binary view but a 26M-character string or key in its arguments is refused at 4 MB (150-200 ms to measure, against 4 ms to decode). The in-flight budget still counts the whole message.
- **#203 LOW-1: a `kill()` that throws is no uncaught error in main.** `stop(link)` in port.ts wraps it, for the quit wait and for the ready timeout; the 2 s wait for the exit event still follows, so a stuck engine still answers `'stuck'` (lock held) and a late exit still releases.
- **#203 LOW-2: source-shape guards for `closeRequested` and reject-unless-exited** (real-path.test.ts): the real `close` sets `closeRequested` first, awaits the supervisor and throws unless the result is `'exited'`; `bootRealEngine` returns when the quit began; `finishQuit` hands `realEngine.close()` to `closeThenRelease`.
- **#203 LOW-3: no startup-error dialog while a quit is in progress.** The start-up `catch` in main/lifecycle.ts returns when `closing` is set (the quit is finishing by itself); guarded by a source-shape test.
- Tests are mutation-checked: each one goes red when its change is reverted (list in the thread report). Not done: pruning the `asked` map on window close (the optional #201 item).

## 2026-11-03 prov-emu: #202 follow-ups (one probe draft at a time, `--phase` runs only phased probes)

- **One probe draft at a time.** `inspect` used to judge the newest of several probe drafts, so an older edited-or-not draft could report `bodyUnchanged=1` for the wrong one. Now `create` stops (`probeDraftsExisting`, status fail, nothing created) when a probe draft is among the newest 25, and `inspect` gives no verdict (fail, `draftsMatching` > 1, no `messageIdIsOurs`/`bodyUnchanged`). The owner discards old probe drafts by hand (the guard refuses every draft delete).

- **Tests.** Create-stop and two-draft no-verdict, inspect sends only GETs and leaves every draft unchanged, the phase filter (each red under its mutation).

## 2026-11-04 prov-emu: an empty 200 is "no items", not an error (live tier M probe)

- **Bug.** `requestJson` (scripts/live/probes/http.ts) threw class `other` on a 200 with an empty body. Gmail answers a masked `drafts.list` with no drafts (the normal state of the spare account) with exactly that, so the first live tier M run failed before any write on its `drafts.list` pre-check.
- **Fix.** A 200 with an empty body, JSON `null` or `{}` returns `{}` with `empty: true`, and the probes report it as `emptyAnswers=1` (new count name), so the evidence tells ok-empty from a failure. A 200 whose body is not JSON, or is JSON but not an object, now fails with its own class `bad-body` (new class name) instead of `other`; no body text reaches the error or the evidence.
- **Tests.** The draft probe's `create` reaches the write against an empty body, `{}` and `null` with zero drafts (and `inspect` reports `draftFound=0`); non-JSON text and a JSON list are `bad-body`, nothing is created and no body text is in the evidence. All red on the old http.ts.

- **Decision (owner, via the orchestrator and the Integrator's STATUS DECIDED of 2026-11-04 20:00):** the neutral engine bundle budget rises from 400,000 to 450,000 bytes **minified**. `BUDGET_BYTES` in `packages/engine/tests/portability.test.ts` and the `size.engine-bundle` row of `budgets.json` say 450,000; plan 10's engine-bundle paragraph and the lane plan carry a dated note; older text (the 2026-10-28 freeze entries, risk tables, historical acceptance lines) is left as written. Small and reversible; **450,000 is the last raise without a new decision.** It stays a budget, not a licence: measure each addition and stop at it.
- **Lazy demo data, no behaviour change:** `Demo/load` and `Demo/reset` read `ai/demo/data.ts` (the original demo mailbox, 11,268 bytes minified, first-run only) through `import()` (`lazyDemoWrites` in `ai/demo/load.ts`), awaited before any commit so `Demo/reset`'s two commits never wait on a load in between. The two fixed demo ids moved into `load.ts` as a literal so nothing needs the dataset until the call; a test pins them to `DEMO_ACCOUNTS`. The portability test now bundles with code splitting and counts the core chunks against the budget, reports the lazy chunk, and fails if the dataset is not a chunk of its own holding nothing else (a static import folds it back into the core: the mutation is red). **Caveat:** the packaged `engine.cjs` (`apps/desktop/scripts/build-main.mjs`) is one file, so on desktop these bytes still ship; they are just evaluated on first use. The saving is real where chunks load lazily (a phone build) and is the measured startup budget, not the installed size. `scripts/audit/measure-budgets.mjs` (plat-ci) still counts the whole minified bundle including the dataset, so its figure reads about 11 KB higher than the test's; left as is (conservative).

Per module (bytesInOutput, core): sync 87,006; outbox 77,213; store 66,564; views 65,302; calendar 20,318; contract (`packages/contract`) 18,516; methods 13,923; clock 11,429; diag 8,352; ai 6,873 (without the lazy dataset); runtime 6,721; ports 1,972; selftest 1,532; index 222; the rest of the 387,869 (1,926) is the test's own export glue.

## 2026-11-03 — eng-contract: #206 LOW-1 (an array longer than the room is refused before its keys are built)

- **Regression fixed:** `arrayContents` checked only `MAX_MEASURED_NODES` (1,000,000) before `ownKeys`, whose `for...in` builds every index key of a dense array before the room check can stop it: 600,000 zeros (3.6 MB, refused) cost 58 ms and 999,999 zeros up to 250 ms and ~35 MB of keys, the stall the #201 LOW named. The check is now `list.length > Math.min(room, MAX_MEASURED_NODES)`, so a window argument longer than its node room is refused on the length alone; a sparse `a[99_990] = 0` is still one entry (it needs room for its length). The hole test now bounds with `MAX_WINDOW_NODES`, and a Proxy test proves a 600,000-slot array lists no key (red with the old check).
- **Nit taken:** `stop()` reports a kill that threw through the supervisor's optional `onKillFailed` (main logs the error name), because an engine that never becomes ready and cannot be killed leaves `start()` waiting with no dialog. A test counts the report (red without the call).
- **Backlog, not built (older, not from #206):** `leafBytes` runs `JSON.stringify` on each `Date` (99,000 Dates, 891 KB, take 270-440 ms): a valid Date could count a flat 26 bytes and an invalid one 4; and 99,999 dense zeros cost 42-127 ms, so a lower node cap for window arguments (for example 50,000) would meet the 50 ms target everywhere.

## 2026-11-04 prov-emu / prov-gmail: probe v2, an emulator that rewrites draft Message-IDs, and which paths depend on OUR Message-ID

- **Probe v2** (`gmail.draft-message-id-after-edit`): per-read salted hash plus shape of the Message-ID (never the value), flags for stability across our API save and the owner's phone edit, a custom `X-Aplos-Probe` header and whether it survives, and read-only `rfc822msgid:` search counts for our id and for the id Gmail reports; the create phase's hashes go to a 0600 state file outside the repo (allowlisted keys and values only, `state.ts`). Usage and every count are in plans/LIVE-RUNS.md.
- **Emulator.** `draftIdentity: 'rewritten'` (and `dropDraftHeaders`) makes the emulator behave like the live finding: every draft the API saves gets a new `<hex@mail.gmail.com>` Message-ID; inserts and sends keep theirs. `APLOS_EMU_DRAFT_IDENTITY=rewritten` switches it on for any suite. `drafts.list` now honours `q=` and hides Trash (it ignored `q` before, which hid every Message-ID lookup bug).
- **What fails when Gmail rewrites our Message-ID (run on revamp): 40 prov-gmail tests, 0 in eng-host, prov-gov and the engine's Gmail kill-point matrix (214 + 64 pass).** The engine paths and adapter paths that DEPEND on our id surviving a save, for engine to redesign (an id of ours would have to travel somewhere Gmail keeps, such as the draft id plus the content fingerprint):
  1. **Adopting a lost-reply create** (`google/drafts.ts` create lookup: `drafts.list q=rfc822msgid`, the newest-drafts scan, the all-mail `messages.list`): it finds our copy by Message-ID, so after a lost reply under rewriting nothing is found and the retry creates a SECOND draft (15 tests in drafts-lookup.test.ts, 5 in drafts.test.ts).
  2. **`DraftUpload.supersedes` replacement** (drafts.ts + draft-compare.ts, after a lost create the next edit replaces that copy): found by the superseded Message-ID, so it is never found and a duplicate is left (11 tests in drafts-supersedes.test.ts).
  3. **Echo recognition** (`draft-echo.test.ts`, engine `isEcho` / `adoptCopy` with `internetId`): our own copy's `internetId` is no longer ours, so it falls to content proof only (4 tests; the engine already requires content proof after #194, the tests that expect the Message-ID to match are what fail).
  4. **The opt-in `drafts.send` route** (`google/send.ts`, `useDraftsSend`, OFF by default; 5 tests in send.test.ts): it assumes Gmail keeps the client's Message-ID onto the sent mail (the comment at send.ts about `drafts.send`) and then finds the sent copy with `findSent` by the pinned Message-ID. Under rewriting the sent copy carries Gmail's id, so the pin check and `findSent` cannot recognise it and an acknowledged send could be treated as not sent. The default MIME route (`messages.send`) is not affected.
  5. **PORT GAP 4** (a create-path conflict names the copy's own Message-ID as `internetId`): it still reports what the copy carries, which is now Gmail's, not ours.
  Not affected: the history path (it reports whatever message id and header Gmail has), the MIME send path (`messages.send` keeps the Message-ID), the Gmail kill-point matrix, the engine overlay, eng-host and prov-gov.
- **Cross-check with engine's assessment (PR #213, plans/engine/draft-identity.md).** The failing emulator tests are the adapter-side mirror of its table: lost-reply create adoption and create-conflict search = rows 5 and 6, `supersedes` = row 7, echo by `internetId` = rows 2 and 3, the `drafts.send` route = rows 8 and 9. Its measurements a, b and c are in the probe (stability flags, custom-header flags, search counts); for b the probe now measures BOTH `X-Aplos-Probe` and `X-Aplos-Draft` (the name its proposal would carry the draft id in, `engineHeaderKept*`). Its measurement d (does `messages.send` of the pinned MIME keep OUR Message-ID on the Sent copy, which decides `findSent`) needs a tier S-self send to the agent's own address and is not in this probe: providers-3 will build it (`gmail.send-message-id`, S-self) on the Integrator's go, because it sends one mail to the agent account itself.
- **What the live probe decides.** If `messageIdStableAcrossApiSave = 0` on real Gmail, items 1-4 are real bugs for the real path, not test artifacts (the first live run already showed Gmail replaced the id). If `customHeaderKeptAfterApiSave = 1`, the engine could carry its own id in `X-Aplos-Probe`, but the `rfc822msgid:` search is the only header search Gmail offers, so a copy could be found only by reading candidate drafts with `drafts.get`; the `searchDraftsReportedCreate` / `searchMessagesReportedCreate` counts say whether the id Gmail reports is findable at all (and after how long: compare `Create` with `Phone`).
## 2026-11-03 — eng-contract: impact of Gmail not keeping our draft Message-ID (assessment, no code)

- Live finding (Integrator 2026-11-04 23:01): a Gmail draft does not keep the Message-ID we upload; the draft id is stable across API saves and the message id changes on every save. The full path-by-path table, what survives and the proposed redesign are in `plans/engine/draft-identity.md`. Summary: nothing loses mail or overwrites or deletes a copy that is not proven ours (the content proof never relied on the Message-ID); what breaks is recognition of our own copy when the upload's answer was lost or arrives after a history pass: a visible DUPLICATE (lost-reply create adoption, create-conflict adoption, `supersedes`/`earlier`) or a spurious conflict between identical texts (`isEcho` rule 2, `explainedByAttempt`). Gmail sending is safe today (`useDraftsSend` is off, the engine records its own id from the answer), but `findSent` rests on an unverified assumption (`send.ts` TODO(live) 2) that Gmail keeps our Message-ID on `messages.send`.
- Proposed (not built; real path off): (1) for updates, the copy's identity is the provider draft id plus the content proof, not the Message-ID; (2) an optional `copyInternetId` in upload results so the engine also remembers what the provider shows; (3) the Gmail adapter identifies lost-reply creates through a custom header token if the probe shows Gmail keeps it, else by content in a bounded Drafts scan; (4) measure `messages.send` with the owner's approval before anything relies on `findSent` for Gmail.

## 2026-11-04 prov-emu: tier S-self probe `gmail.send-message-id` (engine measurement d)

- **What and why.** One `messages.send` of our MIME to the agent account's own address, then a metadata read of the Sent copy: is our Message-ID still there (hash-free: shape code and a 0/1 flag), did our `X-Aplos-Draft` header survive, and does `rfc822msgid: in:sent` find the mail under our id and under the id Gmail shows (retried up to 4 times for index lag). It decides item 4 of plans/engine/draft-identity.md: `findSent`'s Message-ID search and `useDraftsSend`.
- **Guard.** Unchanged and enough: S-self `messages.send` already requires a JSON body of exactly `raw` (and `threadId`) whose every recipient is the account itself; deletes stay refused. The one difference from the other probes: a recipient is needed, so the probe reads the account's own address from the profile inside the probe, writes it into the single `To:` header and does nothing else with it (never in counts, errors, logs, state or evidence; a test asserts the evidence holds no address). If the Integrator prefers that probes never hold the address, the runner could pass a send helper instead; that is a runner change, not made here.
- **Safety bounds.** One send per day (a same-day probe message makes the run fail without sending), no retries of the send, the message stays (no deletes), fixed subject and one-line body.
- **What each answer means.** `sentKeptMessageId = 1`: `findSent` by our Message-ID is sound and the MIME send path needs no change (rows 8 and 9 of the engine table stay safe). `0` with `sentFoundByRfc822msgidGmails = 1`: Gmail rewrites on send too; `findSent` must search by the id the send answer reveals (the send response carries Gmail's message id, from which the Message-ID can be read) or by subject/time with content proof; an uncertain send stays uncertain until then. `sentHeaderKept = 1` means an `X-Aplos-Draft` token could identify the Sent copy.
- **Emulator.** New `sendIdentity: 'rewritten'` option (env `APLOS_EMU_SEND_IDENTITY`), default kept.

## 2026-11-05 prov-emu: #214 LOWs (state written first and tied to the draft, stricter state values and file handling)

- **False 0s from an old state.** `create` now saves its state right after the API save and header reads, BEFORE the searches, and stores a salted hash of the draft id (`draftIdHash`). `inspect` treats a state file whose draft-id hash does not match the draft it found as missing (`stateMissing=1`, no stability flag), so a create that died before saving can no longer make `messageIdStableAcrossPhoneEdit` read 0 against the previous run's hashes. A state file from before this field (the one a live run may already hold) has none to compare and is used as it was.
- **`state.ts`.** The folder is forced to 0700 (`mkdir` leaves an existing one alone); the temp file is removed first and created with `wx` so a planted symlink is never written through; values are checked per key (salt exactly 32 hex, `...Hash` exactly 16, `...Shape` a shape word), so a 16-hex Gmail message id fits only a hash key and is refused anywhere it could be mistaken for one by name.
- **Doc.** A Message-ID absent on both reads counts as stable (read the flag with the shape code).
- **Tests.** The failed-search create, the stale-state inspect, the existing-folder / symlink / per-key value rules; each red under its mutation (state saved after the searches, no stale check, no chmod, symlink followed, loose hash).

## 2026-11-03 — eng-contract: draft identity without the Message-ID (items 1 and 2 of plans/engine/draft-identity.md)

- **Why:** real Gmail shows its own Message-ID for a draft and changes it with every save, while the draft id stays (live probe, Integrator NOTICE 2026-11-04 23:01). Integrator GO 23:51 for the two engine-side items.
- **1. The draft id names the copy.** `sameDraft(row, w)` (the row holds a copy and the fact is about that very `remoteDraftId`) is new in `draft-proof.ts`. `namedByDraftId` = `sameDraft` AND the fact's Message-ID is one this row never used (not in `wrote`, not the running upload's, not `remote_internet_id`). Then `explainedByAttempt` accepts the running upload's content without a Message-ID match, and `provenOurs` accepts the running upload's content and, **only while an upload is running**, that of any attempt set aside (`meta.prints`, at most 5); `supersedesOwn` (moves the base) and `isEcho` rule 2 use it, and a copy under an unknown Message-ID is never an echo for being merely "unchanged" (`holdsOurContent` alone does not qualify). The content proof stays mandatory everywhere. **Two conditions keep another device's edit from passing as ours:** (a) the "never used" condition: a provider that KEEPS our Message-ID shows the id of the very attempt, which names that attempt (keyed `prints[id]`), so a phone edit restoring older content there is still another device's edit; (b) the "upload running" condition (Opus review MEDIUM): with nothing running, a stale phone save that is byte-identical to an older upload of ours is NOT our echo (before this review it moved the base and left Gmail and Aplos holding different texts, both looking synced); it goes the ordinary way (a clean draft takes it, a dirty one asks). Residual, accepted: while an upload runs, a phone save that restores one of our last five contents is taken as its echo and the base moves; the running upload's own base precondition then meets the newer copy and raises a visible conflict.
- **2. `copyInternetId` (optional, additive on `DraftSaveResult` `saved`, `v:1`).** The Message-ID the provider shows for the copy it just stored. `recordSaved` passes it to `op: 'synced'`, which remembers it in `wrote` next to the upload's Message-ID and the message id; the stale-ack branch also adds `prints[copyInternetId]`. A later report under it with the content of our last synced save is the keyed echo (`holdsOurContent`). `remote_internet_id` stays the upload's own id (the send path reuses it). An ordinary save does not touch `prints`, so older attempts are not pushed out. Adapters that keep our id, or did not read it, leave the field out: nothing changes for them.
- **Not done (items 3 and 4, wait for the live answers):** the Gmail adapter's identity for lost-reply creates (custom header token or a bounded content scan) and `findSent`. A lost-reply CREATE on Gmail still makes a visible duplicate until item 3. **Seen while testing:** a lost answer to an update followed by another edit makes the next upload meet the copy that landed (stale base) and raise a conflict; that is the existing safe direction, not changed here.
- **Tests:** `outbox/drafts.gmail-id.test.ts` against a fake provider that shows its own Message-ID (`state.gmailIds`, optionally `reportCopy`): lost answer then history under Gmail's id (echo, base moves); an earlier attempt's copy while a newer upload runs; a phone edit under an unknown id (conflict); a phone edit restoring older content under a known id (conflict); `copyInternetId` makes a bare report an echo and without it the same report goes the ordinary way; a unit test of `provenOurs` for the running-upload and known-id conditions. Mutations red: `namedByDraftId` off, "never used" dropped, `copyInternetId` ignored, recent prints dropped, the running-upload condition dropped, `remote_internet_id` ignored. Engine bundle about +700 B of 450,000 (388,5xx).

## 2026-11-03 — eng-contract: the two #215 follow-ups (a held-upload test, WROTE_KEEP 30)

- **Test gap (Integrator's Opus review of #215):** `explainedByAttempt` accepting the draft id without the content check turned only one unrelated test red. New test in `drafts.gmail-id.test.ts`: an upload is held open with a gate, a phone edit arrives under the same draft id with a new Message-ID and other content, and the draft shows a conflict while `remote_message_id` stays put (the running upload would otherwise overwrite the phone's text). Red when `explainedByAttempt` stops comparing content; the other tests stay green, so this is the one that guards it.

## 2026-11-05 prov-emu: #216 LOWs (send probe: fail-closed and no-retry pinned, the duplicate check also counts the fixed Message-ID)

- **Tests.** A server error on the duplicate-check search sends nothing; a 503 or a dropped connection on `messages.send` is exactly one POST and `fail`. The mutations `.catch(() => 0)` on the search and "retry once on error" each turn them red.
- **Duplicate check.** `probeMessagesExisting` is the larger of the subject-search count (each hit's Subject compared exactly) and an `rfc822msgid:<today's fixed id>` count, so a subject Gmail tokenises differently no longer defeats the refusal. Both searches lag Gmail's index by seconds, so LIVE-RUNS.md tells the operator to check Sent by hand before any rerun after a `network` or `server` failure.
## 2026-11-03 prov-gmail: the `X-Aplos-Draft` identity token (item 3 of plans/engine/draft-identity.md, Gmail adapter side)

- **Why.** The live draft probe showed that Gmail gives every draft saved through the API its own `<...@mail.gmail.com>` Message-ID, while a custom header survives create and API save. The engine (#215) accepts an opaque `internetId` and an optional `copyInternetId`. This is the adapter half of engine's spec (STATUS 2026-11-03 engine NOTICE, accepted by the Integrator 2026-11-05 01:30). No change to `packages/engine` or `packages/contract`.
- **What changed (apps/desktop/src/host/providers/google).** (1) Every draft upload carries `X-Aplos-Draft: <DraftUpload.internetId>` as its first header (`draft-token.ts` `withDraftToken`). `checkDraftMime` now checks the bytes that will actually be uploaded: `x-aplos-draft` is on its allow-list, appears at most once, and must equal `internetId`. The size cap applies to those bytes. The engine's draft MIME and the pinned send MIME are unchanged. (2) A copy's IDENTITY is the token when the copy carries exactly one token matching ``^<[A-Za-z0-9.!#$%&'*+/=?^_`~-]{1,256}@[A-Za-z0-9.-]{1,253}>$`` (after trimming; `TOKEN_SHAPE` in `draft-token.ts`), else the Message-ID header it shows, as before. Every draft read reports this identity: the history facts (`draft-body.ts`, `RemoteDraftFact.internetId`), the create lookup and its conflicts (`remoteInternetId`), and the `supersedes` proof. Anything malformed, duplicated or missing falls back to the Message-ID. (3) Content comparison (`draft-compare.ts` `draftContent` / `sameDraftContent` / `draftHash`) leaves out exactly the two identity headers of the top header block, `Message-ID` and `X-Aplos-Draft`, wherever they sit (Gmail moves its Message-ID to the top), and compares the identity separately (`rawIdentity`). No other header is left out. Line endings and boundaries are handled as before. A copy without a token compares equal to the same bytes with one. `DraftAttempts` hashes that content and stays keyed by the identity, so the recorded-upload proof is exactly as strict as before. (4) After a successful create or replace there is ONE `drafts.get` metadata read (20 units). When the Message-ID it shows differs from `internetId` (and the read shows the same message id) it is returned as `copyInternetId`. A failed read, or one showing another message id, leaves the field out and never fails the save. A lookup hit uses the metadata it already read.
- **Lookup order and units for a create.** (a) `drafts.list q=rfc822msgid:<id> in:drafts` (5, plus 20 per hit read); (b) the newest 10 drafts without `q` (5, plus 20 per read), with `metadataHeaders ['X-Aplos-Draft','Message-ID']`, matched by identity. On Gmail this is where the token finds our copy. (c) `messages.list rfc822msgid` across all mail (5, plus 20 per read). Only when this process recorded an attempt under the same `internetId` (a retry after a possibly-landed create, `DraftAttempts.has`) and (a) to (c) found nothing: (d) the newest 10 of `in:sent OR in:trash` (5, plus at most 10 x 20), where a SENT/TRASH message carrying our identity is `gone`; (e) the content-matched fallback: each token-less draft from (b) (same thread when the upload names one) is read raw (60 each, at most 10). It is adopted as `saved` only when its message id is unchanged and its content equals the upload recorded for this local draft under this `internetId`. Worst case for a retry that finds nothing: about 1,420 units. A first create costs what it did before, plus 20 for the `copyInternetId` read. `supersedes` gets (d) as well when one of its ids was recorded, but no content fallback (engine's spec: content-matched adoption is for a lost-reply create only). Every hit in (a)/(b) is still verified as before: raw read, identity exactly ours, content equal, still a DRAFT, the message id unchanged. The `supersedes` proof (listed identity, thread, recorded upload) is unchanged.
- **When Gmail drops the header (the pending phone-edit question).** The copy shows only Gmail's id. A lost-reply create is then adopted only through (e), against the recorded bytes. A phone edit (different content), or a copy nobody in this process recorded (after a restart), is never adopted: the create goes ahead and leaves a visible duplicate. A later revision cannot prove the earlier copy and also makes a duplicate, never a replace. Nothing is ever deleted or overwritten without proof. History facts then carry Gmail's id, and engine #215 (draft id plus content proof, `copyInternetId`) recognises our updates. Measured: under `APLOS_EMU_DRAFT_IDENTITY=rewritten APLOS_EMU_DROP_DRAFT_HEADERS=X-Aplos-Draft`, 25 of the older draft tests fail, every one in the safe direction (a new create where they expected a conflict, a replace or `gone`; 4 echo tests where the engine takes the same-content copy as theirs).
- **Known limit.** Search cannot find a custom header. A copy outside the newest 10 drafts that the `rfc822msgid` search cannot see is created again on Gmail (a visible duplicate), where a provider that keeps the Message-ID answers index lag through (c). A copy a phone sent or trashed a moment ago may not be listed by (d) yet, which is the same limit as (c).
- **`useDraftsSend` (off) gate.** Both send routes upload the pinned bytes as the media. Those bytes never carry the token (the engine does not write it and send.ts's header allow-list refuses it), so with live-gate assumption (1) holding, no token leaves in sent mail and nothing needs stripping. If the live gate shows `drafts.send` sends the STORED draft instead, the token would leave with it, so do not switch the route on (stripping would mean rewriting the stored copy first). The gone-draft Sent lookup of that route still searches by Message-ID and cannot find a copy a phone sent on Gmail; that case stays uncertain (nothing sent twice). Noted at `SendOptions.useDraftsSend`.
- **What is NOT changed.** The replace precondition (message id), `deleteDraft`'s guard, the `supersedes` proof, `findSent`, the send MIME, the engine and the contract. Tests: new `tests/prov-gmail/drafts-token.test.ts`. Older tests were updated only where they asserted the exact uploaded bytes or request counts (the token line, the extra metadata read) or modelled a phone edit as keeping our Message-ID (it now keeps the token too). Two tests are pinned to `draftIdentity: 'kept'` because they test the Message-ID search paths (the all-mail index-lag case, and the `drafts.send` route's Sent lookup). The send rig replays `drafts.send` with the emulator's send identity instead of the draft-save rewrite.

## 2026-11-05 prov-gmail: `findSent` by an `X-Aplos-Send` token (design: plans/providers/findsent-token.md, #224)

- **Why.** The live send probe showed Gmail replaces the Message-ID of a message sent with `messages.send`, keeps a custom header, and lists the copy in its search only under Gmail's id and a few seconds late. `findSent` by our Message-ID could therefore never confirm a Gmail send.
- **What.** `sendSubmission` uploads the engine's pinned bytes with one extra first header `X-Aplos-Send: <payload.internetId>` (`withSendToken`, written only when it is a well-formed token; the pinned bytes are unchanged and `pinnedMime` still refuses that header inside them). `findSent` accepts a message with the SENT label, no DRAFT label, and exactly one `X-Aplos-Send` equal to `internetId` (or, where Gmail kept it, our Message-ID), found by the `rfc822msgid:` search or by the SENT-label listing (not search-backed), both read with metadata `['Message-ID','X-Aplos-Send']`. The existing time window and "an incomplete answer is thrown, never absent" rules are unchanged. No engine change.
- **No look-again inside the call (a change from the design note, on purpose).** The note proposed up to 3 more passes 3 s apart. It is not built: `findSent` makes ONE pass. The engine already asks again after 60 s before it asks the owner (engine.ts: "findSent, once more after 60 s, then ask the user"), which covers Gmail's few-seconds index lag (live: the 4th try, about 3 s apart); and a sleep inside the adapter call stalls the engine's dispatch and made the engine's Gmail kill-point matrix (child processes on a fake clock) stop settling ("did not settle: state=uncertain"). An error in the pass is thrown, never absent. If the engine's re-check cadence changes, an adapter-side wait must be revisited. The engine still never retries an uncertain send by itself.
- **Visible to recipients.** The header travels with the mail: recipients can see `X-Aplos-Send: <random@domain>` under "Show original". It is the submission's own random Message-ID value and reveals nothing about the owner or the content.
- **Not changed.** `useDraftsSend` stays OFF; its gone-draft check (`chooseRoute`) still searches by Message-ID only, so a copy a phone sent stays uncertain there (a gate item, not enabled). Mail sent before this change has no token and can only come back "unknown" (the owner decides). Mail sent from another device never carries the token.
- **Tests.** `send-token.test.ts` (10 at the time, 17 now, emulator `sendIdentity: 'rewritten'`): the token on the wire as the first header with the pinned bytes unchanged, a malformed id writes none, found by token under a rewritten id, never without a token, never another submission's token, never a DRAFT or a non-SENT message, a doubled header is no match, one pass (a copy the index shows only later is absent now and found by the next ask, no sleeping, an error is thrown); the pinned bytes refuse `X-Aplos-Send`. Mutations red: token not written, token ignored, no label check, duplicate header counted. Existing send tests expect the token line on the wire; the older findSent tests are unchanged.

## 2026-11-05 prov-gmail: #221 follow-ups (extra lookups only after an attempt that may have landed, test gaps, duplicate tokens)

- **"May have landed", not "was recorded".** `DraftAttempts.record` still happens BEFORE the create request (the bytes are the proof an earlier copy needs), but the Sent/Trash scan (up to 205 units) and the content fallback (up to 600 units of raw reads) now run on a retry only after an attempt that `markMayHaveLanded`: the create was answered, or it ended uncertain (any error that is not a classified definite refusal). A definite refusal (a 403, a paused 429: nothing landed) leaves the retry on the cheap lookups, about 615 units instead of about 1,420. Recording the same attempt again keeps its flag. The governor's `PausedError` branch is defensive and untested: a pause can only reach the create through the lookups before it, which throw before the record.
- **Duplicate tokens (optional item).** Two drafts can show one token (a phone duplicate, a copied header). The create lookup now judges each identity match in turn and takes the first that proves ours (same message id, exact token in the raw bytes, equal content); an extra raw read happens only after a mismatch (at most `CANDIDATES` matches). When none proves ours, the first mismatch is the answer, a conflict naming it, and nothing is written. Before, the first match in list order decided, so a phone-edited duplicate listed before our unchanged copy gave a conflict about the duplicate.
- **Tests.** The pinned send refuses a header block carrying `X-Aplos-Draft` (a row in the header-injection table; allowing it turns that row red), the content fallback skips a draft carrying a DIFFERENT well-formed token even when its content equals the recorded upload (made with the emulator's header dropping off for that one draft), the extra lookups' costs after a definite refusal / an uncertain end / an answered attempt, and both duplicate-token cases. Each is red under its mutation.
- **Note for the record.** If the owner sends our draft from the Gmail app and Gmail keeps custom headers on send, recipients could see `X-Aplos-Draft: <random hex@domain>` under "Show original": random, reveals nothing.

## 2026-11-05 prov-gmail: #226 follow-ups (tests, the size after the token, the rewritten kill-point matrix)

- **The pinned-bytes refusal test is back** (`x-aplos-send` in `PINNED_HEADERS` made everything green before): a row in the header-injection table. If the header got through inside the pinned bytes the upload would carry it twice, the one-header rule would reject every copy, and every uncertain send would end at "Send anyway".
- **Two weak tests strengthened.** The SENT requirement: the search is widened so an INBOX message carrying our token IS a candidate (only the label rule refuses it), plus `isSentCopy` (now exported) tested directly for SENT / no labels / SENT+DRAFT, for the token and the Message-ID. The prefix match: SENT copies whose token shares its first characters, differs only in the domain, or is one character short or long are not ours; the exact token (any case) is. Each red under its mutation (SENT dropped; a 6-character prefix match).
- **The limit holds after the token.** `sendSubmission` re-checks the 35 MiB limit on the bytes it uploads, token line included (a message of exactly the limit passed `pinnedMime` and would have gone up 40 bytes over); test: refused as `messageTooLarge`, nothing sent. An `internetId` that fails the shape check is still sent with no token (the engine always writes `<32hex@checked-domain>`; under Gmail's rewrite such a send could only be settled by the owner).
- **Kill-point matrix in Gmail's real mode.** `npm run test:engine:gmail-rewritten` runs the Gmail kill-point matrix with `APLOS_EMU_SEND_IDENTITY=rewritten` and `APLOS_EMU_DRAFT_IDENTITY=rewritten`. It is NOT in `check` yet: `packages/engine/tests/outbox/killpoint-gmail-port.ts` (engine's) counts deliveries by Message-ID only, so 33 of 64 rows fail on the test's own counter until engine counts a delivery by Message-ID or by an `X-Aplos-Send: <id>` line in the raw bytes. With that one-line counter change applied locally (not committed here) the script passes 64/64. Add it to `check` once engine's counter fix is on revamp.

## 2026-11-05 prov-gmail: the `X-Aplos-Send` edge cases engine asked about (tests only)

- **A token candidate still needs SENT, no DRAFT, and the window.** Already so (#226): `isSentCopy` requires the SENT label and no DRAFT label for a token as for a Message-ID, and the SENT-listing path rejects a candidate older than `since` minus `AFTER_SLACK_MS` (3 days). New test: a copy with our token ten days old is not ours, one two days old (inside the slack) is.
- **No malformed or injected header from an odd id.** `withSendToken` writes the header only for a plain `<local@domain>` (`isDraftToken`: no space, tab, CR, LF, NUL, quote, nested angle bracket, non-ASCII or over-long part; JS `$` has no trailing-newline case). New tests: 19 bad ids (CR/LF injection, trailing newline, quotes, brackets, empty parts, 257 characters, non-ASCII) produce no token; the outbox's 32-hex id and a shorter well-formed one produce exactly one header line; a payload whose `internetId` carries a CRLF is refused before any request (`pinnedMime` also refuses it: its Message-ID must equal the payload's, a second line of defence).
## 2026-11-03 — eng-contract: the Gmail kill-point matrix counts deliveries by Message-ID or send token

- `killpoint-gmail-port.ts` counted a delivery by its Message-ID only, so under `APLOS_EMU_SEND_IDENTITY=rewritten` (Gmail replaces the Message-ID on `messages.send`, the live result) it saw none of the adapter's sends and 33 of the 64 Gmail kill-point cases failed. It now counts a Sent message whose Message-ID OR `X-Aplos-Send` header equals the submission's id (the adapter's token, providers #224/#226). Both modes run 64/64; with the Message-ID-only counter the rewritten run is 31/64 again, so the matrix exercises the token path. Test helper only; no engine code changed.
## 2026-11-05 prov-gmail: #223 follow-ups (a 5xx is never definite, the re-record keeps the flag, pinned by a test)

- **A 5xx is not a definite refusal, whatever reason it carries.** A 503 with `rateLimitExceeded` is classified `throttle` and not `uncertain`, but the server may have stored the copy before answering; `createCopy` now counts an attempt as definitely refused only for a pause before the request or an error with no `uncertain` flag AND a status below 500 (429, 4xx). Such a 5xx marks the attempt "may have landed", so its retry keeps the Sent/Trash scan and the content fallback.
- **L4 pinned:** a test of uncertain, then a retry that finds nothing and records the attempt again, then a 429, then a retry that asserts the extra checks still run (recording the same attempt again keeps its "may have landed" flag; red when `record` forgets it).

## 2026-11-05 prov-emu: live probe `gmail.adapter-send-find` (the token path through the real adapter)

- **What.** The Integrator's 06:01 NOTICE: a sibling of `gmail.send-message-id` that sends THROUGH the adapter's `sendSubmission` with the production token and then runs the adapter's `findSentCopy` on the real Sent copy, reporting `findSentByToken` and `sentHeaderTokenKept` (counts only). `--phase adapter`, tier S-self, one self-addressed message, same rules (one send per day, fail-closed duplicate check, never retried, address only in the one `To:`, evidence content-free). Usage in plans/LIVE-RUNS.md.
- **What the guard cannot express, and how it was handled without touching it.** The guard's `send-media` rule admits only `uploadType=media` (the body is the message); the adapter's `transmit` posts `uploadType=multipart` (metadata + message, for threads) and resumable above 5 MB. A probe given the real `transmit` would be refused. The probe therefore passes the adapter a `fetch` that rewrites the thread-less multipart upload into the equivalent simple media upload of the same message bytes (`mediaUpload`; a thread, a resumable session or any other shape is refused in the probe). `guard.ts` is unchanged. Limitation: the multipart/resumable HTTP shapes of `transmit` are not exercised live (the message bytes, the token, the route and the response parsing are). If the Integrator wants those proven live too, the guard needs a `send-media` form for `uploadType=multipart` that checks the message part; that is a guard change and is not made here.
- **Duplicate check includes the adapter's own `findSent`** for today's token, so a rerun is refused even when the subject and Message-ID searches lag.
- **Tests.** `live-adapter-send-probe.test.ts` (8; the emulator has no media upload, so the test's last fetch turns the approved simple upload into the JSON send): rewritten and kept Message-ID, phase gating and `--confirm-mutating`, second run refused, a 503 is one upload and a fail, content-free evidence, `mediaUpload` unit cases. Red under: no token written by the adapter, a thread accepted by the translation, a retry of the send.

Red under: global fetch used, guard flag ignored, uncertain not mapped, signal dropped.

## 2026-11-03 — eng-html: html-4 slice 1, the frame building blocks

Pure, unwired code in `packages/mail-html/src/frame/` (exported as `@aplos/mail-html/frame`) and `packages/mail-html/assets/reporter.js`. The engine does not import it yet; `Mail/frame` stays a stub until slice 2.

- **Token grammar.** 128 random bits from Web Crypto `getRandomValues`, written as 32 lowercase hex. The only accepted URLs are `aplos-mail://m/<token>/` (the document), `aplos-mail://m/<token>/cid/<n>` and `.../img/<n>`, with `n` = `0` or 1 to 6 decimal digits without a leading zero. `Mail/serve`'s `path` is what follows `<token>/`: `''`, `cid/<n>` or `img/<n>`. Everything else is refused: query, fragment, any percent-escape, dot segments, backslashes, doubled or trailing slashes, uppercase or short tokens, leading zeros, signs, exponents, non-ASCII digits, other hosts, ports or userinfo.
- **The issued URL ends with a slash** (`aplos-mail://m/<token>/`), unlike the contract comment's `aplos-mail://m/<token>`: the sanitizer writes relative `cid/<n>` and `img/<n>`, which resolve under the token only against a base ending in `/` (without it they resolve to `aplos-mail://m/cid/<n>` and 404). `MailFrame.url` is a plain string, so no contract change is needed; the comment should be updated by its owner.
- **TTL** 10 minutes (`TOKEN_TTL_MS`) on an injected clock; the registry never reads the time itself. An expired token is deleted on first sight and swept before each issue. Images load on view, so a long-open message can lose its later images after 10 minutes; the reader re-renders on `expiresAt` (or a later slice makes expiry sliding).
- **Cap: evict, not refuse.** At most 64 live tokens per window; issuing the 65th drops that window's oldest. Refusing would let a missed `Mail/release` (a crashed renderer path, a bug) stop the reader rendering any mail at all, while evicting only blanks a frame that was rendered long ago. The bound on memory is the same either way, and only the window's own tokens can go. Other windows are untouched.
- **Single render.** The document is handed out once per token (the first document request marks it served; a second gets the 404). Sub-resources are served only after the document, only for an index the render numbered (`remoteImages[n]`, `cids[n]`), and only while the token lives. `img/<n>` is refused whenever the render's policy is `block`. A frame reload therefore shows nothing until the reader asks `Mail/frame` again.
- **Windows and refusals.** Every lookup names the window; another window's token is refused like an unknown one, and is not spent or released by it. Unknown, guessed, malformed, expired, released, reused, other-window, unissued-index and blocked-image requests all get the same `notFound()` (404, empty body, the same four headers). The registry uses a `Map` lookup, not a constant-time compare; the guard against guessing is the 128 bits.
- **Headers** (lowercase names, one spelling, since `Mail/serve` returns a plain record). Document: `content-security-policy: default-src 'none'; script-src 'sha256-<reporter>'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; img-src aplos-mail: data:; font-src aplos-mail: data:; media-src 'none'; connect-src 'none'; frame-src 'none'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; sandbox allow-scripts allow-popups`, `content-type: text/html; charset=utf-8`, `x-content-type-options: nosniff`, `cache-control: no-store`, `referrer-policy: no-referrer`, `x-dns-prefetch-control: off`. Sub-resources: `content-security-policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox`, the type given by the caller when it is PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP (`content-disposition: inline`), otherwise `application/octet-stream` with `content-disposition: attachment` (SVG and HTML included), plus nosniff, no-store, no-referrer. `cross-origin-resource-policy` is not sent: the sandboxed frame's origin is opaque, so `same-origin` would block the message's own images.
- **Reporter and its hash.** `assets/reporter.js` is the shipped file; `src/frame/reporter.ts` embeds the identical text as `REPORTER_SOURCE` (no Node import in `src`), and `reporterHash()` is the base64 SHA-256 of its UTF-8 bytes through Web Crypto `subtle.digest`. A test hashes the file's bytes with Node, requires byte equality with the embedded text, equality with `reporterHash()`, and the hash inside the CSP that `frameHeaders` builds; editing either copy alone fails. `withReporter(document)` puts `<script>` + the exact text + `</script>` last in the body. The script captures `window` once as `w` and reads everything through it, counts only the sanitizer's `mark.aplos-find` (mail cannot forge the `aplos-` class), posts `{token, h, w, bg, ready, marks}` to `parent` with target `'*'` only when `innerWidth > 0`, clamps h to 1e6, w and marks to 1e5, bg to 64 characters, and goes silent for good if any setup step throws.
- **Security-review fixes (before merge).** (1) `serveFrame` re-checks the token after awaiting `img`/`cid` bytes, so a release, replacement or expiry during the fetch gives the uniform 404. (2) `issue` throws and `serveFrame` answers 404 for an empty or non-string window id, so a relay that defaults to `''` cannot merge windows. (3) The document is kept on the registry entry, not the record, and is dropped as `takeDocument` hands it out. (4) `tokenOf` (release, toggleQuotes) also takes `aplos-mail://m/<token>` without the slash; the serving grammar stays strict.
- **Slice 2 must wire.** (1) `Mail/frame` in `methods/mail.ts`: read the stored original, look up the LRU by `renderKey` (with `MailHtml.version`), render through `MailHtml`, `withReporter`, issue a token with the caller's window id and slot, return `MailFrame` (map the contract's `imagePolicy: 'load'` and the sender allow-list to the port's `'allow'`); text bodies get no token. (2) `Mail/release`, `Mail/toggleQuotes` (`replace`), `releaseWindow` when a window closes. (3) `Mail/serve`: the contract's input is `{token, path}` with no window, so the handler must get the requesting window from main's relay (html-5) and bind it, refusing non-host callers; resolve `reporterHash()` once at start. (4) `cidBytes` from the stored attachment with a sniffed type, `imageBytes` through `ImageProxy` (per-token budget) with the sniffed `mime`. (5) The engine portability and bundle checks once the engine imports the frame code. (6) The LRU of 12 behind `MessageBody/prefetch`.
- **Reviewer's slice-2 recommendations.**
  - (a) The per-window binding needs a real source: Electron's `protocol.handle` gives no `webContentsId`, so html-5 must choose a session partition per window or stamp a window header in `webRequest.onBeforeSendHeaders` (`details.webContentsId`), after proving it fires for `protocol.handle` schemes and overwrites renderer-set values. Its relay test must refuse when no window can be found.
  - (b) The TTL should apply only to a token whose document was not fetched yet (about 60 s); a served token then lives until release, replacement, the cap or its window closing.
  - (c) Pop-out and print get their own token; the reader calls `Mail/frame` again when the frame's `load` fires with no report.
  - (d) Map the contract's `imagePolicy: 'load'` and the sender allow-list to `'allow'` in ONE whitelist function, and key the cache on the resolved policy.
  - (e) Call `withReporter` on every issue, outside the cache.
  - (f) Fix the `MailFrame.url` contract comment: the issued URL ends with a slash.

## 2026-11-04 — eng-html: html-4 slice 2, Mail/* over the store

`methods/mail.ts` is now a thin table over `packages/engine/src/mail/` (lane module `eng-html`, registered in `src/index.ts`; its one service is `mailFrames`: the token registry and the LRU of 12). `Message/get`, `MessageBody/get|prefetch`, `Mail/frame|release|toggleQuotes|serve|allowImages` are real; `Attachment/get` and `Message/original` stay `notImplemented` (slice 3).

- **Window id = connection id.** A token is issued to `ctx.connectionId` of the call that made it, and `Mail/release` and `Mail/toggleQuotes` act only on the caller's own tokens (another window's token is unknown: release still answers `Ok`, toggle answers `notFound` "This message is no longer available.", so no window can probe another). Main cannot learn an engine `connectionId`: connections are made inside the utility process, and main knows only the Electron window id it sends in the `attach` message (`host/transports/port.ts`). **html-5 must** therefore (1) find the requesting window for every `aplos-mail://` request in main (partition per window, or a stamped header from `webRequest.onBeforeSendHeaders` with `details.webContentsId`, proven to fire for `protocol.handle` and to overwrite a renderer-set value) and send its Electron window id with the request, passing none when it cannot find one; (2) have the utility's port host translate that Electron window id to the `connectionId` of the session it attached for that window before it dispatches `Mail/serve` with `windowId` set to it (none when no session is attached), and add `Mail/serve` to `controlMethods`, which today does not list it; (3) close that session on `detach`, which releases the window's tokens. **Closed windows:** `LaneModule` gains an optional `onConnectionClose(connectionId)` hook, called by `buildEngine` when a connection closes (the only edits to eng-contract's `runtime/engine.ts` and `runtime/services.ts`: one call in `onClose`, one guarded loop, one optional member); the `eng-html` module uses it to `releaseWindow`, so a closed window's tokens and any unserved document go with it. `issueFrame` also sweeps every window's overdue unfetched tokens on each issue.
- **Contract change (additive, v1).** `Mail/serve` input gains optional `windowId: line(64)`. Fail closed: absent or empty is the uniform 404 before the registry is asked (and `serveFrame` refuses a non-string or empty id again). Optional only so older callers still validate. `Mail/serve` keeps no other window or session knowledge. The `MailFrame` comment now says the URL ends with a slash and that text mail has an empty `url` (reviewer f). `generate` changed no generated file (host methods are not in the listener tables or OpenAPI).
- **Policy mapping.** One function, `resolveImagePolicy` (`mail/policy.ts`): `'load'` → `allow`; `'block'` → `allow` only when the sender (`messages.from_addr`, trimmed, lower case) equals an allow-list entry, or its domain (after the last `@`) equals an `@domain` entry exactly; anything else → `block`. No suffix or subdomain match (`a@sub.example.com` is not `@example.com`). The LRU key and the token's policy are the resolved value. `Settings.mail.remoteImages` is not read: the reader sends `load` when the owner's setting says so (spec question for ui-reader). The token keeps the REQUESTED policy too: `Mail/toggleQuotes` keeps the find query and resolves the requested policy again against the allow-list as it is then, so a sender taken off the list is blocked on the next toggle while an explicit `load` keeps loading.
- **Reporter outside the cache.** The cache holds the sanitizer's result and the cids it numbered; `withReporter` runs on each issue, so every document has exactly one reporter and the cached document none. `reporterHash()` is memoized in `mail-html/src/frame/reporter.ts` (one digest per process), and a test proves 20 serves digest at most once.
- **Body identity in the cache key.** The key carries which stored copy was rendered: `messages.id` plus `messages.first_seen_seq`. The rowid alone is not enough, because `allocateId` hands out `max(id)+1` within the second, so a message deleted and stored again at the same date gets the same rowid back (the test shows it); `first_seen_seq` is the commit that stored the row. Never `rev`, which flag edits move. Not covered: a body rewritten in place on the same row, which only happens when the provider's original is fetched again (the same bytes).
- **quotesOpen.** The port gains optional `MailHtmlInput.quotesOpen`; the sanitizer writes `<details class="aplos-quote" open>` when it is true. The default output is byte-identical, so `mail-html/1` stays (the flag is in the cache key).
- **TTL.** A token whose document was not fetched expires 60 s after issue (`UNFETCHED_TTL_MS`); once the document was served it has no deadline and ends only on release, replacement, the 64-per-window cap or `releaseWindow`, so lazy images of a message left open keep loading. `MailFrame.expiresAt` is the unfetched deadline; the reader calls `Mail/frame` again when the frame's `load` fires with no report.
- **Sniffing.** `serveFrame` types every `cid/<n>` and `img/<n>` answer from its leading bytes (`sniffRaster`: PNG, JPEG, GIF87a/89a, RIFF…WEBP); a claimed type (the proxy's `mime`, the part's header) is never read, and anything else (SVG, HTML, an executable labelled `image/png`) is the uniform 404, no longer a 200 `attachment` download (Integrator LOW). `ServedBytes` is now `{bytes}`; `resourceHeaders` takes only a raster type.
- **Random source.** `FrameTokens` draws from Web Crypto by default; the injection is `insecureTestRandom` (an old `random` option is ignored), and a test spies on `crypto.getRandomValues` to prove the default uses it (Integrator LOW).
