- **Bodies.** Read with the store's `readBody` (codec 0); a body that is absent or evicted is fetched with `ProviderPort.openThread` (interactive for `Mail/frame` and `MessageBody/get`, prefetch for `MessageBody/prefetch`, `knownCount` from `threads.remote_count`). Gap for eng-store: `StorePort` has no read-side `BodyCodec`, so a host that writes codec 1 or 2 makes every read fail until one is exposed. `cid/<n>` bytes come from `ProviderPort.attachment` for the part the stored `cidMap` names (own keys only), capped at 10 MB and bounded by the proxy's whole-fetch timeout (`IMAGE_PROXY_LIMITS.timeoutMs`, 10 s on the engine clock, raced against the call's abort): a stalled stream is the 404 and its iterator is returned. The engine has no port to the host's attachment files yet, so slice 3 (`Attachment/get`, files) should serve stored parts locally.
- **`MessageBody/get`.** `text` is the message's own text/plain part (empty for HTML-only mail: no HTML-to-text conversion yet), quote ranges are the stored ones that fit, and an HTML body's `quotedCount`/`hasRemoteImages` come from the same render `Mail/frame` with `block` makes, which also warms the cache. **`Message/get`**: attachment `id` is the provider part (`remote_part`), scoped by the message id, for `Attachment/get`. **`MessageBody/prefetch`** answers at once, opens each thread once on the prefetch lane when a named body is missing and warms the cache; `cancel` stops a pending warm-up but cannot abort the provider fetch (`openThread` takes no signal).
- **`Mail/allowImages`.** A new lane write kind `imageAllowList` (eng-html, not the reserved `settings`) edits only `settings.data.mail.imageAllowList`, keeping the rest of the document, and marks a `Settings` change. Entries are trimmed, lower case, deduplicated; at most 5,000 (the schema's bound: `tooLarge` "Images are already allowed from 5,000 senders. Remove some first."; the writer refuses past it too). Idempotent by `opId` (the store's replay). Receipt `undoable: false`. Existing tokens and cache entries are untouched. Not done: the `audit_log` row the contract's `audit: true` asks for (no audit writer exists, `eng-host`); and `Settings/set` (`eng-contract`) should take this write over once it has a settings writer.
- **Accepted limitations, follow-ups.** (a) The allow-list matches the message's From, which is not authenticated: a forged From of an allowed sender loads remote images (an owner-visible privacy leak, not a code path). A product call: gate the allow-list on an authentication result (DMARC alignment) once the store keeps one. (b) `Mail/allowImages` is `audit: true`, but no audit writer exists yet (`eng-host`), and a listener client holding an `admin` grant can edit the list unaudited; this is a repo-wide gap for every audited method. Consider refusing listener callers for audited methods until the audit writer lands.
- **Bundle.** Engine core 388,576 → 410,577 minified bytes (budget 450,000; the frame code from `@aplos/mail-html/frame` is statically imported and counted, about 5.5 KB; the rest is `src/mail/`).

## 2026-11-04 — eng-html: html-5, the protocol relay and main-side glue

The `aplos-mail://` relay is built and runs on the real engine path only (`APLOS_ENGINE=real`, never a release build until the switch). Decisions are pure in `apps/desktop/src/host/protocol/relay.ts`; `apps/desktop/src/main/mail-protocol.ts` hands them to Electron.

- **How a request is bound to its window.** Two steps, because main cannot learn engine connection ids. (1) Main: the session's one `onBeforeSendHeaders` listener (`stampWindow`) deletes every case variant of `X-Aplos-Window` from every request and, for an `aplos-mail:` URL with a `webContentsId`, writes `X-Aplos-Window: String(webContentsId)`; with no `webContentsId` it writes none. The handler reads that header (exactly one positive integer, else 404), maps the `webContentsId` to its `BrowserWindow` id only when it is that window's OWN live webContents (`BrowserWindow.fromWebContents(c)?.webContents === c`). DevTools, webviews, destroyed contents and a `WebContentsView` hosted in a window are the 404: Electron reports a hosted view as type `window` and `fromWebContents` maps it to its host window, so without the identity check a view's requests would be stamped as that window (review MEDIUM; measured in relay.e2e.ts, red under the old mapping) and sends that id as `Mail/serve`'s `windowId`. (2) Utility: `Mail/serve` is now a control method, and `serveControl` rewrites its arguments with `hostServeArgs` (port.ts): `windowId` becomes the engine `connectionId` of the session the port host attached for that window, or `''` (the engine's uniform 404) when the id is malformed, unknown or detached. Main's value is never passed through, and only `token`, `path`, `windowId` reach the engine. To make (2) possible, an engine session now names its `connectionId` (`ConnectedSession`), and `createPortHost` gained `connectionOf(windowId)`.
- **Why the stamp is trusted (measured, Electron 44.7.2, hidden windows).** `onBeforeSendHeaders` fires for `protocol.handle` scheme requests: the sandboxed frame's document, its sub-resources (`img`, `cid`) and an `aplos-mail:` page's own `fetch`; `details.webContentsId` is the embedding window's contents in every case (two windows: their own ids); a page-forged `X-Aplos-Window`/`X-APLOS-WINDOW` is replaced by main's value before the handler sees it. relay.e2e.ts proves each of these against the real handler. Chromium's header map is case-insensitive, so the last-written stamp wins even without the deletion; the deletion matters for a request with no `webContentsId` (unit-tested).
- **Detach releases tokens.** `detach` (and the port closing) closes the window's engine session, whose close reaches slice 2's `onConnectionClose`; proven over the real engine (relay-window.test.ts: `liveCount` drops to 0). A re-attached window gets a new connection, so an old token never follows it.
- **Real path only.** `engine-host.ts` calls `registerMailScheme()` at import (before app ready, as Electron requires) under `if (realPath)`; `bootRealEngine` calls `installMailProtocol(serveMail)` then `allowMailFrames()`. On the legacy path the scheme is not registered, no `webRequest` listener exists, and `security.ts` refuses every `aplos-mail:` subframe. Source pins in main-glue.test.ts. The main-process bundle now includes the pure `@aplos/mail-html/frame` modules (paths, headers and their neighbours; no sanitizer, no htmlparser2); the engine bundle is unchanged (410,605 of 450,000; main code is not in it).
- **GET only.** Any other method (HEAD included) is 405 with `Allow: GET`, the refusal's own headers, an empty body, and no engine call.
- **Uniform 404, no oracle.** Missing or bad stamp, no app window, a URL outside `aplos-mail://m/<token>/<path>` (host exactly `m`, no port or userinfo), more than `MAX_RELAY_IN_FLIGHT` (64) relays open at once (`limitInFlight` in `engine-real.ts`: the 65th never calls the engine, and every exit frees its slot: answer, rejection, the link's timeout, a throw), any engine error or rejection, the 15 s timeout (`SERVE_TIMEOUT_MS`; the engine bounds `cid`/`img` at 10 s), and any answer that is not exactly `{status 200|404, headers, Uint8Array body}` all give slice 1's `notFound()`. Headers are allow-listed to the seven names the engine writes and a CSP must be present; a `set-cookie`, a CORS header or an off-case duplicate refuses the whole answer. Main builds `new Response(body, {status, headers})` from the engine's values and adds nothing.
- **Raw path.** Token and path are cut from `request.url` as text (`splitMailUrl`), never through `new URL()` and never decoded; the path keeps escapes, dot segments and a query, and the engine's grammar refuses them. Chromium canonicalizes a standard scheme's URL before main sees it (dot segments resolved, host lowercased); that leaves only another URL for the engine to judge.
- **Backstop.** The session's one `onBeforeRequest` cancels http, https, ws and wss whose `initiatorOrigin` is `'null'` or whose frame URL starts with `aplos-mail:` (`backstopCancels`). Measured: a CSP-less probe document's img, fetch and WebSocket and an `aplos-mail:` main frame's fetch never reach the loopback canary, while the `file://` app page's own request does (its initiator is `file://`, not `'null'`). The image proxy runs in the engine process, outside Chromium, so it is unaffected.
- **One listener per event.** Electron keeps one `onBeforeSendHeaders` and one `onBeforeRequest` per session and silently replaces on a second call. `mail-protocol.ts` is the only file in `apps/desktop/src` that calls `webRequest.on*`, `registerSchemesAsPrivileged` or `protocol.handle` (pinned by a source test), and `installWebRequestGuards` is idempotent per session (returns false the second time; tested). A future handler (plat-release's shared module) must be added inside this module, not beside it.
- **SUBFRAME rule.** `security.ts`'s `will-frame-navigate` allows `aplos-mail:` only after `allowMailFrames()`, only for a subframe, only the exact issued document `aplos-mail://m/<32 lowercase hex>/` (`isMailDocumentUrl`, slice 1's `parseFrameUrl` imported, not mirrored), and only when the navigation's initiator is the app's own trusted page. A mail frame navigating itself (script or `target=_self` link) to another well-formed token, another mail frame, a web page, an opaque frame or no initiator is denied, and `will-redirect` never allows one. `cid/<n>`, `img/<n>`, the slashless form, queries, other hosts and any main-frame navigation stay denied. `windows.ts`'s `mailFrameURL` has the same strict check and trusted initiator; security.ts now carries the initiator rule for every webContents, and the windows.ts copy is not tested on its own (windows.ts cannot be loaded in a test app). Main never writes a sandbox attribute or `allow-same-origin` (source pin); the frame's opaque origin is checked in Chromium.
- **Frame escape, against the real guards.** relay.e2e.ts installs the real `security.ts` (host page written at its trusted renderer path; each window's open handler replaced by a recording deny so nothing reaches the browser; `windows.ts` is too entangled with the app to load). From inside a served mail frame: `location.href` and a `target=_self` link to another well-formed document are denied (never requested); a `target=_top` link and a form post never request anything (sandbox without `allow-top-navigation` and `allow-forms`, plus `form-action 'none'`); `window.open('javascript:…')` returns null and never reaches the open handler; `window.opener` is null; the top frame stays on the app page; no script but the reporter posts. Unit-pinned: `security.ts`'s open handler denies every URL and passes only http, https and mailto (no credentials) to the system.
- **Links and mailto.** Unchanged and confirmed: a frame link (`target=_blank`) arrives at the embedding window's `setWindowOpenHandler` (measured), which in `windows.ts` sends `mailto:` to `handleLink` (the `deep-link` event) and opens http/https externally through `external()`, which refuses every other scheme. Gap: on the real path the renderer that receives `deep-link` is today's legacy App, whose `newDraft` calls the legacy engine, which the real path refuses; Compose from a mail-frame mailto needs the new renderer to handle `deep-link` (ui-reader / ui-core). Not changed here (no renderer work).
- **Keys: finding, nothing built.** Measured in a hidden window (relay.e2e.ts records it): with focus inside the out-of-process mail frame, `before-input-event` on the embedding webContents fires for a key sent with `webContents.sendInputEvent` (keyDown and keyUp), and `webContents.focusedFrame.url` names the mail frame. A key sent with DevTools' `Input.dispatchKeyEvent` does not fire it. So the main-side relay is feasible, but it is not wired: nothing in the renderer consumes a relayed key from main (the `MailFrame` `onKey` path takes reporter `key` messages), and swallowing arrows, Page Up/Down, Home and End in main would stop the frame scrolling until the renderer scrolls it itself. Today neither relay is active for an `aplos-mail:` frame: the shipped reporter sends no `key` messages and main has no `before-input-event` listener (pinned by a test that fails if both appear). Whoever wires one (keys J, K, arrows, Page Up/Down, Home, End, Esc; never E, Space, R or a dictation letter; only when `focusedFrame` passes `isMailDocumentUrl`) updates that pin and keeps the other off.
- **PROTOCOL.md "Before aplos-mail:// ships", line by line.** (1) Never `allow-same-origin`: main never writes it (main-glue source pin); frames' origin is `'null'` in Chromium (relay.e2e). The renderer's own sandbox constant is ui-reader's test. (2) CSP as a response header on every response: the engine writes it (slice 1); main refuses any engine answer without one and passes it unchanged (relay.test); in Chromium the reporter runs and an inline script and an inline handler do not (relay.e2e). The checklist's `img-src data:` "plus `https:` when loading" conflicted with ARCH 10 and slice 1; its sentence in `apps/desktop/src/renderer/mailframe/PROTOCOL.md` (ui-core's file) now says `img-src aplos-mail: data:`, never `https:`, loaded images coming through the proxy as `img/<n>` (a one-sentence correction, nothing else in that file changed). (3) DNS prefetch off: the `x-dns-prefetch-control: off` header is the engine's and is allow-listed. The `<meta>` is not needed. Measured in relay.e2e.ts (net log, every name but loopback resolving to "not found"): un-hovered http and https links in a mail frame cause no lookup, both in an engine document (header sent) and in a bare one (no header). There is no positive control in an http document, so the reason (Chromium's anchor DNS prefetch runs only for http(s) documents) is believed, not measured; the sanitizer also strips `<link>` (prefetch, preconnect). (4) Navigation denial and the subframe allowance: security.ts's strict rule (main-glue) and the open handler (relay.e2e), as above. (5) Separate process: the mail frames' process differs from the host page's (relay.e2e). (6) Never both key relays: pinned, neither today. (7) Sanitizer cost fences and the flattened/truncated flags: html-3's scope, not re-checked here.
- **Mutation-checked.** GET-only, stamp deletion, fail-closed stamp, host `m`, raw path, header allow-list, utility translation, unknown window to `''`, malformed window, backstop (off and half), SUBFRAME strictness, the legacy gate, single-listener install and widened privileges: each mutation fails the unit tests; GET-only and the backstop also fail relay.e2e. Review round: hosted view counted as its window, trusted initiator dropped, redirect into a mail document allowed (each fails unit and e2e); cap off, cap off by one, slot never freed, engine-real without the limiter, missing stamp treated as window 1 (`windowOf` now answers every id in that test, and must never be asked), and an open handler that opens anything (each fails the unit tests).
- **Not done in html-5, and the hand-check.** Pop-out and print in a `WebContentsView` with `javascript:false` (no pop-out of a mail frame exists yet). The packaged-app hand-check is NOT done: a real key press with focus inside a mail frame (does `before-input-event` fire for native input, not only `sendInputEvent`), mailto from a frame on the real path, and a frame's link opening in the browser. It is an owner step or a background Cua-driver run on the exact window. `relay.e2e.ts` is not part of `npm test` (it starts Electron); run it with `npx tsx --test apps/desktop/tests/eng-html/relay.e2e.ts` (about 4 s).
- **Files touched in other lanes.** eng-contract: `packages/engine/src/runtime/connection.ts` (`ConnectedSession`, the session's `connectionId`), `runtime/engine.ts` (`connect`'s return type), `src/index.ts` (type export), `apps/desktop/src/host/transports/port.ts` (`Mail/serve` in `controlMethods`, `ServedPort.connectionId`, `connectionOf`, `hostServeArgs`), `main/engine-utility.ts` (`serveControl` translates `Mail/serve`), `main/engine-host.ts` (`registerMailScheme` under `realPath`), `main/engine-real.ts` (`serveMail`, `limitInFlight`, install, `allowMailFrames`). eng-host: `main/windows.ts` (`mailFrameURL` strict). plat-release: `main/security.ts` (`allowMailFrames`, the strict subframe rule with its trusted initiator). ui-core: one sentence of `apps/desktop/src/renderer/mailframe/PROTOCOL.md` (item 2's `img-src`). New and not yet in plans/OWNERS.md: `main/mail-protocol.ts` (eng-html; needs a row).

## 2026-11-04 — eng-html: html-5 slice 2, the frame reporter and sanitizer fixes (ui-core REQUEST, part 2)

Answers the ui-core REQUEST of 2026-11-05 16:30 (engine part). `MAIL_HTML_VERSION` is now `mail-html/2`, so every stored rendered body is re-rendered once. The reporter is still one hashed inline script: `assets/reporter.js` is the shipped file, `REPORTER_SOURCE` its byte-identical copy, the CSP hash is computed from it (reporter.test.ts fails on any drift, so the pin updates itself); the size ceiling in that test moved from 2,048 to 4,096 characters (the script is 3.2 KB now).

- **Height is the content.** The reporter reports `max(ceil(documentElement.getBoundingClientRect().height), body.scrollHeight)`. The root's `scrollHeight` is at least the viewport, so a frame could only ever grow. The root box is the content's because the reading CSS (first in `<head>`, ours) now sets `html:root{display:flow-root;height:auto;min-height:0;max-height:none}` and `html:root>body{height:auto;min-height:0;max-height:none}`, all `!important`: a mail's `html,body{height:100%}` cannot pin the frame to its own height. The doubled `:root` raises specificity above a mail rule that names the same element; a mail's own `!important` (the engine does not strip it, unlike the renderer's stand-in sanitiser) with a more specific selector still wins, in which case the frame keeps its height rather than growing. `flow-root` makes the root contain the body's margins, so they are counted. `body.scrollHeight` adds content that spills past the root box (absolute positioning). Measured in hidden Electron: a 200 px letter in a 3,000 px frame reports 232 (200 + the 32 px body padding); a closed `<details>` shrinks the frame; the previous reporter reports 3,000 and never shrinks.
- **Paper colour.** The body's computed background, else the root's when the body's is clear (`''`, `transparent`, or any colour whose alpha is 0), else the body's (so a fully clear letter still reports `rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)` and the host paints light paper). A half-transparent body colour counts as a colour.
- **DOM clobbering.** Findings first. In the sanitized document no name can clobber: `form`, `embed`, `object`, `iframe`, `frame`, `frameset`, `select`, `textarea`, `button`, `svg` and `math` are dropped; `name` survives only on `a`; every `id` and `name` is moved to `m-<value>`, which is not a JavaScript identifier, so no `document.<member>` or `window.<member>` can resolve to a mail element (corpus 064 and the new 137; the oracle fails any name or id outside `m-`). Defence in depth anyway, because the sanitizer is the single fence and Document has `[LegacyOverrideBuiltIns]` (named elements shadow `document.body`, `addEventListener`, `documentElement`...) and Window's EventTarget methods are shadowed by ids. The reporter now takes every member it uses from the browser's prototypes at start (`Document.prototype` getters `documentElement`, `body`, `readyState`, `fonts`; `Element.prototype.scrollHeight` and `getBoundingClientRect`; `Document.prototype.querySelectorAll`; `EventTarget.prototype.addEventListener`, called with `.call`), and reads the rest only through `window` (`Object`, `Math`, `isFinite`, `parent`, `location`, `getComputedStyle`, `ResizeObserver`, `document`, `Document`, `Element`, `EventTarget`: own properties of the global, which named properties cannot shadow). No bare global, no member of `document` or an element read by name after the capture. Verified in Chromium against an unsanitized document that puts `img`, `form`, `embed`, `object` and `iframe` named `body`, `documentElement`, `addEventListener`, `querySelectorAll`, `readyState`, `fonts`... on the page (the previous reporter is silenced by it; the new one reports the right height), and against ids on `a` named like window members.
- **Standards mode.** The sanitizer no longer echoes the mail's doctype: the document always starts with `<!DOCTYPE html>`, with nothing before it (a comment, BOM or XML declaration in the mail is body content, never ahead of the doctype), and the walk no longer returns a doctype. Most mail declares none or an old one, so most frames used to lay out in quirks mode (or limited quirks for XHTML transitional), where `body.scrollHeight` follows the viewport and percentage heights resolve differently. Cost: mail written for quirks gets the standards box model for heights, table-cell image baseline gaps (XHTML transitional mail was "limited quirks": its images in cells now sit on the baseline like in any current browser, which well-built mail already avoids with `display:block`) and `body` no longer fills the window by default. Pinned by: sanitizer test (every doctype shape starts `<!DOCTYPE html><html>`, exactly one doctype), `serveFrame` test (served bytes start with `<!doctype html>`, one doctype, no BOM) with a real render, an engine `Mail/serve` test, and Chromium `document.compatMode === 'CSS1Compat'` for five doctype shapes (control: no doctype is `BackCompat`).
- **Viewport units.** In `css.ts` (`viewportLength`), every dimension token whose unit is `vw`, `vh`, `vi`, `vb`, `vmin`, `vmax` or its `s`/`l`/`d` form (case-insensitive, escapes decoded by the tokenizer, so `100\76 h` too) becomes a length in px against a fixed reading viewport of 640 x 600 (1vh = 6px, 1vw = 6.4px, vmin 6, vmax 6.4; vi = vw, vb = vh), clamped to +-100,000 px. It applies in stylesheets, `style` attributes, `calc()`/`min()`/`max()`/`clamp()`, custom properties, `@media`/`@supports` preludes and `!important` values, one `O(1)` step per token, so the sanitizer stays linear (timed shape in perf.test.ts). Chosen over dropping the declaration because dropping needs declaration boundaries the token writer does not track and loses the author's intent (a `min-height:100vh` letter is 600 px tall, not unsized). `cq*` units were first left alone; the independent review showed that was wrong, see the review section below. The independent oracle fails any sanitized CSS that still holds a viewport unit. In Chromium the sanitized corpus message 136 settles in a handful of reports; an unsanitized `100vh` block with padding grows past 300 reports (control). The host's growth fence stays as the backstop.
- **`Mail/serve` GET only.** Already true and unchanged: the verb never reaches the engine (the contract's `Mail/serve` input is `{token, path, windowId}`), main's `createMailHandler` answers anything but `GET` with 405 + `Allow: GET` + the refusal's headers before any engine call, and binds the window from the `X-Aplos-Window` header main stamps itself (`stampWindow`), translated to a connection id in the utility (`hostServeArgs`). Mutation-checked again here (method guard removed: relay.test.ts "GET only" fails). The trust model is untouched; adding a verb to the contract would be an eng-contract change with no gain, so none was made.
- **Tests, with mutation results.** Unit (fake DOM with prototype-held members and decoys on `document`/`window`, colour fallback, clamps), css (viewport table, bounds, style attribute, non-matches, linearity shape), sanitizer and serve (doctype), corpus 136-139 (viewport units, clobbering of every member the script reads, quirks doctypes with a comment first, root-only background), and `apps/desktop/tests/eng-html/reporter.e2e.ts` (23 checks in the app's own hidden Chromium through `support/reporter-bed.ts`: real reporter, real sanitizer, the engine's headers, a host that follows the reported height; each check has a control that fails with the previous reporter or a raw document). Reverting each fix turns tests red: root `scrollHeight` (6 unit, 3 browser), no colour fallback (1, 1), `document.addEventListener` or `document.body` by name (1, 1), no doctype (3 unit, 8 browser), no unit rewrite (6 unit including the corpus oracle, 1 browser), the two reading-CSS height rules (0 unit, 1 browser each), GET guard in the relay (1).
- **Bundle.** Engine core 410,605 → 412,062 minified bytes (+1,457; budget 450,000); after the review fixes 410,707 → 412,461 (+1,754) on the current revamp.
- **Not done / for others.** (1) ui-core: PROTOCOL.md's "Open for eng-html" items 1-5 are answered here; the host may keep its growth fence and may drop the `scrollHeight` caveat. A letter pinned by its own `!important` height rule keeps the frame's height (it no longer grows, it just cannot shrink). (2) The 640 x 600 reading viewport is a constant in `css.ts`; the host's real frame width is not known to the sanitizer. (3) eng-host's part (1) of the REQUEST is untouched.

## 2026-11-04 — eng-html: review fixes for the frame reporter (independent security review, verdict FIX)

- **Container-query units (MEDIUM, fixed).** `cqw cqh cqi cqb cqmin cqmax` resolve against the small viewport when no ancestor is a size container, which is how every sanitized letter is laid out, so `height:100cqh` grew the frame exactly like `100vh` (132 to 4,900 px, then the browser bed's 300-report cap). They now take the same path as the viewport units (`css.ts` `viewportLength`, pattern `/^(?:[sld]?v|cq)(?:w|h|i|b|min|max)$/`, one O(1) step per token, case-insensitive, escapes decoded by the tokenizer, `calc()`, custom properties, `@container` preludes, style attributes). Decision: rewrite, do not drop `container-type`/`container`. Rewriting is one rule in one place and also covers a letter that does declare a container (a size container's height can still be set by the frame through percentages, and the sanitizer cannot tell which `cq` unit has a trustworthy container); dropping the declarations would need declaration boundaries the token writer does not track, change layouts that use a container for something else, and miss `@container` size queries. The cost is that a letter that really used container units against its own container gets fixed px lengths (bounded, 640 x 600 basis). `container-type`, `container-name` and `@container` rules are kept. The oracle fails any sanitized CSS still holding a viewport or cq unit. Corpus 140; unit rows; reporter.e2e: the sanitized message settles, raw `100cqh` and `100cqb` blocks grow past the cap (control).
- **Colour alpha (LOW, fixed, hash re-pinned by the existing tests).** `clear()` treated any computed colour ending in `, 0)` as see-through, which also matched an opaque `rgb(255, 255, 0)` (yellow) and fell back to the `<html>` colour (a bug in the first version, found while fixing this). It now means: empty, `transparent`, `rgba(..., 0)`, or a slash-alpha colour with alpha 0, 0.0 or 0% (`oklch(0.5 0.2 30 / 0)`, `color(srgb 1 0 0 / 0)`, `lab(50 20 30 /0)`). Unit rows (opaque colours that end in 0, small non-zero alphas stay colours) and two browser checks (yellow body kept; oklch alpha 0 falls back to the root). `color-mix()` and other computed forms that serialise as `color(srgb ...)` are handled by the same slash rule.
- **Root transform and zoom (INFO, fixed).** `html:root{transform:none;scale:none;rotate:none;translate:none;zoom:1}` (all `!important`) in the reading CSS, so a transformed or zoomed root can no longer inflate the root box the reporter reads (it gave 85,250 px for `html{transform:scale(1000)}`). Content transforms below the root still count as overflow through `body.scrollHeight`, as they should. Browser check: a root with transform, zoom, scale and translate reports under 120 px for a short letter.
- **For ui-core (LOW 3, not our file, not edited).** `apps/desktop/src/renderer/mailframe/browser/sealed.browser.spec.ts` (around line 330) builds a forgery with `REPORTER_SOURCE.replace("w.parent.postMessage(message, '*')", ...)`. That string no longer exists in the reporter (it now reads `host.postMessage({...}, '*')` through a captured `w.parent`), so the replace does nothing and the "4326" case is an exact copy of the real reporter: it proves nothing. Rebuild the forgery from the current text (for example replace `host.postMessage(` with a call that posts a forged object), and assert the spec fails if the replace did not change the source.

## 2026-11-04 — eng-html/eng-host: the two relay LOWs and the proxied-image cap (from the Integrator's #241 and #240 reviews)

- **Abort and per-window cap (relay.ts, engine-real.ts, port.ts).** The protocol request's own `signal` now travels with `ServeArgs` to `serveMail`, and `EngineLink.call(method, args, timeoutMs, signal)` rejects at once when it aborts and drops a late engine answer (the engine's work itself is not cancelled; it ends by its own 10 s bounds). `limitInFlight` frees a slot exactly once, on the first of the answer, a rejection, a throw or the abort, so a page that navigated away no longer holds one while the engine finishes. The 64 pool also gains a per-window count (`MAX_RELAY_PER_WINDOW` 16): one window cannot take the whole pool.
- **Response body cap (relay.ts).** `servedResponse` refuses a body over `MAX_SERVE_BODY_BYTES` (10 MiB: the cid cap and the proxy's own cap; a document is far smaller): the uniform 404. The engine's caps are the rule; this is defence in depth against a bug or a compromised utility pushing a huge answer through main.
- **Proxied image deadline and cap (mail/serve.ts).** `proxiedImage` now runs under the proxy's time limit (`IMAGE_PROXY_LIMITS.timeoutMs`, on the engine clock, abortable) and refuses bytes over `IMAGE_PROXY_LIMITS.maxBytes`, like the cid path: a host proxy that hangs or overshoots cannot hold `Mail/serve` or relay 50 MB. Tests: engine (over the cap, exactly at the cap, a proxy that never answers), relay (signal passthrough, abort frees once, per-window cap, body cap) and the link (abort before and during a call). Mutations red: signal not passed, abort not freeing, no per-window cap, no body cap, link ignoring the signal, no engine cap, no engine deadline.

## 2026-11-04 — eng-time: Blob package 4, the outbox reads attachment bytes asynchronously

- **Shape.** `BlobSource.get(blobId): Promise<Uint8Array | undefined>` (outbox/context.ts; ports untouched). `loadBlobs(source, ids)` (new outbox/blob-read.ts) reads the distinct ids concurrently into a fresh `Map` per call, for at most `BLOB_READ_LIMIT_MS` (10 s) on the outbox clock: a gone blob is simply absent; a read that rejects, throws or is still running at the limit rejects the whole load with `unavailable` "Couldn't read an attachment. Try again.", so no message is built from part of its attachments. A late answer after the limit is dropped with its bytes (no signal, no new port: the host read is simply abandoned), so a hung host read can neither queue a send long after the owner was told it failed nor keep a draft's upload in `inflight` forever. `attachmentsOf(bytes, fields)` builds from the map synchronously and keeps the `notFound` "Attach it again, then send." sentence for an absent blob. A synchronous adapter (`syncBlobSource(map | fn)`) lives in tests/outbox/helpers.ts only: the production module still gets no `blobs` (package 3 wires the real store), so it stays out of the engine bundle.
- **Send.** `Submission/set` (`OutboxService.set`) is now `Promise<SetResult>` for every action. A send runs (a) the synchronous draft read and checks exactly as before, (b) `await loadBlobs` only when the draft has blob attachments, (c) then: a replay check of its opId (two concurrent calls with one opId answer with the one submission), the draft re-read with every check again (revision, live send, conflict, incomplete, recipients, discard), and the MIME and Message-ID built from that fresh read only, then the commit, which still compares the revision and the live rule. A draft with no blob attachments never awaits: its row is committed within the call, as before. `set` is deliberately not an `async` function: a call that did all its work within the call returns an already settled promise (it does not adopt the send's promise a few microtasks later). Non-send actions do all their work within the call (undo, cancel, retry and `Submission/get` while a send reads bytes see the same state as before); the drafts' discard still cancels synchronously through `setSubmission(..., release=false)`. The same revision means the same fields (every field write bumps it), so the re-read needs no separate blob-id comparison.
- **Draft upload.** `syncDraft` loads the readable attachments' bytes the same way (no await without blob attachments), then builds synchronously; the partial rule (a gone blob stays listed locally, the copy is flagged partial) is unchanged. After a read, right before the upload's `uploading` mark and with nothing awaited in between, the row is re-read: if its revision or data changed during the read, or a send now pins the draft, the upload is dropped and the deadline source runs the draft again from its new row, so an upload's MIME, revision and fingerprint always come from the one read its bytes were loaded for (the `ifRevision` mark and the `synced` ack stay as they were). A failed or timed-out read logs `draft.sync_failed` (`transient`, provider `engine`, method `blob.read`, reason `blobUnreadable`), counts in the draft's sync meta (`readFails`, additive) and is due again after `DRAFT_RETRY_MS`; the fifth failed read in a row shows the old visible problem (`problemFor`, "This draft could not be saved to your account. It stays on this Mac.") and retries stop. A good read (the upload's mark) and an edit clear the count.
- **Guards proven by mutation** (tests/outbox/blob-read.test.ts, each mutant red): no post-await re-read (build from the pre-await read): red on "a copy synced during the read lends its Message-ID"; re-check but build from pre-await fields: red, same test; skip the commit's revision compare: red together with the re-read removal ("edited during the read"), and alone since the review round (below); ignore a rejected read; await with no blob attachments; drop absent => notFound; one Map shared between loads; no replay check after the read; draft sync without the re-read, without the live-send check, without the retry, without the partial filter. Without the re-check but with a fresh read, every refusal is also made by the commit's own checks (draftMissing, draftRevision, unresolved, bodyPending, live) with the same sentences, so that guard is defence in depth.
- **Review round (independent Opus review, MERGE; fixes in one commit).** The read's time limit (MEDIUM); the five-failure cap with a log line (MEDIUM-LOW); the live-send check moved to right before the upload's mark (LOW: before, a few microtasks separated the check from the mark); test harness: `raceOnce` (review.test.ts) now fails a race whose call is still pending inside the hook and handles its rejection, and the two-engine race sends through `w.act` (an already settled promise) instead of the async `w.send`; the kill-point harness refuses an owner action at the very point its life dies (chosen over journaling synchronously, which `set`'s promise cannot do: the journal's meaning stays exact, and no scenario uses that combination); a test for the commit's own revision compare (another engine edits the draft inside the send's commit hook: `stateMismatch` "changed", no row). Mutants, each red: no time limit; a timed-out read read as gone blobs; failed reads never adding up; no reset on a good read; no reset on an edit; no log line; the check back right after the read (the old gap: red on "reads ending in the same tick, upload first"); no live-send check; the commit's revision compare removed (red on the new race test, so the earlier "survives alone" no longer holds).
- **Review round 2 (Integrator, PR #254, FIX on one finding).** A draft discarded (hidden, `removing`) while its read was failing got `{dueAt, readFails}` from `readFailed`, which held the drafts timer on a row that only waits for its copy's removal. `readFailed` now re-reads the row with the same predicate the success path runs before the upload's mark (`stillCurrent`: same revision and data, no live send) and writes nothing when the draft is gone, hidden, changed (removed or edited elsewhere) or pinned by a send; the failure is still logged. Backstop in the store writer: a `meta` write that would give a `removing` row a `dueAt` or `readFails` is refused (`DraftConflict`, so `setMeta` returns false), whatever path asks. Tests: a discard while the read rejects, and while it hangs to the limit (the hidden row is untouched, the `drafts` source's `next()` holds no stale time after the retry wait, the discard completes, another draft then syncs); the copy removed on another device during the failing read; a send pinning the draft during it; the writer backstop directly. Mutants: no re-read/bail: red (removed elsewhere, pinned; the discard cases are then caught by the backstop); no backstop: red (backstop test); bail only when the row is gone: red (removed elsewhere, pinned); neither: red on all five, and the discard cases stay red with their row assertions removed (the timer/discard checks alone catch it).
- **Left for package 3.** The `Draft/set` blob-ownership check (every new `blobId` in the engine blob index and owned by the caller's principal, else `notFound` "Attach it again") needs the index from package 3 and is not implemented here; neither is the runtime `BlobSource` that ignores principal (decision 10 of plans/engine/blob-area.md).
- **Bundle.** 412,461 -> 413,368 minified bytes (+907) of 450,000; 413,971 (+1,510) after the review round.
## 2026-11-04 — eng-html: the reading rules in a first cascade layer (Integrator REQUEST on #249)

`MAIL_HTML_VERSION` is now `mail-html/3` (sanitized output changed: mail `@layer` names, rewritten signed units, the reading CSS), so stored rendered bodies re-render once. Bundle: see the end of this section.

- **The five vectors, measured.** In headless Chromium each of the reviewer's five documents (html positioned with `bottom:-50px`, body the same, `writing-mode:vertical-lr` with a negative margin, `@layer x{html{height:calc(100% + 50px)!important}}`, an inline `<body style="position:fixed!important;...">`) grew the frame 50 px per report under the previous reading rules (browser controls: over 2,000 px in 5 s, or the bed's 300-report cap), and none grows now (corpus 141-146, each also checked to settle under 400 px).
- **(a) The layer.** The root and body rules are `@layer aplos-reading{...}`, the first rule of the first style in `<head>`, so the layer is declared before any mail CSS. For `!important` declarations the cascade order is reversed: the earliest layer wins over every later layer, over unlayered `!important` rules (it does NOT win over a `style` attribute: element-attached styles rank before layers, see the review section below; this sentence said it did and was wrong). Specificity no longer matters. Only the root and body sizing rules moved into the layer: the other reading rules (`body{margin...}`, quote and mark styles) stay unlayered, so mail rules (layered or not) keep overriding them exactly as before.
- **The mail cannot join the layer.** A mail `@layer aplos-reading{...}` would merge into ours and, coming later, win inside it. The sanitizer now moves every identifier in a mail `@layer` prelude into the `m-` namespace (`css.ts`, `scopeId`, so already-`m-` names are left alone and the sanitizer stays idempotent): `aplos-reading`, the escaped spelling `\61 plos-reading`, a different case, dotted paths (`aplos-reading.x`), statements (`@layer a, aplos-reading;`), a comment in the prelude and `@layer` inside `@media` all become `m-...`; the prelude ends at `{` or `;`; an anonymous `@layer{}` stays anonymous. Layer names are case-sensitive, so no case variant could reach ours either. Mail layers keep working among themselves. The oracle fails any sanitized mail `@layer` name outside `m-`. Corpus 146 holds the same-name vectors.
- **(b) More properties.** Both rules now also set `position:static`, `inset:auto` and `writing-mode:horizontal-tb`, all `!important`. Measured and not needed: a descendant `position:absolute;top:0;bottom:-50px`, `position:relative`/`sticky` offsets, `block-size`/`min-block-size` (they share the cascade slot of `height`), `body{display:flex}`, `html{display:table}`, `contain:size`, `body{transform:scale(1000)}`: each settles. Bounded inflations, not runaways: `html{aspect-ratio:1/3}` (3 x the frame width), `html{padding-bottom:calc(100% + 50px)}` (a percentage of the width); both stay under the 200,000 px clamp. One more found while measuring: `body{zoom:1000}` inflated the reported height to 69,750 px, so the body rule also sets `zoom:1!important` (the root rule already did).
- **(c) The body's inline style is kept.** Dropping it would lose the body's colours, margins and padding (`style="margin:0;background:#f4f4f4"` is the common case in mail; the background paints the whole letter), and moving it to a wrapper would not repaint the canvas or keep `body > x` selectors. (Superseded: the layer does NOT beat an inline `!important`; the body style now carries an allowlist of properties, see below.) `<html style>` stays dropped.
- **LOW: the dropped `+`.** `calc(1px+100vh)` tokenizes as `1px` then `+100vh`; the rewritten unit lost its `+`, giving `1px600px`. `+` is kept (`calc(1px+600px)`, `+30px`), `-` is part of the number token, and `*` and `/` are separate tokens (rows for each; corpus 147). `calc(1px+100vh)` is invalid CSS before and after (a `+` needs whitespace around it): the rewrite changes nothing about validity.
- **Tests and mutations (each reverted to red).** No layer (unlayered rules): 1 unit, 8 browser (the 144 and 146 vectors grow, and every control now needs the layer to be there). No layer-name rename: 5 unit (including the corpus oracle), 1 browser (146). No `position:static;inset:auto`: 5 browser (141, 142, 144, 145, 146). No `writing-mode`: 2 browser (143, 146). No `+` kept: 1 unit. No body `zoom:1`: 1 browser. The new browser controls rebuild the document with the reading rules as they were before this change and show the frame growing past 2,000 px (or the cap) for all six vectors.


## 2026-11-04 — eng-html/eng-host: the three Opus LOWs of #248 and the relay test timeouts

- **Abort listener removed on settle (port.ts `call()`).** The caller's abort listener is taken off the signal when the call settles by any route (answer, error, timeout, abort), so a long-lived signal does not collect one per call. Test: five calls on one live signal add five listeners and remove five (red when the removal is dropped).
- **What the relay cap bounds (relay.ts comment).** `limitInFlight`'s 64 and 16 limit how many requests MAIN keeps waiting and the memory they hold; an aborted or timed-out call frees its slot while the utility process may still be fetching until its own 10 s limits end the work. The engine's per-window token cap and `Mail/serve`'s deadlines bound that side. Not a bug, stated so nobody reads the cap as an engine-work bound.
- **The 10 MiB body cap vs a sanitized document with big inline `data:` images.** The sanitizer keeps raster `data:` URLs in `<img src>` but the document is bounded by its own markup cap (524,288 characters, style text apart, 262,144 more for style); even a 500,000-character data URL or tens of thousands of escaped characters stays far under 5 MiB (test at the limits). The 10 MiB cap is for cid parts and proxied images, not documents, so a legitimate document is never refused.
- **Timeouts on the two hang-detected relay tests** (abort frees once; per-window cap): `{ timeout: 5000 }`, so a regression fails in about 6 s instead of hanging the file.
## 2026-11-04 — eng-host: the sealed mail frame in main (key relay, mailto routing, process isolation)

html-5 part 1, items (a) to (c) of the Integrator's 2026-11-04 03:59 DECIDED. Decisions are pure in `host/protocol/frame-keys.ts`, `frame-links.ts` and `frame-relays.ts` (no runtime Electron import, so node:test runs them); `main/windows.ts` makes one call, `attachSealedFrameRelays(contents, frameLinks)`, per app window, and `tests/eng-host/sealed-frame.e2e.ts` calls the same function in a hidden Electron.

- **(a) Key relay.** Main's `before-input-event` relay is the sealed frame's one key path. The reporter has no key fallback (`main-glue.test.ts` pins it, now `mainKeys: ['host/protocol/frame-relays.ts']`, and fails if a reporter fallback appears while the relay is on). The srcdoc frame (development only) keeps the renderer's own listener (`mailframe/keys.ts`); the two cannot overlap because main relays only when `webContents.focusedFrame` is a direct child of the main frame whose URL is exactly an issued `aplos-mail://m/<32 hex>/` document (`about:srcdoc`, the app page, a `cid/<n>` URL, a grandchild and a frame with no parent are all refused). `Input` carries no frame, so `focusedFrame` is the only source (measured in html-5, and again here). One source of truth for the list: `apps/desktop/src/shared/mail-keys.ts` (`MAIL_KEYS`), because main may not import the renderer and the renderer may not import main; `renderer/mailframe/keys.ts` now builds `FALLBACK_KEYS` from it (same eleven keys: j k J K ArrowUp ArrowDown PageUp PageDown Home End Escape; the plan's "arrows" is the two arrows keys.ts lists). Rules in `mailKeyOfInput`: keydown only, no auto-repeat (neither keys.ts nor PROTOCOL.md relays a held key; the srcdoc listener does not filter repeat, which is its existing behaviour and unchanged), no composition, no Ctrl/Cmd/Alt, Shift only for J and K. E, R, Space, Enter, Delete, Backspace, Tab, ArrowLeft/Right and everything else are not relayed. Main does not cancel the key (PROTOCOL.md says only the host cancels in the srcdoc path), so arrows and Page Up/Down still scroll the letter.
- **The message.** Channel `aplos:mail-key`, payload `{ key: <MAIL_KEYS entry> }` exactly: a plain object with one own data property (no extra key, getter, symbol, inherited key or array). `parseMailKeyMessage` runs in main before `send` and again in preload before delivery; preload hands it to `window.aplos.subscribe` listeners as `{ type: 'mail-key', payload: { key } }` and never queues it. No Electron event object crosses. The consumer (`MailFrame` / the shell) is not wired: nothing in the renderer subscribes to `mail-key` yet (ui-reader; the `MailFrame` `onKey` path takes it).
- **(b) Links.** `classifyFrameLink` parses with `new URL`, caps raw and parsed length at 10,000 (handleLink's and `external`'s own cap), and returns mail, web or deny. `mailto:` goes to `handleLink` (the `deep-link` event to Compose) with the parsed `href`; `http(s)` with no credentials goes to `external` (security.ts: protocol http/https/mailto, no username or password, 10,000 characters: unchanged, so a web link is validated twice) and nothing opens in test mode; everything else is denied: `javascript:`, `data:`, `file:`, `blob:`, `tel:`, `ftp:`, `sms:`, `about:`, `aplos-mail:`, `aplos:` (handleLink itself would pass `aplos:`; a letter must not reach it), credentials, an empty host, an unparsable URL. Not widened, narrowed: a mailto whose recipient or any parameter except `body` holds a control character (CR, LF, NUL: the header-injection shape) is denied, and a malformed percent-escape in the recipient is denied. The parameters themselves (`subject`, `cc`, `bcc`, `body`) still pass through unchanged, as the window-open path always did, and the renderer prefills a draft the owner sees before anything is sent; a hostile letter can therefore prefill a `bcc` (unchanged behaviour, flagged for the Compose consumer to show). Paths: `window.open` and `target=_blank` (measured: arrive at `setWindowOpenHandler`), and a link with no target (measured: `will-frame-navigate` fires with `frame` and `initiator` both the mail frame, for mailto, http, https, data and tel; it does not fire for `javascript:` or `file:`, which Chromium refuses first). A navigation counts as the letter's link only when `frame` is an issued mail document and `initiator` is that frame or null; a mailto/web target from it is cancelled and routed, any other target is left to the existing lock (security.ts and windows.ts), which denies it. windows.ts's open handler now routes through the same classifier for every window (the old `/^mailto:/i` branch is gone), and the old behaviour for http/https/other schemes is the same.
- **(c) Process isolation, measured (Electron 44.7.2, macOS, hidden window, real preload and real `security.ts`/`mail-protocol.ts`).** The sealed frame's `osProcessId` differs from the page's and from main's, and both are live `Tab` renderer processes in `app.getAppMetrics()` (frame 48060, page 48059, main 48003 in one run). In the frame: `window.aplos`, `window.aplosEngine`, `require`, `process`, `Buffer` and `electron` are all `undefined` (the page, with the same preload, has `aplos` and `aplosEngine`: positive control), `origin` is `'null'`, `parent.document`, `parent.aplos`, `top.location` and `localStorage` throw `SecurityError`, and from the page `iframe.contentDocument` is `null` and `contentWindow.document` throws. Not measured: a real key press in the packaged app, and an OS-level process list of the packaged build.
- **Measured limit of the key test.** `webContents.sendInputEvent` is the browser-side input path, but in a hidden, never-activated window it reaches the page's own `keydown` listener as well as `before-input-event` (with `focusedFrame` naming the letter), while the letter's own listener sees nothing. A real key in an active window should go to the focused out-of-process frame only; if it also reached the page, the shell would see J/K twice (once from the page, once from `mail-key`). That is the first thing the packaged-app hand-check must watch. OWNER STEP: in the packaged app (real engine path), click into a sealed letter, press J, K, arrows, Page Down, Esc and E, R, Space: each listed key must act once, and E/R/Space must do nothing; click a mailto link (Compose opens prefilled, nothing is sent) and an https link (default browser).
- **Tests.** `tests/eng-host/sealed-frame.test.ts` (unit, in `npm test`): exact list, modifier/destructive/repeat/key-up refusals, frame filtering with fakes, forged messages at both ends (preload bundled and run against a fake `electron`), link table, caps, CRLF, the navigation guard, the wiring pins and the double-delivery guard. `tests/eng-host/sealed-frame.e2e.ts` (hidden Electron, about 8 s, not in `npm test`; `npx tsx --test apps/desktop/tests/eng-host/sealed-frame.e2e.ts`, `SEALED_DEBUG=1` prints the measurements). Mutation-checked (each went red, unit and e2e where noted in the report): list gains/loses a key, modifiers/shift/repeat/key-up allowed, parse accepting extras or any key, preload delivering unvalidated, relay with the frame check weakened or gone (srcdoc, any frame, main frame), an extra field sent, `javascript:` and other schemes routed, mailto to the browser, CRLF allowed, credentials allowed, both length caps off, `initiator` ignored, windows.ts not using `handleLink` or not attaching the relays, `window.open` allowed, a reporter key fallback added next to main's relay.
- **Files in other lanes.** eng-html: `host/protocol/frame-{keys,links,relays}.ts` (new), `tests/eng-html/main-glue.test.ts` (the pin). eng-contract: `main/preload.ts` (one listener), `apps/desktop/src/shared/mail-keys.ts` (new). ui-reader: `renderer/mailframe/keys.ts` (builds `FALLBACK_KEYS` from the shared list) and `PROTOCOL.md` (Keys, checklist 7). These files are not yet in plans/OWNERS.md: `host/protocol/frame-*.ts` match eng-html's `host/{protocol,proxy}/**`; `shared/mail-keys.ts` matches eng-contract's `shared/**`.

## 2026-11-04 — eng-html: review fixes for the reading layer (two independent reviews, verdict FIX)

`MAIL_HTML_VERSION` is now `mail-html/4` (the reading CSS, the sanitized CSS and the reporter changed; `mail-html/3` was never merged). One PR answers the Integrator's REQUEST of 05:20 and the old Engine thread's review of #249.

- **HIGH: an inline `!important` on the body beat the layer (my earlier claim was wrong).** In the CSS cascade, element-attached styles (a `style` attribute) rank before layers, so a layer never outranks an element's own style attribute, `!important` or not. Measured in headless Chromium: `<body style="position:fixed!important;top:0!important;bottom:-50px!important">` ran away (301 reports, 14,900 px), `writing-mode:vertical-lr!important;margin-bottom:-50px` ran away (303 reports, 7,550 px), `zoom:1000!important` reported 69,750 px. Corpus 145 passed before only because its offsets had no `!important`. Fix: the frame body's `style` keeps an allowlist, `background*`, `color`, `margin*`, `padding*`, `font*`, `text-align` and `line-height` (`css.ts` `keepDeclarations`, `sanitize.ts` `BODY_STYLE_PROPERTIES`); every other declaration is left out, in any case, escape or comment spelling, and a `;` inside a string or `url()` can neither end a declaration early nor smuggle one in. Chosen over the Integrator's fallback (drop the attribute) because the common mail body style (`margin:0;background:#f4f4f4;padding:...`) keeps its visual result, and over stripping only `!important` because an allowlist needs no reasoning about which properties matter and holds for any future CSS property. What is lost: an inline body `width`, `height`, `display`, `position`, `overflow`, `border`, `transform` and the like are ignored (a `body{...}` rule in a `<style>` block still sets what the layer does not guard). `<html style>` stays dropped. Verified in Chromium: corpus 145 (`!important` on every offset), 148 (`zoom:1000!important`) and 149 (`writing-mode:vertical-lr!important`) settle, and their controls (the mail's body style written back onto the sanitized body) run away or inflate. The oracle fails any sanitized frame body style with a property outside the allowlist. No other element can carry these properties inline onto `html` or `body`: only the first `<html>` and `<body>` of a mail lend attributes, and `html` lends `dir` and `lang` only. Corrected in the section above: the layer does not win over a `style` attribute.
- **Item 1: clipped content (measured by the old thread's review, adopted).** The report is `ceil(max(root box, body top + scroll offset + body.scrollHeight))`, and the reading layer makes the body `position:relative` (no offsets), so the body is the containing block of absolutely positioned content and its scroll height takes in positioned and transformed descendants, which the root's box leaves out. Measured, with the body term against the root box alone: `position:absolute;top:1200px` 1,223 against 86; a 900 px absolute child of a relative box 931 against 86; a `top:100%` dropdown 356 against 72; `translateY(800px)` 893 against 109; `body{transform:translateY(500px)}` 585 against 86; `body{margin-bottom:-60px}` 185 against 126 (corpus 150-155, each with a root-box-only control). The scroll offset (`scrollY`) is added because the body's top moves up when the frame is scrolled (a fragment link; a frame the host froze). Interaction with the growth vectors, re-verified with the body positioned: corpus 141-149 all still settle (an absolute body or root with a negative bottom has no offsets to follow: `inset:auto`).
- **Item 2: more measuring-rule guards.** `offset-path:none` (root and body), `container-type:normal` and `contain:none` (both; `container-type:size` is size containment that `contain:none` does not undo), `content-visibility:visible` (a root with `content-visibility:hidden;contain-intrinsic-size:auto 5000px` reported a fixed 5,000 px), `float:none`, `zoom:1` (both), and on the root `margin:0`, `min-height:1px` (an empty letter still has a size) and no `transform`, `scale`, `rotate` or `translate`. Honest note: with the body term in the report, size containment, an offset-path root and a zero-width root are largely masked even without these rules (the browser controls use the previous rules AND the root box alone), so they are belt and braces for the browser; the unit test pins each declaration and the browser checks (corpus 156-158, 163) pin the result.
- **Item 3: a vertical root.** `html{writing-mode:vertical-lr}` reported the frame's own height with the old `scrollHeight` reporter and never shrank. Covered: the layer forces `horizontal-tb` on the root and body; corpus 143; a browser check where a 1,500 px frame shrinks to the letter (control: the root's scroll height reports 1,500).
- **Item 4: corrected wording, and what is built.** The earlier statement that the viewport rewrite stops a letter from depending on its frame was too strong: it covers viewport and container LENGTH units only. Measured by the review: vertical text in a `div` (`writing-mode:vertical-rl`, long CJK) grows 182, 214, 246 ... for ever; `@media (max-height:300px)` flips 532 and 132; `@media (orientation:landscape)` flips 732 and 42; a chain of 50 `min-height` rules climbs to 5,232; swapping padding between `orientation:portrait` and `landscape` oscillates for ever (303 reports in 8 s, the host cap). Built, small and linear (one set lookup per identifier, a look-ahead past whitespace and comments only for `writing-mode`): (1) in `@media` and `@container` preludes the features `height`, `min-height`, `max-height`, `block-size`, `aspect-ratio` (and min/max), `orientation`, `device-height`, `device-aspect-ratio` (and min/max) become `--m-<name>`, an unknown feature, so the query is false (the desktop layout) and cannot toggle; range syntax and escapes are covered; width features and every non-query use of those words are untouched. A mail that styled itself with `@media (max-height:...)` or `(orientation:portrait)` loses those rules. (2) The property name `writing-mode` and `-webkit-writing-mode` (an identifier before a colon, anywhere) becomes `--m-writing-mode`, a custom property that does nothing: vertical text is lost to mail, which is rare; the root and body are held horizontal by the layer. Corpus 159-162 settle in Chromium; their controls write the original names back and run away. Still needing the host's growth fence (ui-core's 100-growth fence): any other way a layout follows its frame (percentage chains with intrinsic sizes, container queries against a size container the mail declares, other `@container` size features). Pre-existing aspect-ratio and percentage padding on the root inflate by a fixed multiple of the frame's width (`html{aspect-ratio:1/3}` reports 3 x the width, `html{padding-bottom:calc(100% + 50px)}` the width and 50): bounded and stable, not a runaway.
- **Item 5: mutation survivors pinned.** Removing `}` from the `@layer` prelude terminator (`@media x{@layer a} .foo{}` renamed `.foo`) is now caught by `css.test.ts` and by the oracle, which stopped at `{` or `;` only and now also at `}`. Removing `min-height:1px` is caught by the guard-list test and by a browser check that an empty zero-padding body reports at least 1.
- **Item 6: output caps.** The unit rewrite can lengthen CSS (`.01vw` is `0.06px`), and the parse limits bound the input only. The sanitized output is now bounded too: a `style` attribute whose rewritten text passes `MAX_STYLE_ATTRIBUTE` (32,768) is dropped, `<style>` blocks together may write at most `maxStyleChars` (262,144), style attributes together at most `maxChars` (524,288). Tests: 256 KiB of `.01vw` values in a block (about 307 KB out, dropped) and 30,000 characters in an attribute (dropped), with the same shapes under the cap kept.
- **Item 7: open note for the relay (not touched).** With Caps Lock on, the key relay (`main/mail-keys.ts`, eng-host, #252) sends `{key: 'J', shift: false}`: its consumer must match the letter case-insensitively and not read `shift` as "an uppercase letter was typed".
- **Browser-level survivors, recorded.** Removing any ONE of `position:static`, `inset:auto`, `writing-mode:horizontal-tb`, `contain:none`, `container-type:normal` or `float:none` alone stays green in the browser (the unit test pins that each is written): with the body term in the report and the body positioned, each is covered by another guard or by the reporter. Removing `position:static` and `inset:auto` TOGETHER turns corpus 141 (and 146) red (a root absolute with a negative bottom), so the pair is needed and each is the other's second line. Browser-red on a single removal: `position:relative` on the body (150), the body top in the report (154), `offset-path:none` (157), `content-visibility:visible` (163), `min-height:1px` (empty letter), `zoom:1` (root and body), the body allowlist (145, 148, 149), the media rewrite (160-162), `orientation` alone (161), the `writing-mode` rename (159).

## 2026-11-04 — eng-host: a sealed letter after an in-mail fragment link (Integrator's MEDIUM from the #252 review)

- **The bug.** A fragment link in a letter (a newsletter's table of contents; the sanitizer keeps them) changes the frame's URL to `aplos-mail://m/<token>/#m-x`. The strict document check then failed, so the key relay stopped (J/K/Esc did nothing) and `isMailFrameLink` refused the letter's later link clicks. It failed safe, never open.
- **The fix.** `isShownMailDocumentUrl` (host/protocol/relay.ts) ignores only the fragment, everything from the FIRST `#`, and then applies the unchanged `isMailDocumentUrl`. `frame-keys.ts` (is the focused frame the letter) and `frame-links.ts` (did the letter start this navigation) use it; both ask about a frame that is already showing its letter. Navigation checks (security.ts and windows.ts `mailFrameURL`, which judge a URL a frame is about to load) keep the strict `isMailDocumentUrl`: a fragment is not an issued document. Still refused, tested: `…/<token>/x#y`, `…/cid/0#y`, `…/<token>/?q#f`, `…/<token>#f` (no trailing slash), `…/m/#<token>/` and a `#` inside the token, an empty token, double slash, uppercase hex, another host, `about:srcdoc#f`, non-strings.
- **Tests, mutation-checked.** Unit (`sealed-frame.test.ts`): the grammar table, keys after a fragment navigation, a link click after a fragment navigation. E2E (`sealed-frame.e2e.ts`): a real click on a `href="#m-section"` link in Chromium moves the frame to `<document>#m-section`, then J is relayed and a mailto and an https link still route. Red when either file goes back to the strict check, when the strip also drops a query, when it strips after the last `#`, when it accepts more than the fragment, and when the navigation check accepts a fragment.
- **LOW, recorded and not changed.** A letter's `mailto:a@b?bcc=evil` still prefills Bcc silently in Compose (legacy `App.tsx` deep-link handler). It is the pre-existing behaviour of every mailto the window-open path ever routed, recorded in the "(b) Links" entry of the section above (2026-11-04, "the sealed mail frame in main"); main's classifier only added the control-character refusal. Whether Compose shows pre-filled Cc/Bcc visibly or drops Bcc for links that came from a letter is a ui-reader / ui-core call.

## 2026-11-04 — eng-contract: Blob package 1, contract and ports (plans/engine/blob-area.md)

- **`BlobFiles` port** (`ports/content.ts`): `put(id, header, bytes)`, `get(id)` (`{header, bytes}` or `undefined`), `remove(id)` (idempotent), `list()` (`{id, header, mtimeMs}[]`), `usage()`. `BlobHeader` is `{principal, kind: 'upload'|'original', mime, name?, size, createdAt}`. The engine never passes or receives a path. With it: `BLOB_ID_PATTERN` (`^b_[0-9a-f]{32}$`, checked by the engine AND again by the host), `BLOB_FILE_LIMITS` (25 MiB per blob, 2 KB header line, 2 GiB folder, 1 GiB free-disk floor; upper bounds an implementation may only tighten) and `BlobFilesError(reason)` with reason codes `id | header | tooLarge | folderFull | diskFull | exists | io` (never a path or OS text). "MB/GB" are binary throughout (25 MB = 26,214,400 B), matching the transport's 26 MiB blob envelope and the fixture's `25 * 1024 * 1024`.
- **`HostPorts.blobFiles`** (additive optional, `EngineServices.blobFiles`): absent stays the `unavailable` stand-in, so `Blob/*` answers "This part of Aplos is not running yet." until a host injects it.
- **`ProviderPort.original?`** (additive optional, doc only; decision 13): `original(account, remoteMessageId): Promise<AsyncIterable<Uint8Array>>`. No implementation in this package; `prov-gov`/`prov-gmail` fill it (package 7).
- **`controlMethods` + `Blob/get`**: main's `File/save`/`File/open` (package 9) read bytes on main's control connection. Main is the `app` principal (control caller kind `host`). `Blob/put` and `Blob/release` stay window-only.
- **Reply buffer transfer** (`host/transports/port.ts`): `EnginePortEnd.post(message, transfer?)`; `serveWindowPort` moves a `blobRoutes` OK reply's `bytes.buffer` (`replyTransfer`) when the view covers the whole, unshared `ArrayBuffer`; a view onto a larger buffer, an error reply and every non-blob route are never moved. The engine must hand out a fresh buffer per `Blob/get` (package 3; `BlobFiles.get` already returns a fresh one per call) so a move never detaches a cached one.
- **Measured limit, recorded:** Electron's `MessagePortMain.postMessage(message, transfer?: MessagePortMain[])` accepts only ports in its transfer list (`electron.d.ts`), so the utility process's `wrapPort` ignores `transfer` and the reply is cloned across the process boundary once, as before. The move applies where the engine's end is a DOM or Node `MessagePort` (tests, an in-process host, a future mobile host). No extra copy existed in `port.ts` before; the change removes the second in-process copy only where the port can move buffers.
- **Generated artifacts:** no contract schema changed; `npm run generate -w @aplos/contract` leaves `methods.generated.ts` and `openapi.json` byte-identical. `contract-fixture` unchanged.
- **Tests and mutations** (`apps/desktop/tests/eng-contract/port.test.ts`, 3 new): the window receives a Blob/get reply's bytes and the engine's buffer is detached; a sub-view is copied (the engine's buffer intact) and a non-blob reply is never moved; `controlMethods` has `Blob/get` and not `Blob/put`/`Blob/release`. Each red under its mutation: no transfer, transfer a view's whole buffer, transfer on every route, `Blob/get` dropped from `controlMethods`.
## 2026-11-04 — eng-html: second review of the reading layer (verdict FIX): cache collision, writing-mode aliases, explicit body allowlist

`MAIL_HTML_VERSION` is now `mail-html/5` (sanitized output changed).

- **HIGH: the body's style shared a cache key space with other elements' styles.** The body's filtered style was cached under `'!' + style`, every other element's unfiltered style under the bare text. A `<div style="!x;P">` stored its unfiltered result under `!x;P`, and a later `<body style="x;P">` looked up `!x;P` and was handed it: the body kept `position:fixed!important` and `zoom:1000!important` and ran away (268 reports, 650 to 13,800 px; 108,500 px with zoom). The body's style is no longer cached at all (`sanitize.ts` `bodyStyle`: there is one body, and a cache buys nothing). Audit of every cache in the sanitizer for the same class: `styleTokens`, `hidingStyles` and `cleanStyles` are each keyed by the bare style text for exactly one transformation, so nothing else can alias; `images.ts` keeps separate maps per kind (`byUrl` for normalised remote URLs, `cidIndex` and `cidIndexFolded` for content ids), so no prefix trick is possible there either. The rule: a key may never be built by prefixing another transformation's key; use a separate map. Corpus 164 (the div and body shape), a unit test over six prefix shapes and both orders, and a browser check with a control (the body style written back runs away). Mutation: the shared cache restored turns the unit test, the corpus oracle (both image policies) and the browser check red.
- **MEDIUM: `-epub-writing-mode`.** Chromium honours `-epub-writing-mode` (and `-webkit-writing-mode`) as aliases of `writing-mode`; only the unprefixed and `-webkit-` names were renamed, and a vertical block under `-epub-writing-mode` hit the host cap (632 to 5,400 px). Now every identifier before a colon whose lowercase name ends in `writing-mode`, under any prefix, escape or case, becomes `--m-<name>`; custom properties (`--...`) are left alone, which keeps the sanitizer idempotent. Chromium sweep: `CSS.supports` is true for `writing-mode`, `-webkit-writing-mode` and `-epub-writing-mode` only; `-moz-`, `-ms-`, `-o-` are unknown. Measured to settle by themselves (long CJK in a block, no writing mode): `text-orientation`, `-webkit-text-orientation`, `text-combine-upright`, `glyph-orientation-vertical`, `direction` with `unicode-bidi`, `-webkit-line-break`, `display:-webkit-box;-webkit-box-orient:vertical`, a flex column; they only matter inside a vertical writing mode, which no longer exists. Corpus 165 and a browser check with a control (the original names written back run away).
- **LOW: the body allowlist is explicit names, with bounded type.** `body-style.ts` lists the property names: `background`, `background-color`, `-image`, `-repeat`, `-position`, `-size`, `-attachment`, `-origin`, `-clip`; `color`; `margin` and `padding` with `-top`, `-right`, `-bottom`, `-left` and the logical `-block`, `-block-start`, `-block-end`, `-inline`, `-inline-start`, `-inline-end`; `font`, `font-family`, `-size`, `-style`, `-weight`, `-variant`; `line-height`; `text-align`. Anything else is dropped, including any other property under those prefixes (`font-size-adjust:1000` gave 16,917 px). `url()` in `background` and `background-image` still goes through the image book. Bounds, decided: `font-size`, `line-height` and the `font` shorthand are dropped when a length is over 200 px or the equivalent (`em`/`rem`/`ch`/`ex`/`lh` count 16 px, absolute units converted, a percentage is a multiple of 16 px, an unknown unit such as `vh` or any function such as `calc()` is dropped, a unitless line height over 10 including the number after the `/` in `font`). `margin` and `padding` are not bounded: a large margin is a spacer, stable and no taller than a spacer `div`, and the clamps bound it. Tests: explicit unknown names under each prefix are dropped, in-bound type is kept, corpus 166, and a browser check that a 900 px font written back onto the body inflates it past 5,000 px while the sanitized body stays small.
- **Mutations (each red).** Shared cache restored: 1 unit, the corpus oracle (images off and on), 1 browser (164). Alias regex back to `-webkit-` only: 1 unit, 1 browser (165). Prefix patterns instead of names: 1 unit. Type bound removed: 1 unit, 1 browser (166).

## 2026-11-04 — eng-html: CSS if(media()), scroll-driven animations, display:contents and late moves (review of #255, Integrator 07:44)

`MAIL_HTML_VERSION` is now `mail-html/6` (sanitized CSS and the reporter changed). Earlier sections still say /3, /4 and /5; the last one wins. New branch `rv/eng-html/css-if-media` (the old one was merged without this).

- **HIGH: `if(media(...))` bypassed the media rewrite.** Chromium 137 and later (so Electron 44) evaluates CSS `if()`, whose `media()` conditions take the very features the `@media` rewrite renames. Measured in the bundled Chromium: `height:if(media(max-height:300px):532px;else:132px)` flipped 564 and 164 px (217 reports in 3.5 s); `margin-bottom:if(media(orientation:landscape):700px;else:0px)` through the allowlisted body style flipped 786 and 86. Fix, the smaller one of the two offered: `if` joins `DROPPED_FUNCTIONS` next to `attr`, so the whole function becomes `""`, the declaration is invalid and the browser ignores it (in `<style>`, in `style` attributes, in the body's allowlisted properties, inside `calc()` or `var()`, in any case, and through an escaped name since the tokenizer decodes it). Chosen over renaming features inside `media(` because `if()` also takes `supports()` and `style()` conditions, those cannot follow the frame, and dropping loses little: `if()` is new and almost unused in mail. Corpus 167 and 168, unit rows, and browser checks whose control runs the original text as written (a raw document) and flips.
- **Other condition-capable functions, measured in the same Chromium, all settle:** `if(supports())`, `if(style())`, `calc-size(auto, size + 100px)`, `calc(sibling-index() * 100px)`, `random()`, `anchor-size()`, `env(safe-area-inset-top, 50px)`, and `@container scroll-state(scrollable: bottom)`. Nothing else added.
- **LOW: container `min-block-size` and `max-block-size`** join `HEIGHT_FEATURES` (with `block-size`), and a unit row pins `min-aspect-ratio` and `max-aspect-ratio` too (the reviewer's survivor).
- **MEDIUM: scroll-driven animations.** `animation-timeline:scroll(root)` ties an animation's progress to the scroller, which is the frame: a 1000 px height keyframe flipped the letter between 86 and 1086 (217 reports in 3.5 s). `animation-timeline`, `animation-range` (`-start`, `-end`), `scroll-timeline*`, `view-timeline*` and `timeline-scope` become custom properties through the same look-ahead as `writing-mode`; `scroll()` and `view()` are dropped as functions. Measured: the `animation` shorthand with `scroll(root)` is ignored by this Chromium, so the function drop is belt and braces (removing it alone stays green in the browser; the unit row pins it). Ordinary animations and transitions are untouched. Corpus 169; the letter settles at 2,086 (two 1000 px keyframe blocks, no timeline) steadily.
- **MEDIUM: the clipped-content measurement was bypassable.** (a) `body{display:contents}` removed the body's box, so `position:relative` made no containing block and the report was 54 against a true 1,224: the reading layer now sets `display:block!important` on the body (the root is already `flow-root`). Corpus 170, control 54-ish with the previous rules. (b) A box moved after load by an animation changes no size the ResizeObserver sees: reported 86 against 1,224. The reporter now (1) observes the body as well as the root, (2) listens, in the capture phase, for `animationend`, `animationcancel`, `transitionend`, `load` and `error` as well as `toggle` and `resize`, (3) looks at the page on every animation frame for 3 s after the last change or event (`requestAnimationFrame`; none in a frame that is hidden, which has nothing to show), and (4) posts only a report that differs from the last one, at most 20 a second, and a report held back by the limit is posted by the frame loop later (so a burst never loses its last state). The first report is still posted synchronously, with no frame needed. The `report storm` LOW is closed by (4): an infinite height animation costs the host at most 20 messages a second (the browser check allows at most 85 in 3 s) instead of about 80. Corpus 171 (an animation that ends), 172 (a jump with no end event for 100 s: only the frame loop sees it) and 173 (a move that starts after the quiet period: only the `animationend` event sees it); each browser check has a control with that part of the reporter removed.
- **What remains uncovered, honestly.** A move that starts more than 3 s after the last change AND has no end event (an infinite or very long animation that starts late, a `steps()` jump after 3 s, a CSS transition that never ends because it is retargeted) is not seen until the next resize, toggle, load, font or image event. Layout that changes only through a script cannot occur (the mail has none). A letter whose height keeps changing is limited to 20 reports a second and the host's own fence still applies. A body `font-size` set from a `<style>` rule (`body{font-size:900px}` measured 3,227 px) or `font-size-adjust` is not guarded by the reading layer: the font size is inherited into every `em` and `rem` length of the letter, so changing it is a layout change, and it is a tall letter, not a runaway (bounded by the clamp). The body-style bound is therefore a bound on that attribute only; the comment in `body-style.ts` now says so. The `body{margin-bottom:-60px}` measurement in an earlier section (185) is the corpus 155 content; the review's content measured 124.
- **Mutations (each red).** `if` not dropped: 2 unit, 2 browser (167, 168). `scroll`/`view` not dropped: 2 unit (browser: green, as above). Timeline properties not renamed: 2 unit, 1 browser (169). Block-size features removed: 1 unit. `max-aspect-ratio` removed: 1 unit. `display:block` removed from the body rule: 1 unit, 1 browser (170). Frame loop removed: 2 unit, 2 browser (172 and its control). Animation events removed: 2 unit, 1 browser (173). Dedupe removed: 2 unit, 1 browser (171). Rate limit removed: 1 unit, 1 browser. Body not observed: 1 unit. `keepDeclarations` splitting at any bracket depth: 1 unit (a `;` inside a function).

## 2026-11-04 — eng-html: reporter lifetime, event cost and the off-screen frame (Integrator 08:49, old thread's re-check of #257)

`MAIL_HTML_VERSION` stays `mail-html/6` (no sanitized output changed). The reporter text changed, so its hash is new (`reporter.test.ts` recomputes the pin from the shipped file; the CSP carries it).

- **One design for the watch loop.** (1) The change key is height, width, readiness and marks. The background colour still rides in every message but is not a reason to post. (2) Only a new height or width, or a `load`, `error`, `DOMContentLoaded`, `toggle`, `resize`, font or resize-observer event, extends the 3 s watch; an `animationend`, `animationcancel` or `transitionend` event asks for ONE look on the next frame and extends the watch only if that look finds a new size (80 staggered 0.01 s animations used to keep the loop alive for the whole window, and a mail's size could stretch it to hours). (3) An event does no measuring: it sets a flag and asks for a look; only the first report is measured synchronously. (4) The marks (`mark.aplos-find`) are counted once at the start: no script runs in a mail, so they cannot change, and no query over the letter remains on any path that runs per event or per frame. (5) At most 20 reports a second (as before), and at most 2,000 in all: the 2,000th carries the state of that moment and the reporter then stops for good (`live = false`).
- **Why the lifetime cap stops for good instead of posting once per N seconds.** A letter still changing size 100 s into a stream capped at 20 a second is the pathology the cap is for; a trickle would keep the pathological case alive for the life of the window and the host's freeze counts only growth steps. The host keeps the size of the 2,000th report. A genuine letter never comes near 2,000 distinct sizes.
- **Colour-only letters.** `body{animation:b .2s infinite alternate}` on a background colour posted for as long as it was open (about 23 reports a second measured, 171 in 8 s, never frozen); now it posts its first report and its readiness and nothing more (browser check: at most 4 reports, the same count at 3 s and at 7 s; the control with the colour back in the key and every post extending the watch keeps posting). The cost: a colour that changes after the last size change is not reported (the paper tint is what the first reports saw, and any later size, readiness or marks report carries the then-current colour).
- **Request to ui-reader (their file `reports.ts`, not edited here).** Freeze the frame in the host after N accepted reports of ANY kind (a few hundred), as the reporter's own cap is a backstop of 2,000 and the host's freeze still counts growth steps only.
