- **The off-screen frame (the old thread's HIGH by the rubric).** Chromium stops animation frames, and resize observers, in an off-screen cross-origin frame, and the sandboxed mail frame counts as one. A look is now asked for twice, an animation frame and a timer 250 ms later (`setTimeout` captured from the window at the start like every other member, and only as `w.setTimeout`; a test pins that), and whichever comes first runs it (a numbered ticket makes the other a no-op). Without it the held-back last report never arrives: measured with the frame 3,000 px down a 900 px host window and forty images that fail late, the final report (true height, `ready:true`) arrives within 4 s with the timer and stays stale without it (browser check and control). The earlier wording "no frame in a frame that is hidden" was wrong: off screen is not hidden.
- **Hot-path numbers (15,000 `<i>` elements ending an animation together, three runs each, in the frame's own process, time from the first to the last `animationend` handler).** Before: 115, 112 and 117 ms with the reporter against 16-17 ms with no reporter (a 7x cost, the review's 1.71 s against 0.80 s at 20,000). After: 36-39 ms against 17-18 ms (about 2.2x: the handlers are a flag and a timer request). The browser check allows 3x plus 30 ms; the control puts a synchronous report and a marks query back into the handler and is slower.
- **Late moves with no end event (the old thread's finding 4, kept open).** A move that starts more than 3 s after the last change AND has no end event (an infinite or very long animation, or a `steps()` jump, that starts late) is not seen until the next resize, toggle, load, font or image event. Listening for `animationstart` would catch the first but keep the loop alive for a plain spinner; a slow idle re-measure (about once a second) would catch the second at the price of a reporter that never sleeps. Not done; the host's own measurement on resize and visibility still corrects it.
- **The four harmless survivors, listed.** (1) Dropping `dirty` from the loop condition stays green and cannot be pinned: a report is held back only when 20 were posted in the last second, which means a size change in the last second, so the 3 s watch is still on; the flag is kept as a guard against a future change of the limit. (2) A `load` listener without capture: now pinned by a unit test that records the capture flag of every non-bubbling listener (the resize observer covers it on screen). (3) The body not observed: pinned by a unit test; the browser checks stay green because the root's box already moves with the body. (4) `scroll()`/`view()` not dropped as functions: browser green, as recorded above (the property rename covers them).
- **Mutations (each red).** Lifetime cap removed: 1 unit, 1 browser (175). Colour back in the key: 1 unit, 1 browser (174). Every post extending the watch: 1 unit. Animation events always re-arming: 1 unit. Marks counted per report: 1 unit. No timer: 4 unit (two timing flakes among them), 1 browser (below the fold). Loop without `dirty`: green (survivor 1). Stale looks not ignored: 1 unit. `load` without capture: 1 unit. Measuring inside the event: 2 unit, 1 browser (15,000).

## 2026-11-04 — eng-host: Blob package 2, the host blob store (`apps/desktop/src/host/blobs.ts`)

- **Ownership.** `apps/desktop/src/host/blobs.ts` is eng-host's (Integrator DECIDED 2026-11-04 03:45, answer 1). The OWNERS row is added by this package's own `OWNERS:` commit, as that answer asked; until it is on `revamp`, `owners:check` (which reads the rules from `origin/revamp`) reports the file as unowned.
- **Layout.** `<dataDir>/blobs/` (0700) with `<id>.blob` (0600) and `.tmp/` (0700). A file is the magic line `aplos-blob/1`, one JSON header line (exactly the `BlobHeader` fields, re-validated on write and on read, at most 2 KB) and the bytes.
- **Write.** Checks run synchronously first (id against `BLOB_ID_PATTERN`, 25 MiB, header describes the bytes, id not already stored or being written, folder cap), so a second put of the same id in the same process is `exists` before any I/O. Then the free-disk floor (`statfs`; a failure or NaN fails closed as `diskFull`), a temp file `open(O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_WRONLY|O_NOFOLLOW, 0o600)`, write all, `fsync`, **`link`** to the final name, unlink the temp, `fsync` the folder (best effort). `link` instead of the design's `rename`: `rename` silently replaces an existing name, `link` refuses it with `EEXIST`, which is what "refused if it exists" needs across two processes on one folder (tested). A failure before the link removes the temp and releases the reservation.
- **Read.** `open(O_RDONLY|O_NOFOLLOW)`, `fstat` regular file within the size cap, header parsed and checked, `offset + size` must equal the file size (a truncated or padded file is gone), and the bytes are read into a fresh `Uint8Array` of exactly `size` per call (whole buffer, safe to transfer).
- **Start.** The folder and `.tmp` are created if missing, then `lstat`ed: a link, a non-directory or a folder another user owns is refused (`BlobFilesError('io')`), else `chmod 0700`. `.tmp` is emptied. Every name in the folder that is not `<id>.blob`, is not a regular file (a planted symlink is unlinked, its target untouched) or does not read back is deleted, and the rest are sized, so the 2 GiB folder cap counts blobs from before a restart. `list()` re-runs the same scan.
- **Compose.** `openBlobPort` (compose.ts) opens `<dataDir>/blobs` unless `ports.blobFiles` was injected. An unsafe folder logs `rpc.failed {method: 'Host/blobs', reason: <code>}` (no path) and leaves the port out, so `Blob/*` is `unavailable` and the rest of the host starts.
- **Per-principal quotas are not here.** The host knows no principals beyond the stored header; `app` 512 MB/500 and `client:<id>` 100 MB/50 are the engine runtime's (package 3), which keeps the index.
- **Tests** (`apps/desktop/tests/eng-host/blobs.test.ts`, 13, real temp folders; `compose.test.ts` +2): round trip with fresh, transferable buffers; 0700/0600 under `umask 0` and an existing 0755 folder; 17 bad ids (traversal, case, length, `/`, newline, non-strings) refused with no write step and no file outside; symlink planted as the folder, as a dangling folder, as `.tmp`, as a file; a write killed at `link` or `fsync`; a crash's temps, a truncated blob, a lying header and foreign names cleaned at start; 25 MiB exact accepted and +1 refused; folder cap (also across a reopen), free-disk floor, NaN and a failing `statfs`; concurrent puts of one id in one store and across two stores; the real `FileHandle.sync` calls observed in order (file before link, folder after); no refusal names a path.
- **Mutations, each red then restored (16):** host id check skipped; `O_NOFOLLOW` dropped on read; 25 MB cap dropped; `rename` instead of `link`; no same-id reservation; no file `fsync`; no folder `lstat` check; no folder `chmod 0700`; no temp cleanup at start; no free-disk floor; no folder cap; header size not checked; truncated file accepted; temp file opened 0644 without `O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW`; compose throwing on an unsafe folder; compose not injecting the store. One survivor, by design: the `fstat` size cap on read is redundant with the header's `size <= maxBytes` and `offset + size == file size` checks (an equivalent mutant), kept as the design's first line.

## 2026-11-04 — eng-host: Blob package 2, fixes from the independent Opus security review (FIX)

- **HIGH, FIFO hang.** A blob file is now `lstat`ed first and must be a regular file with exactly one link before it is opened, and it is opened `O_RDONLY|O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`; the open file's `fstat` must again be a regular file with one link and the same `dev`/`ino` the `lstat` saw. A FIFO (or socket, device, directory) named like a blob is positively no blob: the scan deletes the name, `get` answers `undefined`, nothing blocks.
- **HIGH, a passing read error no longer deletes a blob.** A scan deletes only names that are not `<id>.blob`, and files positively seen to be no blob (not regular, more than one link, over the size cap, or a bad magic/header/size after a full read of the head). Any other failure (`EACCES`, `EMFILE`, `EIO`, a swap between `lstat` and `open`) is `skip`: the file stays, keeps counting against the folder cap with its `lstat` size, is left out of `list()`, and `get` answers `undefined`. Held drafts' attachments survive a file-handle shortage at start.
- **MEDIUM, folder swapped after start.** `privateFolder` opens the folder with `O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW` and checks and `fchmod`s the open handle (no path `chmod` that a swap could redirect). Every `list`, `put`, `get` and `remove` re-`lstat`s the folder first (a real directory of ours, not a link) and refuses with `io` (`get`: `undefined`).
- **MEDIUM, no raw OS error leaves the module.** `list()` and the folder reads run under one `coded()` wrapper; a failed delete of a stranger is skipped. Every error out of `blobs.ts` is a `BlobFilesError` reason code.
- **LOW, accounting.** A scan builds a fresh size table and swaps it in at the end, keeping puts that finished while it ran; a name whose put is between `link` and the temp unlink (two links for a moment) is never judged mid-write. `remove` forgets a size only after the unlink succeeded (or `ENOENT`).
- **Tests added (blobs.test.ts, now 22):** FIFO named like a blob at open and after; a mode-000 blob survives `list()` and a restart, is still counted, and reads whole once readable; the folder swapped for a link after start (put, list, remove refuse, get is `undefined`, the target and its `.tmp` untouched); a `list()` failure is `io` with no path, an undeletable stranger is skipped; a second hard link; a slow scan keeps old blobs counted and keeps a put that finished mid-scan; a scan between `link` and temp unlink; a failed unlink keeps the size.
- **Mutations, each red then restored (24):** FIFO guards (lstat-first and `O_NONBLOCK` together); an open error deleting the blob; no folder re-check in scan; no folder re-check in put; `list()` and readdir both uncoded (each alone is covered by the other layer, an equivalent pair); sizes cleared before the scan; puts finished mid-scan dropped; scan judging a file mid-write; size dropped before a failed unlink; no `nlink` check; host id check; `O_NOFOLLOW` plus the `lstat` link check on read (`O_NOFOLLOW` alone is now an equivalent mutant behind the `lstat`); 25 MB cap; `rename` for `link`; no same-id reservation; no file `fsync`; folder opened without `O_NOFOLLOW`; no folder `fchmod 0700`; no temp cleanup; no free-disk floor; no folder cap; truncated file accepted; plus the two compose mutations from the first round.

## 2026-11-04 — eng-host: Blob package 2, Opus re-check (FIX) round 2

- **MEDIUM, a second hard link is not corruption.** A blob whose file has `nlink > 1` (a hard-link backup such as rsnapshot or `cp -al`, or a write whose temp unlink failed after the `link`) is `skip`: not served by `get`, never deleted by a scan, still counted in `usage()` with its `lstat` size. It is served again once it has one link (the next start empties `.tmp`, which ends the crash case).
- **LOW, removes and puts during a scan.** Each running scan records the ids removed and put while it runs and applies them to its fresh size table before the swap, so a `remove` that lands mid-scan stays removed (a later put of that id is not `exists`) and a put that finishes mid-scan stays counted. A name whose put is between `link` and the temp unlink is not judged; the put's own record counts it.
- **LOW, no recursive delete by path.** Strays in the folder and in `.tmp` are removed one entry at a time: a file is unlinked, an empty folder `rmdir`ed, and a folder with anything in it is left alone (Node has no `unlinkat`, so a recursive delete by path could follow a link swapped in after the folder check). A planted non-empty folder therefore stays, harmless: it is not a blob name and is never read.
- **LOW, the lstat-to-open window.** A test-only `peek` seam runs between a read's `lstat` and its `open`; a test swaps the blob for a FIFO there and the read returns `undefined` at once (the open is `O_NONBLOCK`, the `fstat` inode differs, the entry is left alone). Without `O_NONBLOCK` that test hangs, so the pairing (lstat type check first, `O_NONBLOCK` for the swap window) is now proven, not only defence in depth.
- **Tests (blobs.test.ts, 24):** the hard-link test now asserts survive/not served/counted/served again; removes during a scan; a FIFO swapped in between `lstat` and `open`; a non-empty planted folder is left alone.
- **Simplified:** the scan no longer skips names whose put is in flight; such a file has two links for a moment and is `skip` (never deleted) like any other, and the put's own record sets its size. The race tests now use the `peek` seam (the change lands at the scan's 20th read of 40 blobs), so they no longer depend on timing.
- **Mutations, each red then restored (9):** second hard link deleted as corrupt (also red in the mid-write scan test); second hard link served; recursive discard; removes during a scan ignored; puts during a scan dropped; sizes cleared before the scan; `O_NONBLOCK` alone dropped (the FIFO-swap test hangs); plus the round-1 set re-run where the code changed.

## 2026-11-04 — eng-store: Blob package 5, the host attachment store (`apps/desktop/src/host/files.ts`)

`AttachmentFiles` (`packages/engine/src/store/port.ts`, published in store-3) is the port behind `attachments/.noindex/` (ARCH 3.3): one raw file per received attachment part, fetched by `Attachment/get` in package 6. This PR is only the host implementation and its tests; nothing wires it into the engine yet.

- **Layout.** `<dataDir>/attachments/.noindex/<sha256(accountId)>/<sha256(remoteId)>_<sha256(remotePart)>`, folders 0700, files 0600, writes through `.noindex/.tmp/` (`O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_WRONLY|O_NOFOLLOW`, fsync, then `rename`). The path is made of hashes only: a sender-chosen name or id can never reach the disk as a path component, and `read`/`remove` accept only that exact grammar (`^[0-9a-f]{64}/[0-9a-f]{64}_[0-9a-f]{64}$`), so no stored path can ever point outside the tree or at a sibling file.
- **Replace, not refuse.** Unlike `BlobFiles.put` (which links once and answers `exists`), `write` renames over an existing file: a re-fetch after an eviction or a fetch interrupted before the state commit must be able to write the same part again, and the provider part is immutable, so the last writer wins. The rename keeps reads atomic either way.
- **No deletes at open.** `openBlobFiles` scans its folder and deletes what is positively not a blob. `openAttachmentFiles` only empties `.tmp`: the store's rows hold the paths, a file this module does not recognise may still be named by a row (an older grammar, a pending commit), so nothing here may throw it away. What goes is decided by the `file_removals` queue the host drains (separate work), plus `remove` on a grammar-checked path.
- **Read safety mirrors blobs:** lstat first (regular file, one link, at most the cap), `O_RDONLY|O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`, fstat must be the same inode, a fresh buffer per call so a caller may transfer it. Missing, swapped, linked or oversized entries are `undefined`, never an error, so `Attachment/get` can treat them as "fetch again".
- **Errors are reason codes only** (`AttachmentFilesError: io | path | tooLarge | diskFull`), mirroring `BlobFilesError`; no OS text or path escapes the module. Caps: 25 MB per file and a 1 GB free-disk floor, both frozen upper bounds (options may tighten, never loosen).
- **Usage.** `usage()` walks `.noindex/` and sums regular files under account folders, for the store-5 retention cap; nothing is cached, so a file another actor adds or removes between calls is always counted fresh.
- **Mutations each pinned red then restored:** the path grammar on `read` and on `remove` (a planted `../victim` and a flat hex-named file stay untouched); `O_NOFOLLOW` (a link where the file was); the lstat regular-file check plus `O_NONBLOCK` (a FIFO swapped in by the `peek` seam; without the flag the test hangs); the inode/dev match (a same-size file swapped in the peek window); the `nlink` check; the 25 MB cap; the free-disk floor incl. NaN failing closed and a looser option never lowering it; the folder link checks at open and per call; temp cleanup after a failed write; per-call account-folder check.

- **Two SWE-2 review passes on the PR found one root cause twice, now fixed.** Every defensive check was path-based with only the leaf and `.noindex` guarded per call: `read`/`remove` never verified the account subdir (an intermediate symlink or a `mv`-swapped folder would let a leaf-shaped file outside the tree be served or unlinked — both now `checkFolder(sub)` per call), `.tmp` was verified once at open then used by path (now `checkFolder(tmp)` before every write, and `emptyTemp` only deletes `.part` names so a swapped `.tmp` cannot hand out deletes for arbitrary victim files), and temp names collided across store instances in one process (now include a `randomBytes(4)` token — a failed write can never delete a live writer's temp). A residual path-based TOCTOU window is accepted and named: Node has no `openat`/`unlinkat`, so every folder check is lstat-then-use and a fast enough same-UID swap can still pass it (the merged `host/blobs.ts` accepts the same shape); the leaf checks bound what it can do. New pins: over-cap file planted on disk is not served, an account folder swapped for a link serves nothing and deletes nothing, two stores write without collision, post-rename failure still reports the write done, `.tmp` keeps non-`.part` entries.
## 2026-11-04 — eng-host: host blob store, the four LOWs from the Integrator's #258 review

- **(1) Traversal test gap.** The traversal test now plants a VALID blob file (magic, header, bytes) at `<root>/victim.blob`, one level above the folder, so `get('../victim')` would really serve it without the id check; the test asserts it is refused and the file is untouched.
- **(2) Detached buffer mid-write.** `writeAll` reads the length once, and `write` checks `fstat().size` equals the expected total (taken before any await) before the `link`, throwing `io` and removing the temp otherwise; a short file is never linked and `put` never says ok for it. Measured: the size check alone already refuses it (removing it is red even with the saved length); the saved length is kept as the first line.
- **(3) `.tmp` swapped after start.** `put` re-checks `.tmp` as it does the folder. `checkFolder` now also refuses a folder opened to group or others (`mode & 0o077`). At start `privateFolder` already opens `.tmp` with `O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW` right before the cleanup, so no extra check there (an extra one was an equivalent mutant and is not kept).
- **(4) Downgrades keep newer blobs.** A file with another `aplos-blob/` magic version, or a v1 header whose `kind` this version does not know, is `skip`: kept, counted in `usage()`, never served or deleted. A v1 file that is simply corrupt is still deleted.
- **Tests (blobs.test.ts, 28):** the valid victim; a buffer detached at the write step (put `io`, nothing linked, `.tmp` empty, usage 0); `.tmp` swapped for a link after start (put refuses, nothing written into the target, a restart refuses); another version and an unknown kind kept and counted, a corrupt v1 deleted; a folder or `.tmp` opened to group/others refused. **Mutations, each red then restored:** id check dropped (now red: it survived before); length re-read with no size check; size check dropped alone; no `.tmp` re-check in put; foreign version deleted; unknown kind deleted; folder mode not checked.
## 2026-11-04 — eng-contract: Blob package 3, the engine blob runtime and `Blob/put|get|release`

- **Where.** `packages/engine/src/runtime/blobs.ts` (index, caps, principals, held check, sweep source, lifecycle) is composed as a module (`blobModule`, lane eng-contract) right after `clock` and before `outbox` in `src/index.ts`, so the outbox can take `services.blobs` when it is created (package 4). `methods/blob.ts` replaces the three `notImplemented` stand-ins. Without `HostPorts.blobFiles` the module registers no sweep and every `Blob/*` answers "This part of Aplos is not running yet."; a `list()` failure at start logs `rpc.failed {method: 'Blob/start'}` and leaves `Blob/*` unavailable while the rest of the engine runs.
- **Ids and principals.** `b_` + 32 hex from 16 bytes of `services.randomBytes`. Principal from `principalOf(caller)` (windows and main `app`, a listener `client:<id>`). A blob of another principal, an unknown id and an id that is not a blob id answer the same `notFound` "This file is no longer available."; only minted or listed ids that match `BLOB_ID_PATTERN` ever enter the index, so nothing else reaches the host.
- **Caps.** 25 MiB per put ("Files can be at most 25 MB.", the fixture's sentence); per principal `app` 512 MiB / 500 blobs and `client:<id>` 100 MiB / 50, counting puts still in flight (reserved synchronously); the host's `tooLarge`/`folderFull`/`diskFull` read the same as a quota: `tooLarge` "Aplos is holding too many files. Send or remove some attachments first."
- **Reads in flight (coordinator ask, after the Integrator's 08:00 NOTICE).** On the real path a `Blob/get` reply is cloned once across the utility boundary (Electron's `MessagePortMain` cannot move a buffer; only a DOM or Node `MessagePort` end moves it), so one read can hold up to 25 MB in the engine, 25 MB in flight and 25 MB in the receiver. Chosen: at most 2 `Blob/get` per principal at once and 64 MiB of blobs being read in all (a single read always fits, so a 25 MB file is never refused for size alone); the rest answer `unavailable` "Aplos is busy reading other files. Try again in a moment." with `retryAfterMs: 1000`. A slot is held from the check until the handler returns (the reply is serialized right after), and is given back on every path.
- **Mime and name.** Mime: the part before `;`, trimmed, lower case, must be a `type/subtype` token, else `application/octet-stream`. Name: C0/DEL/C1 controls, `/`, `\` and the bidi controls (U+061C, 200E/F, 202A-202E, 2066-2069) removed, trimmed, at most 255 code points (never splitting a surrogate pair), `.`/`..`/empty dropped.
- **Release (decision 7).** Own blob else `notFound`; named by a draft whose submission is in any state but `sent`/`cancelled` (the outbox's live set, read as `NOT IN ('sent','cancelled')` so the runtime imports nothing from the outbox) is `stateMismatch` "This file is attached to a message waiting to send."; otherwise the index entry goes in the same turn and the file after.
- **Held and lifecycle (decision 8).** Held = any draft row's `attachments[].blobId` (one read over `drafts` joined with `submissions`). A closed connection drops the unheld blobs it made (a held one just forgets its creator); a put that finishes after its connection closed is undone. The sweep (`blobs` deadline source, every 10 minutes) drops unheld uploads 24 h after creation and unheld `original` handles 30 min after their last read. At start the index is rebuilt from `list()`: unheld `original` files go at once, unheld uploads older than 24 h go, held ones and fresh uploads stay. A blob the host cannot read right now answers `notFound` but stays in the index (the host may read it again; the lifecycle ends it).
- **TOCTOU (decision 9).** The ownership check, the held/live check and the index change run in one turn; the file delete follows. A read re-checks ownership after its file read, so a release that lands while a read is in progress answers `notFound`, never stale bytes.
- **Interface for package 4 (the old Engine thread, `Draft/set` and the outbox):** `services.blobs.owns(caller: Caller, blobId: string): boolean` is the ownership check: synchronous, true only when the id matches `BLOB_ID_PATTERN`, is in the index, and `principalOf(caller)` made it (false while `Blob/*` is unavailable). Call it inside the same turn as the `Draft/set` commit for every new `attachments[].blobId`, and answer `notFound` "Attach it again" when it is false. The outbox reads bytes with `services.blobs.load(blobIds: readonly string[]): Promise<Map<string, Uint8Array>>`, which ignores principal, leaves missing blobs out of the map and returns a fresh buffer per blob (it is not counted against the read cap; a draft's own 25 MB rule bounds it).
- **Known limit (Integrator answer 2):** 25 MB per blob in v1; larger files need an additive `offset/length` on `Blob/get`.
- **Not done here:** the headless real-path test (decision test plan) needs package 2's host store on the same branch; it is package 10's.
- **Tests** (`packages/engine/tests/methods/blob.test.ts`, 17, in-memory `BlobFiles` with the host's contract, the real engine and store, the fake clock): round trip with cleaning; a moved reply buffer never takes the next read's bytes (through a session and at the runtime); principals (second window, two listeners, the same `notFound` as unknown); bad ids including ones a host listed at start never reach the host; caps (25 MB exact/over, 51 concurrent puts against 50, four 25 MB files then one byte, host refusal); release for every live state and for drafts with no live send; close keeps held, drops unheld; a put finished after close is undone; the sweep for uploads, handles and held blobs; the start rules across a restart; TOCTOU both orders with a hanging host delete and a read in progress; `load`; no port; the read cap (per principal, per bytes, slots given back); a transiently unreadable blob; the cleaning table; the sweep source period.
- **Mutations, each red then restored (18):** principal compare dropped; live-send check dropped in release; held check dropped in the sweep; release on disconnect applied to held blobs; held check dropped at start; engine id check dropped at start (alone, and together with the one in `own()`, which alone is an equivalent mutant behind the index); 25 MB cap dropped; a read cache reusing the buffer; file deleted before the index entry; a read not re-checking after its await; puts in flight not reserved; no read cap; read slot never given back; a put finishing after close kept; host refusals not mapped to the quota sentence; the creating connection not recorded; bidi controls kept in names. ("No read cap" is red by a test timeout: the gated reads never settle.)

## 2026-11-04 — eng-contract: Blob package 3, items from the independent Opus review (MERGE)

- **Read budget, corrected wording and an owner allowance (LOW1 + INFO).** `BLOB_READS` bounds concurrent reads in the engine, from the check until the handler returns its bytes; the clone in flight across the utility boundary and the receiver's copy are NOT counted (the slot is given back before the reply is serialized; the engine has no hook after the post). This supersedes the earlier "25 MB in flight + 25 MB in the receiver" wording above. Now: two reads per principal; listener clients together hold at most 32 MB of blobs being read (one read always fits); the owner's windows (`app`) are not counted against that budget, so a client looping reads on its own 25 MB blobs can never make a window's read busy. Worst case about 2 x 25 MB for `app` plus 32 MB for clients.
- **Clock moved back (LOW2).** At start a blob's `createdAt` (and so `lastReadAt`) ahead of the engine clock is clamped to now, so an upload stamped in the future still ends 24 h after the start instead of living until that date.
- **Release after the fact (LOW3).** `Blob/release` takes the index entry out first; a failed file delete after that is logged (`rpc.failed {method: 'Blob/remove', reason: 'release'}`) and the call still answers `{ok: true}` (a retry would only get `notFound`); the next start's rules clean the file.
- **Names (NIT).** `cleanName` now drops every Unicode `Cc`, `Cf`, `Zl` and `Zp` character (C0/C1 controls, bidi controls, zero-width characters, U+FEFF, U+2028/2029) and `/`, `\`, so nothing reaches a MIME `filename=` in package 4 that could break the header or disguise an extension.
- **For package 4 (recorded, harmless):** the outbox's `refreshRemote` deletes a draft row on a remote removal before its `sendPins` check, so a live submission can be left without its draft row; its blobs are then no longer held and become releasable and sweepable. Harmless because the submission's MIME is already pinned in `submissions.mime`; package 4 may want to keep the row (or count the submission's own attachment ids as held) if it ever re-reads bytes for a pinned send.
- **Tests (blob.test.ts, now 19):** a client looping two 25 MB reads never makes the window's read busy (three rounds), and a second client's 25 MB read is busy while the first holds the budget; a future `createdAt` ends 24 h after start; a release whose file delete fails answers ok and logs; the Zl/Zp/Cf cleaning cases. **Mutations, each red then restored:** `app` sharing the client budget; no client budget; `createdAt` not clamped; release reporting a failed delete; Zl/Zp/Cf kept in names ("no client budget" is red by a test timeout).

- **`Draft/set` ownership (`methods/draft.ts`, `outbox/draft-owner.ts`).** Every NEW `attachments[].blobId` (one the stored draft does not already name) must pass `services.blobs.owns(caller, blobId)`, else `notFound` 'This file is no longer available. Attach it again.' The check is synchronous and shares its turn with the commit (nothing awaits between them), so a release cannot slip in. A listener cannot attach a window's file by id nor another client's; every window and main are one app principal and may attach each other's files (the Integrator's accepted answer 4). An id the draft already names is not asked again (a draft keeps saving after its file lost its owner; a held file is never released while a draft names it), so only a new or swapped id is checked. With no host blob port every blob id is refused ('Blob/* unavailable'); a draft with no attachments is unaffected.

The 10 s limit, the re-checks and the draft-sync fallback from the async-read PR apply to it unchanged.
- **Known, harmless (package 3's note):** the outbox's `refreshRemote` deletes a draft row on a remote removal before the `sendPins` check, so an orphan live submission's blobs become releasable; its MIME is pinned, so nothing breaks.
- Tests (in `tests/methods/blob.test.ts`, the real engine over the in-memory `BlobFiles`): a new attachment must be the caller's (windows share the app principal), listeners are isolated from each other and from windows, only new ids are checked and a swapped id is refused without saving, the port-absent case, a send pins the bytes read through `services.blobs` and holds the file (release is `stateMismatch`), a send whose file is gone is refused and queues nothing. Mutations red: no ownership check, checking every id instead of the new ones, the check not called from `Draft/set`, the outbox without a blob source.

## 2026-11-04 — eng-time: Blob package 4, ownership for EVERY new blob id, and the stable-client-id requirement on the listener transport

- **Why every new id is checked (Integrator, 10:00 NOTICE on #259).** Blob ids are not secret: a draft row carries `attachments[].blobId` and a listener with `read` scope may read drafts, and the outbox's `services.blobs.load` ignores principals. So `Draft/set` asks `services.blobs.owns(caller, id)` for every id that is new to the draft, and a `client:<id>` caller attaching an `app` blob (a window's file) gets `notFound` "This file is no longer available. Attach it again." The test is in `tests/methods/blob.test.ts` ("a listener cannot attach a window's file by id, nor another client's; its own file works"); mutations red: no ownership check, checking only the first id, the check not called from `Draft/set`.
- **Requirement on the listener transport (record, no code).** The per-client blob quota (100 MB / 50 blobs per `client:<id>`, decision 4 of the blob design) assumes STABLE, owner-granted client ids. A transport that lets a listener mint a fresh `clientId` per session would let it fill the host's 2 GB blob folder, one quota at a time. Whoever wires the socket / HTTP / MCP listener must derive `clientId` from the owner's grant (not from anything the client sends per connection) and must reject a connection without one (`owns` already throws `forbidden` for a listener without a `clientId`). Also: `Blob/*` refuses a `client:<id>` principal for files another principal made, so a wrongly shared id can only hurt its own quota.

- **SWE-2 review pass 2 of #254 (ownership), applied.** A corrupt row (`data` unreadable, so `attachments` reads as absent) no longer throws a bare TypeError in the ownership check (`?? []`: every id is then checked). Three tests were missing and are added to `tests/methods/blob.test.ts`: every new id is checked in any position (a mixed request `[own, foreign]`, `[foreign, own]` and a three-id request are refused whole; two own files save), the already-attached exemption is per draft (a file another draft names is still foreign to a different principal, in an edit and in a new draft), and a listener client's send with its OWN file reads the bytes through the outbox (`via: 'http'`, scopes read/draft/send). Correction: the earlier line listing 'checking only the first id' as a red mutant was wrong before these tests (every refused input had one id); with them it is red (also red: ignoring the stored row, and an outbox source that reads as the app principal).

- **SWE-2 review pass 1 of #254 (send path), applied.** `readFailed` now re-checks `stillCurrent` for a draft with no attachment bytes too (`bytesFor` still yields once, and a commit may land in that gap). `loadBlobs` got two tests: a read that rejects after the limit raises no unhandled rejection, and a load that succeeds disarms its limit timer (mutation-checked: dropping `abort.abort()` turns the second red). The `work.catch` / `limit.catch` lines in `withinLimit` were dead code (`Promise.race` already handles both promises) and are gone. Noted, not changed: the `source`-type attachments carry no blob ids (nothing to read), and `close()`/`idle()` do not track a send waiting on a hung read (the 10 s limit bounds it).

## 2026-11-04 — eng-time: #254 follow-ups (Integrator's 09:22 REQUEST and 09:55 requirements)

- **`readFailed` re-reads the row before writing retry metadata.** A discard while the bytes were read hides the row WITHOUT changing its revision (`applyDraftDelete` writes `_sync.removing` into `data`, no `revision` bump), so the retry write — guarded by `ifRevision` alone — could land `{dueAt, readFails}` or a problem onto a hidden row, leaving the uploads index pinned on a past deadline until the provider confirms the copy. `readFailed` now re-reads the row and returns when it is gone or `meta.removing` (a live send still decides via `stillCurrent`). Backstop: `syncDraft` clears `dueAt` when it finds `meta.removing`, so a hidden row can never stall the drafts timer. Test: a draft with a remote copy is discarded while its failing upload read runs — the hidden row keeps `removing:true` with no `dueAt` and no `problem`, and is removed once the held provider delete confirms.
- **The per-client blob quota assumes stable owner-granted client ids.** `client:<id>` quotas are enforced per `clientId`; a listener transport that minted a fresh clientId per session would let one listener fill the host's 2 GB folder across quota resets. This is a requirement on the listener transport when it is wired: the client id must be granted by the owner and stable across reconnects. (Integrator's second item from the #259 review; `Draft/set` already checks `owns()` for every NEW `blobId` in `draft-owner.ts` — a `client:<id>` attaching an `app` blob gets `notFound` "Attach it again", pinned in blob.test.ts.)

- **SWE-2 pass 1 note (all LOW, no functional bug).** The `readFailed` re-read is the third of three layers, not the only one: `stillCurrent` already required the re-read row's `data` to be byte-identical (the discard always rewrites it), and the in-tx `meta` writer already refuses `dueAt`/`readFails` on `removing` rows — so the integrator's scenario was already prevented twice over; the guard keeps the invariant literal at the call site and covers the `problemFor` path's ordering too. The `syncDraft` backstop exists for rows damaged before those guards landed (unreachable through any current writer). A no-remote-copy draft discarded mid-failing-read is pinned too (`!current` → `deleteHidden` path, row stays gone). Known-benign, pre-existing: a permanent `refused` after a discard can still stamp `problem` on a hidden row (invisible; `abandoned` keeps the Message-ID so a landed copy is adopted and deleted) — if the invariant is ever meant literally, the `meta` writer would also refuse `problem` on removing rows; not done here.

- **Two SWE-2 review passes on the PR found the same MEDIUM, now fixed.** The `ResizeObserver` was wired to `recheck` (unconditional `until` extension): a sub-pixel oscillation (`body{animation:w .5s infinite alternate}` between `width:50%` and `60%`, or any border-box wiggle under the `ceil()` band) fires it every frame and keeps the watch measuring at ~60 Hz for as long as the letter is read, at zero post cost — the 2,000-report cap never engages. The observer now asks for a `watch` look like the animation events: a real size change still re-arms through the posted-shape path, a look that finds nothing lets the loop sleep (unit test pins both). Two LOWs with it: after `live = false` events still armed a no-op rAF+timer pair each (now gated by `live` in `watch`, pinned), and the `now()` `0` fallback could never satisfy `now() < until`… it *always* did (`0 < 3000`), so a missing clock meant a watch that never sleeps — the fallback is `-Infinity`, a look never re-arms (pinned). e2e 175 now polls for the cap and accepts the keyframe endpoint (wall-clock flake vectors).

- **The Integrator's Opus pass on the PR found the wiggle was only halved, not fixed.** `new w.ResizeObserver(watch)` still armed one look per frame during a sub-pixel storm (6,000 frames -> 6,000 looks; the 2,000 cap counts posts, not looks). Now: once a look finds nothing, observer and animation events ask for the timer alone (~4 looks/s, and a `LOOKS` lifetime budget of 100,000 stops even that for good); `live` still gates them. LOW 1: the `-Infinity` clock claim was wrong -- with no clock `time - since` never resets, so after 20 posts `dirty` re-armed a look every frame forever with the last height held stale; the rate limiter now steps aside when `now()` is not finite (every look can post, still bounded by LOOKS/LIFETIME). LOW 2: a window or sidebar drag could spend the 2,000-post budget on honest width reports (~20/s for 100 s) and leave the letter clipped forever; a host-set width now restarts the post budget (the letter cannot game its width). Informational noted: stale rAF callbacks queue off-screen (bounded no-ops), and ~15 timers can sit pending during a watch (one LOOK at a time is the real invariant). Unit pins: calm events arm timer-only, a 40-look variant dies at the cap, width resets keep it alive (25 posts across 6 widths at LIFETIME=5), a clockless page posts every change yet never re-arms; the perf control's `from` string tracks the renamed `look` listener. Corrects the earlier note's "a look that finds nothing lets the loop sleep" claim.
## 2026-11-04 — eng-contract: a hidden draft row does not hold its blobs (Integrator 10:00 REQUEST)

`references()` (`runtime/blobs.ts`) counted EVERY draft row's `attachments[].blobId` as holding the blob, including rows already hidden by `_sync.removing` (a discarded or sent draft kept until the provider confirms its copy is gone — `draft-sync.ts` "however many times it takes"). While a provider refuses the delete, those rows would pin their bytes against the app's 512 MB / 500-blob quota for ever. A row now holds only when `removing` is absent OR a submission is still live (`state NOT IN ('sent','cancelled')`): the bytes of a dead send may be swept while the row itself waits on the provider; FR-7 still holds for a live one. Test: a removing row's blob is swept after 24 h, a removing row with a `sending` submission keeps it (mutation-checked: dropping the `removing` filter turns it red).

- **Memory note (the Integrator's second item).** A 25 MB `Blob/put` or `Blob/get` briefly holds about 2 x 25 MB inside the engine process: `connection.ts` `structuredClone`s every result and `copyArgs` every argument, beyond what the read byte budget counts; on the real path a further clone crosses the utility boundary (`MessagePortMain` cannot transfer). Size in-flight budgets accordingly.

- **Two SWE-2 review passes on the PR found the same MEDIUM, fixed by moving `removing` out of the SQL.** `json_extract(d.data,'$._sync.removing')` would throw "malformed JSON" on one unreadable `drafts.data` row — a shape `parseData` deliberately tolerates ("an unreadable row reads as empty") — and abort `references()` entirely: `start` fails → every `Blob/*` answers `unavailable` for the session, every sweep throws (swallowed by the timer loop, so nothing is ever swept), `release` leaks a raw `SqliteError` to the caller. `attachmentsOf()` now reads `_sync.removing` from the same `JSON.parse` it already does for the ids: a malformed row contributes no ids AND no removal flag, and a falsy-but-present `removing` (`false`, `0`, `""` from a foreign writer) keeps the row holding — JS truthiness matching `parseData`, the conservative direction. The EXISTS subquery now uses the shared `LIVE_SQL` constant instead of a hardcoded `NOT IN ('sent','cancelled')`, so a future third terminal state cannot silently count as live. Tests: hidden row with a `cancelled` submission is swept (the discard-with-queued-send case), `release` on a hidden+live blob still rejects (FR-7), and a malformed `data` row does not wedge `release`/`refs()`. NOTE: `draft-due.ts`'s `scanDue` still runs unguarded `json_extract` over all drafts (eng-time's, pre-existing): a malformed row makes each pass throw inside the source — harmless to blobs now, but the same blast-radius shape; flagged for the #254 follow-up since it sits in that lane.

## 2026-11-04 — eng-html: Blob package 6 (`Attachment/get`)

- **`Attachment/get` hands out `attachment` blob handles, never a second copy.** A `present` row whose cached file reads answers at once; otherwise the method commits `fetching`, streams `services.accounts.attachment` under a 25 MB cap and a 120 s total / 30 s idle deadline tied to `ctx.signal`, writes into `attachments/.noindex/` (`AttachmentFiles`, new `HostPorts.attachmentFiles` + `EngineServices` field; absent → `unavailable`), and commits `present` with the path. Any failure reverts the row to `absent`, so nothing sticks `fetching`. One fetch per part is shared (`mailFrames.parts`, keyed account+message+part — each caller registers its OWN handle) and at most three run per account (`busy`, retryable). Over 25 MB is `tooLarge` "This attachment is too large to open in Aplos."
- **`blobs.attach` registers the handle in memory only** (`runtime/blobs.ts`): `kind:'attachment'` entries carry the cache `path` and `Blob/get`/`load` read them through `AttachmentFiles` (a forwarded received attachment keeps its bytes); `drop`/`release` never call `BlobFiles.remove` (the cache's lifecycle ends the file); they live like `original` handles — 30 min after the last read, dropped on close when unheld, never persisted. The blob stays held-able: a draft naming its id pins it.
- **Name and mime come from the stored row's declared values**, run through the same `cleanMime`/`cleanName` the blob surface uses; the mime is never used to decide anything.
- Verified: `attachment.test.ts` 6/6 (fetch+cache+handle, cache hit, dedupe one fetch two handles, 25 MB tooLarge + absent revert, stalled-read cancel + absent revert, unavailable without the port); blob.test + mail suites 92/92; engine typecheck clean.

## 2026-11-04 — eng-html: #267 review fixes (two SWE-2 passes)

- **`stateCommit` opIds carry a per-attempt nonce + accountId.** The `commits` table dedupes by opId forever, so the first scheme (`remoteId-part-state-path`) replayed every second-ever `fetching`/`present`/`absent` as a no-op — a `present` commit on the same path after an `absent` revert would silently skip and wedge the row. Each attempt now mints a unique opId; these are distinct events, not retries.
- **The shared fetch answers to its own signal.** A starter's session close aborts only its own wait (`within(work, deadline(ctx.signal))`), never the fetch — a joined caller's bytes still land and the row still commits `present`. Each caller cancels via its own deadline.
- **One 120 s budget, not two.** `total` is created once in `fetchPart` and shared by open, `collect`, and the cache write (the write is now bounded too).
- **`attachment` handles don't count toward `usage()`** — they own no `BlobFiles` bytes; `Blob/get` on them is bounded by the file cap. A client opening attachments can no longer wedge its own `Blob/put`.
- **Dedup key is `JSON.stringify([accountId, remoteId, remotePart])`** — a `:` inside an opaque remote id or part can no longer collide two different parts onto one fetch.
- **A row deleted mid-fetch un-orphans:** `stateCommit('present')` failing with `StoreError` removes the just-written file and answers `notFound`, not `internal`.
- **`idle` ends in `finally`** — a rejecting `iterator.next()` no longer leaks its timer/listener.
- **Deferred, stated honestly:** `attachment` handles are memory-only — an engine restart drops them even when a draft names the id; a send then refuses with the attach-again sentence (the user re-opens the attachment to re-pin). A held handle does not pin the cache file — host retention can evict the bytes under a live handle, after which `Blob/get` answers `notFound`. Both follow from "never a second copy"; pinning/rebuild needs a port API that does not exist yet. The cache-hit `files.read` is the port's only existence probe (no stat API), so it buffers up to the 25 MB cap once — bounded and load-bearing for stale-path repair.
## 2026-11-04 — eng-store: files.ts three LOWs (Integrator's 11:35 REQUEST)

- **`remove` no longer rmdirs the account folder.** An `rmdir` racing a concurrent write could land between that write's folder checks and its `rename`, failing the rename ENOENT and losing the write. An emptied hash folder costs nothing to keep; `privateFolder(sub)` re-validates it on the next write. Chosen over rename-retry because it removes the race rather than recovering from it.
- **`.tmp` is re-checked per write.** The new test swaps `.noindex/.tmp` for an outside symlink after `open` and proves `write` refuses `io` with the outside folder left empty (the per-write `checkFolder(tmp)` already made this true; the test pins it). A residual swap-between-check-and-open window remains — it needs a same-uid attacker writing inside a 0700 tree, and worst case is a cleaned-up temp.
- **Ids must be well-formed Unicode.** `write` rejects `accountId`/`remoteId`/`remotePart` failing `isWellFormed()` as `path` — a lone surrogate is not a real id and hashing it names a stable-but-wrong file.

- **`isWellFormed` needed `ES2024.String` in both engine and desktop `lib` arrays** (types-only; Node 24 and Electron's V8 already implement it). Without it `npm run typecheck` — the `npm run check` gate — was red in both workspaces.

## 2026-11-04 — eng-html: Blob package 7 (`Message/original`)

- **`Message/original` fetches the raw RFC822 live** through the optional `ProviderPort.original` (Gmail `format=raw`; absent → `unavailable`), capped at 25 MB under a 120 s total / 30 s idle deadline on the caller's signal, then stores it through `services.blobs.put` as an `original`-kind blob — a real `BlobFiles` file (`message/rfc822`, name = cleaned subject + `.eml`), quota-counted like any put, dropped at start when unheld. Every call mints its own blob under the caller's principal; no dedup (there is no shared cache row for originals). Verified: `original.test.ts` 5/5.
- **`main/windows/onboarding.ts` dropped from the ui-onboard PR (2026-11-04, eng-host asked, supersedes the entries above that name it).** It was a parallel copy of the revamp controller (`host/lifecycle.ts` plus `createWelcomeWindow` in `main/windows.ts`) and left out the engine-port `webPreferences()` arguments. The onboarding window and its 120 ms handoff fade stay eng-host's (#268); this lane keeps only `renderer/onboarding/**` and its tests. Once ui-shell routes `window=onboarding` in `main.tsx`, eng-host removes the `APLOS_ONBOARDING_WINDOW` gate in `windows.ts`.
- **Correction (WP3, engine-3):** `Settings.mail.remoteImages` IS now read by the engine (`resolveImagePolicy`'s `global` param): 'allow' loads remote images for every sender, 'block' leaves the allow-list to decide. An earlier note saying the setting was unread is superseded; a reader-side `load` request still resolves to `allow` either way.

- **Empty-html proof now built (WP4, engine-3).** `asWritten` canonicalises a body with no visible text to `''` before the fingerprint hash — `<p></p>`, `<div><br></div>`, `&nbsp;`-only and `''` all hash the same — using the same `htmlToText(...).trim()` rule `isEmpty` uses. A Gmail copy of our empty draft now proves ours instead of showing as another device's (or opening a conflict about our own text). A body that only looks empty but carries content still differs.

- **Correction to the empty-html entry above (first-pass review, engine-3).** "No visible text" is too broad a test — `htmlToText` drops non-textual elements, so `<img>`, `<hr>`, `<video>`, `<svg>` and `<table>` bodies would have hashed as `''` and the proof could swallow an edit as our own echo. Shipped instead: `emptyMarkup` canonicalises only *trivially-empty container markup* — no text AND every element an empty-body container (p/div/br/span/formatting); `<img>`, `<hr>`, `<table>`, `<a>` keep their own hash. Caveat kept: fingerprints stored by the previous build for empty-markup bodies hash the old way, so an echo of such an upload under a new remote id reads foreign once (a conflict, the safe direction) and self-heals on the next upload.

## 2026-11-04 — engine: `Event/set` with an occurrence id and scope `series` (ui-calendar's question)

- **The contract today (`EventChange`):** `update { id, scope, fields: Partial<EventFields> }` carries whole values, never deltas, and `Event/set` is still `notImplemented` in the engine; the recurrence helpers (`editOccurrence`, `editSeries`, the split) are absolute on the object they are given. This entry fixes what the handler will do, so the Calendar can build against it.
- **Decision: `fields` describe the occurrence the owner is looking at (the one `id` names); for `series` the engine maps them to the master, and the time fields move as a shift, not as a copy.**
  - `title`, `location`, `description`, `attendees`, `allDay`, `timeZone`: copied to the master as given (they are not per-occurrence facts).
  - `start` and `end`: the engine reads the occurrence's current wall-clock start and end in the series' zone, takes `delta = new − current` for each, and adds them to the master's start and end (so the occurrence's pattern shifts: a Tuesday 10:00 occurrence dragged to Wednesday 11:00 moves a weekly Tuesday 10:00 series to Wednesday 11:00; a longer end makes every occurrence longer). Wall-clock in the series' zone, so a DST change does not skew the series. Copying the occurrence's date literally onto the master would move the series start to that occurrence and drop or reshuffle everything before it, which no owner means.
  - A move between timed and all-day is a master change as a whole (`allDay` flips; `editSeries` already drops overrides then).
  - `occurrence` scope keeps the absolute reading: the override holds exactly the fields given. `following` stays refused with a plain reason until built.
  - An id that is a master or a single event (no `@`) takes the fields as absolute: there is no occurrence to read a delta from.
- **For the UI:** send what the user sees (the occurrence's new title or times), with `scope: 'series'` and the occurrence id; do not compute the master's new start yourself. Until the handler lands, the Calendar's stand-in should apply the same rule so the harness and the engine agree.
- **Owner of the code:** `methods/event.ts` and the calendar module are not built; this is the contract for whoever builds them (eng-contract's registry row for `Event/set` gets the sentence, no schema change).
- **Empty-markup attributes (WP4 follow-up, engine-3).** Attributes on allowed empty containers carry no weight: `<p style="color:red"></p>` and `<span title="x"></span>` with no text hash as `''` like `<p></p>` — the attribute is styling on nothing. Pinned also: entity text (`&amp;`, `&lt;`) and a raw zero-width space are content, never `''`. And `isEmpty` now uses the same `emptyMarkup` rule as `asWritten`, so an image-only draft (no recipients, subject or attachments) is content and does upload instead of being silently suppressed.

## 2026-11-04 — eng-host: desktop methods answer failures as a tagged envelope; the v1 desktop table is wired before the go-live flip

- **A failed desktop method keeps its reason.** `ipcRenderer.invoke('aplos:desktop')` flattens a handler's rejection to one wrapper sentence, so every failure read as the generic "unavailable" line. Main now ANSWERS a failure with `{ 'aplos:desktop-failure': ErrorBody }` (`shared/desktop-error.ts`: a ContractError keeps type, message and reason code; any other Error keeps its sentence as `unavailable`, cut to 300 characters; a stack or cause never travels); preload (`exposeEngineBridge`) rejects with the plain error body, the shape every other failed call has. A caller that is not the app's page (`assertCaller`) is still a bare rejection and is told no reason.
- **Served on the v1 path now (`engine-real.ts` `legacyNames` + `v1Result`):** App/info, Window/thread|closeReady|closeCancel|claim|release|openOnboarding|finishOnboarding, Shell/open, Notification/test, Update/status|check|later|restart, System/openSettings|login|notifications|defaultMail|microphone. `System/login` now reads when `enabled` is absent (the contract's optional input); `System/notifications` answers `unknown` (Electron's main process cannot read the macOS permission); `Window/claim` answers `{ claimed }`.
- **New handlers:** `Engine/restart` (the supervisor's new `restart()`: a running engine is asked to quit and the next starts at once, without counting toward the park; a parked or waiting app starts one now; a closed supervisor refuses), `Diagnostics/copy` (the content-free HOST bundle to the clipboard, `source: 'host'`: the engine's own bundle is not on main's control connection yet; test mode never writes the clipboard), `File/save` and `File/open` (bytes read through main's own `Blob/get`, so 25 MB never sits in a renderer; the legacy save/open helpers are shared).
- **Still not served on the v1 path (they answer the plain "not available on the new engine yet" sentence):** `Symbols/render` (the native SF Symbols renderer is not built) and `Ai/pasteKey` (needs `Ai/setKey` on main's control connection, which is not in `controlMethods`). Both are needed only by screens that have a fallback today.

## 2026-11-04 — eng-host: #306 fix round (the Integrator's Opus pass)

- **Quarantine, by the xattr, not the plist key.** `File/save` and `File/open` mark the file `com.apple.quarantine` themselves (`shared/quarantine.ts`: `/usr/bin/xattr -w com.apple.quarantine "0083;<hex seconds>;Aplos Mail;" <file>` through `execFile` with an argument array, never a shell string, so a hostile name stays one argument). An open FAILS CLOSED (a file that cannot be marked is not handed to an app); a save still saves on a volume with no extended attributes (the file is where the owner put it). `LSFileQuarantineEnabled` in `extendInfo` was not used: it quarantines EVERY file the app creates (the mailbox database, logs, exports), which is wrong for the engine's own data. Tests assert the exact call and the order write, quarantine, open.
- **File/open sniffs again (blob-area decision 12).** `shared/file-sniff.ts`: each safe type has a first-bytes check (%PDF, the image magic numbers, RIFF/WEBP, ISO-BMFF for mp4/mov/heic, `PK\x03\x04` for OOXML, ID3 or an MPEG frame for mp3, RIFF/WAVE, `{\rtf`, and no NUL in the first 4 KB for plain text types); a mismatch is refused with the same plain sentence, before anything is written. The legacy OLE types `.doc`, `.xls` and `.ppt` are DROPPED from the safe list (they can carry macros and cannot be told from a macro document by the header alone): the owner saves them first. The extension list and the sniffers are one table, so they cannot drift apart.
- **fileNameOf** now removes Unicode format characters (Cf: bidi overrides and isolates, zero-width, BOM) and DEL, and trims leading dots (no hidden files) and trailing dots and spaces; control characters still become underscores.
- **The three surviving mutations are now tests:** `openAttachment` refuses `.app`, `.pdf.app`, no extension, a legacy type and a type/bytes mismatch before any write (`attachment-files.test.ts`); `File/save` has no path parameter and the contract strips one (`saveAttachment.length`, `parseArgs`); the trust check is the first argument of `guardedDesktopCall`, tested for "the handler never runs for an untrusted caller", and `desktopMethod` is pinned to be exactly that one return.
- **Tickets taken in the same push:** the opened-attachment temp folders are swept at start-up (`aplos-attachment-*` only); an asked restart clears the park count at most 3 times per 10 minutes (the engine still restarts every time, and the stops keep counting); a restart asked while the FIRST engine is still starting does nothing and is not misread as a crash.
- **Not taken:** the error `type`/`reasonCode` a trusted caller sees is still open-ended for plain-`Error` sentences and engine reason codes (no paths or internals reach it: system errors and subclasses are generic); closing the set is a contract question for the renderer's copy table. `Diagnostics/copy` may overwrite the clipboard (accepted: it is the owner's own click).

- **SWE-2 review of the fix round, applied.** HIGH: `createProcessState.noteEngineExit` answered `false` for an exit while PARKED, which made a crash after a capped (not-clearing) asked restart look un-parked and loop forever; it now answers `true` (an exit while parked is still parked), with a test of the exact sequence. MEDIUM: the temp sweep also runs on the default (legacy) engine path (it lives in `bootEngine`) and never in test mode (test instances share the per-user temp folder); UTF-16 text (a byte order mark, NULs in one lane) now passes the text sniff, as do a PDF with a header up to 1 KB in and the QuickTime `pnot`/`PICT` atoms. LOW: a file that cannot be quarantined has its temp folder removed; a second ask while a quit is pending no longer spends an un-park slot; `fileNameOf` also drops C1 controls and U+2028/2029. Accepted as noted: a save onto a volume with no extended attributes is not quarantined; a file still open in an app after a restart can be swept (it is the app's copy of a temp file the owner can save again); the blob is read in full before the 25 MB check (a metadata call would avoid it, a contract change).

## 2026-11-05 — eng-contract: Session/get, Session/changes, Settings/get and Settings/set on the real engine

- **What was missing:** the four handlers were `notImplemented` stubs, so a window on `APLOS_ENGINE=real` landed on the fatal screen at its first call. They are real now (`methods/session.ts`, `methods/settings.ts`, `runtime/settings.ts`).
- **Session/get** reads the store (`dbEpoch`, `schemaVersion`, `{dbEpoch, seq}`, whether any account row exists), the engine clock (`now`), and the host's facts through a new `services.host` (`HostFacts`: app version, device id and name, resident, the desktop's capability keys), which the host injects at start (`host/compose.ts`, `main/engine-utility.ts` `hostFacts`; a bare build named "This Mac" when absent). A running engine reports `ready`: a parked app is main's to say, with no engine to answer. The engine adds its own capabilities and says `false` for what is not built (calendar writes, listener, clients, MCP, diagnostics, transcription, workflow sync, one-click unsubscribe); the host adds `desktop`, `notifications`, `loginItem`, `menuBar`, `microphone`. `setup` reads `meta` keys `setup.completedAt` and `setup.skippedAt`, which `Setup/end` (eng-ai) will write; until then it is `{}`. Providers: Google available, the rest say when they arrive.
- **Session/changes** is the store's `changesSince` with the contract's shape: another database, a future seq or a seq below the compaction floor is `cannotCalculateChanges`; rows are deduplicated per `(type, id)` (the last wins), filtered by `types` after the page is read (so `newState` still moves past rows the client does not want), and paged by whole commits.
- **Settings** is one JSON document in `settings` (id 1) that may be PARTIAL (the image allow-list writer touches `mail.imageAllowList` alone): a read lays each stored section over the defaults, a write is the new `settings` commit kind (`settingsExtension`, lane eng-contract, registered by `settingsModule` in `src/index.ts`) which merges the patch section by section (a field named in the patch replaces that field whole). Defaults are the contract's first-run values with Groq for every AI feature and an EMPTY signature (the stand-in's "Warmly, Alex" is demo data). `Settings/set` checks `ifInState` against the last `Settings` change (`stateMismatch`, and another database is a mismatch), answers the whole document and a receipt, writes an audit row for a listener client in the same commit, and tells main a NEW theme once on the host channel (a replay or an unchanged theme says nothing). The window-only `ai` section is refused for a listener by the access layer before the handler runs.
- **Not done here:** the stand-in's consent re-prompt when an AI provider, model or address changes (`consentKey`) is eng-ai's, with the consent tables; it is not part of this handler.

- **SWE-2 review of the Session/Settings handlers, applied.** MEDIUM: `Settings/set`'s `ifInState` check now also treats the compaction floor as a settings change (a state below the floor may have missed one), and a retry of an opId already applied skips the staleness check and gets the first receipt (the check used to run against the retry's own commit); `Settings/get` reads each section as the SHAPE of its default (`coerce` in `runtime/settings.ts`: a wrong type, an enum value outside its set, a partial nested object or a section that is not an object is the default, a stored field that is valid survives, unknown keys are dropped, free-form records such as the keymap are kept), so a downgrade, a hand edit or a damaged row cannot make the output schema-invalid; `Later/presets` read the week start from `$.calendar.weekStart`, a path the contract does not have: it is `$.ui.calendar.weekStart` (`clock/sources/later.ts`, tested through the real settings write). LOW: a change that changes nothing writes nothing and marks nothing; `deviceIdOf` is read once. Left as noted, for the owner or the contract: `engine.resident` is `false` from the utility (it cannot see main's menu-bar mode; no consumer reads it); a read-scoped listener sees the device name in `Session/get` (the schema requires `device`); an `admin` listener can change `retention.*` and `host.*` (only `patch.ai` is window-only in the access table); `Session/changes` caps change ROWS (the stand-in caps unique rows) and a `types` filter can return an empty page with `hasMore` (progress is guaranteed by `newState.seq`); `parseStored` reads a corrupt document as empty while the allow-list writer refuses to write over one; the replay receipt names the first commit while `settings` is current state.

## 2026-11-05 — eng-contract: Blob package 10, the headless real-path test

- **`apps/desktop/tests/eng-contract/blob-real-path.test.ts` proves the blob surface over the REAL path** (`plans/engine/blob-area.md`, work package 10's test plan): `runEngineUtility` + `composeEngineHost` on a temp data folder behind the real supervisor and port broker, so `Blob/put`'s 5 MB lands as a real file under `<dataDir>/blobs/` (the production `host/blobs.ts` store, fsync + rename), and `crash()` drops the ports and reports exit 137 the way a SIGKILL does — though the composed host still gets its `close()` (it must, to release `mail.db`), so a mid-write kill is not modelled here (the host tests own torn writes; everything this test asserts was committed before the crash). After the supervisor restarts and the broker re-issues the port, `Blob/get` answers the same bytes — the index was rebuilt from `list()` at start, not from memory. The queued `Submission/set` then drains on the NEW engine and the injected fake `SendPort` receives the pinned MIME with the attachment's bytes inside — the bytes the dead process wrote are what leave.
- **The rest of the plan in the same test:** a second window reads the same blob (every window is the `app` principal, FR-6); on the restarted engine the window puts a draft-held blob and an unheld one, and revoking that window drops only the unheld one — the disconnect sweep is exercised for real, since a rebuilt index entry carries no `creator` and could never prove it; a `listener` caller with `via` + `clientId` + `read` scope gets `notFound` — an app's blob is never another principal's bytes. No Electron, no network (`fetch` throws), a fake clock drives the 10 s undo window, nothing is sent anywhere real.
- **Two SWE-2 review passes, both FIX, applied.** The disconnect step was vacuous (a rebuilt index entry has no `creator`, so `connectionClosed` could never reach it — fixed as above); the MIME assertion checked only the first 48 bytes (now the whole 5 MB payload, CRLF-normalized); the `dbEpoch` wait could never block (now the second port delivery and the same `dbEpoch` as the `ready` hello); two `byteLength`-only reads became full `deepEqual`s; `pattern()` covers 0x00–0xFF; and the teardown removes the data folder only after the supervisor closes.
- **In-process, not the packaged app:** the plan's "one `APLOS_TEST=1 APLOS_ENGINE=real` launch" shape is honoured in kind — the same `runEngineUtility`, the same `composeEngineHost`, the same store — because a committed Playwright launch of the built app is platform's sweep job (`verify-hardening-real-engine.mjs`), not a unit test. `rig.ts`'s `inProcessUtility` gained an optional `ports` parameter so a test can inject the host ports compose does not build itself (`send`, here); no production file changed, the engine bundle is untouched.
## 2026-11-05 — ui-shell: shell chrome fixes from the owner's hand-check (windowed row, sidebar toggle, drag, focus, regions)

- **Top of the sidebar.** On macOS in a windowed window the sidebar top is two rows: the traffic-light row (toggle and Compose at its right end) and the account switcher on its own row below. Fullscreen keeps one row. Main sends `{type:'fullscreen'}` on enter/leave and at did-finish-load (`windows.ts`, named in `shared/event-hub.ts` so the seed waits for the renderer's subscriber); the renderer reads it in `shell/chrome.ts`.
- **Sidebar toggle.** A button next to Compose and `app.sidebar` (keys `[` and ⌘S; nothing else binds ⌘S; the palette and Settings → Keyboard list it from the catalog). Collapsed = no sidebar, a 40 px drag strip with just the toggle (right of the traffic lights when windowed), panes below it. The collapsed flag lives in localStorage `aplos.sidebar.collapsed`, not in `settings.ui`: it is window chrome, and a settings key would have cost an eng-contract change. The minimised ☰ mode is unchanged.
- **Drag.** `-webkit-app-region: drag` on the shell gutter, sidebar background, the collapsed strip and the pane header; `no-drag` on every control, list, pane body, dock, menu, dialog and toast. No custom drag code.
- **Tab model.** Tab/Shift+Tab move between regions (sidebar, main list or reader, dock) only from the body, the sidebar, a list/reader surface or the Ask field; in forms (Settings, compose, onboarding, dialogs) Tab stays native, because each region already has one roving tab stop. ↑/↓/Home/End walk the sidebar as one list. `list.nextSection`/`previousSection` lost their Tab keys (still in the palette) and Tab/⇧Tab are reserved chords, so nothing can be remapped onto them.
- **Focus.** Containers with a focus target draw no ring; rows and controls keep `:focus-visible`. Root cause of the big outline was `.ui-list-box:focus-visible`. Root cause of the launch "C" tip was a pointerenter fired under a parked cursor; a tip now needs a real pointer move, or a real keydown before focus.
- **Help** opens Settings → Help & about (`settings/help`).
- **Open items.** The View → Toggle Sidebar menu item (⌘⇧S) is still unwired in the new renderer (command events are held for a general subscriber that the new renderer never installs). Header buttons and the MiniMonth are reachable by pointer and their own keys, not by the region Tab. Settings → Help's "Show shortcuts" stays disabled until a shortcut-sheet host exists. DESIGN §7.2/§11 still list Tab for next section.

## 2026-11-05 — eng-host: the #313 tickets that are eng-host's

- **A revoked client is refused on its next call, whatever its transport does (prov-cal's ticket 2).** `dispatchWith` asks `clients.revoked_at` for a listener caller's `clientId` before any handler runs and answers `forbidden` "This tool is no longer allowed." (one indexed read per listener call). A client the engine has no row for is the transport's to authenticate (not refused here); a window or main is never refused. Closing the open stream and socket is the transport's, when the real engine has one.
- **`Clients/audit` shows a `target` only for methods whose target is a client id** (`Clients/revoke`, which only the app can call, so in practice none today); the allowed sender behind a `Mail/allowImages` row stays in the table and never reaches a reader. `Clients/revoke` stamps `revoked_at` once (a repeat does not move the time).
- **Not eng-host's, for eng-ai (prov-cal's tickets 1 and 3):** `Ai/*` and the background job runner must refuse unless consent is granted and not `needsReconsent`; the `Ai/providers` fingerprint origin and path must match the configured address it reports. `audit_log` age-out (HANDOFF.md) is still open.
## 2026-11-05 — eng-views: #317 fix round (Search/remote cool-downs)

- **A skipped account is a partial run, not `done` (HIGH).** When some accounts were skipped for a cool-down and the others answered, the run ends with a progress `error` frame whose `problem` is `{reasonCode: 'paused', message, resumeAt}` (the windows keep what the healthy accounts found; `done` would read an empty list as "nothing matched anywhere"). All accounts cooling is still the `paused` error that ends the windows. `Problem` gained an optional `resumeAt` (additive, `contract/src/schemas/common.ts`).
- **Pauses are clamped (MEDIUM).** A stored `until` is at most now + 24 h (a huge or garbage `retryAfterMs`, `resumeAt` or `PausedError.until`), the same number goes on the wire, and an entry more than 24 h ahead of now is expired when read (a clock set back).
- **A project-wide refusal cools that provider's accounts only (LOW):** the accounts of the same provider as the one that got it (`SELECT ... WHERE provider = ...`), not the other providers'.
- **The default breath (10 s, not on the wire) applies to the governor's budget classes `throttle`, `daily`, `anti_abuse` (and a concurrency refusal)**; `quota` and `concurrency` were not classes (`ports/governor.ts`).
- **`askProvider` is total** (a store failure is an error body, as before the PR) on top of the shared-run backstop in `runSearch`.
- Tests: `tests/views/remote-pause.test.ts` (8, fake clock, each guard mutation-checked: the pause ending, the exact 10 s, anti_abuse, the 24 h clamp on the wire and in the store, the project scope, the partial run, a joiner's own frames, a store failure) and the two older tests in `windows.test.ts` updated to the partial / three-run shapes.


## 2026-11-06 — prov-gov: Google Calendar scope is least-privilege (Integrator DECIDED on #345)

- **Round-one calendar asks `calendar.events.readonly`, never `calendar.events`.** Round one only READS the calendar (`events.list`/`calendarList.list`; `put`/`remove`/`rsvp` are `notImplemented`), and the Testing-mode weekly reconnect re-prompts consent anyway — so asking the write scope would only put "See, create, change and delete events on your calendars" on the consent screen for a sync that never writes. `calendar.events` stays in `googleScopeManifest` as a separate `deferred: true` entry (feature `calendarEventsWrite`): never newly requested by any flow until round 2 ships, but still kept when a grant already holds it, and it counts for the `calendar` capability since write covers read. `grantPermissions` asks every NON-deferred optional scope.
- **A `permission` refusal on a calendar scope is a feature gap, not an account halt (D12).** `stepFailed` routes `permission` on `calendars`/`cal:*` scopes to `calendarScopeGap` + `missingPermissions` (the Settings "Allow calendar access" row) with the usual escalating backoff; mail status is untouched. A `permission` refusal on a mail scope still halts as `needsPermission`.

## 2026-11-06 — eng-contract: `Account.calendarListedAt` ("No calendars" vs "Syncing…", ui-screens' REQUEST from #356)

- **The fact:** `Account.calendarListedAt` (optional epoch ms) is when the account's calendar list was FIRST committed, even if the list was empty. `baselineDoneAt` is the mail marker and says nothing about calendars, so the UI could not tell an account with no calendars from one still listing them. A calendar-capable account without it is syncing its list; with it and no calendars, there are none.
- **Where it is stamped:** the sync actor's `calendars` step (`sync/actor-calendar.ts`) adds `{kind: 'account', id, calendarListedAt: now}` to the same commit as the list, so the stamp and the list land together or not at all. First write wins in the store (`COALESCE(calendar_listed_at, ?)`): a later list never moves it. A new account row may carry it from its first write.
- **Storage:** `accounts.calendar_listed_at INTEGER`, migration 0006 (additive; accounts from before it stay NULL until their next list, which is one `calendars` step away). `readAccount` exposes it; the stand-in's demo accounts carry it, and its setup flow stamps it when setup completes.
- **Not done here:** the Calendar UI's use of it (ui-screens, a follow-up to #356); a halted account (no list ever) keeps showing nothing, as before; a disconnect-reconnect creates a new row, so it re-lists.

## 2026-11-07 — eng-contract: AI inputs with raw html name their account

Context: eng-ai's REQUEST (07:15). `Ai/polish` without a `draftId` (pasted text) and Ask's `context.draft {to,cc,subject,html}` carry raw html and no account, so the `ai_allowed` check cannot run on them; only the provider gate (`trainsOnInputs`) applies.

Decision (a): add an optional `accountId` to `Ai/polish` and to `Ask/run` `context.draft`. A caller that has the composing account passes it, and an account left out of AI is `forbidden` with `ai.accountExcluded`, like a `draftId` in that account. Absent, the call stays allowed (the text is what the user typed; the provider rule still applies), so existing callers keep working. Not (b): documenting an unchecked path leaves the toggle bypassable by any composer that has an account.

How to apply: additive optional fields (no `currentVersion` change, openapi regenerated); the fixture refuses an excluded or unknown `accountId` and the conformance suite pins it for both calls. eng-ai implements the same check in the engine (`ai/impl/gate.ts`); the composer and Ask docked panel (ui lanes) pass the draft's `accountId`.
