## 2026-11-10 — eng-contract: Message.notification (the reader bell) and no Contact/photo yet

Context: ui-reader's REQUEST (owner request: a bell in the card header that is slashed when the email did NOT notify; circles are monograms).

Decision: `Message.notification?: 'sent' | 'suppressed' | 'none'`, additive and optional, set by `Message/get` from the notification outbox row (`notifications.state`): pending, claimed and posted are `sent`; dropped (Focus, the OS refused, or the mail was read or moved out of the Inbox before the banner posted) is `suppressed`; no row is `none`, which includes notifications turned off for the account (no row is ever written) and the baseline sync. A pending row reports `sent` before the banner has posted and may still become `suppressed`. The outbox keeps rows 30 days, so a message older than that carries no field (unknown, not `none`) and the bell stays neutral. Within v:1, an open enum, no `currentVersion` change.

`Contact/photo` is NOT added now. Avatars would mean fetching a remote image or reading a contacts photo for every sender, which is a privacy and egress decision (the renderer never loads a remote URL). Monograms stay; if the owner wants avatars, the method is designed then (bytes or a `data:` URL, from sources already on this Mac).

How to apply: the reader shows the bell from `notification` (absent: neutral outline bell, no claim). Engine side is `mail/rows.ts` (eng-html's file, approval requested on the PR); fixture marks unread inbound demo mail `sent`.
## 2026-11-10 — prov-cal: Google Calendar WRITING (create, edit, delete, RSVP) behind an explicit "Enable editing" re-consent

Owner approved building calendar writes ("do all that"); round one was deliberately read-only (DECISIONS 2026-11-06, #345). This entry records the scope and consent design. Observed, chosen and unknown are kept apart.

- **Chosen: the write scope is `calendar.events` and is asked for only by an explicit opt-in.** `Account/grantPermissions` gains an optional `enable: 'calendarWrite'` (contract-change, additive). Only that call adds `calendar.events` (with the calendar list scope, so the calendar capability is true), on top of whatever the stored grant already holds. It asks for nothing else: not Gmail filters, not any other optional scope. A new sign-in, a plain reconnect and a plain re-grant never ask for it (they ignore the field), and a grant that already holds it keeps it on a reconnect. The scope stays `deferred: true` in the scope manifest; `explicitEnables` (oauth/scopes.ts) is the one table that names which opt-in may ask for which scope. An unknown opt-in is refused before Google is asked.
- **Chosen: read-only stays the default and still works.** Read access is unchanged. A write on a grant without the scope is refused BEFORE any request with `ContractError` type `permission`, reason code `permission.calendarWrite` (new, additive) and a plain sentence ("Aplos can only read this calendar. Turn on editing in Settings → Calendar, then try again."). The refusal also sets an `Account.missingPermissions` row, capability `calendarWrite`, `missingScopes: [calendar.events]` — only after a write was actually refused, never as a standing nag for every read-only account (the onboarding reconnect check treats any missing permission as a reason to prompt). `Account.grantedScopes` already tells the UI whether editing is on. A reconnect clears the row, and the next refused write sets it again if the grant still lacks the scope.
- **Chosen: the engine's `CalendarPort` is now wired** (`ActorHost.calendarPort`, composed in `host/compose.ts`). Before this it was the host's `unavailable` stand-in, so `Event/set` and `Event/rsvp` could not reach any adapter. The port looks the adapter up by the account the caller named, so one account's edit cannot reach another's calendar, and it checks the account id again.
- **Observed (Google docs, not yet by a live run in this repo):** `calendar.events` is a *sensitive* scope, not a restricted one. The project already requests sensitive calendar scopes (`calendarlist.readonly`, `events.readonly`) and the restricted `gmail.modify`, so adding the write scope does not move the app into a stricter review tier; it adds one more scope the verification submission must justify, and its consent text ("See, create, change and delete events on your calendars") is the strongest in the calendar group. Writing and deleting events does not need the broader `calendar` scope.
- **Verification implications (the OAuth app is in Testing mode):** nothing here needs Google verification while the app stays in Testing with test users — the owner is the only user and already re-consents weekly because Testing-mode refresh tokens expire after 7 days. A public release would need the consent screen verified with `calendar.events` justified (the least-privilege argument: it is requested only on an explicit tap, never at sign-in, and read-only works without it). **Unknown, to confirm at the hand check:** whether a Testing-mode project must list `calendar.events` under the consent screen's data-access scopes before a test user can grant it (the read scopes were listed for the same reason, `plans/HAND-CHECK.md`); if the consent screen refuses it, the owner adds that one scope in Google Cloud and nothing in the code changes.
- **Chosen threat model for every write PR:** wrong calendar (the port and the adapter address `calendarRemoteId` from the event's own id, and a read-only calendar's `accessRole` is refused locally), wrong account (the port resolves the started account and re-checks the id), duplicate writes (create uses a client-chosen event id so a retried POST answers 409 instead of creating twice), scope creep (the opt-in table above plus tests that pin which flows may ask for the write scope).

## 2026-11-10 — prov-cal: how Google Calendar writes behave (create, edit, delete, RSVP)

- **Chosen: `this event` and `all events`, not `this and following`.** `Event/set` and `Event/rsvp` accept `occurrence` (one instance of a series, through Google's instance id `<series>_<recurrenceId>`, which creates or updates that one exception row) and `series` (the master, or a single event). `following` answers `unsupported` (reason `event.followingUnsupported`) before any request; the session flag `eventFollowingScope` stays false. The contract has no recurrence field on `EventFields`, so a new event is never recurring and an edit never changes a repeat rule.
- **Chosen: conflicts are a result, not a guess.** Edits and deletes send `If-Match` with the etag the engine last stored for that exact resource (the master for a series, the exception row for one occurrence, nothing for an occurrence that has no exception yet). Google's 412 becomes `conflict`: the adapter reads the event back, the port commits that newer copy locally, and the method answers `conflict` / `calendar.conflict` ("This event changed elsewhere. The newer version is shown; make your change again."). Nothing is overwritten and nothing retries on its own. An RSVP reads the event first and answers `conflict` when its etag no longer matches.
- **Chosen: duplicates are impossible by construction.** A create picks its own event id (26 random base32hex characters), so the same request sent twice answers 409 and the adapter reads the event back instead of making a second one. Edits and RSVPs use the governor's `write` kind, which is never re-sent; a lost answer says `calendar.uncertain` ("cannot tell whether the change was saved. Check the calendar before trying again") instead of repeating. A delete is idempotent (404 and 410 mean it is already gone).
- **Chosen: guests are told only on request.** `sendUpdates=none` unless the caller passed `notify`; an RSVP defaults to telling the organizer (`Event/rsvp`'s existing `notify ?? true`). Editing guests keeps the answers already given: the existing list is read first and each guest still on the event keeps their `responseStatus`.
- **Chosen: the local calendar shows the saved event at once.** The port commits what Google confirmed (the event row, a removal, or the newer copy after a conflict) in the same shape a sync step writes, so there is no wait for the next 2 to 5 minute poll. A sync page fetched before the write and committed after it can briefly put the old copy back; the next incremental page corrects it.
- **Chosen: a calendar that cannot be changed is refused locally.** A calendar row marked `readOnly` (Google `accessRole` reader or freeBusyReader) answers `unsupported` / `calendar.readOnly` before the port is called. Honest errors: a `permission` refusal from Google becomes the same Enable-editing answer and sets the `calendarWrite` row; auth becomes `needsReauth`; a throttle, an anti-abuse stop or a transient failure becomes `unavailable` with "Nothing was changed"; a 404/410 `notFound`; 403 `forbidden`; 400 `validation`. No provider text, token or scope URL reaches the caller.
- **Not built yet (honest scope):** the optimistic overlay (`Event.sync: pending`) and the undo window for guest-notifying changes (`Receipt.undoUntil`). They need `Operation/undo` to reach calendar writes, which today only knows mail ops (`eng-views`), so they are a separate PR with the lane requests posted in STATUS. Until then these methods are write-through: the receipt is `undoable: false` and the screen shows the saved event as soon as Google confirms it.
## 2026-11-10: Home lanes were never switched on in the app (ui-home)

The owner saw one mixed Home with no Low importance. Cause: `host/compose.ts` opened the store without `ingest: { laneFor }`, so no thread was ever ranked and all stayed in the schema-default lane `do`. Decisions:

- The host passes `laneFor` through a new `@aplos/engine/ai/lanes` subpath export, so the rules stay out of the engine core bundle (450,018 of 470,000 after the change).
- `store/ingest.ts` ranks mail stored before the wiring in bounded batches of 100 threads per sync commit (only in sync commits: never in the owner's own actions, so an archive stays quick, nor in import, system or AI executor commits): first the threads Home can show, newest first, so the first commit fixes the top of Home; then the rest by thread id; the place is kept in `meta` (key `lane_backfill`, ends at `done`). Only `rank_source = 'heuristic'` threads change; user, rule and AI ranks are never touched.
- Known limit: the catch-up is one pass. If a build ever opens the store without `laneFor` again, mail synced in that window is not re-ranked; `lane_backfill` is a reserved meta key, so no commit can reset or skip it, and a thread that `laneFor` leaves in Do now is still stamped `ranked_at`.

## 2026-11-10: the reader's email card (owner request; ui-reader)

Context: the owner asked, by name, for the reader / one-by-one email to be one rounded card with the subject on its top line and a header row with the sender, time, "To" with overlapping recipient circles and a chevron (details popover), and on the right an Importance menu, a bell and ⋯ (sketch images A, B and C of 2026-11-10). DESIGN 3, 6 and 13 and the aplos-design skill forbid "avatars or monograms in mail", "Important ▾ in the reader header" and a card-less reader.

Decision: the owner's request wins over those lines for this card only. The circles are monograms (never a remote avatar loaded by the renderer); the details popover lists only what `Message` carries (From, To, Cc, Bcc, Reply-to when it differs, Date); the bell shows a neutral state until the engine reports whether the email notified. The summary stays big above the card; Space, Reply, Later, Done and the docked Ask are unchanged. Not built (owner skipped): the "New 6" counter, the left tick-marks, the "Full email" button.

How to apply: plans/DESIGN.md section 16 holds the superseding entry (integrator, 2026-11-10); the code follows it in #391 (card header and details popover), #396 (Importance menu, bell, ⋯) and #403 (the one-by-one view mounts the same card). No other screen gains avatars, monograms or an Important menu.

A band hidden at 0 left no way into Low importance, which the owner reported as "no Low importance section". Home now shows the band while it has other mail: 0 on the right, a click or Return-free open (an empty band is not a keyboard cursor stop, so the cursor never settles on nothing) into the sheet's "No low-importance mail" with "Back to Home". This departs from "empty sections don't render" (DESIGN 7.2) for this one band, on purpose. The cause of the mixed list itself (the app never switched the lane rules on) is fixed in the engine and host PR.

Follow-up to the entry above, from the review of #388 (merged before these fixes landed). The band's visibility now comes from the view's totals (`layoutOf` reads `total` and `low.count`, the same test as the empty Home) instead of the windowed ids, so section headers and the band can never disagree. An empty band is a cursor stop (J/K walk onto it, Return opens "No low-importance mail", so the Return in its tooltip is true), but the cursor never settles on it by itself: when the row under the cursor leaves, it goes to the row above. `LowBand`'s optional `commandId` (#402) is not needed by Home.

## 2026-11-10: the one-by-one card follows the owner's second sketch, which overrides DESIGN section 16 on five points (ui-reader)

Context: the owner's feedback on the one-by-one card, relayed by the orchestrator, 2026-11-10: "They say it does not follow their design closely enough. Treat their sketch as the exact spec" (`df6a1482-0483-40bf-8a55-d48f8f2404ff.png`, in the owner's attachments); "REMOVE the summary headline above the card and the 'Due tomorrow' chip. Nothing sits above the card except the existing top bar"; "REMOVE the subject title row inside the card and its divider"; "REMOVE the blue vertical line to the left of the sender name"; "Sender avatar: the small rounded square before the name in the sketch"; "bell (slashed when it did not notify), then 'Important ⌄', then ⋯"; "no white container for plain emails ... in the app's own body font, size and text colour in BOTH light and dark mode. Keep the light 'paper' frame only for rich HTML"; "If the sketch and earlier decisions conflict, follow the sketch."

Decision: the sketch wins over DESIGN section 16 (the first owner-override paragraph of 2026-11-10) and over DESIGN 7.5 on these points, for the email card only:

Also by the sketch: the card is nearly as wide as the pane and fills its height in Go one by one, with no Reply stub under it (Reply is on the pinned bar).

## 2026-11-10 — prov-cal: calendar writes that tell guests are held for the undo window (`services.calendarHolds`)

Follows the eng-contract DECIDED on the seam (STATUS 2026-11-10 13:58); eng-views' file `methods/operation.ts` is changed by a small routing edit that needs `lane-approved:eng-views` from integrator-3.

- **Chosen: only a write that tells someone is held.** `Event/set` with `notify: true` and `Event/rsvp` with `notify` (its default is true) wait the owner's undo-send window (`mail.undoSendSeconds`, 8 s by default; the same window a mail send uses). The receipt is `undoable: true` with `undoUntil`. A write that tells no one goes at once, as before, with `undoable` absent: it has no inverse (Google keeps no "undo" for a patch), and the contract says an operation without an inverse is not undoable.
- **Chosen: a hold is data, not a timer.** It is a `meta` row `calendar.hold.<opId>` holding the exact `CalendarPort` call, so it survives a restart. A `calendarHolds` deadline source wakes the working half; after a restart a hold whose window ended while the app was closed is sent on launch (same rule as an undo-window send interrupted by a quit). A record found `syncing` that this process did not start is reported failed with `calendar.uncertain` and is never sent again (it may already have reached Google).
- **Chosen: `Operation/*` reach it through the agreed seam.** `has(opId)`, `get(opIds)`, `undo(args, caller)`, `retry(args, caller)`; `operation.ts` asks the hold first for an op id it holds, else the mail operations, and `get` splits the ids and returns one list with `notFound` for ids neither side knows. The hold records the scope `send` (the originating method's) and `undo`/`retry` check it with `requireOriginating`, exactly as `threadOps` does (FR-3); a caller with only `triage` is `forbidden` and the hold stays queued. `get` returns the contract shape: `queued` during the window, `syncing`, `synced`, `failed` (with the plain error, so the inline Retry exists) or `cancelled`.
- **Chosen: undo cancels, nothing else.** While `queued`, undo marks it `cancelled`: Google never hears of it, no guest is emailed, and the re-read before each send means an undo that lands while an earlier hold is sending still stops the next one. Once sending or sent, undo answers `conflict` ("has been sent, so it cannot be undone") instead of claiming to restore. Retry is offered for a failed hold and sends at once (the person pressed Retry), including a conflict at send time (`calendar.conflict`, the event changed during the window).
- **Not built: an engine-side optimistic overlay.** A held create returns no `event` (nothing exists yet) and the local row is not touched during the window; the screen shows its own pending state from the receipt (`undoUntil`) and the calendar updates when the send is confirmed (the port commits the saved facts). Writing a placeholder row and rolling it back would need a pending marker on `events` (a schema change, `eng-store`) and a revert path that races the sync; the window is eight seconds. Revisit if the owner finds the pause noticeable.
- **Engine core:** the always-loaded part is `holds-core.ts` (about +2.4 KB; 465,883 of 470,000 when measured on 2026-11-11); the working half (`holds.ts`) is a lazy module listed in `tests/portability.test.ts`.
## 2026-11-10: the three idle budget rows are recorded from the first resident-mode measurements (plat-ci)

`scripts/audit/measure-idle.mjs` launches a packaged app headless in test mode on a FRESH, EMPTY data dir (no account, no mailbox, in-memory keychain), closes every window over CDP to reach resident mode, waits 60 s, samples the engine utility process for 10 minutes (`ps` every 5 s) and reads wake-ups from a 30 s `top` sample (median). Because the profile is empty, nothing syncs during the settle and the numbers are the lightest possible idle, not an idle with a signed-in mailbox: treat them as a floor. Two complete runs on one Mac (a shared agent Mac, not the owner's quiet machine), the hand-check build of revamp 665ea4fe (ad-hoc, `~/Applications-test`), 120 samples each, no engine restart:

| Run | Script | Engine RSS median (max) | CPU over 600 s | Wake-ups per s |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | first version (wake-ups from 5 one-second samples) | 97.3 MB (122.6) | 0.77 percent | 2 |
| 2 | PR head (30 one-second samples, median 4, max 7) | 123.1 MB (131.7) | 0.74 percent | 4 |

The RSS medians differ by 26 MB between two identical runs, so one run is not a limit. `budgets.json` records run 2 (the PR-head script):

- `idle.engine-rss` (eng-host): 123.1 MB, `ps` rss, not Activity Monitor's footprint.
- `idle.cpu-10min` (eng-host): 0.74 percent of one core over the 600 s window (cputime delta over wall time).
- `idle.wakeups` (eng-host): 4 per second (`top` IDLEW median of a 30 s sample).

Each row keeps its gate `record`: these are baselines from an empty profile, not limits. Turning them into tighten-only limits needs headroom, repeated runs on the owner's Mac and a run with a synced account, which is eng-host's call. The engine process is found by the `node.mojom.NodeService` marker (the preferred `--aplos-engine=real` marker is not on the real utility's command line); only one such utility exists. Re-run with `node scripts/audit/measure-idle.mjs <app> --json` (about 11.5 minutes).

## 2026-11-11 — prov-cal: a series' time change follows the series' own zone, whatever form the request takes

- **Chosen:** a series edit opened from one occurrence shifts the master by the owner's change measured on the series' wall clock in the series' own zone. `utc` times for a series that has a zone are converted to that zone's wall clock first (otherwise an elapsed-time shift is an hour off across a daylight-saving change: Oct 28 10:00 PDT moved to Nov 1 10:00 PST put the master at 11:00). A series with no zone has only elapsed time to go by, so `utc` stays elapsed. A wall-clock time for a series with no zone, or in another zone than the series', or in a zone the engine does not know, is refused `calendar.floatingSeries` instead of rewriting the series into a zone it never had.
- **Known UX gap (ticket for ui-calendar, not a blocker):** the Calendar screen builds wall-clock times in the calendar's zone, so a series whose own zone differs from the calendar's cannot have its time changed from an occurrence, and the refusal's sentence points at editing the series' first event. Say something truthful there, or send the series' own zone.
## 2026-11-11 — prov-cal: held calendar writes after the Opus review of #437

- **Chosen: every save of a hold carries its own commit id.** A `meta`-only commit does not move the store's `seq`, and the old id used `seq`, so a second save of the same state (a Retry's `syncing`) was skipped as already applied; two Retry presses then both sent. Ids now carry a random part, and a second Retry sees `syncing` and answers `conflict`.
- **Chosen: a hold is not sent on its own when it is late** — its window ended more than 15 minutes ago (the app was closed) or the event has already started. It becomes `failed` / `calendar.late` ("was not sent in time… Check the event, then press Retry to send it now") and goes only on an explicit Retry: guests should not get an invitation days late or for a meeting that is over.
- **Chosen: one pending change per event.** A second held change to an event (or occurrence) whose earlier change is still `queued` or `syncing` is refused `conflict` / `calendar.holdPending` ("Your earlier change to this event has not been sent yet. Undo it, or wait a moment and try again."), because the second would meet the first's new version and fail with a 412. Different events, and creates, are unaffected. A refusal beats a silent merge of two edits the owner may not remember making.
- **Chosen: a finished hold keeps no guest addresses.** Once sent or undone its stored call is dropped; every finished hold is purged after a day (the source wakes for it), and an unreadable row is cleared instead of stalling the loop.
- **Chosen: a replayed op id answers the same receipt** and makes no second hold; `Operation/get` answers in the order asked.
- **Known limit:** after the app reloads, a failed hold is visible only to a screen that kept its op id (`Operation/get`); there is no list of failed calendar writes. A failed hold is never purged on a timer; it stays until retried and finished. If that proves too quiet, the follow-up is a notification or a list surface, which needs a contract addition.

## 2026-11-11 — prov-cal: two more guards on held calendar writes

- **Chosen:** the deadline source does not wake for a send that is under way in this process (it wakes for one a dead run left `syncing`). Before, an in-flight send made `next()` answer "now" for as long as it lasted, a busy loop on the one-second back-off.
- **Chosen:** a wall-clock time with no zone of its own is read in the calendar's zone when deciding whether the event "has already started", the way the engine reads it everywhere else; read as UTC it could be hours off and send a change late, or hold one that is not late.

## 2026-11-11 — prov-cal: held calendar writes, second Opus review (what is and is not guaranteed)

- **Chosen: a hold announces every state it moves through** as an `operation` frame (queued, syncing, synced, failed, cancelled), the same object `Operation/get` answers, so an open window sees a failed invite without asking. After a reload a screen still needs the op id to ask; a list of failed calendar writes in `Queue/list` (kind `op`, content-free label "Calendar change", `canRetry` on failed, `canDiscard` while queued) is the follow-up eng-contract described (STATUS 2026-11-11 20:03), not built here.
- **Chosen: Google accepting a change that this device then cannot record** (the `synced` save throws) is `failed` / `calendar.uncertain`, never "try again": a second send would tell the guests twice.
- **Chosen: a hold that comes due before its account has started** (launch) waits and is looked at again 30 seconds later; nothing was sent, so it is not a failure. The 15-minute limit still ends a hold whose account never starts.
- **Precision on privacy:** "no guest addresses kept" holds for FINISHED holds (sent or undone). Queued, sending and failed holds keep the full call, because a Retry needs it; a FAILED hold is never purged on a timer (it must still be there when the owner looks): it is kept until it is retried and finishes, or undone; only then does the one-day purge start.
- **Precision on the read-only calendar check:** it is made when the change is MADE (`Event/set`), not when the hold is sent; at send time the grant is checked again and Google's own refusal (403) is the backstop.
- **Measured:** engine core 465,883 of 470,000 bytes (the earlier figure of 465,795 was before the series rules).
## 2026-11-11: earlier messages in a thread get a visible Show full / Show less (ui-reader)

Context: the owner's round-3 feedback: the compact earlier messages show a snippet but nothing says they open; "give each compact earlier message a visible 'Show full' ... button/indicator on the row ... make the whole row clickable and keyboard accessible ... an expanded earlier message gets a 'Show less'/collapse indicator in the same place ... Keep the thread-level summary where the design already puts it (don't add it back above the card)", and "AI summaries are per thread, not per message, so do NOT invent per-message summaries".

Decision: a folded earlier message's row ends in "Show full" and a chevron; the whole row stays the click target (the one button is stretched over the card) and Enter, Space and O keep working; opening it shows the full message in place with the card's header row (sender mark, name, time, To circles) and the same button, now "Show less" with an up chevron, in the same place. The button is one DOM node, last in the head, folded or open, so focus survives a fold. The row's text stays the message's OWN first sentence or two (from its snippet; no body fetch, no AI); the thread summary is not shown per message and is not added above the card. The cursor edge and keys are unchanged. ThreadMessage (ui-foundation) restructures its fold head so the button is no longer the wrapper of the head (a button cannot hold the To button of the opened header).

## 2026-11-10: Home rows name the other person, not the owner (ui-home, break-ui pass on sender names)

The engine's `ThreadRow.fromName` is the newest sender of the thread, which is the owner whenever they answered last. `senderOf` (rows, "Always do this for …", the one-by-one and reader titles) now prefers the newest sender who is not the owner, then the owner's own name, then the subject; names lose control characters, bidi overrides and invisible marks and fold whitespace (joiners stay for emoji families and Persian). A thread the owner wrote entirely still shows their own name, because `ThreadRow` has no recipients to name instead (requested from eng-contract).

## 2026-11-11: the one-by-one card is a reading column at most 680 px wide (ui-reader)

Context: the owner's round-3 feedback on the one-by-one card ("that's good", two changes), relayed by the orchestrator: the card #421 drew nearly as wide as the pane is too wide; the reference (another app's screenshot, 2x) shows a card about 625 CSS px wide with about 470 px of body text; "a centered column roughly 620-680 CSS px wide at a normal window, shrinking with the window (keep a comfortable side margin, 16-24 px at narrow widths ... no horizontal scroll) and NEVER wider than a max reading width"; "add the max to the tokens/DESIGN.md as an owner decision".

Decision: new token `layout.card-w` = 680px (`--card-w`, 42.5rem). In Go one by one the card is `min(100% - 2 x clamp(16px, 3%, 24px), --card-w)`, centred; the header, its hairline and the pinned bar are the column's own. A plain body keeps the existing `--measure-read` (64ch, about 65 to 70 characters) inside the card; a rich email's paper frame uses the card's width and its images already scale to it. This amends the 2026-11-10 entry's "the card is nearly as wide as the pane" (and DESIGN section 16's amendment of 2026-11-11, which says the card fills the pane's width).

How to apply: the integrator amends DESIGN section 16's 2026-11-11 amendment and 4.x's layout table (`--card-w`); no other screen changes.

## 2026-11-11: the reader's loading line names the sender like Home's row does (ui-reader, handoff from ui-home #454)

Context: `loadingLabel` fell back to the raw `row.fromName` for a thread with no subject, so a worst-case sender name (the owner's own name when they answered last, bidi controls, runs of whitespace) leaked into "Loading …".

Decision: the subject and the sender both go through Home's `cleanName`, and the sender is Home's `senderOf` (the other person, not the owner). A thread with neither still gets no label, and the caller says "this email".

## 2026-11-10: Narrow Home panes: nothing squashes or covers anything (ui-home, on ui-polish's review of #454)

With the Ask dock open at the minimum window the Home pane is about 324px: the focus letter's Reply collapsed to a 24px stub over Later's icon, and a row's due chip and bookmark and attachment icons overlapped. `screens/screens.css` now makes the focus letter a size container: under 380px Later and Done keep only their icons (label and key stay for assistive tech and the tooltip), under 250px Reply also drops its Space cap, under 189px its label too (it keeps its icon and its name), and it never shrinks. (Thresholds are the card's content width.) Rows keep their trailing icons at their own width, give the sender a 48px floor and clip the middle column (summary and due chip) instead of letting it spill. Wide layouts are unchanged (same measured geometry at 1280px).

## 2026-11-10: Follow up names who it waits on; Sent names who it went to (ui-home, on eng-views' ThreadRow.recipients)

`ThreadRow.recipients` (the To and Cc of the newest message the owner sent, added by eng-views) lets a thread the owner wrote entirely read as the person being waited on instead of the owner's own name: `senderOf` uses the sender who is not the owner first, then `recipients` (owner removed, up to three names then "and N more"), then the owner, then the subject. Sent's "who" column now prefers `recipients` too (who the thread went to) before the other senders in the thread. The shared helpers `otherNames` and `joinNames` live in `screens/home/homeRows.ts`.
## 2026-11-11: Low band owner test in SQL, with a perf note (ui-home, on the #464 review)

The Low band's "is this participant the owner" test (`NON_OWNER` in `views/sections.ts`: any account's address, or an address that sent a non-draft from-me message in the thread, empty addresses never counting) is a correlated lookup per participant. The sender-row expression now works out the other person's address and name once per thread instead of up to six times, and an empty `from_addr` no longer counts as the owner (the row builder and the reference never did). Measured on a synthetic 3,000-thread Low band with four participants each, best of 12 runs: about 180-250 ms against 267 ms before, on a loaded machine, so the gain is real but modest. The remaining cost grows with participants per thread (up to six on a real mailbox); if the 5,000-row `lowGroups` read ever shows in a real hand-check, the next step is one owner set per thread kept by the store at commit instead of a lookup at read.

They mostly unioned, but #458 hid the main label at 189px and #466 does not. The focus-letter half of the screens.css block is deleted; the row rules stay (trailing icons at their own width, a sender floor, the middle column clipped), still scoped to `.aplos-list-column`. Checked at the 320px pane with the Ask dock open (letter 240px: no spill, Reply keeps its label, Later, Done and ⋯ as icons) and at a 1100px window (labels visible).

## 2026-11-11: the focus letter's narrow-pane rules live in the component, once (ui-core/ui-polish decided)

Context: two rule sets for the same narrow-pane behaviour landed within an hour — #458's in `screens/screens.css` scoped to `.aplos-list-column` (380/250/189 px) and #466's in `ui/list/list.css` on the component itself (24.5/20/15 rem). They union with no breakage except one conflict: #458 hides the Reply label under 189 px while #466's rule is that the main action keeps its label at every width.

Decision (ui-core + ui-polish, addressed to ui-home): keep #466's component rules; delete the focus-letter part of #458's block in `screens/screens.css` — the `.aplos-list-column .ui-focus-letter` container line, the `__suggest` flex rules and the three `@container focus-letter` blocks — keeping the `.ui-list-row`/`__line`/`__meta` rules below it. Pinned by `tests/visual/focus-letter-narrow.spec.ts`; the Reply label never clips.

## 2026-11-11 — prov-cal: held calendar writes, two small rules from the third review

- **Chosen:** undo of a hold that failed as `calendar.uncertain` is refused (`conflict`: "may already have been sent"). The change may already be at Google; recording it as "undone" would be a claim nobody can keep.
- **Chosen:** a whole-series hold (including an "All events" edit opened from one occurrence, which names that occurrence but has scope `series`) and a `following` hold (which rewrites every later occurrence) clash with a hold on any occurrence of the same event (the second is refused `calendar.holdPending`); two holds on different occurrences of one event do not. Only scope `occurrence` is about one occurrence, the way `targetId` picks Google's target.
- **Open ticket (not a blocker):** a recurrence id with no `Z` is read in the calendar's zone for the "already started" check, not in the event's own zone.

## 2026-11-11: the reader-focus spec drops its Tab assertion (integrator decision)

Context: `tests/visual/reader-focus.spec.ts` "Tab to a card and O opens that card" fails deterministically: shell region navigation owns Tab, so cards are not in the tab order (found in the post-#462 dark-run full-harness triage; ui-core request to integrator-ui and ui-reader).

Decision: the spec is rewritten — the card gets focus by click / `.focus()` and O-to-open stays the assertion. Shell-region Tab navigation is the intended model; whether cards should join the tab order (roving tabindex) is a product/accessibility call for the owner, not a defect to fix in a test. The spec file is ui-foundation's (tests/visual); implementation goes to them or to ui-reader with `lane-approved:ui-foundation`.

## 2026-11-11: The Low band's owner test stays per thread, with the account half built once (ui-home, taking over eng-views' #475 on the integrator's review)

#475 made the "sent a non-draft from-me message" half of `NON_OWNER` global to build it once per statement. That changed the rule: an alias that sent in thread A became the owner in thread B, where it only appears as a participant, so a shared or list address once replied from vanished from the Low band, and the band disagreed with `participants[].me` (`rows.ts`, still per thread). The rule stays per thread (the same as #464): the accounts set is uncorrelated (built once per statement) and the from-me half is a `NOT EXISTS` probe on the thread's own messages by `thread_id`. The slow oracle (`tests/views/reference.ts`) keeps the per-thread rule, and `people.test.ts` pins it with a two-thread case. Measured on a synthetic 4,000-thread Low band (four participants and an owner message each), best of 10 runs: about 190-250 ms against 323 ms for #464's correlated UNION and 170 ms for the wrong global set, on a loaded machine.

## 2026-11-11 — prov-cal: Retry never repeats a change whose answer was lost

- **Chosen (integrator's decision):** Retry on a hold that failed as `calendar.uncertain` is refused (`conflict` / `calendar.checkFirst`: "Check your calendar: this change may already have gone through. Make it again if it is missing."). A create is idempotent (its event id is derived from the op), an edit is guarded by `If-Match`, but an RSVP email is not, so one rule covers all. A hold waiting for its account to start reads `queued` but no longer `undoable`, because its window has closed.
- **What the owner can see, in one sentence each:** a failed calendar change is never sent again unless they press Retry; after a reload only a screen that kept the change's id can see it; and a change more than 15 minutes late, or for an event that has already started, also waits for Retry.
